# Nano Studio feature modules

Nano Studio (formerly PyIDE) is an Electron + Monaco Python IDE modeled on PyCharm. The core (`main.js`, `preload.js`, `renderer/core/`) owns the window, editor, tabs, project tree, and the registries below. Every IDE capability beyond that is a feature module that owns its own files.

## Layout of a feature named `foo`

- `main/features/foo.js` exports `register(ctx)`. It is loaded automatically at startup, after the main window has started loading, one module per event-loop turn. Use `ctx.ipcMain.handle('foo:…', …)` with channels prefixed by the feature name. Only plugin scripts may call feature channels: `core:features` answers after the last module registered, and core renderer code must not depend on a feature channel.
- `renderer/features/foo/index.js` is loaded automatically after Monaco and the core are ready, before the project opens. All plugin scripts are fetched in parallel and run one after another in load order (`async = false`). Wrap it in an IIFE and use the global `ide`.
- The IDE page loads from its file (`file:`), for which Chromium keeps no V8 code cache. Serving it over a custom scheme with the `codeCache` privilege (`protocol.handle` reading the app's files) was measured on 2026-10-31 with `verify/perf.mjs` (PERF_ONLY=startup, 5 runs, two interleaved rounds). The cache filled (185 entries, 6.3 MB), yet Monaco's ~610 ms stayed the same: it is Monaco initializing, not compiling. Each of the ~180 script requests then crossed main's JavaScript handler, which took the plugin scripts from ~220 to ~390 ms and the start from ~1.41 s to ~1.56 s. Startup work belongs in what Monaco and the plugins do when they load, not in caching their code.
- `renderer/features/foo/style.css` is loaded automatically if present. Reuse the CSS variables in `renderer/core/styles.css` (`--gray1`..`--gray12`, `--blue6`, `--green6`, `--red`, `--yellow`, `--mono`).
- `verify/foo.mjs` plus `verify/fixtures/foo/` hold the end-to-end check (see Verification).
- `renderer/features/foo/plugin.json` makes the feature a bundled plugin: `{id, name, version, vendor, description, main?: ['foo.js'], depends?: [ids], required?: true}`. `main` names its modules in `main/features/`. Disabled plugins load neither their renderer nor their main modules.
- `renderer/features/foo/locales/ko.json` holds its Korean strings (see Localization).

## Nano Studio: the rename from PyIDE

The app is **Nano Studio**. Each start opens one of its two apps, Nano Studio Code (the IDE) or Nano Studio Craft (the Minecraft app); see App modes.

- **Identity** (`package.json`):
  - name `nano-studio`, productName `Nano Studio`, appId `dev.nanostudio.app`, version 0.3.0, a new MSI upgradeCode;
  - installers `NanoStudio-Setup-<v>.exe` and `NanoStudio-<v>.msi`, shortcuts "Nano Studio";
  - the exe is `Nano Studio.exe`, installed per-user in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Nano Studio`;
  - "Open with" on .py uses the ProgID `NanoStudio.py` (`build/installer.nsh`, `build/msi-project.js`).
  - Window titles, the welcome heading, About ("About Nano Studio": the app's title, Code or Craft), the updater, diagnostics reports, the Claude Code IDE lock (`ideName: 'Nano Studio'`; stale locks of a dead Nano Studio or PyIDE are removed), the User-Agent `NanoStudio/<version>`, `TERM_PROGRAM=NanoStudio` and every UI string say Nano Studio.
- **Updates and releases:**
  - The feed is the `nano-studio` release of mandur23/Portfolio-Nano- (`updateFeed`, `build.publish`, `scripts/release.mjs`), which must exist before the first release.
  - electron-updater's cache is `%LOCALAPPDATA%\nano-studio-updater`.
  - An install is NSIS when `Uninstall Nano Studio.exe` sits next to the exe; MSI installs are pointed at `NanoStudio-<v>.msi`.
- **Moving over from PyIDE** (`main/migrate.js`, before anything reads the user data):
  - When `%APPDATA%\Nano Studio` (the user data) has no `settings.json` and no `migrated-from-pyide.json`, and `%APPDATA%\PyIDE` is beside it, Nano Studio copies `plugin-state.json`, `interpreters.json`, `project-roots.json`, `trusted-projects.json`, `plugins\` and, last, `settings.json` (with the Claude Code IDE settings). A stop half-way runs again at the next start, and items that failed to copy (recorded as `failed` in the marker) are tried again at later starts.
  - The trusted projects come along because without that file every recent project would be trusted, also those PyIDE kept in Safe Mode.
  - PyIDE's own update feed setting is dropped. Logs, caches, indexes, local history and backups stay behind.
  - The PyIDE folder is only read: never moved, changed or deleted.
  - The import is logged (`#migrate`) and recorded in `migrated-from-pyide.json`. The first window shows the notification "PyIDE settings imported" once (`core:migrationNotice`).
- **Kept for compatibility:**
  - project descriptors `pyide-*.json`;
  - the install root `~/.pyide`;
  - `PYIDE_*` environment variables;
  - `pyide-preview://` and `--pyide-page`;
  - Gradle `pyide.*` properties;
  - the settings archive's `pyide-settings.json`;
  - the `AboutPyIDE` action id, `pyide.log`, and the Python helpers' module names.
  - Settings Sync exports `product: 'Nano Studio'` and imports archives of both `'Nano Studio'` and `'PyIDE'`; the import dialog names the product that exported the archive.
- **Tests:** `verify/rename-migration.mjs` (ports 9905-9906):
  - plain-Node checks of the identity and of `migrate()`;
  - two real starts on a temp folder laid out like `%APPDATA%`, with `PyIDE\` and `Nano Studio\`: what is copied, what stays, the one-time notice, the PyIDE folder unchanged, the names in the window.

## App modes: Code and Craft

The app runs in one of two modes per process: `code` (the IDE) or `craft` (the Minecraft app). Running both takes two processes; there is still no single-instance lock.

- **Choosing the mode** (`main.js`):
  - `--mode=code|craft` picks it.
  - A file or folder argument means code.
  - Otherwise the start chooser opens: `renderer/chooser.html` with `renderer/core/chooser.js` and `chooser.css`, a 640x380 frameless window from `main/windows.js` `choose()`. It shows a Code card and a Craft card, and has no remember option. Clicking a card opens the main window in that mode (the chooser closes only after the main window exists, so the app does not quit in between). Esc or the close button quits.
  - The chooser page gets only `localeSync`, `loadSettingsSync`, `saveSettingsSync`, `pick` and `close` from the preload (`--pyide-chooser`).
  - `core:relaunch` restarts in the same mode.
- **Plugins declare modes.** `plugin.json` takes `"modes": ["code"]` (the default when absent), `["craft"]` or `["code", "craft"]`. `main/plugin-manager.js` (`setMode`) leaves out plugins of other modes everywhere:
  - renderer entries, the Plugins list and locale dictionaries;
  - main modules: a module is claimed if any plugin of any mode claims it; unclaimed files in `main/features/` still load in both modes;
  - a plugin depending on one from another mode cannot load.
  - Required plugins load in every mode.
  - Craft runs diagnostics, notifications, problems, project-roots, run, settings and terminal (the required plugins), plus minecraft, port-sharing, installer and updater.
- **The renderer** knows its mode from `?mode=` (and the product name from `?product=`): `ide.appMode`, `ide.productName`, `ide.appTitle`. Window titles are "<project> - Nano Studio Code", "Nano Studio Code" without a project, and "Nano Studio Craft"; they follow package.json `productName` through `app.getName()`.
- **Craft** sets `body.app-craft`. CSS hides the toolbar (main menu, project widget, run widget, search), the stripes, the project panel, the editor area (tabs, breadcrumbs), and the status-bar items other than background tasks, IDE errors, update and install status. `#craftHost` fills the window above the bottom tool windows, which start collapsed and show Run consoles when a run opens them.
- **The craft workspace** is `%USERPROFILE%\Nano Studio Craft` (`PYIDE_CRAFT_HOME` for tests).
  - main.js creates it, trusts it (trusted-projects.json) and opens it as the project, so there is no Trust question and `ide.root` works for every Minecraft call.
  - It is never Code mode's last project or a recent project.
  - In craft, `ide.openProject` of another folder and `ide.closeProject` are refused.
  - Once it is open, `ide.emit('craftReady', {host})` fires with `#craftHost`.
- **Tests:** `verify/lib.mjs` `launch()` passes `--mode=code` unless given `mode: 'craft'` or `mode: null`. `verify/app-mode.mjs` (ports 9642-9647) covers the chooser and each card, Esc, Korean, craft chrome, workspace and trust, `craftReady`, plugin sets per mode (bundled and user plugins, renderer scripts and main modules), the restart keeping craft, and a path argument going to code. Screenshots: `chooser.png`, `chooser-ko.png`, `craft-empty.png`.

## Plugins

A plugin is a folder with `plugin.json`, plus optional `index.js`, `style.css`, `locales/<lang>.json`, and main-process modules listed in `main` (paths relative to the plugin folder). It uses exactly the same `ide` API as bundled features. Bundled plugins are the folders in `renderer/features/`. Installed plugins live in `<userData>/plugins/<id>/`. `plugins-catalog/` ships sample plugins that Settings › Plugins › Marketplace installs. `main/plugin-manager.js` owns discovery, enable/disable state (`<userData>/plugin-state.json`), install from a folder or `.zip`, and uninstall. Install copies with `copyTree`, not `fs.cpSync`, because in the installed app `plugins-catalog/` sits inside `app.asar` and `cpSync` cannot read an asar (`verify/plugin-install-asar.mjs`). It copies into `<userData>/plugin-new-*`, renames the installed folder aside to `<userData>/plugin-old-*`, renames the new one in and then deletes the old copy, so a folder another program holds (a helper running in it, an antivirus scan) is never left half deleted: the install fails with "The folder of <id> is in use by another program (EPERM). Close the program and try again." and the old version stays complete (if the second rename fails, the old folder is renamed back). Uninstall renames aside before deleting too. The staging and old folders sit in user data, outside `plugins/`, so discovery never sees them; the next install removes leftovers (staging folders only when older than 10 minutes, since another instance may be installing). Every change applies after a restart (`ide.restart()`), as in PyCharm. A plugin whose `depends` entry is disabled or missing does not load, so features must treat other plugins' APIs (`ide.lsp`, `ide.debugger`, `ide.run`…) as optional. Renderer scripts load in discovery order, except that a plugin's `depends` load before it, so it can use their APIs at load time. An installed plugin whose plugin.json is missing or not valid JSON is listed by its folder name as broken (`broken: 'missing' | 'invalid'`), never loads, and can be uninstalled from the Plugins page; the catalog and the Marketplace's "installed" ignore it, so the Marketplace offers Install to repair it. A main module that fails to load is reported as "Plugin <name> failed to load (<file>)" and kept per file by main/diagnostics (`diagnostics.loadFailure(file)`, not cleared with the error list); `plugins:list` gives its first line as `loadError`, and the Plugins page marks the row "Broken" or "Failed to load" with the reason (`#pluginError`) and offers no Enable/Disable for a broken one (`verify/env-failures.mjs`). A renderer `index.js` that cannot be read or throws while it runs (`loadFeatures` marks each plugin's script with `data-plugin`; renderer/core/diagnostics.js takes an error raised while it is `document.currentScript` as its load failure) is reported once as "Plugin <name> failed to load (index.js): <reason>", kept in `ideDiagnostics.pluginLoadErrors`, and marked "Failed to load" with the reason on the Plugins page like a main module; the plugins after it still load, and an error a plugin throws later is an ordinary internal error (`verify/plugin-load-errors.mjs`, port 9412).

## Plugin Marketplace (online)

Settings › Plugins › Marketplace lists the sample plugins of `plugins-catalog/` together with an online index; for an id in both, the online entry wins when its version is newer. The index is the setting `plugins.marketplaceUrl` (gear › Manage Plugin Repositories…): an https URL, http on 127.0.0.1/localhost, or a local folder (its `index.json`) or `.json` file or `file:` URL. Unset, an installed copy uses `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mandur23/PyIDE/master/marketplace/index.json`; source runs have none; `PYIDE_MARKETPLACE_URL` replaces the default (`off`: none; `verify/lib.mjs` sets `off`); an empty setting turns the online part off. Main reads it when the Plugins page opens (never at startup), at most every 10 minutes per URL unless Retry or the repository dialog asks again (`plugins:marketplace(value, {refresh})` -> `{plugins, online: {state: off|ok|error, error?, hidden?}, defaultUrl}`; each plugin has `origin: bundled|online`, `installedVersion`, `update`).

- Index format 1 (`main/marketplace.js` owns it and checks every field at the boundary; one bad field rejects the index with a message such as `Marketplace index is not valid: plugins[0].sha256 must be 64 hexadecimal digits`): `{format: 1, plugins: [{id, name, version, vendor, description, i18n?: {ko: {name, description}}, minIdeVersion?, url, sha256, size, homepage?}]}`. Ids are 2-64 lowercase letters/digits joined by `.`, `_`, `-` (no Windows device names); versions are semver; `url` is relative to the index or absolute (https, loopback http, or a local file when the index is local); `size` at most 20 MB; `homepage` https. A higher `format` says it needs a newer PyIDE. Index at most 1 MB, 500 plugins. Plugins whose `minIdeVersion` is above this version are hidden (the list says how many).
- Install (`plugins:installListed(id)`, from main's last listing, never a URL from the renderer): an online plugin first shows Install Third-Party Plugin (name, vendor, download URL, and a warning that plugins run with PyIDE's access). The download stops past the index's size, must match its size and sha256, and the zip's `plugin.json` must have the listed id and version; it then installs through `install(zip)`. Update appears in the Marketplace and on the Installed tab (row button, "Version x available" badge, Update in the details) when an installed plugin is older than the listed one. Changes apply after a restart, as before.
- Zips are unpacked by `main/zip.js`, not `tar`: no entry may leave the target folder (`..`, absolute paths, drives, `:`, device names, trailing dots, duplicate names, links), at most 50 MB packed, 100 MB unpacked and 2000 entries, each entry inflated only up to its declared size and CRC-checked. Names without the UTF-8 flag (Windows Explorer's compressed folders) are decoded as CP949 when they are valid CP949, else as CP437 (iconv-lite).
- Failed reads show "Could not load the online Marketplace", the reason and Retry above the bundled samples.
- Publishing: `node scripts/marketplace.mjs [plugins folder] [--out folder]` (defaults `plugins-catalog`, `marketplace`) zips each plugin folder reproducibly (sorted entries, fixed dates: an unchanged plugin keeps its sha256), writes `index.json` with relative URLs, validates it with the same parser, and removes zips the index no longer lists. `plugin.json` may add `minIdeVersion` and `homepage`. Commit `marketplace/` to `master`.
- Test: `verify/marketplace.mjs` (ports 9080-9081; `--pure` runs only the Node part): the script and the committed folder being current, the validator, zip-slip/bomb/CRC/link guards, install, tampered zip, version mismatch, update, offline, a CP949-named zip, a reinstall and an uninstall while a process runs inside the plugin folder (version 1 intact, readable error); then the page with a local HTTP server and a folder index (listing, search, trust dialog and Cancel, tampered download, install, update, bad index, offline and Retry, newer-PyIDE filter, empty = off), and after a restart the plugins running and the page, trust dialog and repository dialog in Korean.

## Editor groups, split panes and windows

`renderer/core/editors.js` owns the editor area. A document (one per file: `{path, model, savedVersion, encoding}`) can be shown in several editor groups. A group is a split pane with its own tab bar and Monaco editor, and groups form a split tree (Split Right `Ctrl+\`, Split Down `Ctrl+Shift+\`, drag a tab onto a pane edge, Unsplit). Clones share the model, so edits appear in every pane. Model decorations (`model.deltaDecorations`) and markers show in every pane automatically.

- `ide.editor` is the active group's editor and changes when the user focuses another pane. Never cache it. Anything attached to an editor (`onMouseDown`, `onDidChangeCursorPosition`, `addAction`, `createDecorationsCollection`, content widgets, view zones) must go through `ide.onEditor(editor => …)`. It runs for every existing editor and for each pane created later, including editors that viewers embed. It returns an unsubscribe function. `ide.editors()` lists them, and `activeEditorChanged(editor)` and `editorCreated(editor)` are events.
- Editor options that apply everywhere (font, wrap, rulers, bracket colors) go through `ide.updateEditorOptions(opts)`, never `ide.editor.updateOptions`.
- Context menu: every editor the core creates (group panes and `ide.createEditor`) shows the IDE's popup instead of Monaco's (`contextmenu: false`), PyCharm style: Show Context Actions, Cut, Copy, Paste and the common actions at the top, the rest under Copy / Paste Special, Refactor, Folding, Go To, Generate, AI, Bookmarks, Local History and Git (only in a repository). `EDITOR_MENU` in `renderer/core/mainmenu.js` names each entry's IDE action and the editor action that does the same in one editor: a tab runs the IDE action, an embedded editor (a notebook cell, a viewer) the editor action. An entry is left out when its IDE action's `when` is false or its editor action's precondition fails. An editor action a plugin adds with a `contextMenuGroupId` that no entry names is listed at the end (`ide.editorMenuActions(editor)`). A right click outside the selection moves the caret there first; the Menu key and Shift+F10 open the menu below the caret without moving it (Monaco's controller would move it to the line start). Test: `verify/context-menu.mjs` (port 9298); suites click its items with `lib.mjs`'s `clickMenuPath(ctx, ['Git', 'Show History'])`.
- Tooltips: Monaco puts a button's tooltip in the editor's container and places it from its measured size; `styles.css` keeps it `max-content` wide, since near the container's right edge it wrapped after it was placed and covered the button (the find widget's Close blinked).
- `ide.tabs` lists one tab per open document plus custom tabs. `ide.findTab(path)` prefers the active group. A file tab has `{kind: 'file', path, title, model, doc, group, pinned, preview}`. `ide.openFile(path, {line, col, focus, group: 'right'|'down'|'window'|group, preview, asText})`. Also `ide.closeTab(path, force)`, `ide.closeAllTabs(force)`, `ide.getDocument(path)`, `ide.saveAll()`, `ide.reloadFromDisk(paths)`.
- Do not rely on DOM ids inside the editor area except `#editor` and `#customHost`, which always belong to the first group. Put overlays in `#editorArea`.
- File viewers: `ide.registerFileViewer({id, match(path), binaryOnly?, text?, mount(host, {path, doc, tab}) -> {dispose?}})`. A matching viewer opens in a custom tab instead of a text editor. `text: true` viewers also get the file's document (use `ide.createEditor(host, {model: doc.model})` to embed an editor that plugins attach to). `binaryOnly` viewers take only files the text loader rejects. "Open as Text" (tab menu, and a button in the binary viewer) bypasses viewers and reads even a file that looks binary as text.
- Detached windows ("Open in New Window", `Shift+F4`) run the same renderer with `ide.detached === true` and load only plugins whose plugin.json has `"detached": true`. They show only editors. A document open in one window is that window's alone; once another window opens it, main/windows.js holds the shared text and numbers the edits: each window sends Monaco's change deltas with the revision they were made on (`doc:op`), an edit made on an older revision is refused (`doc:nack`) and sent again shifted past the edits it missed (operational transformation in editors.js), so edits typed in two windows at once are all kept. Ties follow the shared order in every window: a replacement's new text goes where a plain insertion at the start of its range would, and text one window typed inside a range another replaced lands after the replacing text (`verify/doc-sync-fuzz.mjs`: every pair of one-change edits on a short text, and 20,000 random two-window sessions under the main process's protocol, end with one text). When only one window is left the document is unshared again. Saves, reloads and encoding changes are told to the other windows (`doc:saved`), and renames and deletes too (`ide.documentsMoved(from, to | null)`, called by the tree, the refactor plugin and any plugin that renames files: documents follow with their unsaved text, tabs keep their group, position, pin and caret, deleted ones close in every window, compare tabs included). An explicit save of a document whose file no longer exists asks "{name} no longer exists on disk. Save it again?"; auto save skips it. Closing the main window asks once about every window's unsaved files (one Save Changes? dialog listing them; Save All saves each window's own, Cancel keeps every window), and closes nothing before the answer. Op positions and text count a line break as one '\n' whatever the separator (main holds the shared text that way, with its `eol`), so Convert line separators is an op without text (`{c: [], eol}`) and edits another window makes at that moment keep their place. Text typed in a window while it joins a shared document is kept: it is moved past the difference between its text and the shared text and sent (`verify/multi-window.mjs`, also a CRLF file and a conversion during edits).
- Encoding: documents are read and written through `fs:readText` / `fs:writeText` (UTF-8, UTF-8 BOM, UTF-16 with or without a BOM, CP949), and the encoding is kept on save. A file that is not valid UTF-8 is CP949 only when CP949 decodes it cleanly; otherwise UTF-8 with replacement characters. UTF-16 without a BOM is recognized by its many NUL bytes beside ASCII ones (`main/text-codec.js`). Changes on disk (focus checks, auto save, File Cache Conflict) are checked by decoding the file in the document's own encoding (`fs:decode`); a reload, Load File System Changes and `ide.reloadFromDisk` detect the encoding again unless the user chose it (Reload in, Convert to, Open as Text pin it), so a file re-encoded elsewhere keeps its new encoding (the same text in another encoding is adopted only when the old encoding no longer reads the file). Convert to saves through the normal save (File Cache Conflict, save actions, other windows told), also for a modified document, and is undone with a message when PyIDE could not read the result back (BOM-less UTF-16 of Korean-only text looks binary). A CP949 save refusal (characters CP949 cannot store) is an `expected` error with a translated message, never an internal error. Plugins that write files that may be open should still use `fs:write` and then `ide.reloadFromDisk`.
- Safe save (`replaceFile` in main/text-codec.js): a save writes `.<name>.<pid>.pyide-tmp` next to the file and renames it over (a symlink's target is the one replaced), so a full disk or a crash never leaves a half-written file and a failed temp write leaves the original untouched. It writes in place, as before, for a new file, a hard-linked file (its other names see the save), a folder where the temp file cannot be created and a file held open without delete sharing; a read-only file is still refused. The temp file starts as a copy of the file (`fs.copyFileSync`, CopyFile), so the file's attributes (Hidden, System…) and alternate data streams (Zone.Identifier) carry over, and the new text is written over the copy (opened `r+` and cut to length: Windows refuses to recreate a Hidden or System file; the in-place writes do the same, so a Hidden hard-linked file saves too). A 2.4 KB save takes about 3 ms instead of 1 ms, mostly opening the fresh copy (`verify/encodings.mjs`). Known limit of the rename: the saved file gets the folder's inherited ACL instead of its own (CopyFile does not copy it; ReplaceFileW would, but Node has no binding for it).
- Case-sensitive folders (`fsutil file setCaseSensitiveInfo <dir> enable`, folders WSL made): a file's identity is `ide.util.pathKey(path)`, the lower-cased path except the names inside such folders, which keep their case, so a.py and A.py there are two documents with their own tabs, saves, problems and shared edits; elsewhere names that differ only in case are one file, as before. `samePath` and `isUnder` compare keys. main/path-case.js finds the folders without a child process: a folder is case-sensitive when one of its entries, looked up with its letters' case swapped, is missing or another file; the answer is cached (a folder without a lettered entry is asked again). The renderer asks (`fs:caseSensitiveDirs`, `ide.learnPathCase(folder)`) when a project opens and with each document's first read, and main keys shared documents the same way (`verify/case-sensitive.mjs`, port 9413). Find in Files and the TODO scan key unsaved texts the same way (main/features/search.js keys them with path-case.js's `pathKey` and passes the case-sensitive folders to its worker), so a.py and A.py there are each searched in their own unsaved text (`verify/search-cache.mjs`). Known limits: the project tree's own keys and crash-recovery backup names stay lower-case, and a folder whose case sensitivity is changed while PyIDE runs keeps its first answer until a restart.
- Tests: `verify/encodings.mjs` (port 9270): stray-byte UTF-8, a reload encoding that sticks, re-encodings made outside, Convert to on a modified document and an unreadable conversion, the CP949 refusal (no internal error), a CRLF notebook, and the codec's safe write (a failed write, hard links, read-only). `verify/multi-window.mjs` (port 9271): edits typed in two windows at once (also random concurrent edits converging), a reload, rename (API and the tree's Rename) and delete across windows, a save of a deleted file, File › Exit with unsaved files in another window.
- Files dragged in from Explorer onto a pane open there (a pane edge splits, as for a tab), also over the editor text. The pane takes tab, tree-row and file drags in the capture phase, before Monaco's drop-into-editor (other drags, such as plain text, still reach Monaco), and offers files as a copy, since a copy-only source refuses 'move' and then sends no drop at all. A dropped path's kind comes from `ide.api.stat(path)` (`fs:stat` -> `{isDirectory, size, mtimeMs}` or null), never from a failed `fs:list`, which main records as an internal error. Tested in `verify/editors.mjs` (real CDP drags with file paths: the tab strip, over the text of an open file, a pane edge).
- Dragging out of the project tree: a drag the user starts on a row (a trusted `dragstart`) is a system drag of the selected rows' files and folders, through main's `tree:startDrag` (`webContents.startDrag` with the file's shell icon), so Explorer, the desktop and other apps (mail, chat) take them. Windows allows such a drag to copy or link, never move, so nothing leaves the project. Back over the window it arrives as files: `ide.treeDrag` (`{paths, entry}`) tells the tree, which still moves the row onto a folder (showing a copy cursor), and the editors, which open a file and never open a dragged folder as a project. Main reports the drag's end (`tree:dragEnded`, after Windows' nested drag loop returns) and the renderer forgets the drag 1.5 s later, so a drop still on its way is not taken for one from Explorer. Synthetic drags (the suites') stay in the page with `ide.TREE_PATH_TYPE`. `verify/tree-drag-out.mjs` (port 9789, in CI) drags with real mouse events through CDP, intercepts the request to main, and checks the selection, a move onto a folder, a folder dropped on the editor, a file opened there, and main's handler run with no button held.

## Localization

UI text stays in English in the source and is translated by lookup, gettext style: the global `tr('Find in Files')`, or with placeholders `tr('Run {name}', { name })`. It is also available as `ide.t`. Each plugin's `locales/ko.json` maps the exact English string to Korean. A disabled plugin's file is not loaded, except for the entries for its `plugin.json` name and description, so the Plugins page still shows those in Korean. `renderer/locales/ko.json` covers the core. The language (`appearance.language`: `system`, `en`, `ko`) is fixed for a session, so call `tr()` wherever text is produced. Calling it at module load is fine. Static markup uses `data-i18n`, `data-i18n-title` and `data-i18n-placeholder`. Never translate user data: code, file names, paths, process output, package names, git messages. Keep product names (Python, Git, Pyright, ruff, pytest, pip, venv) and key chords in English, as PyCharm's Korean language pack does. Use PyCharm's Korean terminology, for example 프로젝트, 실행, 디버그, 터미널, 문제, 커밋, 구조, 설정, 인터프리터, 중단점, 단계 건너뛰기 (Step Over), 단계 진입 (Step Into), 단계 나가기 (Step Out), 프로그램 재개, 파일에서 찾기, 사용 위치 찾기, 이름 변경, 코드 서식 다시 지정 (Reformat Code), import 최적화. `verify/i18n-lib.mjs` has `englishLeaks(ctx, selector)`, which lists visible English UI text, and `missingTranslations(ctx)`, which lists `tr()` strings without a Korean entry. Launch with `launch({..., lang: 'ko-KR'})` to test Korean. Tests default to English. Pyright runs in the UI language: `lsp:start(root, python, locale)` passes it and main sets `VSCODE_NLS_CONFIG={"locale": …}` (Windows sets none of the variables Pyright otherwise reads), so its diagnostics are Korean in a Korean session (`verify/i18n-lsp.mjs`). Features that read Pyright diagnostics use `code` and the range, never the English message text; the import quick fix takes the undefined name from the diagnostic's range.

## Main-process context (`ctx`)

`ipcMain`, `app`, `send(channel, payload)` to the renderer, `startProcess(cmd, args, {cwd, env}) -> id` (output streams to the renderer as process events, see `ide.proc`; a pipe error is logged, never thrown, and a spawn that fails with ENOENT in a working directory longer than Windows allows (258 characters) prints "Cannot start <cmd>: the working directory path is too long for Windows (N characters): <cwd>"), `killProcess(id)`, `procs` (id -> ChildProcess, for stdin), `getWindow()`, `appDir`, `unpackedDir`, `vendorDir`, `diagnostics` (the IDE log and internal errors, see Diagnostics; also `require('../diagnostics')` from any main module).

`appDir` is `resources/app.asar` in an installed build: fine for `require` and `fs` reads, but other programs cannot open files inside it. Paths handed to another program (Python scripts, `ruff.exe`, pyright run as Node) use `unpackedDir`, the same tree on disk (`app.asar.unpacked`; equal to `appDir` from source), and must match `build.asarUnpack` in package.json. `vendorDir` is already under `unpackedDir`.

Bundled tools: `node_modules/pyright` (`langserver.index.js`, run with `process.execPath` and env `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`), and `vendor/` (a pip `--target` dir with `debugpy` and `ruff`; run ruff via `vendor/bin/ruff.exe`, and debugpy with the selected interpreter and `PYTHONPATH=vendor`).

## Renderer API (`window.ide`)

- State: `ide.root`, `ide.monaco`, `ide.editor`, `ide.tabs`, `ide.activeTab()`, `ide.findTab(path)`, `ide.currentInterp()` -> `{cmd, args, executable, version, label}`, `ide.interps`, `ide.reloadInterpreters()`. `ide.interps` comes from `py:interpreters` in `main.js`: the project's venvs (trusted projects only), `interpreters.extra`, then the py launcher and `python` from PATH, all probed at once. It keeps one entry per real file (`fs.promises.realpath` of each `sys.executable`), so a junction or an 8.3 spelling of a listed python.exe appears once in the status-bar picker (`verify/new-project.mjs` checks it). When `interpreter.<project>` names an interpreter that is not listed, main's `py:diagnose(root, exe)` (-> `{state: ok|missing|fails|untrusted, version|detail}`, stderr decoded as UTF-8, else CP949) says why: one that starts is added as a normal entry; one that is gone or does not start stays selected as `{invalid: true, reason, name, venv}`, with "Invalid interpreter: <name>" in red in the status bar and one warning notification per project, executable and state offering Choose Interpreter… and Recreate .venv. While it is selected `ide.currentInterp()` is null, so Run, the console and the rest say "No Python interpreter found." instead of running another Python, and code that falls back to another interpreter skips invalid entries (the New Project wizard, diagrams, Check RegExp, semantic highlighting). In Safe Mode project venvs are not started, as before. Entries older than Python 3.8 carry `unsupported: true` and the label "… [unsupported]"; selecting one warns once per executable ("need Python 3.8 or newer", with Choose Interpreter…) and it stays selectable. Test: `verify/interpreter-health.mjs` (port 9274).
- IPC: `ide.api.invoke(channel, ...args)`, `ide.api.on(channel, fn)`, plus `ide.api.read/write/list/stat/walk/exists/create/rename/trash/openFolder/openFileDialog`. `fs:realpath` resolves links, junctions and 8.3 names (the path itself when it cannot). `fs:walk` skips a folder below the root it cannot read (the root itself still fails). Errors from worker threads (`main/worker-host.js`: fs-scan, search, workspace) keep their `code` (EPERM, ENOENT), so diagnostics files a user's file problem on `fs:list` or `fs:walk` as expected, not as an internal error (`verify/tool-failures.mjs`).
- Events (`ide.on(name, fn)` returns an unsubscribe): `ready`, `projectOpened(root)`, `fileOpened(tab)`, `activeTabChanged(tab|null)`, `beforeSave(tab)`, `fileSaved(tab)`, `fileClosed(path)`, `filesChanged({created?, deleted?, renamed?})`, `interpreterChanged(interp)`, `problemsChanged`, `settingsChanged(key, value)`, `toolWindowShown(id)`, `treeRefreshed`.
- Files and tabs: `ide.openFile(path, {line, col, focus})`, `ide.openCustomTab({id, title, icon, mount(host, tab), onClose})` for non-text editors such as a diff view, `ide.closeTab(path, force)`, `ide.saveAll()`, `ide.reloadFromDisk(paths?)` after a tool rewrote files, `ide.isDirty(tab)`, `ide.renderTabs()`. A file tab is `{kind: 'file', path, title, model, savedVersion}`.
- Actions and keymap: `ide.registerAction({id, title, keys, run, when, terminal, context})`. While a terminal has focus, only actions with `terminal: true` fire (tool-window toggles do automatically), so programs such as Claude Code keep Esc, Ctrl+K, Ctrl+T and the rest. Keys look like `'Ctrl+Shift+F10'`, `'Alt+ArrowLeft'`, `'Shift Shift'`. The last registration wins on a clash, so use PyCharm's default keymap and do not take a key another feature owns (table below). An action registered without `keys` takes PyCharm's key from the core table `KEYMAP_DEFAULTS` (`renderer/core/ide.js`) when its id is there (GotoImplementation, GotoSuperMethod, GotoTest, Rerun), as PyCharm's keymap binds keys by action id; `keys: []` means no key. `ide.runAction(id)`. The user can change any action's keys in Settings › Keymap (see Keymap editing), so read `action.keys` (or `ide.keymap.label(id)`) when showing a shortcut instead of writing it into the text. `context: 'editor' | 'projectView'` says where `when` lets the action fire (core's editor actions with `when: inEditor` get `'editor'`); two actions of different contexts never fire together, so Settings › Keymap does not report them as a conflict (Ctrl+D: Duplicate Line in the editor, compare's Compare With… in the project tree, as in PyCharm).
- Tool windows: `ide.registerToolWindow({id, title, icon, side: 'left'|'bottom'|'right', keys, order, mount(container, tw)})` returns `tw` with `show()`, `hide()`, `toggle()`, `isVisible()`, `setActions([{icon, title, run, id?, toggled?}])` (`id` becomes the button's `data-action`; a boolean `toggled` draws a pressed toggle button with `aria-pressed`), `setBadge(text)`. The sidebar shows the pinned tool windows (`sidebar.pinned`) in the order they were pinned, so one pinned from More Tool Windows goes to the end of its group and of the bottom tabs, as in PyCharm; a window shown while unpinned follows them by `order`.
- `ide.util.observeSize(el, fn)`: a ResizeObserver that calls `fn(contentRect)` in the next animation frame and only when the size changed, so a callback that changes layout cannot loop. Use it instead of `new ResizeObserver`. The browser's `ResizeObserver loop` notice is logged once per window as a warning, never listed as an internal error (`renderer/core/diagnostics.js`; test `verify/resize-observer.mjs`, port 9875).
- UI icons: `ide.icon(name, fallback?)` returns a 16px icon element with `data-icon-name`. The names come from `window.PYIDE_ICONS`: `renderer/core/icons.js` (tool window and toolbar icons from PyCharm's jars, built by `tools/build-icons.mjs`), `renderer/core/icons-ui.js` (action, node, status and file type icons from the IntelliJ Community repository at a fixed commit, built by `tools/build-ui-icons.mjs`, which also writes the dark drawings of the colored icons, icons.js's included, to `window.PYIDE_ICONS_DARK`; CSS shows the drawing of the theme) and `renderer/core/icons-extra.js` (PyIDE's own, drawn on the same grid: AI chat, hierarchy, Minecraft, game view, Claude, sparkle, cart). Every `icon` a plugin passes is such a name: tool window actions, menu and popup items, custom tabs, run configuration types, a run or debug tab's `setIcon(name, cls)`, the launchers' kinds and the project wizard's types. A text glyph still works and shows as text in black and white; an unknown name shows nothing. A file viewer's tab shows its file's type icon, as a text editor's does. Test: `verify/ui-icons.mjs` (port 9630, in CI).
- File type icons: `ide.fileIcon(nameOrPath, {dir})` returns a 16px icon element (PyCharm New UI file type icons, both theme drawings inside, CSS shows one) and `ide.fileIconType(...)` its type; names (Dockerfile, .gitignore, requirements*.txt...) win over extensions, unknown files get `anyType`. The table is in `renderer/core/file-icons.js`; the drawings are generated into `renderer/core/file-icons-data.js` by `tools/build-file-icons.mjs` (video, audio and PDF, which PyCharm lacks, are drawn in `tools/file-icons/`). Popup items accept an icon element. Test: `verify/file-icons.mjs` (port 9800).
- Toolbar and status bar: `ide.registerToolbarItem({side: 'left'|'right', order, el})`, `ide.registerStatusItem({order, el})`. The main toolbar follows PyCharm's New UI. Left: main menu (`Alt+\`), project widget, branch widget (vcs order 10), workspace home (20). Right, by order: right-side items ordered 60-99 go inside the run widget pill (run target 60, run 70, debug 80, stop 90), then Claude Code 150, Search Everywhere 180, settings gear 200. The interpreter picker is the status-bar widget; `ide.setInterpreter(executable)` selects one. `ide.icon(name, fallbackGlyph)` returns a PyCharm New UI SVG icon from `renderer/core/icons.js` (generated by `tools/build-icons.mjs`).
- Projects: `ide.beforeProjectChange` (an array of async hooks `(nextDir, {reload}) => false` to cancel, asked by `openProject`, `closeProject` and Trust Project before anything closes), `ide.openProject(dir)`, `ide.closeProject()` (back to the welcome screen, emits `projectClosed`), `ide.newProject()` (the New Project wizard, `renderer/core/project-wizard.js` + `main/new-project.js`), recent projects in the `recentProjects` setting. `ide.openProject(dir)` answers false when another open (or Close Project) started meanwhile: after each await (Trust, `beforeProjectChange` hooks, closing tabs, tree and interpreters) an overtaken open stops, so only the latest sets `ide.root` and emits `projectOpened`. Recent Projects is built from the list on disk (unless this window's own change is not written yet), so another instance's projects stay (`verify/core-lifecycle.mjs`). With no project the window is the welcome screen: `body.no-project` (set at startup without a project and by `closeProject`, removed by `openProject`) hides the tool window stripes, panels, splitters and the run widget, and `ide.actionEnabled(action)` is false for tool window activations (`needsProject: true`) and the run/debug actions (`PROJECT_ACTIONS` in ide.js), so `runAction` and the keymap refuse them and the main menu shows them, and View › Tool Windows, disabled. The layout change is CSS only, so a project opened afterwards gets its layout back as it was. Plugins add New Project types with `(ide.projectTypes ||= []).push({id, name(), icon(), main, form({name, dir, step})})`: the form's element replaces the interpreter section, and its `files()`, `gitignore`, `validate()`, `setup(dir)`, `opened(dir)` and `main()` replace the Python steps (see Java and C/C++). In the New Project wizard the project types are a listbox (`ide.ui.listKeys`) and the interpreter lists keep one entry per python.exe (`fs:realpath`); Add Local Interpreter… with a file already listed selects that entry. Tested in `verify/new-project.mjs`. The main menu (`renderer/core/mainmenu.js`) lists actions by id and hides those whose plugin is disabled. Toolbar left: VCS branch 10. Bottom tool window orders: Run 10, Debug 15, Find 18, Hierarchy 19, Find in Files 20, Python Console 25, Terminal 30, Problems 40, Git 45, TODO 50, Python Packages 60, Coverage 65, Profiler 66, HTTP Client 68. Until a window is shown, an area's default window is the one with the lowest order.
- Project tree: `ide.registerTreeDecorator(entry => ({color, badge, iconType}))` (`iconType`: a file icon type drawn instead of the entry's own; the first decorator that gives one wins), `ide.registerTreeAction({title, when(entry), run(entry)})`, `ide.refreshTree()`, `ide.revealInTree(path)`, `ide.selectedTreeEntry()`. The tree is a flat list of visible rows built from listings of the open folders: expanding or collapsing a folder lists only that folder (and its open subfolders, level by level in parallel) and splices its rows, and `refreshTree()` lists every open folder again. Only rows in view have DOM nodes (`#tree .node`, 24 px each), so code that looks for a row must scroll it into view first, and decorators run for drawn rows only (keep them cheap, a map lookup). Keys while the tree has focus: Up/Down/Home/End/PageUp/PageDown move the selection, Right opens or enters a folder, Left closes it or goes to the parent, Enter opens a file or toggles a folder, Delete moves to the Recycle Bin. Dragging a row onto a folder moves it there after a confirmation; dragging it onto an editor opens it (data type `ide.TREE_PATH_TYPE`). Files and folders dragged in from Explorer are copied (`fs:copyInto`, originals kept) into the folder under the pointer, the folder of the file under it, or the project root below the rows, as in PyCharm; names that exist there ask first (Copy: Overwrite, Skip, Cancel). Each source is copied on its own with `fs.promises.cp` (`fs.cpSync` aborts Electron 38's main process on non-ASCII paths such as 과제), and `fs:copyInto` answers `{copied, failed: [{name, code, error}]}`: the failures are listed by name in one message (a second dropped item with the name of an earlier one is not copied over it), and what was copied is still shown and reported in `filesChanged`. Dropped anywhere else in the window they still open (a folder as the project). Tested in `verify/tree.mjs` with real CDP drags (`Input.dispatchDragEvent` with file paths), including a Korean folder with nested files, overwrites, and a mixed drop with failures. The context menu starts with PyCharm's New ›: File, Directory, Python File (Alt+Insert; core's own item only when no plugin offers Python File), Python Package and the plugins' items; an `ide.treeNewItems` item labelled New with a submenu is merged into it, other items stay at the top level (also tested in `verify/tree.mjs`). A row cut with Ctrl+X or Cut is drawn dimmed (class `cut`) until it is pasted, Escape in the tree, another cut, or the project closes; the mark is drawn from the cut entry, so it survives scrolling and refreshes. Open folders are saved per project in the `projectTree.expanded` setting and listed again when the project opens; open folders under `ide.registerTreeRoot` nodes (Scratches and Consoles) are one global setting, `projectTree.rootsExpanded` (`{rootId: [paths relative to the node, '' for the node]}`), restored in every project and after a restart; a folder inside a closed node keeps its state. `treeRefreshed` fires after `refreshTree()`, not after an expand or collapse.
- Problems: `ide.problems.set(source, path, [{line, col, endLine, endCol, msg, severity: 'error'|'warning'|'info'}])`, `ide.problems.clear(source)`, `ide.problems.all()`. Markers on open models are handled for you.
- Processes: after a main handler returns an id from `ctx.startProcess`, call `ide.proc.attach(id, {data(text, stream), exit(code)})`. Output that arrived early is replayed. Also `ide.proc.input(id, text)`, `ide.proc.kill(id)`.
- UI: `ide.ui.console(host, {input, placeholder, onInput})` gives a console for program output: clickable tracebacks, `\r` rewriting the current line, `\b`, ANSI colors (16 themed colors, 256 and 24-bit, bold, dim, italic, underline; `ide.ui.ansi.fragment(text)` and `ide.ui.ansi.strip(text)` for other output) and a cap of `console.cycleBufferKB` (1024 KB; Settings › Editor › General › Console: Override console cycle buffer size (KB), 64-1,048,576, unticked means 1024) that drops the oldest lines. `append(text, cls)` writes at once; only the scroll to the end waits for the next frame. A stray ESC ] (an OSC with no end) or the start of an escape sequence longer than a real one (an OSC start over 510 characters, a CSI start over 32; an OSC never spans a line break) is written out as text instead of holding back later output, and an escape sequence split by the other stream's output still applies (one held tail per stream). `ide.ui.pick({placeholder, items | async query => items})` with items `{label, detail, hint, icon, value}`. `ide.ui.prompt(label, initial)`. `ide.ui.contextMenu(x, y, items)` with items `{label, hint, icon, checked, disabled, detail, run, submenu: items | () => items}` or `{separator: true}` / `{header}` (submenus open on hover or Right; Up/Down/Enter/Left/Esc work). `ide.ui.dialog({title, body, buttons, width})` focuses its first field (else the primary button), keeps Tab/Shift+Tab inside (from a focused element that is no Tab stop, such as a scroller Chromium focuses, Tab goes to the next stop after it and Shift+Tab to the one before), presses the primary button on Enter (except in a textarea or on another focused button; a dialog without a primary button leaves Enter to its content), cancels on Esc and gives the keyboard back to where it was; popups do the same on close. `ide.ui.listKeys(host, {rows, selected, select, open, expanded, toggle, parent, id})` makes a tool window's list or tree keyboard-driven like PyCharm's (Up/Down/Home/End, Enter, Right/Left to expand, collapse or go to the parent). A tool window opened by its shortcut takes the keyboard (its field or list, else the window); Esc goes back to the editor and Shift+Esc hides the window that has the keyboard. Test: `verify/keyboard.mjs` (port 9843). `ide.ui.addConsoleLink(pattern, link)` adds a link kind to every console: `pattern` is a global regex over program output and `link(match)` returns the element shown in place of the match, or null to leave it as text (traceback frames are the built-in kind; import-install adds the install button).
- Settings: `ide.settings.get(key, fallback)`, `ide.settings.set(key, value)`, persisted and broadcast via `settingsChanged`. The changes made in one task are written to `settings.json` together right after it (`ide.settings.flush()` writes now); pending changes are written before the window unloads and before `ide.restart()`. Main-process code that reads `settings.json` therefore sees a change one task later, not during the `set` call. A write carries only the keys the window set since its last write (`settings:saveSync(settings, changedKeys)`); main merges them onto the file's current content under an exclusive `settings.json.lock` (stale after 5 s, given up after 1 s; an undefined value removes the key) through a per-process temp file, so two PyIDE instances on one user data folder, and detached windows, keep each other's settings. With no file (or no key list) the settings are written whole; an unreadable file is still kept as `settings.json.bak`. A failed write keeps the keys pending and is retried every 2 s; when the retry fails too, one "Settings cannot be saved" notification says why (again only after a write succeeded). Test: `verify/settings-instances.mjs` (ports 9274-9275).
- Log: `ide.log.info/warn/error/debug(msg)` or `ide.logger('#my-plugin')` writes to the IDE log in every window (see Diagnostics).
- Helpers: `ide.util.el(tag, cls, text)`, `basename`, `dirname`, `joinPath`, `samePath`, `isUnder(path, dir)` (same path or inside it), `pathKey(path)` (a path's identity: lower case, except names inside a case-sensitive folder; `samePath` and `isUnder` compare it), `rel(path)`. `ide.util.errorText(e)` turns an IPC error into a sentence: network failures (ECONNREFUSED, ECONNRESET, ENOTFOUND, ETIMEDOUT, EAI_AGAIN, EHOSTUNREACH, ENETUNREACH, `net::ERR_*`) read "Cannot reach the server ({code}). Check the internet connection or the proxy settings." unless the text names a loopback address (127.x, localhost, ::1: a local helper such as debugpy keeps its own text), and "spawn X ENOENT" reads "X is not installed or not on PATH." (`verify/core-lifecycle.mjs`).

## Cross-feature APIs

- `ide.lsp` (lsp): `request(method, params, token?)` -> result or null (a Monaco CancellationToken: once cancelled the request answers null and Pyright drops it if it has not started on it; pass the provider's token from Monaco providers), `documentSymbols(path)` -> LSP DocumentSymbol[] with `kindName` and 1-based line/col (an open file's come from one shared request, see Runtime performance), `ready()` -> Promise<bool>, `status()`, `restart()`. Pyright is the only syntax/type diagnostics source (`ide.problems` source `'pyright'`). A Pyright process that exits on its own (an `lsp:status` error of the current server) is restarted after 0.5, 1, 2, 4 and 8 s; a restart that fails to start counts as a crash. After more than 5 crashes within 3 minutes it stays stopped and an error notification ("Pyright stopped … crashed N times in the last 3 minutes") offers Restart. A user restart (status bar, action, notification, `ide.lsp.restart()`), another project or another interpreter start the count over; closing the project cancels a pending restart. Each distinct crash is still one internal error ("Pyright language server exited unexpectedly"), since diagnostics watches the process (`verify/run-debug-edges.mjs`). Language servers: `main/lsp-server.js` runs one server per project under a feature's channels (`serve(ctx, channel, makeSpec)`: `<channel>:start/stop/request/cancel/notify/log`, events `<channel>:status/diagnostics/message/applyEdit`), with what differs between servers in a spec (command, settings, watched files, when it is ready); main/features/lsp.js gives Pyright's (channel `lsp`). In the renderer `ide.lsp.createClient(spec)` adds another language's client, with its own status item, document sync, diagnostics (`ide.problems` source of its own) and the same Monaco providers; Ctrl+B, Alt+F7, Shift+F6, Ctrl+Q, Alt+Enter and F2 go to the client of the file in the editor. `ide.lsp.clientFor(path)` finds it, and `ide.lsp.editedText(tab, 'organizeImports' | 'format')` gives a file's text after its server's own Optimize Imports or Reformat Code (null when no server other than Pyright handles it), which the tooling plugin's Ctrl+Alt+O and Ctrl+Alt+L use for such files. A workspace edit may create, rename and delete files (a Java class renamed with its file, a package moved): a file it creates is written with its text, renames and deletes come after the text edits (edits to a file the edit moves, or to a file in a folder it moves, apply at the old place first) into folders made as needed, and open documents follow through `ide.documentsMoved`. A closed file that cannot be read refuses the whole edit before anything changes; files that could not be written, moved or deleted afterwards are named in one message. `stop()` sends shutdown and exit and kills the server after the spec's `shutdownMs`; a spec with `exclusiveData` (JDT LS, which locks its workspace folder) is not started again before the old process has exited.
- `ide.run` (run): `registerType` (a type with `needsInterp: false` starts no Python: it needs no interpreter, `start` gets `interp` null and executors do not apply), `registerGutterProvider`, `runConfig(cfg, {executor?, confirm?})` (see Stop and rerun), `debugConfig(cfg, opts)` (see Debugging tests; a type with `debug(cfg, opts)` debugs its configurations itself, as Java Application does), `debugSession(cfg)` (a Run window tab apart from the configuration's runs, for what the debugger does not show; no bundled type uses it since the Test Results moved into the Debug window), `runContext`, `runSelected(opts)`, `addTemporary`, `editConfigurations`, `sessions`, `activeSession`, `resolvePython(cfg)` (with `interpOptions` and `stdin`), `registerExecutor` (see Coverage and profiling). `ide.tests` (tests) and `ide.pyconsole` (pyconsole: `execute`, `restart`, `interrupt`, `isRunning`, `evaluate`; see Python Console and Debug console input).
- Debugger: action ids `Debug` (Shift+F9) and `StopDebug` (Ctrl+F2 while a session is active, registered on `ready`), event `debugSessionChanged(active)`; the toolbar Stop button stops a debug session when no run is active. `ide.debugger.debugJvm(spec)` debugs a JVM through its JDWP agent (see Java debugging) and resolves the same way. `ide.debugger.debugFile(file, cfg)` resolves to `{ended}`, a promise of `{replaced}` that settles when that session ends, and `cfg.tabs` adds plugin sub-tabs to the session (see Debugging tests). `SetValue` (F2) and `CopyValue` (Ctrl+C) act on the Variables and Watches lists (see Variables: Set Value).
- `ide.projectRoots` (project-roots): the project's Sources, Test Sources and Excluded folders (see Source roots (Mark Directory As)).
- `ide.openSettings(pageId)` (settings) and `ide.tooling` (`reformat`, `optimizeImports`, `fixAll`, `lint`). Ruff lint results use problems source `'ruff'`. `tooling:check` answers the diagnostics array, or `{error, config?, line?, col?}` when ruff could not check (exit code 2: a configuration it cannot read, its "Cause:" chain made one sentence such as "Failed to parse ruff.toml: TOML parse error at line 1, column 15: …"; -1: ruff.exe did not start). The plugin then shows one error problem (source `'ruff'`) on the configuration file at that place, or on the checked file when no configuration is named, and an editor hint once per distinct error, cleared by the next successful lint; Reformat, Optimize Imports and Fix All give the same text. An unreadable pyproject.toml no longer throws (`verify/tool-failures.mjs`).
- `ide.database` (database): `addDataSource(cfg)` -> id, `list()`, `query(id, sql, opts)`, `openConsole(id)`, `drivers()`, `activeConsole()`.
- `ide.installer` (installer): `open(category?)`, `refresh()`, `register(item)`, `pip({package, name, description, category})`, `run(id, op = 'install')` (an item's operation started from elsewhere, with its card log, task and notification; it resolves when the operation ends and `state(id)` says how), `job(spec, log, progress, signal)` (a job kind of `main/features/installer.js` for another plugin's installable). Any plugin lists installables by pushing to `ide.installables` and emitting `installablesChanged` (see Install Manager below), so load order does not matter.
- Settings pages: a plugin pushes `{id, title: () => string, keywords, group?: 'ai' | 'editor', defaults?: {key: value}, index?: false, mount(host, ctx)}` to `ide.settingsPages` (`group: 'editor'` lists the page under Editor; `defaults` are what its controls show for unset keys; `index: false` keeps Settings search from mounting it to read its labels, see Dialog and Settings layout rules); `ctx.ui` has `section`, `fieldRow`, `checkbox`, `numberInput(key, {min, max, fallback})` and `selectInput(key, options, fallback)`, whose values follow the dialog's OK/Apply/Cancel.
- `ide.aiComplete` (ai-complete): `status()`, `mode()`, `isVisible(editor)`. `ide.aiEdit` (ai-edit): `open`, `session`, `nes`, `nesEnabled`, `setNes`.
- `ide.localHistory` (local-history): `checkpoint(label, {paths, user})` before anything that rewrites files (English label such as `'Before Refactoring'`, translated where it is shown; `user: true` for a name the user typed), `show(path, isDir)`, `putLabel()`, `ready()`. `ide.bookmarks` (bookmarks): `list()`, `toggle()`, `gotoMnemonic(c)`, `showPopup()`, `show()`.
- Key handoffs through `when`: Ctrl+F2 is run's Stop unless a debug session is active; Ctrl+Shift+F10 is run's context run unless the caret is in a test (tests); Ctrl+1..3 focus editor groups (core) unless a numbered bookmark with that digit exists (bookmarks).

## Keymap ownership (PyCharm default keymap)

| Owner | Keys |
| --- | --- |
| core | Ctrl+S, Ctrl+Shift+N, Shift Shift, Ctrl+Shift+A, Ctrl+F4, Alt+Left/Right, Alt+Insert, Shift+Esc, Esc, Ctrl+D, Ctrl+Y, Ctrl+/, Ctrl+Shift+/ (Comment with Block Comment), Ctrl+G, Ctrl+F, Ctrl+R, Ctrl+W, Ctrl+Shift+W, Alt+Shift+Up/Down, Ctrl+-/=, Alt+1 (Open Folder… and Reopen Closed Tab have no key, as in PyCharm) |
| run (built in) | Shift+F10, Ctrl+Shift+F10, Ctrl+F2, Alt+4, Ctrl+F5 (Rerun, through the core keymap table) |
| terminal | Alt+F12, Ctrl+Esc (Claude Code), Ctrl+Alt+K (send file reference to Claude Code), Ctrl+Shift+T inside a terminal (new session) |
| problems (built in) | Alt+6 |
| lsp (Python, and Java through java-lsp) | Ctrl+Space (Monaco), Ctrl+B, Ctrl+Click, Ctrl+Shift+B (Go to Type Declaration), Alt+F7, Shift+F6, Ctrl+Q, Ctrl+P, Alt+Enter, F2 / Shift+F2 |
| debugger | Shift+F9, F8, F7, Shift+F8, F9, Ctrl+F8, Ctrl+Shift+F8, Alt+F9, Alt+F8, Alt+5, Ctrl+Alt+F5 (Attach to Process), F2 / Ctrl+C in the Variables or Watches list (Set Value, Copy Value) |
| search-nav | Ctrl+Shift+F, Ctrl+Shift+R, Ctrl+E, Ctrl+Shift+E, Alt+7, Ctrl+F12, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+N, Ctrl+N, Ctrl+Alt+Left/Right, Ctrl+Shift+Backspace |
| vcs | Ctrl+K, Ctrl+Shift+K, Ctrl+T, Alt+0, Alt+9, Ctrl+Alt+Z, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Down/Up |
| run-tools | Alt+Shift+F10, Alt+Shift+F9, Alt+8 (Python Console), Ctrl+Shift+F10 on a test (via `when`), Shift+F9 on a test file or a selected pytest configuration (via `when`, ahead of the debugger's Debug), Ctrl+D in the Test Results tree (View Difference) |
| coverage, profiler | none (Run with Coverage, Profile and Hide Coverage are in the Run menu, the run widget's More Actions and the gutter run menu) |
| tooling | Ctrl+Alt+L, Ctrl+Alt+O (in a Java file: JDT LS's Reformat Code and Optimize Imports through `ide.lsp.editedText`), Ctrl+Alt+S, Ctrl+Shift+Alt+S |
| semantic | Ctrl+Alt+F (Semantic Search popup) |
| database | Ctrl+Enter (Execute Statement, only while a query console has focus) |
| ai-complete | Tab / Ctrl+Right / Esc while ghost text is visible, Ctrl+Alt+\ (Trigger AI Completion) |
| ai-edit | Ctrl+I (Edit with Claude), Ctrl+Enter / Tab / Esc while reviewing, Tab / Esc for a visible next edit suggestion (never while ghost text, the suggest widget or a snippet is active) |
| ai-chat | Ctrl+Shift+L (AI Chat, ahead of Monaco's Select All Occurrences), Enter / Shift+Enter / Esc inside the chat input |
| notebook | Shift+Enter, Ctrl+Enter, Alt+Enter, Esc inside notebook cells, and Jupyter's single-letter command-mode keys while the notebook (not a cell) has focus; none are global |
| refactor | Ctrl+Alt+V, Ctrl+Alt+C, Ctrl+Alt+P, Ctrl+Alt+M, Ctrl+Alt+N, Ctrl+F6, F6, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+T, all only in a Python editor; Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Shift+Z only in a file a refactoring just moved (elsewhere Monaco's own undo starts the cross-file undo) |
| code-generation | Ctrl+O (Override Methods…, only while a Python editor has the keyboard; Open Folder… has no key, as in PyCharm) |
| bookmarks | F11, Ctrl+F11, Shift+F11, Ctrl+0..9 (while that numbered bookmark exists), Ctrl+Shift+0..9, Alt+2 |
| frameworks | Ctrl+Alt+R (Run manage.py Task, Django projects only), Ctrl+B on a template, URL-name or urlconf string (registered on `ready`, so it runs before lsp's Ctrl+B and falls through to it elsewhere) |
| navigation-bar | Ctrl+Alt+H (Call Hierarchy, Python editors only) |
| type-hierarchy | Ctrl+H (Type Hierarchy, Python editors only; elsewhere Monaco's Replace keeps it); Go to Implementation(s) and Go to Super Method have no key of their own (core's `KEYMAP_DEFAULTS` gives them Ctrl+Alt+B and Ctrl+U) |
| goto-test | none of its own (core's `KEYMAP_DEFAULTS` gives Go to Test Ctrl+Shift+T); Create New Test… is unbound, as in PyCharm |
| editor-assists | Shift+Enter, Ctrl+Shift+Enter, Ctrl+Alt+Enter, Ctrl+Shift+J, Ctrl+Shift+U, Alt+J, Alt+Shift+J, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+J, Ctrl+Shift+Up/Down, Ctrl+Shift+- / Ctrl+Shift+= (and NumPad +), Ctrl+Shift+I, Ctrl+Shift+P; only while the main editor has the keyboard |
| http-client | Ctrl+Enter (Run HTTP Request, only while a `.http`/`.rest` editor has focus) |
| search-everywhere | Shift Shift (takes over core's fallback); Tab / Shift+Tab / Enter / Esc only inside its popup |
| live-templates | Tab right after a template abbreviation or `expr.key` postfix key in Python, Java, JavaScript/TypeScript and C/C++ code (also while the suggest widget shows; never while ghost text or a snippet is active), Ctrl+Alt+J (Surround With Live Template) |
| language-basics | Enter in a Markdown list item, past its marker (`markdownListItem`; not while the suggest widget shows or a snippet is active) |
| scratches | Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Insert (New Scratch File) |
| appearance-modes | none (Enter Distraction-Free Mode, Zen Mode, Full Screen and Presentation Mode are unbound, as in PyCharm's Windows keymap) |
| settings (font size) | Alt+Shift+. / Alt+Shift+, (Increase / Decrease Font Size in All Editors), Ctrl+Mouse Wheel over an editor; Increase/Decrease/Reset Font Size are unbound, as in PyCharm |
| layouts | Shift+F12 (Restore Current Layout, ahead of Monaco's Go to References) |
| power-save | none (File › Power Save Mode is unbound, as in PyCharm) |
| clipboard-history | Ctrl+Shift+V (Paste from History; inside a terminal it stays the terminal's paste) |
| run-anything | Ctrl Ctrl (Double Ctrl, Run Anything); Enter / Shift+Enter / Tab / Delete / Esc only inside its popup |
| file-templates | none (New › … in the project tree; core's New Python File keeps Alt+Insert) |
| compare | Ctrl+D while the Project tool window has the keyboard (Compare With…; core's Duplicate Line keeps it in editors), F7 / Shift+F7 while a diff tab is active (Next / Previous Difference, registered on `ready`, ahead of the debugger's Step Into, whose F7 also stands back while `ide.compare.diffFocused()`) |
| visual-script | none global (canvas keys only while the canvas has focus; Shift+F10 / Ctrl+Shift+F10 are run's, through the visual-script configuration type) |
| diagrams | Ctrl+Alt+Shift+U (Show Diagram); Delete / Enter / Esc / + / - / Space only while a diagram has focus |
| regex-check | none (Check RegExp is a context action under lsp's Alt+Enter); Esc only inside its popup |
| database (export) | none (Export Data… is in the result bar, the grid and tree context menus and Find Action) |

## Untrusted content

Projects, notebooks, READMEs, HTTP responses, AI replies and program output are untrusted, and the IDE page holds the whole IPC API (files, processes), so content must never run in it. `main/security.js` keeps every window on the IDE page: navigations elsewhere (a clicked link, a dropped URL, `location.href`) are refused, `window.open`/`target=_blank`/middle-click open http(s) in the system browser and nothing else, `<webview>` is refused, and permissions (camera, microphone, location, notifications, devices, external protocols) are denied; only the IDE page may read the clipboard. A `file:` URL on a network share (`file://server/share/…`, which makes Windows sign in to that server) is fetched only from shares PyIDE runs from or the user opened a project, folder or file on (`allowShare`), so a docstring image in a Monaco hover cannot reach an arbitrary server; `main/security.js` owns the session's `webRequest.onBeforeRequest`. The preload exposes `window.api` only to the IDE's own `renderer/index.html` (passed as `--pyide-page`), and the renderer runs sandboxed. Show untrusted text with `textContent` (`el(tag, cls, text)`, a string `ide.ui.dialog` body), sanitize Markdown with an allowlist and handle link clicks (`a` and `area`) yourself, and put untrusted HTML in a sandboxed iframe without `allow-same-origin`. A Content-Security-Policy on the IDE page is not used because `srcdoc` frames inherit it, which would stop the scripts of notebook HTML outputs. Test: `verify/security.mjs` (port 9870).

## Verification

`verify/lib.mjs` launches an isolated instance: a temp copy of `verify/fixtures/<fixture>/` as the project, temp user data, and a CDP port. It returns `js(expr)`, `until(expr, label)`, `press('Ctrl+Shift+F')` (real key events), `type(text)`, `click(selector)`, `shot(file)`, `check(ok, label)`, `finish()`. A run fails on any failed check or uncaught renderer exception. See `verify/core.mjs`. Each feature uses its own port so instances can run side by side.
Temp folders: every `%TEMP%pyide-*` folder a suite makes (its launches' project copies and user data, and its own `mkdtempSync` folders, which `lib.mjs` registers by wrapping `fs.mkdtempSync`) is removed when the suite exits, after its instances are ended and their helper processes have let go (about 1.5 s). `PYIDE_KEEP_TEST_DIRS=1` keeps them for debugging. Clipboard: before a suite's first launch, `verify/clipboard-keeper.cjs` (a windowless Electron run) saves the OS clipboard (text, HTML, RTF, image) to `%TEMP%\pyide-clipboard-keeper`; a page hook notes whether a test window wrote to the clipboard, and at exit the last running suite restores it if it changed, so tests that press Ctrl+C never leave the user's clipboard replaced (`PYIDE_TEST_CLIPBOARD=off` turns this off). Pure-Node suites `verify/redact.mjs` (report redaction table), `verify/notifications-log.mjs` (batched log writes) and `verify/recovery-main.mjs` (crash recovery's main module) run in CI too. `verify/clean.mjs` (`npm run clean:tests`, `--age <minutes>`, default 360, `--dry-run`) removes what a crashed suite left; `lib.mjs` starts it detached at most once an hour. The `*-venv` folders and `downloads-cache` in `verify/out` are reused caches and are not removed.
Process trees: suites that look for the processes PyIDE started use `lib.mjs`'s `processSnapshot()` (one `Win32_Process` query: pid, ppid, name, cmd, `created` in ms, working set, CPU ms) and `descendants(rows, rootPid, { includeRoot, since })`, never their own ParentProcessId walk. A child counts only if it started no earlier than its parent, because PIDs are reused: on the CI runner csrss/wininit/services, whose long-gone parent had PyIDE's PID, otherwise showed up as PyIDE's children. With the root gone, its children must have started after `since` (default: when the suite started). `verify/process-tree.mjs` (pure Node, in CI) checks this on a fake snapshot and on real child processes.
Core lifecycle: `verify/core-lifecycle.mjs` (ports 9276-9277; 9277 is the main process's Node inspector) checks overtaken project opens, `ide.util.errorText` (network, spawn, file and loopback texts), `project:exec` in a 287-character folder, a user plugin whose main module throws, and two leaks after a forced GC: Compare Files opened and closed 30 times (0 DOM nodes and listeners per round) and 12 project switches (project-watch's 'destroyed' listeners stay at 3).

## Semantic search (EmbeddingGemma 2)

`renderer/features/semantic` and `main/features/semantic.js` add meaning-based search with Google's EmbeddingGemma 2 (`google/embeddinggemma-2`, Apache 2.0) running locally. The model runs in a Python sidecar (`main/semantic/embed_server.py`) inside a private venv at `~\.pyide\semantic\venv` that reuses the system PyTorch. Setup adds only the torchvision build paired with that torch (the model's processor needs it even for text) and never replaces torch itself. The model itself sits in the standard Hugging Face cache. Files are split into code-aware chunks (functions, classes, and each method for Python; heading sections for Markdown; line windows elsewhere). They are embedded with the model card's CodeRetrieval prompts, truncated to 256 dimensions, and stored per project in `<userData>/semantic/indexes`. Only the shown project's index and the one shown before it stay in memory; another is released once its run has stopped and its changes are saved, and read again when needed. Only files whose text changed are embedded again: each record keeps a hash of its file's text (`hash`), and a file whose modified time or size moved with the same text (a branch switch, a sync tool) is only restamped, its chunks and vectors kept (records from before hashes are embedded once more when their file is touched). Files changed outside the IDE (git pull or a branch switch in a terminal, another editor) reach the index 1.5 s after the project watcher (`watch:changed`) reports them; only paths with an indexable extension, or without an extension (a folder), start a run, so a program writing logs into the project does not, and an overflowing batch checks the whole project. A save or such a batch of files looks only at those paths (`quickFiles`): what the last full walk left out (Git-ignored, virtual environment and excluded folders) stays out, `git check-ignore` is asked about the paths themselves, and the data folder rule stays exact through that walk's census (every file it counted and every folder's counts), which each path that appears, disappears or changes size updates; when a folder would start or stop being a data folder, or a path is a folder, or the project has not been walked yet (or holds more than 300,000 files), the full walk decides. Every watched path goes to main, so a folder filling with images still becomes data, and a quick look that changes nothing the index holds (a log written into the project) ends without an indexing phase. On a 20,000-file project a save takes about 80 ms instead of 650 ms and a log write about 10 ms; the census adds about 100 ms to the full walk on open. A restamped record keeps its chunks' rows in the vector file (`c.row`), so it appends a log line and no vectors; a rewrite numbers every chunk again. The per-project index is an append log, `index.jsonl` (a header line, then one `[rel, record|null]` line per change; the last line for a path wins) plus the `vectors-<gen>.bin` its header names. Saves append the changed files 2 s after an update, the log is compacted into a new vector file once it is mostly stale, and pending changes are written at quit. Reading, chunking and (with the fake backend) embedding run on a worker; the main process reads and encodes the log in short slices, so indexing 20,000 files never holds it for more than ~50 ms at a time. Queries combine cosine similarity with a small keyword score. Other plugins can call `ide.semantic.query(text, {limit, similarTo})`. Tests use `PYIDE_SEMANTIC_BACKEND=fake`, a deterministic hashing embedder; `SEMANTIC_REAL=1 node verify/semantic.mjs` runs against the real model once `node verify/semantic-setup.mjs` has installed it.

What gets indexed: the walk (on the worker) leaves out `SKIP_DIRS`, virtual environments at any depth (`venv*`/`.venv*` names, `pyvenv.cfg`, `conda-meta`), whatever Git ignores (`git ls-files --others --ignored --exclude-standard --directory`; a folder that is not a repository keeps the old behaviour), folders excluded by the user (project tree context menu *Exclude from Semantic Index* / *Include in Semantic Index*, or the tool window's ⊘ action; stored in `<userData>/semantic/exclusions.json`), and data folders: more than `semantic.dataFolderFiles` files (default 1000) or `semantic.dataFolderMB` MB of indexable text (default 50), of which less than a fifth is source. A skipped data folder does not count toward its parent, so a package keeps its code next to a large fixtures folder. Settings › AI › Semantic Search holds both limits and `semantic.confirmChunks` (default 5000): a first index larger than that waits for a dialog listing its files, chunks and largest folders, where folders can be unticked; *Not Now* leaves the project unindexed until *Index Now* in the tool window. A run puts each file into the index as soon as its last chunk is embedded and saves every 40 batches or 20 s (and on pause and at quit), so a killed or restarted IDE resumes with the files not yet on disk. Indexing waits while the user types (the renderer sends `semantic:typing` while a run is going; 1.5 s of quiet resumes it), rests 30 ms between batches, and can be paused from the tool window or the project tree (`semantic:pause`, for every project until resume or the next launch). The status item shows the percentage and the time left from the recent rate. The sidecar uses half the threads torch would take (`PYIDE_SEMANTIC_THREADS` overrides) at below-normal priority. On a generated project with git-ignored `data/` (4,300 files), `outputs/` and `.venv-rvc/` plus a 1,500-file JSON `datasets/labels/`, the run went from 6,361 files / 6,901 chunks to 61 files / 601 chunks. `PYIDE_SEMANTIC_FAKE_PAGE_MS` slows the test backend per page of 200 files so verify/semantic.mjs can pause and kill a run part-way (ports 9530-9538).

## Database

`renderer/features/database`, `main/features/database.js` and `main/db/drivers.js` add PyCharm-style data sources. The driver table in `main/db/drivers.js` holds one entry per kind: SQLite (built in, Electron's `node:sqlite`), PostgreSQL (`pg`), and MySQL and MariaDB (both `mysql2`). Each entry carries its fields, default port, identifier quoting and an adapter with `query`, `schema` and `close`. External drivers install on demand with npm into `~\.pyide\db-drivers` (`PYIDE_DB_DRIVERS` overrides it). Data sources live in `<userData>/database/sources.json`. Passwords are encrypted with safeStorage and never reach the renderer, which only sees `hasPassword`. A saved password is kept on save or Test Connection only while the connection stays the same (`main/settings-sync/same-connection.js`: kind, host, port, user, database or file, and the URL without its password); changing any of them, or importing a source with the same id but another host, drops it so it is never sent to a different server. Errors cross IPC as `{ok: false, error, missingDriver}`.

The Database tool window (bottom, order 55) shows sources, schemas, tables, views and columns, loading a source's schema when it is expanded. A query console is a custom tab with a Monaco SQL editor over a result grid. Ctrl+Enter runs the selection or the statement at the caret; the splitter respects quotes, comments and `$tag$` bodies. Console text is saved under `<userData>/database/consoles/`. Opening a `.sqlite`, `.sqlite3` or `.db` file with a SQLite header adds it as a source. For server kinds, the data source dialog links to the Install Manager's Databases category. Tests: `verify/database.mjs` (ports 9600-9603); `DB_REAL=1` also installs the real `pg` driver.

Generate ERD (the context menu of a SQL data source, after New Query Console, and of a schema): `db:schema` and `db:foreignKeys` give the tables, views, columns and foreign keys, which `ide.diagrams.showModel` draws in a diagram tab (`pyide-diagram:erd:<source id>:<schema or empty>`, titled with the source, or `source: schema`): a box per table or view (views marked «view», the schema under the name), a column per row with its type and primary key columns marked k, and an association per foreign key from the child table to the parent, labelled with its columns (a composite key is one association). Refresh in the tab reads both again; a source whose keys cannot be read shows its tables with a warning. A kind adapter gives the keys with `foreignKeys()` (`lib.groupForeignKeys` groups per-column rows): SQLite (`PRAGMA foreign_key_list`; a key naming no parent columns points at the primary key, refColumn empty), PostgreSQL and CockroachDB (`pg_constraint`), MySQL and MariaDB (`KEY_COLUMN_USAGE`), SQL Server (`sys.foreign_key_columns`), Oracle (`ALL_CONSTRAINTS` type R) and DuckDB (`duckdb_constraints()`, parent in the child's schema); ClickHouse and the document stores have none. Only SQLite is tested here; the other queries have not run against a server. The tree draws SVG icons (`database`, `dataSchema`, `dataTable`, `dataView`, `dataColumn`, `key` for primary key columns). Test: `verify/database-erd.mjs` (port 9636, in CI).

Project SQL files with a data source (PyCharm's and DataGrip's SQL file attachment; `renderer/features/database/sql-files.js`). A `.sql` file of the open project can be attached to a SQL data source, and to one of its schemas when the source has several (the schema picker lists them, plus Default schema). It can be attached from the status bar widget (`#statusSqlSource`: `⛁ <No data source> ▾`, or `⛁ name · schema ▾`), the editor context menu (Attach Data Source…, shown only for project SQL files) or Find Action (`Database.AttachDataSource`, no shortcut). The same picker has `<No data source>` to detach and New Data Source…. Attachments live in settings per project (`database.sqlFiles.<project>`: relative path with `/` -> `{source, schema?}`). They follow a rename or move made in the IDE (`filesChanged`), go with a deleted file, and are cleared in every project when their data source is removed. An attached file completes like a console of its source, because `completionHost.consoleFor(model)` answers for it: tables, columns, alias.column, the kind's keywords, functions with signature help and snippets. The attached schema counts as implicit, so its tables are inserted unqualified and resolve first. The language-basics keyword provider stays out for such a file, so keywords are not listed twice. Without an attachment nothing changes. Ctrl+Enter (`Database.RunStatement`) in a project SQL file runs the selection or the statement at the caret through the console's own run and result view (`resultView()` in index.js: status line, grid/JSON, Copy as CSV/TSV, Export Data…). The result shows in the Query Results tool window (`DatabaseResults`, bottom, one view per file, the active file's view in front). A file with no data source asks for one first. As in the console, a modifying statement runs without a question. Files outside the open project get no widget, no menu item, no completion and no Ctrl+Enter. In Safe Mode a statement from the project runs only after a dialog shows it (Cancel is the default). The schema choice applies to completion; statements run on the source's own default schema. Test: `verify/database-sql-files.mjs` (ports 9888-9889).
