## Install Manager

`renderer/features/installer`, `main/features/installer.js` and `main/installer/servers.js` add one tab (action `InstallManager`, Tools menu) for installing, updating and removing what a Python developer needs. An installable is a plain object pushed to `ide.installables`: `{id, category, name, description, size?, homepage?, status(), install(log, progress, signal), uninstall?, update?, start?, stop?, actions?: [{label, run, when?}], confirm?, confirmUninstall?}`, where `status()` resolves to `{installed, version?, running?, update?, detail?, available?, reason?}`. Built in are Python tools for the project interpreter (pip), portable PostgreSQL 18.6 and MariaDB 11.8.9 servers, and Git, Python 3.13, Node.js and the GitHub CLI through winget after a confirmation dialog. Other plugins add the PostgreSQL and MySQL drivers (database), EmbeddingGemma 2 (semantic) and Claude Code (terminal).

PyIDE Python (category Python Interpreters, `main/installer/python.js`): the official python.org build from nuget.org (`python` 3.13.17, 14.8 MB, a pinned sha256, `PYIDE_PYTHON_FEED` serves it from another folder URL in tests). Its `tools/` folder is a complete CPython with pip and venv (no tkinter), extracted with `tar.exe` to `<install root>/python/<version>` (41 MB), then `python -c "import venv, ensurepip, pip"` must succeed. Installing adds its python.exe to `interpreters.extra`, so the picker and the New Project wizard list it as "PyIDE Python (Python 3.13.17)" (main.js names extra interpreters under the install root so); Use as Project Interpreter selects it. At startup an installed copy missing from the list is listed again, and with no usable interpreter at all one notification per session (group Python Interpreter) offers to install it. Removing it first takes it off the list and moves the project to another interpreter that is not it or a venv made from it (pyvenv.cfg `home`), or to none, so the IDE's own helpers (semantic highlighting and the other sidecars) stop; then main refuses while any of its .exe, .dll or .pyd files cannot be opened for writing (a running program loaded it; Windows would still let the folder be renamed and the rest deleted, leaving a broken interpreter), retries for 3 s, renames the folder aside and deletes it (a leftover `<version>.remove-*` goes with the next install); a refused removal puts it back on the list. Test: `verify/installer-python.mjs` (ports 9626-9627; PATH without Python, a local HTTP feed with the real package cached in `verify/out/downloads-cache`): the notification, install, the label, a run, the in-use refusal, removal, a tampered package, Korean.

Main runs jobs in one queue per target (an interpreter's pip, winget, or one server), with cancel, streaming `installer:log` and `installer:progress`. Servers download with a pinned sha256 to `~\.pyide\servers\<name>-<version>` (`PYIDE_INSTALL_ROOT` overrides it), initialize with a generated password kept with safeStorage in `<userData>/installer/servers.json`, take the first free port from 5432 or 3306, run as children of the IDE and stop when it quits. A running server offers Add as Data Source, which calls `ide.database.addDataSource`. `PYIDE_INSTALLER_RUNNER` swaps the winget and detection runner for tests. Tests: `verify/installer.mjs` (ports 9620-9625); `INSTALLER_REAL=1` really downloads, starts, connects through the Database plugin, stops and removes both servers.

## Java and C/C++

The `toolchains` plugin (`renderer/features/toolchains`, `main/features/toolchains.js`, `main/installer/toolchains.js`; depends on run) lets PyIDE create and run Java and C/C++ projects with toolchains it installs itself, with Java Application, Maven / Gradle and C/C++ Application run configurations. Spring Boot is the `spring-boot` plugin (`renderer/features/spring-boot`, `main/features/spring-initializr.js`; depends on run and toolchains), which can be turned off on its own in Plugins: its New Project type (listed after Java), the application-class resolver (`ide.toolchains.addRunResolver`) and the server URL watcher on Maven / Gradle output (`ide.toolchains.addOutputWatcher`). It builds on `ide.toolchains` (`ui.{chooser, radios, field, section}`, `defaultKey`, `saveConfig`, `buildDefaults`, `sync`, `afterSync`, `javaIgnore`, `javaPackage`). Java code insight is the `java-lsp` plugin (below) and Java debugging the debugger plugin's (see Java debugging); clangd and C/C++ debugging are not part of it.

- Built-in toolchains (Install Manager, category Toolchains): Eclipse Temurin JDK 25.0.4.1+1 (GPLv2 with the Classpath Exception; a 141,167,264-byte zip, sha256 `00c847d8…` as Adoptium publishes it) and w64devkit 2.10.1 x64 (GCC 16.2, G++, GDB, make; the kit is public domain, GCC and GDB are GPL; a 67,263,552-byte 7-Zip self-extractor, sha256 `add1edf9…` as the GitHub release lists it, which Windows `tar.exe` (libarchive 3.8) extracts without running it, about 730 MB on disk). They follow the database servers: `download()` from `main/installer/servers.js` with progress, cancel and the pinned sha256, then `tar.exe` into a staging folder that is swapped in, under `~\.pyide\jdk\temurin-<version>` and `~\.pyide\cpp\w64devkit-<version>` (`PYIDE_INSTALL_ROOT`), per user without administrator rights. The job kinds `toolchain-install` / `toolchain-uninstall` run in `main/features/installer.js`, and `installer:toolchains` answers their status. `PYIDE_TOOLCHAINS=<json>` overrides the downloads (`{jdk?, cpp?: {url, size, sha256, version, top}}`), and `system: false` in it leaves this computer's toolchains out (tests).
- Detection (`toolchains:list`): JDKs from the built-in one, `JAVA_HOME`, `javac.exe` on PATH (Oracle's `javapath` links resolved), `Program Files\{Java, Eclipse Adoptium, Microsoft, Zulu, Amazon Corretto, BellSoft, Semeru, OpenJDK}` and IntelliJ's `~\.jdks`, with version and vendor from each JDK's `release` file; C/C++ compilers from the built-in w64devkit, PATH, MSYS2 (`ucrt64`, `mingw64`, `clang64`), `C:\w64devkit`, `~\w64devkit`, `C:\mingw64` and `C:\MinGW` (GCC before Clang in one folder), with the version from `gcc -dumpfullversion` (a file Windows refuses to start has none). `ide.toolchains`: `list(kind)`, `refresh()`, `resolve(kind, id)`, `label(t)`, `install(kind)`, `parseJavac`, `parseGcc`.
- New Project: Java (build system IntelliJ: `src\Main.java`; Maven: `pom.xml` with `maven.compiler.release` from the JDK and exec-maven-plugin, so the Launchers tool window offers `mvn exec:java`; Gradle: `build.gradle` with the application plugin and a Java toolchain, and `settings.gradle`; both with `src\main\java\<GroupId>\Main.java`) and C/C++ (C++ with `main.cpp` or C with `main.c`, language standard C++17/20/23 or C11/17/23). Each chooses a JDK or toolchain. While the built-in one is not installed the wizard offers "Download Eclipse Temurin JDK 25.0.4.1 (135 MB)" / "Download w64devkit 2.10.1 (64 MB)", which runs the Install Manager item in place (`ide.installer.run`) and shows its percentage, and it says "No JDK was found on this computer." when nothing was found; Create without one keeps the wizard open. The project remembers its choice (`toolchains.jdk:<project>`, `toolchains.cpp:<project>`) and gets a saved, selected configuration ("Main", or the project name for C/C++).
- New Project: Spring Boot (the `spring-boot` plugin), made by Spring Initializr (`main/features/spring-initializr.js`), in IntelliJ IDEA's two pages. The first page, under the wizard's name, location and Git fields: Server URL (start.spring.io by default, `spring:defaultServer`; changing it reloads the options), Language (Java, Kotlin, Groovy) and Packaging (Jar, War) as the server offers them, Type (Maven, Gradle - Groovy, Gradle - Kotlin), Group, Artifact and Package name (they follow the project name until typed), JDK and Java (the newest version the JDK runs). Next checks that page and shows the second: the Spring Boot version (Initializr's default) and the dependencies, a tree of collapsible groups with a search box, the added ones listed beside it with × to remove them, the hovered one described below, and the ones outside the version's range disabled ("Needs Spring Boot [4.0.0,4.2.0-M1)"); Spring Web is added to start with. Previous goes back with the fields kept. The options come from `spring:metadata(server)` (the `application/vnd.initializr.v2.2+json` metadata; "Could not reach Spring Initializr" with Retry when it fails). Create downloads `starter.zip` first (`spring:download`; a refused request such as an unknown dependency shows Initializr's message and creates no folder), then the wizard makes the folder and `spring:unpack` extracts the zip there with `main/zip.js` (Initializr's .gitignore replaces the wizard's), finds the `@SpringBootApplication` class (.java, .kt or .groovy) to open, remembers the JDK, the server, language, type, group and packaging for the next project (`newProject.spring`), and saves and selects a Maven / Gradle configuration named after that class (`spring-boot:run` or `bootRun`, so Run and Shift+F10 start the application); the project tree stays in view and Launchers also lists `mvnw spring-boot:run` or `gradlew bootRun`. Then the dependencies download in the background (below). Choosing the type suggests the project name `demo`. Network failures, refused requests and a lost download are marked `expected`, so they are shown in the wizard and do not count as IDE internal errors. Project types' forms get `log(text)` for the wizard's setup log, and may have pages (`pages`, `showPage(n)`, `validatePage(n)`): the wizard then shows Next, and Previous and Create on the last page, and hides its shared fields after the first.
- Run configuration Maven / Gradle (`build`): build tool, goals or tasks, working directory, environment variables and JDK. Debug (Shift+F9, and the gutter's or project tree's Debug on a Spring Boot application class) runs the same goals with the application's JVM listening for the debugger (`toolchains:build`'s `debugPort`, `-agentlib:jdwp=transport=dt_socket,server=y,suspend=y,address=127.0.0.1:<port>`): Maven's `spring-boot:run` gets it as `-Dspring-boot.run.jvmArguments` (the plugin forks that JVM), other Maven goals through `MAVEN_OPTS` (`exec:java` runs in Maven's own JVM), and Gradle's JavaExec tasks (`bootRun`, `run`) through a `--init-script` written to the temp folder and deleted when the build ends. `ide.debugger.debugJvm` waits up to 30 minutes for the JVM to listen while the build runs, with `src\main\java`, `src\main\kotlin` and `src\main\groovy` as source roots; it waits for New Project's dependency download first, like Run. `toolchains:build` runs `.\mvnw.cmd` / `.\gradlew.bat` when the folder has one, else `mvn` / `gradle` from PATH, through `cmd.exe` with the JDK as `JAVA_HOME`; goals are limited to words of letters, digits and `: . = , @ / + -`, so nothing reaches cmd.exe as syntax. When `~\.m2\repository` (or `~\.gradle\caches`, and the wrapper's `wrapper\dists`) does not exist yet, the Run tab says the first run downloads Maven or Gradle and the dependencies (`PYIDE_BUILD_HOME` stands in for the home folder in tests). A server port line (Tomcat, Netty, Jetty or Undertow started on port N) adds "The application is running at http://localhost:N/" and an Open in Browser button to the Run tab's toolbar; "Port N was already in use" adds a hint to stop the other run or change `server.port`. The Java Application type's `forFile` hands a Spring Boot project's application class (`@SpringBootApplication` in the open editor, or a name ending in Application, under the `src\main\java` of a project the Launchers scan found with `spring-boot:run` or `bootRun`) to this type, so its gutter icon and the project tree's Run '…' use the build tool instead of javac, which has no Spring classes. Only start.spring.io is contacted; `PYIDE_INITIALIZR_URL` points the tests at a local server. Project types' forms get `log(text)` for the wizard's setup log.
- Dependency download after New Project (Spring Boot), like IntelliJ IDEA's import: `ide.toolchains.sync(dir, tool)` runs `toolchains:build` with `sync: true`, whose goals main picks whatever the renderer sends (Maven `-B dependency:go-offline`; Gradle `classes testClasses`, since its `dependencies` task reads only metadata and leaves the jars), with the project's JDK. It shows as a background task "Downloading dependencies…" whose text is the project and its latest output line (a URL as its file name) and whose Cancel kills the wrapper, and as a "Sync: <project>" Run tab (`ide.run.outputSession`; Stop cancels, Rerun downloads again) that the Run tool window shows when opened but does not open by itself. The end is "Dependencies downloaded" (an `ok` balloon) or "Dependency download failed" with the exit code (an error balloon), both with Show Output (group Build Tools); a cancel is only a line in the tab. While it runs, a Maven / Gradle configuration (Run, Shift+F10, the gutter) and a Maven or Gradle launcher in the same folder wait for it (`ide.toolchains.afterSync(dir, say, stopped)`) and say so, since two wrappers would fill the same cache at once; Stop in that Run tab cancels the run, not the download. A folder downloaded in this session gets no first-run notice. Safe Mode: nothing starts (`ide.trusted === false`), and main refuses `sync` for a project `main/trust.js` does not trust (New Project trusts the folder it makes).
- Launchers (My Workspace) use a project's Maven Wrapper: with `mvnw.cmd` next to `pom.xml` the launcher is `mvnw <goal>` and runs `.\mvnw.cmd <goal>` (likewise `.\gradlew.bat`, spelled with `.\` because cmd.exe skips the current folder when `NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath` is set), and the wrapper scripts are no longer listed as batch scripts of their own. Maven and Gradle launchers get the project's JDK (`toolchains.jdk:<project>`, set by New Project) as `JAVA_HOME`, so a project made with the built-in JDK runs without one on this computer.
- Java Application configuration (`java`): Main class (build, then `java -cp out\production\<project>;<classpath> <class>`) or Single source file (`java File.java`, JDK 11 and later), source root (default `src`), classpath, VM options, program arguments, working directory, environment and JDK (Project default or any found). The build runs `javac -encoding UTF-8 -g -d out\production\<project>` over every `.java` file under the source root (through an @argfile, so long lists fit). Java runs with `-Dstdout.encoding=UTF-8 -Dstderr.encoding=UTF-8`, so Korean output does not arrive as CP949. A `.java` file under `src\main\java`, `src\test\java` or `src` runs as its class; any other as a single file.
- C/C++ Application configuration (`cpp`): source files (default: every `.c`, `.cpp`, `.cc` and `.cxx` in the project outside build, out, target, cmake-build-* and venvs), executable (default `build\<project>.exe`), compiler options (`-std=c++20 -g -Wall` from the wizard), program arguments, working directory, environment and toolchain. `g++` builds when any source is C++, `gcc` otherwise, with `-fdiagnostics-color=never`; the program runs with the toolchain's bin folder first on PATH (its runtime DLLs).
- Both: the compiler's output streams into the Run tab first and Stop ends the build. Errors and warnings (javac `file:line: error:` with the column from its caret line, GCC/Clang `file:line:col: error:`) replace the previous build's in Problems (sources `javac` and `gcc`), editor markers included, and the Problems tool window opens when a build fails with errors. The program runs in the same tab with the stdin input line and "Process finished with exit code N". Without a toolchain the Run tab says so and offers Download JDK / Download C/C++ Toolchain (the Install Manager). `main` methods (JDK 25's instance `void main()` too) and C `int main(` get a gutter run icon, and the project tree's Run '…' covers `.java`, `.c` and `.cpp` files. Processes start through `toolchains:start` (`{toolchain, cmd: a tool name or an absolute path, args, cwd, env, argfile?, makeDirs?}`), which puts the toolchain's bin folder first on PATH and sets `JAVA_HOME` for a JDK.
- Tests: `verify/toolchains.mjs` (ports 9770 and 9772; offline: fake archives from a local server, a checksum mismatch, Cancel, Remove, the wizard's offer and in-place install, the Run tab without a toolchain, the Korean UI) `verify/spring-boot.mjs` (ports 9773, 9776 and 9777; a local Initializr: Retry, both pages with Next and Previous, language and packaging, dependency ranges, search, collapsing, remove and descriptions, a refused request, the unpacked project, the remembered choices, the background download (fake wrappers wait for a gate file: the status bar task, the Sync tab, Run and a launcher waiting, Stop, Cancel, a failure and Rerun, Safe Mode, no first-run notice after it), Shift+F10 and `mvnw spring-boot:run` with JAVA_HOME and the Tomcat link, a Kotlin Gradle war project, both pages in Korean; `SPRING_REAL=1` creates a project from start.spring.io) and `verify/java-cpp-projects.mjs` (port 9771; a JDK on this computer, else that part is skipped; `TOOLCHAINS_CPP_ARCHIVE=<w64devkit-x64-2.10.1.7z.exe>` installs that archive from a local server and `TOOLCHAINS_REAL=1` downloads the pinned ones; plain, Maven and Gradle Java projects, C++ and C projects, real output, stdin, Korean text, compiler errors in Problems).

## Java code insight (Eclipse JDT LS)

The `java-lsp` plugin (`renderer/features/java-lsp`, `main/features/java-lsp.js`; depends on lsp and toolchains) gives Java files IntelliJ IDEA-style code insight from the Eclipse JDT Language Server through the lsp plugin's client (`ide.lsp.createClient`, channels `java-lsp:*`): completion (`.`, `@`, `#`; JDT LS's postfix templates such as `.var` and `.par` are listed after the members, as in IntelliJ IDEA; Spring and other library classes once a Maven or Gradle project's dependencies are resolved), errors and warnings as you type in the editor and in Problems (source `java`; unused imports are warnings), Ctrl+Q / hover documentation (Javadoc), Ctrl+B / Ctrl+Click (a JDK or library class, a `jdt://` location, opens read-only in a tab from `java/classFileContents`, the JDK's own source when it has src.zip), Alt+F7, Shift+F6 (a class renamed with its file), Alt+Enter quick fixes (JDT LS's `java.apply.workspaceEdit` command is applied by the client), Ctrl+Alt+O Optimize Imports and Ctrl+Alt+L Reformat Code.

- The server: Eclipse JDT Language Server 1.63.0 in the Install Manager (category Toolchains, `toolchain:jdtls`, `main/installer/toolchains.js` entry `jdtls`): a 51,143,565-byte tar.gz from download.eclipse.org with the sha256 Eclipse publishes next to it (`b1f2abe0…`), extracted with tar.exe under `~\.pyide\jdtls\jdtls-1.63.0` (`PYIDE_INSTALL_ROOT`; `PYIDE_TOOLCHAINS` `{jdtls: {url, size, sha256}}` serves it from elsewhere in tests). While it is not installed, a Java project shows "Java: not installed" and one notification per session (group Java) offers Download, which installs it through `ide.installer.run` and starts the server.
- When it runs: once a trusted project has a build file at its root (pom.xml, build.gradle(.kts), settings.gradle(.kts)), a JDK chosen for it (`toolchains.jdk:<project>`), or a `.java` file open; never in other projects (it takes several hundred MB). In Safe Mode it does not start: Maven and Gradle imports run the project's build scripts and plugins (main refuses an untrusted root too, `main/trust.js`), and the item reads "Java: Safe Mode" (click: Trust Project). It stops when the project closes or another opens, and with the IDE.
- JDK: JDT LS needs Java 21 or later. Main runs only a JDK that toolchain detection (`toolchains:list`) finds, 21 or later, whatever path the renderer sends, and passes the project's JDK only when detection finds it too. It runs on the project's JDK (`ide.toolchains.resolve('jdk')`) when that is 21+, else on the newest JDK 21+ found (the built-in Temurin 25 among them); none gives "Java: no JDK 21+" and an offer to download the JDK. The project's JDK, whatever its version, is passed as the default `java.configuration.runtimes` entry (and Gradle's Java home), so code compiles against it. A change of the project's JDK restarts the server; new source roots (Mark Directory As) reach it as new settings. Removing the server in the Install Manager stops it first (Windows keeps its jars open).
- Projects: Maven and Gradle projects are imported by JDT LS itself (m2e, Buildship), which downloads their dependencies. A folder without a build file is a plain project whose sources are the marked source and test roots (Mark Directory As), else `src`, with `lib/**/*.jar` on the classpath. `-Djava.import.generatesMetadataFilesAtProjectRoot=false` keeps `.project`, `.classpath` and `.settings` out of the user's project. Its workspace (OSGi configuration, imported projects, indexes) is `<userData>/java-lsp/<name>-<hash of the path>`, one per project; the install folder is the read-only shared configuration (`osgi.sharedConfiguration.area`).
- Status item (`#javaLspStatus`, order 21, hidden while off): "Java: starting 27%" while JDT LS starts and imports (its `language/status` Starting messages, forwarded as `java-lsp:progress`; the tooltip has the step, such as the artifact being downloaded), then "Java: ready"; `java-lsp:start` answers once JDT LS reports Started, ServiceReady or Error (an import that failed: it still answers for the rest), so requests wait for the import. Click restarts it (or installs / downloads a JDK / trusts, per state); Find Action has Restart Java Language Server. Crashes restart it like Pyright.
- Test: `verify/java-lsp.mjs` (ports 9790-9791; a JDK 21+ on this computer, else SKIP; the archive cached in `verify/out/downloads-cache`, downloaded once, SKIP when offline): the Korean item and offer before the install, Download from the offer, then a plain project (completion of the project's own method listed before the postfix templates, a postfix template applied, a live error in the editor and Problems, hover Javadoc, Ctrl+B into another file and into `String`, Ctrl+Alt+O, Ctrl+Alt+L, Alt+F7, Shift+F6 renaming a class with its file, no Eclipse files in the project, the workspace under user data) and a Maven project (import progress, completion, an error, hover, Ctrl+B), and Close Project ending the process.

## AI runtime, completion and edits

`main/ai/runtime.js` owns the one private Python environment (`~\.pyide\semantic\venv`, reusing the system torch) and the JSON-lines sidecar client that semantic search and AI completion share. It makes the environment from an interpreter that already has torch 2.6 or later (transformers 5 turns older torch off), or else one torch has a build for. pip decides that with `pip index versions torch` against the user's index, and a "no build" answer counts only when the same index lists pip itself, so an offline check never rejects an interpreter. The project interpreter is tried first, then the other interpreters the IDE lists (PyIDE's own Python among them), every interpreter `py -0p` lists, and PATH's `python`. A torch older than 2.6 is replaced inside the environment, and the status counts such an environment as not set up (read from torch's dist-info, without importing it). torchvision is first the build paired with torch; when that build has none for the Python (every torchvision since 0.26 excludes Python 3.14.1), pip resolves torch and torchvision together. `PYIDE_TORCH_INDEX` replaces PyTorch's wheel index in tests. Semantic search setup needs torch, so an existing environment made from a Python torch has no build for (newer than torch supports, 32-bit, free-threaded) is recreated from one it has, after the running AI servers are stopped. With no such interpreter, setup stops with "PyTorch has no build for Python X.Y (64-bit)" instead of pip's error. `verify/ai-runtime-python.mjs` checks this with real interpreters and a local index that offers torch only for chosen versions.

Both sidecars unload when idle: once no request has been in flight for `ai.idleUnloadMinutes` (Settings › AI › AI Completion › Local Models; default 10, 0 keeps the models loaded), the client closes the server's stdin and the server exits on end-of-input with code 0, so the diagnostics never count it as a crash. A request in flight always completes first, because the timer is only armed while nothing is pending. The next request starts the server again and the status bar shows the plugin's usual loading hint ("Loading EmbeddingGemma 2…", "AI Completion: loading…") until the model is back. Main learns the setting from the renderer: each plugin sends `semantic:configure` / `ai-complete:configure` `{idleMinutes}` at load and on `settingsChanged`, the same way the plots plugin configures its main side, so main never parses `settings.json` itself. `node verify/ai-idle.mjs` (ports 9822-9823) checks the settings field with the fake backends and, when both real models are installed, unload, reload and the loading hint against them.

AI completion (`renderer/features/ai-complete`, `main/features/ai-complete.js`, `main/ai/complete_server.py`, `main/ai/complete-text.js`) shows Cursor-style ghost text from a local fill-in-the-middle model: Qwen2.5-Coder 0.5B by default or 1.5B (Apache 2.0, Q8_0 GGUF on llama.cpp through `llama-cpp-python`). The prompt uses Qwen's repo-level format with the current file's prefix and suffix, up to two similar open-tab windows and one semantic search hit. Requests are debounced, a newer keystroke cancels the older request, and results are cached. `complete-text.js` cuts a suggestion at the end of the current block (the sidecar applies the same rule to stop early) and replaces auto-closed brackets the suggestion writes itself. Automatic suggestions skip comments, selections, multiple carets, mid-word positions and active suggest/snippet sessions. Settings live in Settings › AI › AI Completion. The models install from the Install Manager. `PYIDE_AI_COMPLETE_BACKEND=fake` (the test default) answers from a fixed rule table; `AI_COMPLETE_REAL=1 node verify/ai-complete.mjs` uses the real model (ports 9640-9641).

AI edits (`renderer/features/ai-edit`, `main/features/ai-edit.js`, `main/ai/claude.js`) call the Claude Code CLI headless with the user's subscription: `claude -p --output-format stream-json --model <haiku|sonnet|opus> --tools "" --max-turns 1 --safe-mode --no-session-persistence --system-prompt-file <file>`, run in an empty temp folder. `main/ai/claude.js` also owns the Claude Code lookup the terminal plugin uses (`PYIDE_CLAUDE_PATH` overrides it). Ctrl+I opens a prompt over the selection; the answer streams in as an inline diff whose hunks are accepted or rejected one by one, each accept being one undo step. Next edit suggestions (off by default, Settings › AI › Claude Edits) send the recent edits after a pause, at most one request per interval, and show the predicted edit with a gutter hint: Tab jumps, Tab applies, Esc dismisses. Tests use a fake CLI; `AI_EDIT_REAL=1 node verify/ai-edit.mjs` makes two real calls (ports 9650-9652).

Signing in: the CLI keeps its own sign-in (`~/.claude/.credentials.json`), separate from the Claude desktop app's, and once it can no longer renew it, every request fails with "Failed to authenticate: OAuth session expired and could not be refreshed" although the user is signed in to the app. AI Chat, AI edits and the AI actions then say that Claude Code is not signed in or its sign-in has expired, and that it signs in apart from the Claude app, with a Sign in to Claude Code button: action `ClaudeCodeLogin` (`ide.terminal.claudeLogin()`), a terminal tab (kind `claude-login`, outside the project) running `claude auth login`, which finishes in the browser. An AI Chat reply keeps `errorCode: 'auth'` so a reloaded chat still shows the button. `verify/ai-chat.mjs` checks it with the fake CLI answering that error; `verify/ai-edit.mjs` and `verify/ai-generate.mjs` check the button label.

## Git-ignored files

`git:status` runs with `--ignored=matching`, which lists an ignored folder once (`build/`) and ignored files matched by a pattern (`*.log`), and returns them as `ignored`. The project tree colors those paths and everything inside an ignored folder orange (`--vcs-ignored`: #e0913d dark, #c26a00 light); a changed or new file keeps its status color. Test: `verify/vcs-ignored.mjs` (port 9860).

## Git log and activity

The Git tool window (Alt+9) has three tabs. Log draws the commit graph (`layoutGraph` in `renderer/features/vcs/index.js`: each commit takes the lane waiting for it, its first parent continues the lane and merge parents branch out), marks merge commits, lists a merge's parents in the details, and filters by message/hash/author or by user, where "Me" is `git config user.name`. A filtered list shows dots only, since lines across hidden commits would mislead. Activity is `git reflog` for HEAD (`git:reflog`) as a timeline grouped by day: commits, amends, merges, checkouts, pulls, resets, finished rebases, cherry-picks and reverts each get a badge and a sentence from the `ACTIVITY` table, with git's own text (branch names, messages) kept as user data. Clicking an action's hash shows the commit in Log. The log's filter bar wraps as a whole (the user filter is one unit, `.vcs-log-user`), the commit subject keeps at least 120 px while author and date shrink, and below 720 px the changed-files side goes under the commits (a container query on the Git tool window). Tests: `verify/vcs-log.mjs` (ports 9660-9661; the narrow layout at 1008x681 with the Notifications window open, in Korean).

A commit's context menu is PyCharm's: Copy Revision Number (full hash), Cherry-Pick and Revert Commit (vcs-merge), Checkout Revision (detached HEAD), Reset Current Branch to Here… (the Git Reset dialog: Soft, Mixed, Hard, Keep; a Local History label first), Undo Commit (the last commit: `git reset --soft HEAD~1`, its message back in the commit box), Edit Commit Message…, New Branch… (checkout optional) and New Tag…. Undo Commit and Edit Commit Message are off for commits a remote branch has (`git:unpushed` with the log) and main refuses them too. Edit Commit Message rewords in memory: the commit and the ones after it on HEAD's history are rewritten with their trees, authors and messages, hashed in main and written by one `git hash-object --stdin-paths`, then `git update-ref` moves HEAD, so the work tree, the index and local changes are untouched (Activity: "Edited a commit message"). Interactive rebase, Fixup/Squash/Drop, Create Patch and Show Repository at Revision are not offered. Tests: `verify/vcs-log-actions.mjs` (ports 9432-9433).

## Git merge, rebase and stash

`renderer/features/vcs-merge` (plugin `vcs-merge`, depends on `vcs`) builds on extension points the vcs plugin exposes as `ide.vcs`: `addBranchActions`, `addLogActions`, `addCommitHeader` and `setConflictHandler`, plus `invoke`, `notify`, `reload`, `openTextDiff` and `diffLines`. The branch popup offers Merge into Current…, Rebase Current onto…, Stash Changes… and Unstash Changes…. `git:status` reports `op` (merge, rebase, cherry-pick or revert) from the marker files in the git dir. While git is stopped, the popup leads with Resolve Conflicts…, Continue, Skip and Abort, the branch widget says Merging or Rebasing, and the Commit window shows the same controls above a separate Merge Conflicts group, whose files are never ticked for commit. Commands that can stop on conflicts go through `runStoppable` in `main/features/git.js`, which returns the conflicted files, and then open the Conflicts dialog: each file's change on Yours and Theirs, Accept Yours / Accept Theirs (`git checkout --ours/--theirs` and `git add`, or `git rm` where that side deleted the file) and Merge…. The merge tool is three Monaco editors, Yours | Result | Theirs. `mergeChunks` groups the base→yours and base→theirs hunks that touch into chunks (conflict, one side only, or the same change on both). The result starts as the base, and each side of a chunk is accepted (» «; the second side of a conflict is appended) or ignored (✕) from the strips between the panes. Apply writes the result and runs `git add`; the file's open editor shows the result before the tool closes (an editor with unsaved changes keeps them). During a merge, a commit takes the whole index (git refuses partial commits then) and the message is prefilled from MERGE_MSG. Unstash lists the stashes and their files (untracked ones come from the stash's third parent), diffs a file, and applies or pops, optionally reinstating the index or onto a new branch; Drop asks first. Its Cherry-Pick and Revert Commit entries in the Log's context menu cherry-pick or revert a commit (`-m 1` for a merge). Stash commands run without `--literal-pathspecs`, which would keep `stash -u` from removing untracked files. Tests: `verify/vcs-merge.mjs` (ports 9680-9681).

## Git daily flows: Amend, branch Delete and Rename, Show History

In the vcs plugin (`renderer/features/vcs`, `main/features/git.js`). Amend (`#vcsAmend`, a row of its own between the commit message and the buttons) fills the message box from `git:lastCommit` and brings the earlier text back when it is turned off with the message unedited; the buttons read Amend Commit and Amend Commit and Push…. `git:commit` with `{amend: true}` runs `git commit --amend` on the ticked files, or `--amend --only` with none, which changes only the message and leaves other staged changes out. When `git:lastCommit` reports `pushedTo` (HEAD is on its upstream) the commit asks first. Amend is off without a commit and while git is stopped in an operation. Commit-check hooks get `amend` in their argument. A push git rejects as non-fast-forward (typically Amend Commit and Push… on a pushed commit) opens PyCharm's Push Rejected question: `git:push` answers `rejected` (git's reason) and, for non-fast-forward, `lease` (the remote branch's commit as last fetched) and `dropped` (the commits there HEAD lacks, listed in the question as what Force Push removes). Force Push sends `git:push(root, {lease})`, which runs `git push --force-with-lease=refs/heads/<branch>:<lease> <remote> HEAD:refs/heads/<branch>` (never plain `--force`), so a push made after the question is refused even when a fetch has seen it ("Force Push was refused: … changed after the Push Rejected question"). Cancel leaves a "Push rejected" warning; a "fetch first" rejection keeps git's message. Branches matching `vcs.protectedBranches` (Settings › Git › Protected branches, default `master, main`, `*` globs, comma separated) ask again with a warning before a force push (Force Push to Protected Branch), as PyCharm's protected branches do. Test: `verify/vcs-force-push.mjs` (ports 9420-9421, in CI; a bare local remote).

The branch popup is `ide.ui.pick`, which has no submenus: while it is open the plugin watches it, and Right on the selected local branch (caret at the end of the filter) or a right-click on one closes it and opens Checkout, Rename… and Delete (`#vcsBranchMenu`) beside the row; Enter still checks out. `git:deleteBranch` runs `git branch -d` and never deletes the current branch; on "not fully merged" it returns the branch's commits missing from HEAD (or from its upstream, which git judged by) for the Branch Is Not Fully Merged question, whose Delete runs `-D`. The Deleted branch notification restores it with its upstream: `git:deleteBranch` returns `upstream` (`branch.<name>.remote` and `.merge`, which git drops with the branch) and `git:restoreBranch` writes them back. Rename… (`git:renameBranch`, `git branch -m`, or `-M` for a change of case only, which Windows otherwise refuses) works for the current branch too.

Show History (`Vcs.ShowTabbedFileHistory`; in the Git menu after Show Git Log, and Git: Show History in the editor and project tree context menus) shows the Log of one file: `git:fileHistory` runs `git log --follow --name-status -z` and gives each commit the file's repo-relative path there (and its old path for a rename). The Log bar shows a Path chip whose × lists every commit again (as Show Git Log does, and a commit opened from Annotate or Activity), rows are dots without graph lines, and the side shows the selected commit's change of the file as an inline diff instead of the changed files (details hidden; below 720 px the side takes 3/5 of the height), or "Binary file" for a binary version (`git:fileAt` with `{binary: true}` returns `{binary: true}` instead of text, by the editor's rule: a NUL in the first 8000 bytes and not UTF-16); Show Diff and Compare with Local then say the file is binary instead of opening a diff. Enter or Show Diff opens that change in a tab, and Compare with Local diffs that version with the open editor's model (editable) or the file on disk (`openLocalDiff`, shared with Show Diff with HEAD).

- Test: `verify/vcs-daily.mjs` (ports 9056-9057, in CI): English and Korean, 1008x681, the history of a binary file (no text sent to the window, the binary warning), plus main-process checks for a project in a subfolder of the work tree, `--only`, an unmerged-to-upstream branch, Restore bringing back the upstream, and a case-only rename (a rename onto another existing branch is still refused).

## Missing Git and damaged repositories

`git:status` outside a working repository answers `{isRepo: false, reason?}` (`probeRepo` and `notRepo()` in `main/features/git.js`): `gitMissing` when git.exe cannot be spawned (not on PATH), `damaged` when git fails while a `.git` exists in the project or a folder above it (with `top` and `error`, git's `fatal:` line), and no reason for a folder that is simply not a repository. The Commit window then names the cause ("Git is not installed or not on PATH…", or "Git cannot use the repository in <path>. It may be damaged:" and git's message) with Refresh, and offers Create Git Repository only when there is no reason; `git:init` passes the reason, so Git.Init refuses to run `git init` over a damaged repository and shows the translated reason when Git is missing. Test: `verify/tool-failures.mjs`.

## Packaging

`npm run dist` builds two Windows installers with electron-builder (config in package.json `build`): `dist/PyIDE-Setup-<version>.exe` (NSIS) and `dist/PyIDE-<version>.msi` (WiX, which electron-builder downloads into its cache). electron-builder rather than Electron Forge because its NSIS target does what the installer needs out of the box: a per-user install with no admin prompt (`perMachine: false`, `allowElevation: false`), a choice of install folder, a Start menu shortcut, an uninstaller, and `asarUnpack` globs. Forge's Windows makers are Squirrel, MSI and MSIX, so an NSIS installer would need a third-party maker. The build packs Electron from `node_modules/electron/dist` (no second download), keeps only the `en-US` and `ko` Chromium locales, and skips native rebuilds (`npmRebuild: false`), since `@lydell/node-pty` ships N-API prebuilds. `vendor/` is not in git: it must hold ruff and debugpy (pip `--target`) before you build. Do not edit files while a build packs `app.asar`: electron-builder reads them as it writes the archive, and a file that changes size mid-pack shifts the files after it.

- App code goes into `resources/app.asar`. Anything that another program opens is unpacked into `app.asar.unpacked` (`build.asarUnpack`): `vendor/` (ruff.exe, debugpy), `node_modules/pyright`, `node_modules/@lydell` (ConPTY binaries and the conout worker), and every `.py` under `main/` and `renderer/features/` (AI and semantic sidecars, the Python Console REPL, test runner helpers). Features build these paths from `ctx.unpackedDir` (see Main-process context). Monaco, xterm, marked and the locale JSON files load from inside the asar. Neither installer is signed.
- Icon: `build/icon.ico`, rendered from the welcome screen's "Py" tile by `node_modules\electron\dist\electron.exe tools/build-app-icon.js`.
- "Open with PyIDE" for `.py` files: a `PyIDE.py` ProgID plus a value in `Software\Classes\.py\OpenWithProgids` (NSIS: `build/installer.nsh`; MSI: `build/msi-project.js`, the `msiProjectCreated` hook that adds the same values to the WiX project). Neither changes the default program for `.py`, and each uninstaller removes only what it added. Launched with a file, PyIDE opens the file's folder as the project and then the file (the `file` query of `renderer/index.html`). It also opens a folder given on the command line.
- NSIS, per-user: `PyIDE-Setup-<version>.exe /S /D=<folder>`. Uninstall: `"<folder>\Uninstall PyIDE.exe" /S`.
- MSI, per-user by default (no UAC prompt) into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\PyIDE`: `msiexec /i PyIDE-<version>.msi /qn /l*v install.log`. Uninstall: `msiexec /x PyIDE-<version>.msi /qn` (or the product code). `msi.upgradeCode` is fixed (`C8A5FFE8-0263-4874-B6AC-E39E095BE819`), so a newer MSI upgrades an installed one in place. Deployment: run the per-user command in each user's context, for example an Intune Win32 app or Configuration Manager application with install behavior "user", or a logon script. For a machine-wide install, run `msiexec /i PyIDE-<version>.msi ALLUSERS=1 /qn` elevated (or as SYSTEM). That installs into Program Files with an all-users Start menu shortcut, and the "Open with" values go to HKLM (the hook writes them to HKMU). The machine-wide mode has not been tested here.
- The MSI has no dialogs (electron-builder `oneClick`), so double-clicking it installs per-user at once and starts PyIDE when it finishes. It has no install-folder choice (except `APPLICATIONFOLDER=<folder>` on the msiexec command line) and no running-app check, both of which the NSIS installer has.
- App data in `%APPDATA%\PyIDE` and `~/.pyide` survives either uninstall.
- WiX 4.0's `light.exe` is not long-path aware. When the checkout is deep enough that `dist\win-unpacked` paths pass 260 characters, `build/msi-project.js` points the WiX file sources at a temporary directory junction to `dist\win-unpacked` in `%TEMP%`, and removes the link when the build exits.
- `node verify/installed.mjs [nsis|msi|all] [installer file]` runs each installer in turn: it installs the build silently and per-user (NSIS into a temp folder, MSI into its default folder), then runs the installed `PyIDE.exe` over CDP. It opens a `.py` the way "Open with" does and checks Pyright diagnostics, the terminal, ruff reformat and the Git log. Finally it uninstalls and checks that the files, the shortcut, the install record and the `.py` registration are gone. Shells started by the Claude desktop app see that app package's private copy of `HKCU\Software\Classes`. There the NSIS installer's "Open with" values are checked in that copy, and the MSI's, which the msiexec service writes to the real registry, are checked from its verbose log.

## Updates

`renderer/features/updater` and `main/features/updater.js` update PyIDE with electron-updater and a generic feed.

- The feed is the setting `updates.feedUrl` (Settings › Updates). It is an http(s) URL, or a local folder (or `file:` URL) holding `latest.yml` and the installers, which the main process serves on 127.0.0.1 while PyIDE runs. Until the user sets it, an installed copy uses package.json `updateFeed`: the GitHub release tagged `pyide` in `mandur23/Portfolio-Nano-` (`https://github.com/mandur23/Portfolio-Nano-/releases/download/pyide/`). Source runs have no default, and `PYIDE_UPDATE_FEED` overrides it (`off` turns it off; `verify/lib.mjs` sets `off`, so tests never reach GitHub). An empty setting, typed by the user, turns updates off. `build.publish` names the same URL, so electron-builder writes `dist/latest.yml` and `resources/app-update.yml` (cache folder `%LOCALAPPDATA%\pyide-updater`); the feed in use replaces its URL at run time.
- Publishing a release: add a `## <version>` section to `CHANGELOG.md`, commit, then `npm run release -- <version>` on a clean `master` (`scripts/release.mjs`, plain Node, needs `gh` signed in). It checks the version is semver x.y.z and above package.json's, that tag `v<version>` exists neither locally nor on origin, and that local `master` contains origin's (read with `git ls-remote`, nothing fetched; behind or diverged stops it), sets it in package.json and package-lock.json (top level and `packages[""]`, formatting kept), runs `npm run dist` and checks `dist` for both installers, a `latest.yml` of that version whose sha512 matches the exe, and the exe's `.blockmap` (its blocks must add up to the exe's length), and runs `verify/installed.mjs all` through `verify/on-desktop.ps1`. It then commits `Release <version>` (author PyIDE), tags `v<version>` and pushes both with one `git push --atomic`, before anything is uploaded. Publishing to the `pyide` release (uploads use `--clobber`) uploads both installers, `PyIDE-Setup-<version>.exe.blockmap` and a copy of the exe named `PyIDE-Setup.exe` (the README's stable link), sets the title to `PyIDE <version>` and the notes (the changelog section in the usual frame, or `--notes <file>` as is), uploads `latest.yml` last (the moment installed copies see the new version), deletes older versions' files except the previous version's `PyIDE-Setup-<previous>.exe.blockmap` (the previous version is the newest one below this one on the release; copies of it need that blockmap for a differential update, and the run says whether it is there), and reads `latest.yml` back from the feed. A stop prints what to do for how far the run got: before the push, how to undo (`git tag -d`, `git reset --hard HEAD~1`; package.json is put back only when nothing was committed); after the push, not to reset but to finish with `npm run release -- <version> --publish-only`, which skips steps 1-6 and uploads from `dist` once `dist/latest.yml` names that version (hash checked) and the tag is on origin; after `latest.yml` is uploaded, that the release is live. That repository's other releases carry the Melody Breeze APK, which its site links as `releases/latest/download/…`, so the `pyide` release must never be Latest: every edit passes `--latest=false`, and if `gh release list` shows `pyide` as Latest afterwards the script sets the previous Latest back and fails. `--skip-tests` skips the installer tests. `--dry-run` runs the checks (problems become warnings), the build and the tests, prints the git and gh commands it would run, and puts the version and `dist/latest.yml` back; `--no-build` (a testing aid, `--dry-run` only) skips the build and checks whatever `dist` holds. The installer tests install the MSI into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\PyIDE`, the default folder of a per-user NSIS install, so with `Uninstall PyIDE.exe` there the script stops before building; `--replace-local-install` (refused while `PyIDE.exe` runs; it closes nothing) uninstalls that copy with `/S /currentuser` (settings in `%APPDATA%\PyIDE` stay), runs the tests and installs the new build there silently (the previous version's installer from `dist` if the tests failed). Once the uninstaller has started, every way out reinstalls, including Ctrl+C, Ctrl+Break and SIGTERM (their handlers also put the version back when nothing was committed). If the reinstall fails, the release stops before committing, exits non-zero and names the installer to run by hand. A dry run never touches that install. `PYIDE_DIST_DIR=<folder>` builds into that folder instead of `dist/` (also read by `verify/installed.mjs`), for when another program keeps files in `dist/win-unpacked` open and electron-builder fails with EBUSY. A beta (`npm run release -- 0.2.5-beta.1`) is built and tested like a release (electron-builder writes `dist/beta.yml` instead of `latest.yml`), committed and tagged `v0.2.5-beta.1`, and publishes only its installers and blockmap and then `beta.yml`: `latest.yml`, `PyIDE-Setup.exe`, the release title and notes and every stable version's files stay, so only Beta installs see it. Its notes are `## <beta>` or else `## <x.y.z>` in CHANGELOG.md (used by What's New; the release page is not edited). A stable release uploads `latest.yml` and a copy of it as `beta.yml` together, last, unless the release holds a newer beta, whose `beta.yml` and files then stay. Deletes: a stable release removes the files of older versions except the previous stable's and the previous beta's blockmaps; a beta removes only older betas' files except the previous beta's blockmap; newer versions are never removed. Step 8 reads back each channel file it uploaded.
- Checks: on startup (3 s after the main window is ready, unless `updates.checkOnStartup` is off; a version in `updates.skippedVersion` is passed over), then daily or weekly while PyIDE runs (see Periodic update checks at the end), and Help › Check for Updates… (`CheckForUpdates`), which also reports "latest version" and errors and ignores a skipped version. Settings › Updates has the feed with Browse…, the startup check, this installation's version and kind, the skipped version with Clear, and Check Now. Check Now shows its result on the page (`#updatesCheckResult`: checking, no feed, latest, a failure in red, a newer version) instead of a dialog over Settings (`ide.updater.check({report})`); only the MSI case, which has a choice to make, still opens its dialog.
- Deadline: a check has 30 s (`PYIDE_UPDATE_TIMEOUT_MS` in tests) and then fails with "The update server did not answer. Check your internet connection or proxy settings.": electron-updater's own timeout arms on a 'socket' event Electron's net requests never emit, so a server that accepts and never answers would keep it going. A timed-out check is abandoned and electron-updater's cached `checkForUpdatesPromise` is cleared, so the next check starts afresh. A check from the menu shows a "Checking for updates…" background task. Offline, timeout, HTTP (`HTTP_ERROR_*`) and feed (`ERR_UPDATER_*`) errors are marked expected: the dialog or the log tells them, not IDE Internal Errors. Test: `verify/env-failures.mjs` (a server that accepts and never answers, a 4 s deadline, a second check, an offline check).
- Channels (0.2.5): Settings › Updates › "Update channel:" Stable (default) or Beta (`updates.channel`, passed with `updater:check(feed, {channel})`). Stable reads `latest.yml`; Beta reads `beta.yml`, which every release keeps at the newest beta or stable version, so a beta install also gets stable releases, and reads `latest.yml` when the feed has no `beta.yml`. electron-updater turns `allowDowngrade` on when a channel is set; main turns it off again, so a copy switched from Beta back to Stable keeps its beta until a newer stable release comes out (the page says so). Versions order as semver (`0.2.5-beta.1 < 0.2.5-beta.2 < 0.2.5`, `compareVersions` in `main/features/updater.js`, which `scripts/release.mjs` also uses). What's New: `## 0.2.5-beta.1` headings are sections; a stable build leaves beta sections out, and a beta build also shows the section of the release it previews (`## 0.2.5` in 0.2.5-beta.1).
- An NSIS install (`Uninstall PyIDE.exe` next to `PyIDE.exe`) downloads the update in the background, differentially when it can (below), with "Downloading PyIDE 0.1.1… 42%" in the status bar (order 7), or "Downloading PyIDE 0.1.1 (delta 4.5 MB)… 42%" for a differential download. It then offers Restart to Update in the status bar and in a dialog with Skip This Version and Later. Restart saves modified files first, because the installer closes PyIDE even if a window would refuse. It then runs the installer silently (`--updated --force-run`), and the installer starts PyIDE again through its Start menu shortcut.
- Differential (delta) downloads (0.2.4): electron-updater rebuilds the new installer from the old one plus the blocks that changed, so a small release downloads a few MB instead of the ~98 MB installer (0.2.4 -> 0.2.5 test builds: 4.5 MB in 10 Range requests, 4.6%). What it uses, from `electron-updater` 6.8.9 (`NsisUpdater.doDownloadUpdate`, `AppUpdater.differentialDownloadInstaller`, `GenericDifferentialDownloader`): the old installer is `%LOCALAPPDATA%\pyide-updater\installer.exe`, which every NSIS install (and update) copies itself to; never the feed. The new installer's blockmap is `<installer URL>.blockmap`; the old one is `%LOCALAPPDATA%\pyide-updater\current.blockmap` if electron-updater kept one from the last download, else the new installer's URL with the new version replaced by the running one plus `.blockmap` (`<feed>/PyIDE-Setup-<running version>.exe.blockmap`), which is why a release keeps the previous version's blockmap. The changed runs of blocks come from the new installer with one single-range request each (`useMultipleRangeRequest: false`: GitHub's release downloads and the local folder server answer single ranges; the folder server answers `Range: bytes=a-b` with 206 and sends the whole file for anything else). The rebuilt file must match latest.yml's sha512. Any failure (no cached installer, a 404 for the old blockmap, no Range support, a hash mismatch, a corrupt cache) is logged by electron-updater (`Cannot download differentially, fallback to full download: …`) and the full installer is downloaded; `main/features/updater.js` wraps that step to log which happened (`downloaded 0.2.5 differentially: 4.5 MB of 98.2 MB`, or `differential download of 0.2.5 not possible …; downloading the full installer`) and to keep `download` ({mode: delta | full, size, bytes, reason: off | fallback}) in the updater state. A `current.blockmap` whose length is not the cached installer's (left by an update that was downloaded but never installed) is removed first, so the feed's blockmap is used instead of a hash mismatch. Off switches: Settings › Updates › "Download only the changed parts of an update when possible" (`updates.differential`, default on; passed with `updater:download`) and `PYIDE_UPDATE_DIFFERENTIAL=0`, which wins; both log the full download. 0.2.3 and older download 0.2.4 in full (they turned differential downloads off), and the `pyide` release had no blockmaps before 0.2.4, so the first differential updates are 0.2.4 -> the next release.
- An MSI install downloads nothing. Its dialog offers Download the new installer, which shows `PyIDE-<version>.msi` in a folder feed or opens `<feed>/PyIDE-<version>.msi`, because MSI installs are upgraded by Windows Installer. A source run only reports what the feed offers.
- What's New: `CHANGELOG.md` ships in the app. At startup main compares this version with `seenVersion` in `<userData>/updater.json` and records this one; the first window then shows "What's New in PyIDE <version>" with the `## <version>` sections newer than the one that ran before, up to this one (an older `updater.json` without `seenVersion`, from 0.2.4 or before: only this version's section; no `updater.json`, a fresh install: nothing). It shows once per update, and never in detached windows. Help › What's New (`WhatsNew`) lists every section up to this version. Sections are rendered as text: `- ` bullets become list items and backticks `code`. `PYIDE_CHANGELOG` replaces the shipped file in tests.
- IPC: `updater:state`, `updater:releaseNotes` ({version, afterUpdate (given once), all}), `updater:check(feed)`, `updater:download({differential})`, `updater:install`, `updater:openInstaller`, and the event `updater:state` ({kind, version, phase, available, percent, download}).
- Tests: `verify/updater.mjs` (ports 9800-9801, 9805, 9070) runs from source: no feed, a newer version, the same version and an unreadable feed in a local folder, the Settings page (with the differential switch and Check Now's results on the page), a skipped version, What's New (port 9805: after an update, the next start, Help, an older `updater.json`, a fresh install), the delta status text (states set with `PYIDE_UPDATE_TEST=1`'s `updater:testPhase`, which also takes an object of state fields), and Korean; the channels (port 9070: a Stable install sees 0.3.0 from latest.yml and not 9.9.9-beta.1 from beta.yml, a Beta install sees the beta, a stable release in beta.yml, latest.yml when there is no beta.yml, no downgrade on either channel, Check Now with the page's unsaved channel), and in pure Node the semver order and the What's New sections of betas. `verify/release-harness.mjs` also covers the beta channel (a beta uploads only beta.yml after its installers and leaves latest.yml, the title and stable files alone; a stable release uploads latest.yml and beta.yml last, keeps beta.yml at a newer beta, and keeps the previous stable's and beta's blockmaps). `verify/update-delta.mjs [--old <dir> --new <dir>] [--keep-builds]` (no CDP ports; not in CI: it builds) proves differential downloads without installing anything: without folders it builds this version and the next patch with `npx electron-builder --win nsis --x64 -c.extraMetadata.version=… -c.directories.output=%TEMP%\pyide-delta-*` (never `dist/`) and deletes them afterwards (~600 MB each). Each case runs `verify/update-delta-main.cjs`, a windowless Electron main process with the old version's package.json that registers `main/features/updater.js` as an NSIS install (`PYIDE_UPDATE_TEST_KIND=nsis`, honoured only with `PYIDE_UPDATE_TEST=1`), with `LOCALAPPDATA` in a temp folder whose `pyide-updater\installer.exe` is the old installer, and downloads through the real electron-updater and Electron net. It checks the downloaded file equals the new installer byte for byte and counts what the feed server sent: delta over http and from a folder feed (only the plan's ranges, both blockmaps read), a stale `current.blockmap`, fallbacks with no old blockmap on the feed, no cached installer and a corrupt one (sha512 mismatch), and both off switches; and that the real `%LOCALAPPDATA%\pyide-updater` is untouched. `verify/release-harness.mjs` (pure Node, in CI) runs `scripts/release.mjs` against a fake git/gh/dist: failure advice by phase, the order of a release, local-install handling, signals, and the blockmap rules (uploaded with the installers before `latest.yml`, previous version's kept, older ones deleted, a missing or mismatched blockmap stops the release, dry-run output). `verify/update-e2e.mjs [feed folder]` (ports 9802-9804) builds the next patch version into a temp feed (`-c.extraMetadata.version`). It installs `dist/PyIDE-Setup-<version>.exe`, updates it through the UI, and checks that PyIDE restarts by itself and comes back as the new version with its settings. With the MSI in `dist`, it also checks the MSI dialog. The installer restarts PyIDE as the signed-in user, outside the test's environment and profile, so the test closes that instance at once and starts it again with the test profile. It refuses to run while PyIDE is installed per-user in `%LOCALAPPDATA%ProgramsPyIDE`: the NSIS installer removes an existing per-user install before installing anywhere, so the test copy would uninstall the real one.
## Jupyter notebooks

`renderer/features/notebook` and `main/features/notebook.js` open `.ipynb` files as notebooks, like PyCharm's Jupyter support. The notebook is a `text: true` file viewer over the file's document: every change is written back into the document as nbformat 4 JSON (sorted keys, one-space indent and a final newline, as Jupyter writes it), so dirty state, Ctrl+S, close prompts and "Open as Text" come from the core. Fields the plugin does not edit (notebook and cell metadata, ids, attachments, `nbformat_minor`) are kept as they are; new cells get ids when the file is nbformat 4.5. The file's BOM and line separator are kept: the JSON is compared with the model's text as LF and, after `setValue`, the model gets its EOL back, so a CRLF notebook stays CRLF (`verify/encodings.mjs`). An empty `.ipynb` opens as a new notebook; invalid JSON or nbformat 3 shows an error with Open as Text. New Jupyter Notebook is an action and a project tree item.

Cells are Monaco editors created through `ide.createEditor` (lazily, as cells approach the viewport) with models on the `pyide-nb:` scheme. Code cells complete names and show hovers from the running kernel (Jupyter `complete_request` and `inspect_request`); Pyright only serves `file:` models. Markdown cells render with the bundled `marked` through an allowlist sanitizer (attachments and relative images resolve against the notebook). Outputs: stream text with `\r` progress handling and ANSI colors (core's `ide.ui.ansi`, shared with the consoles), tracebacks, `image/png`, `jpeg`, `gif` and `svg+xml`, Markdown, JSON, and `text/html` in an `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts">` that has an opaque origin and reports its height by `postMessage`. `update_display_data` and `clear_output(wait=True)` work, and `input()` shows an input field in the cell.

Keys follow Jupyter: in a cell Shift+Enter runs and moves on (adding a cell at the end), Ctrl+Enter runs in place, Alt+Enter runs and inserts below, Esc and Enter switch between edit and command mode, and Up/Down at a cell's first or last line move into the neighbour. In command mode: A/B insert, D D delete, Z undo delete, M/Y/R change the type, C/X/V copy, cut and paste cells, I I interrupt, 0 0 restart. The toolbar adds Run All, Run All Above, Run Cell and All Below, Interrupt, Restart, moves and Clear All Outputs; Run All Cells, Restart Kernel, Interrupt Kernel and Clear All Outputs are also actions.

Each open notebook has its own kernel, started on the first run in the project interpreter with the notebook's folder as the working directory and the project root on `PYTHONPATH`. `kernel_bridge.py` runs in that interpreter, starts ipykernel through jupyter_client with a private kernelspec (a user-installed `python3` spec cannot redirect it to another Python), owns every ZMQ socket in one thread, and talks JSON lines over stdin/stdout through `ctx.startProcess` and `ide.proc`. `kernel_launcher.py` starts ipykernel with an interrupt that also wakes blocking calls such as `time.sleep` on Windows (ipykernel's own only sets Python's SIGINT flag). Run All stops at the first error (`stop_on_error`). Closing the tab shuts the kernel down. Without ipykernel the notebook offers Install ipykernel, which runs the Install Manager's pip item for the project interpreter (ipykernel is also listed under Python Tools). Tests: `verify/notebook.mjs` (ports 9670-9671; it keeps a venv with ipykernel and nbformat in `verify/out/notebook-venv`, and `NOTEBOOK_INSTALL=1` also installs ipykernel into a fresh venv through the Install button).

Colab runtimes (prototype): clicking the kernel label in a notebook's toolbar chooses where the kernel runs, Local Python or a Colab runtime. `main/notebook/colab.js` calls Google's Colab API (`colaboratory.googleapis.com`, v1beta: subscription, runtimespecs, runtimes, and the create operation waited for through `v1/operations/*:wait`) with the Application Default Credentials of a gcloud login, as Google's Colab CLI does by default. PyIDE has no OAuth client of its own, so signing in is gcloud's: Sign In with Google in the notebook's banner runs `gcloud auth application-default login` with the openid, cloud-platform, userinfo.email and colaboratory scopes, gcloud opens Google's page in the browser, and the notebook goes on to the runtime list once it finishes (Open the Sign-in Page if no browser opened, Cancel ends gcloud). Without gcloud the banner offers Install Google Cloud CLI, which runs `winget install --id Google.CloudSDK -e` in a terminal tab (Google's install page where there is no winget), and starts the sign-in once gcloud is in place; gcloud is looked for on PATH and in the installer's folders, since a fresh install is on PATH only for processes started later. The access token is refreshed from the ADC file in memory, and the list names the account from the ID token. The list offers the runtimes already assigned to the account and new runtimes of the specs it is eligible for (GPU T4, CPU, ...). The kernel then runs through `main/notebook/colab-bridge.js`, run by the IDE's Electron as Node, which speaks the JSON lines of `kernel_bridge.py`: it starts a kernel through the runtime's Jupyter REST API and runs its channels over one WebSocket, both with the runtime's short-lived proxy token in `X-Colab-Runtime-Proxy-Token`; the notebook fetches a new token a few minutes before it expires. Execute, outputs, display updates, input(), completion, inspection, interrupt and restart work as with a local kernel. Release the Colab Runtime gives the runtime back; a runtime the notebook asked for is also released when the notebook closes, since it uses the account's Colab compute while assigned, and when the IDE quits (main holds the quit for up to 10 seconds while it releases them). Colab errors reach the notebook as `{code} message` and the known ones are worded for the user: a sign-in Google no longer takes (expired or revoked, no Colab scope, not a personal sign-in) offers Sign In with Google again, while no network, Google refusing the account and usage limits (429) offer Try Again or Choose Another Runtime with Google's message below. The Colab API takes a gcloud sign-in's requests only with a quota project ("requires a quota project, which is not set by default"): the banner then offers Set Up a Project..., which lists the account's Google Cloud projects (Resource Manager v1) and a new "PyIDE Colab" one, creates the new one if picked, turns on colaboratory.googleapis.com in it (Service Usage v1, both operations polled), and makes it the ADC file's quota project with `gcloud auth application-default set-quota-project`, after which every Colab request carries it in `x-goog-user-project`; a project Google will not create yet (the account has not accepted Google Cloud's terms) is explained with Open Google Cloud Console. Install Google Cloud CLI runs winget in a Command Prompt tab, since Windows PowerShell would first run the user's profile, which a Restricted policy refuses with an error. A runtime Colab took back (idle, or out of time) shows as such with New Colab Runtime...: the bridge counts a refused WebSocket handshake as a failed reconnect, which Electron's Node reports only as an error event, and after three it reports the kernel dead; the notebook then finds the runtime gone (404) and goes back to local Python. Upload Files to the Runtime... (the kernel menu, on Colab) writes local files into the runtime's working directory (/content) through the kernel itself, in 512 KB pieces of silent execute requests, so it does not depend on where the runtime's Jupyter server is rooted. Keep the Runtime When the Notebook Closes leaves the runtime and its kernel running when the notebook closes or the IDE quits; userData/colab.json remembers the runtime and kernel id by notebook path, and the reopened notebook, after an IDE restart too, reattaches to that kernel (the bridge's connect with kernelId checks `GET /api/kernels/{id}` and starts a new kernel only when it is gone), so its variables are still there. Turning it off, or switching away, makes the runtime the notebook's to release again. `google.colab` asks Colab's web page for things with a `colab_request` on iopub (metadata `colab_request_type` and `colab_msg_id`) and reads the answer from stdin as an `input_reply` whose value is `{type: 'colab_reply', colab_msg_id, data | error}` (googlecolab/colabtools `_message.py`); the bridge passes the request to the notebook, which always answers, so no cell waits for nothing. `userdata.get('KEY')` (GetSecret) gets the project .env's value once the user allowed that key for that notebook (the banner's Allow and Run Again, kept in colab.json), "does not exist" for a key .env lacks, and "no access" until allowed. `drive.mount()` and `auth.authenticate_user()` (request_auth) get an error that explains why PyIDE cannot do them: Colab's page supplies those credentials, and the Colab API offers other apps no way to. Other requests get an "unsupported" error. Not yet: forms and other output that needs Colab's JavaScript. `main/security.js` is unchanged for this. Tests: `verify/colab.mjs` (ports 9675 and 9676, the second for the IDE restarted on the same profile) against a stand-in gcloud (it appears after Install, prints the sign-in page and waits for the "browser"), token endpoint, Colab API and Jupyter server whose kernel only answers requests with the runtime's proxy token, keeps its variables while it runs, sends userdata.get and drive.mount as colab_request and records the uploaded files.

## Diagnostics: IDE log and internal errors

The core part loads before everything else: `main/diagnostics` is installed at the top of `main.js`, and `renderer/core/diagnostics.js` is the first script in every window, main and detached. The required `diagnostics` plugin (`renderer/features/diagnostics`, `main/features/diagnostics.js`) is the UI.

- Log: `<userData>/logs/pyide.log` in idea.log's format (`2026-10-20 14:03:12,345 [ uptime ms]  LEVEL - #category - message`, stack traces on the following lines). It rotates by size to `pyide.1.log` … `pyide.5.log`. The level (DEBUG, INFO, WARN, ERROR) and the size cap (MB) are the settings `diagnostics.logLevel` and `diagnostics.logSizeMB`, read at startup and set in Help › Debug Log Settings…; `PYIDE_LOG_LEVEL` and `PYIDE_LOG_MAX_BYTES` override them. Every line is scrubbed of passwords, tokens and API keys (`main/diagnostics/redact.js`). The log gets main-process `console` output, renderer `console.warn/error` and `ide.log`, failed IPC handlers with their stack, `ctx.startProcess` launches and exit codes (never the program's output), the debugpy adapter connection and failed DAP requests, and the stderr of watched sidecars. In main: `ctx.diagnostics.logger('#cat').info(msg)`.
- Internal errors: uncaught exceptions and unhandled rejections in the main process and every window, scripts that fail to load, `console.error(…, err)` with an Error, main modules whose `register` throws, renderer crashes, and sidecars that exit unexpectedly. `ctx.diagnostics.watchProcess(proc, {name})` watches a helper process: its stderr goes to the log, and a non-zero exit it did not ask for (`proc.kill()`, quitting) becomes an error with the last stderr lines as its trace. Pyright (lsp) and the AI sidecars (`main/ai/runtime.js`) are watched. Main has listeners for `uncaughtException` and `unhandledRejection`, so Electron's "A JavaScript error occurred in the main process" dialog never shows, and `dialog.showErrorBox` with that title becomes an internal error. Each error's plugin is the first stack frame inside a plugin's folder or one of its main modules (`plugins.ownerOf(file)`, so `renderer/features/<id>`, `main/features/<module>` and `<userData>/plugins/<id>`); without one it is PyIDE core. Identical errors are counted, not repeated. Report a caught error yourself with `ideDiagnostics.report(err)` (renderer) or `ctx.diagnostics.report({error, title})` (main). Not internal errors, on any IPC channel nor in the console.error hook: errors marked `expected: true` (a CP949 save refusal, "Debugging stopped" when the user stops a debug session that is still starting, `diagnostics.markExpected(err)`) and failures of the environment, whose code (or message prefix, for worker errors) is EPERM, EACCES, ENOENT, ENOSPC, EROFS, EBUSY, a network code (ECONNREFUSED, ECONNRESET, ECONNABORTED, ETIMEDOUT, ENOTFOUND, EAI_AGAIN, EHOSTUNREACH, ENETUNREACH, ENETDOWN) or Electron's `net::ERR_*` (`diagnostics.isEnvironmentError`). A main module that fails to load is titled "Plugin <name> failed to load (<file>)".
- UI: a red status-bar icon (order 1) blinks while there are errors nobody has looked at. It opens IDE Internal Errors (action `ShowIdeErrors`): the list, and for the selected error its plugin, source, window, time, count and stack trace, with Copy, Clear All and Disable Plugin (not for core or required plugins; a plugin with three or more errors is called out as one that keeps failing). Help adds Show Log (`OpenLogInEditor`, opens pyide.log in an editor tab), Show Log in Explorer, Collect Logs and Diagnostic Data (`CollectZippedLogs`), Debug Log Settings… (`LogDebugConfigure`) and About PyIDE (the plugin's About replaces the core one: PyIDE, Electron, Chromium, Node, V8, Pyright, ruff, the project interpreter and the OS, with Copy).
- Collect Logs and Diagnostic Data saves a zip where the user chooses (`main/diagnostics/zip.js` writes it; nothing is uploaded): `logs/` (scrubbed again), `about.txt`, `versions.json` (with every detected interpreter), `plugins.json`, `settings.json` with every secret-looking key and value redacted, and `internal-errors.txt`. Data source and server files (safeStorage blobs) are never read.
- Tests: `verify/diagnostics.mjs` (ports 9750-9751) installs two failing test plugins from `verify/fixtures/diagnostics-plugins/`, and uses `PYIDE_DIAGNOSTICS_TEST=1` (the `diagnostics:test` IPC: core exceptions, watched processes, attempted raw dialogs) and `PYIDE_DIAGNOSTICS_SAVE_TO` (skips the save dialog). `verify/env-failures.mjs` (port 9282) covers environment failures: an update server that never answers, a missing shell, broken plugins, and a user data folder denied with `icacls` (no internal error in any of them).

## Local History and Bookmarks

`renderer/features/local-history` and `main/features/local-history.js` keep PyCharm's Local History, independent of Git. The main process owns one store per open project under `<userData>/local-history/<id>/`: `blobs/` holds each distinct content once (sha1, gzipped) and `index.json` lists each file's revisions `{t, h, k, l?}` (time, content hash or null once deleted, kind, label) plus the labels. As in PyCharm, a file is recorded from the first time it is opened in the editor or changes, so opening a project stores nothing (on a 20,000-file project the tree shows in about 0.4 s with or without the plugin). Revisions come from saves in the IDE (`fileSaved`), from a recursive watcher for changes made elsewhere (other programs, git, formatters, a detached window), and from rechecking the sizes and times of recorded files when the project opens, so their edits made while the IDE was closed are recorded too. Kinds: initial, save, external, create, delete, revert, checkpoint. A file seen first through a change counts as created only if it was born in the last 10 s. One burst of outside changes adds at most 200 files the history has not seen (a branch switch or a code generator records only the known ones). Only text files up to 1 MB outside `.git`, caches and virtual environments are kept. Reading, hashing, compressing and garbage-collecting blobs run on a worker thread (`main/worker-host.js` evaluates the same file with role `pyide-local-history`); the watcher callback only collects names, and all store operations run in one queue so a checkpoint right after a save sees the save. A burst can overflow the Windows change buffer; the watcher then reports a change without a name and goes quiet, so the store restarts it and rechecks the recorded files. Revisions older than `localHistory.days` (default 5) expire and the oldest go first above `localHistory.maxMB` (default 200), but the latest revision of every recorded file is always kept, so a file deleted long after its last change can still be recovered (Settings › Local History). Renames from the project tree carry a file's history along.

Several PyIDE instances can share the user data: every index write (the async flush and the one at close or quit) and every prune first re-reads index.json and merges it (revisions by time and hash, labels by time and text, the newer record wins); what a store removed itself (prune, size limit, rename) is tombstoned so it does not come back, a revision only this store lists whose blob the other instance collected is dropped, a revision with the same content as the one before it is left out (one change seen by both), and garbage collection keeps every blob the merged index uses. A blob stored again is touched so the other instance's 60 s grace covers it. Limit: two writes within a few milliseconds can drop the other's latest additions until that instance writes again; every instance writes with a merge when it closes the project or quits, so the final index has both. A file saved by another instance (a new file from the safe save's rename) gets a new birth time, so an instance that has not recorded it yet may label the change "Created". When a revision or the index cannot be written because of the environment (permissions, a full disk) the record is skipped and main sends `localHistory:writeFailed` ({root, dir, error}) once per store, which the plugin shows as one warning ("Local History is not being saved", the folder and the reason); history resumes by itself once the folder is writable, but a second outage in the same project session is not told again. Revert of a deleted file that is still open saves with `overwrite: true`, so it does not stop at the "no longer exists on disk" question. Test: `verify/history-instances.mjs` (ports 9282-9283; the write failure is in `verify/env-failures.mjs`).

`ide.localHistory.checkpoint(label, {paths})` adds a label and records the unsaved text of open documents (or only `paths`) first; saved ones are already on disk. The vcs plugin calls it in its `invoke` before every git command that rewrites the working tree (pull, checkout, rollback, merge, rebase, cherry-pick, revert, reset, stash, unstash, accepting a side, resolving, continue/skip/abort), lsp before applying a workspace edit (rename, quick fixes), and ai-edit before the first accepted hunk of a Ctrl+I session. Show History (editor and project tree context menus, actions `LocalHistory.ShowHistory`, `LocalHistory.ShowProjectHistory`, `LocalHistory.PutLabel`) opens a tab: revisions and labels newest first, and a diff of the selected one against the current text. For a file the current side is its live document: Revert replaces the whole text as one undoable edit and saves it, and Revert Selected Changes (or Monaco's margin arrows) puts back only the changes touching the caret or selection. A folder's history lists every file under it; a deletion row shows the content just before it and Revert recreates the file, and a label row reverts every file that changed since the label. Each revert is preceded by a "Before Local History Revert" label.

`renderer/features/bookmarks` adds PyCharm's bookmarks: F11 toggles one on the caret line, Ctrl+F11 opens the mnemonic grid (typing 0-9 or A-Z assigns it at once, with an optional description), Ctrl+Shift+digit toggles a numbered bookmark, Ctrl+digit jumps to it, Shift+F11 lists all, and the Bookmarks tool window (left, Alt+2, order 25) groups them by file with their mnemonic, line and description (context menu: Jump to Source, Edit Description…, Delete). Gutter icons are glyph-margin decorations in the left lane (a ribbon, or a tile with the mnemonic). While a file is open its bookmarks are model decorations covering the line's text, so they move with inserted and deleted lines; a whole-text replacement (reload from disk) keeps their line numbers. They are saved per project in the `bookmarks.<root>` setting with paths relative to the root, and follow renames and deletions from the project tree.

Tests: `verify/history-bookmarks.mjs` (ports 9710-9712) saves, diffs, reverts selected changes and whole files, records outside changes, creations and deletions, recovers a deleted file, checks the label before a Git rollback, toggles, assigns and jumps to bookmarks, follows edits, edits a description in the tool window, restarts with the same user data, and repeats the surfaces in Korean.

## Django, Flask and FastAPI
