`renderer/features/frameworks` (plugin `frameworks`, depends on `run`) and `main/features/frameworks.js` add PyCharm Professional's web framework support. `pyide_frameworks.py` inspects the project statically with `ast` (nothing is imported or run) using the project interpreter, on project open, interpreter change and every `.py` save. It finds Django from `manage.py` and its `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE` (settings split with `from x import *` are followed), local apps from `INSTALLED_APPS`, their models and fields, `admin.site.register` and `@admin.register`, and the URL patterns from `ROOT_URLCONF` through `include()` with app namespaces. Flask apps (`Flask(...)` at module level or in a factory), blueprints and their prefixes, FastAPI apps and `APIRouter`s with `include_router` prefixes give the other routes. Every route has one shape, `{framework, methods, path, name, view, file, line, viewFile, viewLine}`. The result also lists the template folders (every `templates` folder plus `template_folder=` and `Jinja2Templates(directory=)`), the templates in them by the names templates use, and the Django tag libraries in `templatetags/`. `ide.frameworks` exposes it: `info()`, `kinds()`, `refresh()`, `onChange(fn)`, `openRoute(route, {external})`, `openUrl`, `runServer(fw, {debug})`, `runManageTask(task)`.

- Run configuration types `django-server` (runserver with host, port, settings module and autoreload), `flask-server` (`python -m flask run` with `FLASK_APP`, `--reload` and `FLASK_DEBUG`) and `fastapi-server` (`python -m uvicorn module:app`, `--factory` for a factory, `--reload`). A detected framework gets one saved configuration per project, selected when nothing else was. Debugging (Alt+Shift+F9, the Debug Development Server action) goes through the run plugin's new `debugTarget(cfg, interp) -> {script, cwd, params, env}` type hook (`script` may be `-m`) into `ide.debugger.debugFile`, without the reloader, whose worker process debugpy did not attach to before 0.2.9 (with Debugging subprocesses it would; allowing the reloader under Debug is not done or tested). Run configuration fields can also be `kind: 'checkbox'`.
- Ctrl+Alt+R opens the manage.py task popup with the commands from `manage.py help --commands` (cached per interpreter); typed arguments are kept and anything else runs as typed. Tasks, Make Migrations and Migrate run in one `django-manage` Run tab. These and the server actions are also in the Tools menu.
- Tool windows: Django Structure (left; apps, models with fields, admin, URLs, settings) and Endpoints (bottom; method, path, endpoint and source for every framework, with a filter). Rows jump to the view; the context menu opens the route.
- View functions and class-based views get a gutter icon (left glyph lane) whose menu opens the route in the browser pane or the external browser, starting the server first if needed. Path parameters get the sample value 1. The browser pane is a custom tab with an iframe; main strips `X-Frame-Options` and CSP `frame-ancestors` from loopback sub-frame responses only, so Django's clickjacking middleware does not blank it.
- Templates: `.html` files in template folders open as `django-html` (Django projects) or `jinja2` (Flask, FastAPI). Both reuse Monaco's HTML grammar with `{% %}`, `{{ }}`, `{# #}`, `{% comment %}` and `{% verbatim %}`/`{% raw %}` states, and Monaco's HTML language service. Completion: tags (the innermost open block's end tag first, and `{% load %}`ed custom tags), filters after `|`, template names in `extends`/`include`/`import`/`from`, URL names in `{% url %}`, endpoints in `url_for()`, block names from the extended templates, Jinja2 globals. Ctrl+B and Ctrl+Click go from `{% extends/include %}` to the template, from `{% url %}`, `url_for()`, `reverse()` and `redirect()` to the view, from any Python string naming a template (`render(request, 'x.html')`) to it, from `include('app.urls')` to that urlconf and from a urls.py pattern string to its view.
- The Install Manager lists Django, Flask, FastAPI, Uvicorn and Jinja2 under Web Frameworks (pip in the project interpreter).

Tests: `verify/frameworks.mjs` (IDE ports 9740-9743, dev servers 9745-9747) creates a temp venv and installs Django, Flask, FastAPI and uvicorn into it through the Install Manager cards (`FRAMEWORKS_VENV=<python.exe>` reuses a venv that has them). On the `frameworks-django`, `frameworks-flask` and `frameworks-fastapi` fixtures it checks detection, the tool windows, the manage.py popup with makemigrations and migrate, an HTTP 200 from each server, the browser pane, a breakpoint hit in a Django view, template highlighting, completion and Ctrl+B, and the Korean UI without English leaks.

## Refactorings

`renderer/features/refactor` (plugin `refactor`), `main/features/refactor.js` and `renderer/features/refactor/refactor_server.py` add PyCharm's Extract Variable (Ctrl+Alt+V), Extract Constant (Ctrl+Alt+C), Extract Parameter (Ctrl+Alt+P), Extract Method (Ctrl+Alt+M), Inline (Ctrl+Alt+N), Change Signature (Ctrl+F6) and Move (F6), the Refactor This popup (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+T) and the main menu's Refactor submenu (Rename stays lsp's Shift+F6). Action ids are PyCharm's (`IntroduceVariable`, `IntroduceConstant`, `IntroduceParameter`, `ExtractMethod`, `Inline`, `ChangeSignature`, `Move`, `Refactorings.QuickListPopupAction`). The project tree offers Move… for Python files and folders.

- Engine: rope (pure Python, LGPL) in the project interpreter. Measured on 2026-10-20 against the bundled Pyright 1.1.406: its `codeActionProvider` lists only `quickfix` and `source.organizeImports`, a code action request on a selected expression returns `[]`, and `textDocument/inlayHint` is an unhandled method (refactorings and inlay hints are Pylance features). Rope 1.15 did every refactoring on a three-module fixture in 4-140 ms per request, including rewriting imports across files. It runs in the project interpreter rather than the shared private venv because it parses with the running Python's `ast` (new syntax needs the project's Python) and resolves imports through that interpreter's `sys.path`, and the private venv only exists after AI setup. Rope is installed through the Install Manager's pip item (`pip:rope`, Python Tools). When it is missing, every refactoring offers Install rope, which runs that item and then retries.
- The sidecar speaks JSON lines, one process per interpreter. Each request carries the project root and the unsaved text of open Python documents. Rope reads files through an overlay file system and multi-step refactorings (Change Signature renames, then removes, adds and reorders) keep their intermediate results in memory. Nothing is written: the reply lists each file's old and new text and any moves. Each request opens a fresh rope project with `ropefolder=None`, so there are no stale caches and no `.ropeproject` folder.
- Rope's output is adjusted to read like PyCharm's. Extract Constant and Extract Parameter are implemented on `ast` instead (rope puts constants after the current function and introduces parameters only for plain names), and constants go after the imports. Move imports names as `from module import name` (rope's `add_import` is replaced), appends the moved definition to the destination (rope inserts it above a docstring) and creates a destination file that does not exist. Imports that rope drops although the module still uses them (or star imports) are put back, and PEP 8 blank lines are kept around new and removed definitions. Extract Parameter adds `name=<expression>` and leaves calls unchanged, as PyCharm does for Python, so it refuses expressions that read the function's locals. Change Signature renames the function and its parameters, removes, adds (with a default and/or a value for existing calls), reorders and changes defaults. Keyword-only parameters are kept as they are, because rope does not list them.
- With no selection, the Extract refactorings offer the expressions around the caret. Problems found before a dialog opens (no expression, a local in a constant, no function under the caret, syntax errors) show as an editor hint "Cannot perform refactoring. …". Problems with the dialog's input stay in the dialog. Rope's own errors map to these messages where they match and are shown verbatim otherwise.
- Every dialog has Preview, Refactor and Cancel. The preview lists the changed files and moves with a diff for each; Do Refactor applies and Cancel changes nothing. Removing a parameter the body still uses, or adding one with neither a default nor a value, always goes through the preview with a Problems detected note.
- Applying: each open document gets one undoable edit (a line diff, so carets and decorations elsewhere stay put), closed files are written with their encoding and line endings, and then files and folders move (their open tabs close and reopen at the new place). `ide.localHistory.checkpoint('Before Refactoring')` runs first when that plugin is present. Undoing the refactoring's edit in any of its documents (Ctrl+Z or Edit › Undo) undoes the whole refactoring: the other documents, the closed files (unless they changed since) and the moves. Redo (Ctrl+Shift+Z) works the same way. A file that moved has a fresh document with no undo history, so there the plugin takes Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Shift+Z until the document is edited.
- Tests: `verify/refactor.mjs` (ports 9690-9691; it keeps a venv with rope in `verify/out/refactor-venv`) runs every refactoring through its key on `verify/fixtures/refactor`. It checks the code and imports in open and closed files, compares the fixture program's output before and after, and covers preview Cancel, cross-file undo and redo, the error messages, the install offer, inlay hints and the Korean UI.

## Quick fixes and code generation

`renderer/features/code-generation` (plugin `code-generation`, depends on `refactor`) adds PyCharm's code-writing Alt+Enter fixes and Code › Override Methods… (`OverrideMethods`, Ctrl+O while a Python editor has the keyboard; Open Folder… has no key, as in PyCharm) and Implement Methods… (`ImplementMethods`, no key: Ctrl+I is ai-edit's). The refactor plugin's sidecar writes the code with Python's `ast` (no rope needed: `quickFixes`, `generate`, `classEntry`, `classMembers`, `insertMethods` in refactor_server.py) and Pyright resolves what lives in other files. Every edit applies through `ide.refactor.applyResult` (one undo step, across files) and is parsed again first, so a fix never leaves a syntax error. The sidecar makes path fields long at any depth (`long_paths`), so a fix's file and the class graph's files match the project under an 8.3 TEMP too.

- Undefined name (`reportUndefinedVariable`, read by code and range): when called, Create function '<name>' and Create class '<name>' (class first for a capitalized name), placed above the module-level statement holding the call (and the comments right over it), but below the shebang, the coding line and file-level `# type: ignore`/`# mypy:`/`# pyright:` lines, with PEP 8 spacing; parameters are named after the call's arguments (`param`, `param1` for literals, keywords kept, `*args`, `**kwargs`), `async def` for an awaited call, and a class gets `__init__` with them. Otherwise Create local variable '<name>': `<name> = None` above the statement (above the whole `if` for an `elif` test, above the compound statement for one on its `else:`, `except …:` or `case` line). The caret selects `pass` or `None`.
- Unresolved method call (`reportAttributeAccessIssue` on `x.name(...)`): Add method name() to class C. For `self`/`cls` the class is the one around the method (`cls` adds a classmethod); for another receiver Pyright's `textDocument/typeDefinition` must name a class in a project source file (not site-packages), which is then opened at the new method. Methods go at the end of the class; a body of only `pass` or `...` is replaced.
- On a class header with unimplemented abstract methods (`@abstractmethod` and the abstract* decorators, or a body that only raises NotImplementedError): Implement abstract methods, which opens the Implement chooser.
- On a `%` or `.format` string: Convert to f-string literal, offered only when the conversion is exact (printf flags, width and precision become format specs, `%(k)s` needs a dict literal, `{}`/`{0}`/`{name}` with attributes, indexes, conversions and specs; braces doubled, raw kept). Not for `*` widths, `%c`, nested specs, `*args`/`**kwargs`, bytes, implicitly concatenated literals, escapes that make braces (or `%` signs) in the value, or when the f-string would evaluate the arguments differently: an argument that is not a name, constant or attribute/subscript of those must be used exactly once and in the original order, and no such argument may be dropped. The other quote is used when an expression brings the string's quote in (valid before Python 3.12 too).
- The fixes are tied to Pyright's marker and preferred, so they list after lsp's import fix and above the suppress actions (ruff's "# noqa" included). A name used twice on a line gives each fix once.
- The chooser (Override and Implement) lists the bases' methods grouped by defining class in MRO order (C3 over the graph `ide.typeHierarchy.supertypes` builds through Pyright, typeshed stubs included; without the type-hierarchy plugin only object's methods), then object's; methods the class defines, name-mangled ones and later definitions of a name are left out; Implement shows only abstract ones, checked. Up/Down, Space, Ctrl+A, Enter, Esc. Override writes `return super().name(args)` (no `return` for `__init__`, setters of attributes and `-> None`; `await` for async; a property returns `super().name`; a staticmethod calls the base by name when the module has it), Implement writes `pass`. Signatures keep `/`, `*`, annotations whose names are builtins or bound at module level in the target file, and defaults as written, importing the names they use the way the base module has them (as PyCharm does); a default no import can reach (a class attribute) becomes `...`. They go after the class statement at the caret (end of class from the header). Messages: "Caret should be inside a class.", "No methods to override have been found".
- Known limits: Alt+Enter pressed right after an edit can show nothing until it is pressed again (Monaco replaces the explicit request with its automatic one when the file's markers change, and lsp's `diagnosticsSettled` waits for Pyright, not for ruff's lint). Add method is not offered when Pyright cannot name the receiver's class (an untyped parameter), and there is no Add field for a `self.x` that is not called. `%d` of a float becomes `{x:d}`, which raises for floats, as PyCharm's conversion does. An unresolved base contributes nothing to the chooser. A default written as `...` (no import reaches it) passes Ellipsis to the base when a call omits that argument. An f-string formats each field as it goes, `.format`/`%` after evaluating every argument, so the result differs when a later argument's call changes an earlier argument object (not refused: that would refuse `'{}: {}'.format(name, len(items))`, which PyCharm converts).
- Tests: `verify/code-generation-sidecar.mjs` (pure Node, any Python; `PYIDE_TEST_PYTHON` picks one; in CI) and `verify/code-generation.mjs` (ports 9610-9611, in CI): every fix through Alt+Enter on the fixture, one undo step, Add method into another file, the chooser from the class header and Ctrl+O, Esc, the outside-a-class hint, the files compiling, an 8.3 short-path project, and the Korean UI.

## Inlay hints

`renderer/features/inlay-hints` (plugin `inlay-hints`, depends on `lsp`) shows inferred variable types (`count: int`), parameter names in calls (`net_price(price: 9.5, quantity: 4)`) and inferred return types (`def f() -> str`) as Monaco inlay hints. Pyright 1.1.406 has no `textDocument/inlayHint`, so the plugin finds the places in Monaco's tokens and asks Pyright for the text: hover for types, signatureHelp for parameter names (once per callee signature as its hover prints it; see Runtime performance). The places are assignment, `for` and `with … as` targets at the start of a statement, positional call arguments up to the first keyword argument or unpacking, and `def`s without `->`. Like Pylance it skips literal assignments, `x = Name(…)` when the type is `Name`, Unknown types, positional-only and variadic parameters, and arguments that already carry the parameter's name. Answers are cached per document version, and hints refresh when Pyright publishes diagnostics. Settings › Editor › Inlay Hints has a master switch (`editor.inlayHints`) and one switch per kind (`editor.inlayHints.variableTypes`, `.parameterNames`, `.returnTypes`), all on by default. `ide.inlayHints.hints(model)` returns the hints for a whole document. Nothing is shown or asked while Power Save Mode is on, as in PyCharm (the editor option goes off and the provider answers nothing); turning it off asks again. Test: `verify/inlay-hints.mjs` (port 9516, in CI; hints shown, none and no hover/signatureHelp request while typing in Power Save Mode, back after it).

## Type findings as warnings (lsp)

`main/features/lsp.js` runs Pyright in `standard` mode with `diagnosticSeverityOverrides` that turn its type checker's findings into warnings (`TYPE_RULES_AS_WARNINGS`: attribute access, the six Optional rules, argument, assignment, call, index, operator and return types, overrides, overloads and the like), as PyCharm shows type findings. Errors stay for code that fails whatever the types are: an undefined or never-bound name (`reportUndefinedVariable`, `reportUnboundVariable`), an import that cannot be resolved (`reportMissingImports`) and syntax errors. Before, a program that ran fine showed red errors where a library's types are wider than its values, such as BeautifulSoup's `a["href"].strip()` ("Cannot access attribute "strip" for class "AttributeValueList"", since an attribute value is typed `str | AttributeValueList`) and `soup.find("img")["src"]` (`Tag | None`). A project's own `pyrightconfig.json` or `[tool.pyright]` still decides, as Pyright lets it override client settings. `verify/lsp-type-severity.mjs` (port 9786, in CI) checks both shapes with a stand-in module, that an undefined name, a missing import and a syntax error stay errors, that the editor draws a warning squiggle, and that a project's `pyrightconfig.json` makes the finding an error again.

## Error explanations (error-help)

`renderer/features/error-help` (plugin `error-help`) puts an Explain button before the exception name on a traceback's last line in every console (Run, Debug, Python Console, tests), through `ide.ui.addConsoleLink`. The line has to start with a dotted name ending in Error, Exception, Warning, Exit, Interrupt or Iteration (PEP 8 names exceptions so), followed by ": " or the line's end; other program output that starts with a capitalized word gets nothing. The button is CSS `::before` from `data-explain`, so the console's text and copies keep only what the program printed, and on a ModuleNotFoundError line it stands beside import-install's Install package button.

- The dialog (`#errorHelp`) shows the error line, what the exception means and its usual fixes (`ERRORS`: 31 exceptions, 30 built-in ones and json's JSONDecodeError), hints read from the message whatever the type (`HINTS`: a None value, text plus a number, the argument count, a call on a non-function, a number parsed from text, an unclosed bracket or quote), and "Raised at <file>, line N", the last traceback frame inside the project (else the last frame). Another exception (a library's, or the program's own) gets a general explanation naming its module.
- Buttons: Ask AI (when the ai-chat plugin is on) opens the raising file at its line, opens AI Chat and sends the traceback with a request to explain the cause and fix the code; AI Chat sends the open file along, so the answer sees the code. Go to Error opens the line.
- The explanations are the IDE's own strings, translated like the rest of the UI; Python prints its messages in English.
- Test: `verify/error-help.mjs` (port 9787, in CI): a TypeError from a None value (button, unchanged console text, meaning, the None hint, the line, Go to Error, Ask AI sending the whole traceback with the file open), a ModuleNotFoundError keeping its Install package button, a SyntaxError with its line, and an exception class of the program's own.

## Type warning fixes (type-fixes)

`renderer/features/type-fixes` (plugin `type-fixes`, depends on `lsp`) adds quick fixes (Alt+Enter, the lightbulb) for the type warnings of "Type findings as warnings", on BeautifulSoup's shapes in particular (`find()` → `Tag | None`, `a["href"]` → `str | AttributeValueList`). The value a warning is about is its range (`reportOptionalSubscript`, `…Iterable`, `…Call`, `…ContextManager`, `…Operand`), or for the attribute rules (`reportOptionalMemberAccess`, `reportAttributeAccessIssue`, whose range is the attribute name) the expression before the dot: names, dots, and calls or subscripts with their brackets.

- A value that may be None, when it is a name or dotted name: "Add 'assert x is not None'" on its own line before the statement (the statement's first line, found by scanning brackets, strings and backslash continuations from the top of the file), and for a one-line expression statement (not an assignment, return or compound header) "Wrap in 'if x is not None:'".
- An attribute one member of a union lacks, when the value is a name: "Add 'assert isinstance(x, T)'", with T the members other than the failing class and None, read from Pyright's hover at that point (`(A, B)` for several), offered only when each is a builtin or a class the file imports or defines.
- The same where the attribute is a str method: "Convert '<value>' with str()", for any expression, unless the same value may also be None (`str(None)` is "None").
- Nothing goes before a line that continues a compound statement (elif, else, except, finally, case), nor for a name the statement itself binds (a comprehension or lambda variable). The failing class is the quoted name in Pyright's message that is not the attribute, which holds in every language Pyright speaks.
- Test: `verify/type-fixes.mjs` (port 9788, in CI) applies each fix from the Alt+Enter menu and waits for its warning to go, and checks that an elif line gets no fix and a value that may also be None gets no str().

## Build Executable (exe-builder)

`renderer/features/exe-builder` and `main/features/exe-builder.js` (plugin `exe-builder`, depends on `run` and `packages`) pack a Python program into a Windows program with PyInstaller, so it runs on a computer without Python.

- Run › Build Executable… (action `BuildExecutable`) builds the open Python file, else the selected Python configuration's script; the project tree's and the Python editor's context menus have it for their file. Its dialog asks for the name (the script's by default; a typed `.exe` is dropped, PyInstaller adds it), one file (a single .exe) or one folder (faster to start), no console window (on by default for a .pyw) and an .ico icon.
- The choices go into a saved "PyInstaller" run configuration for the script (type `pyinstaller`; made once, then updated), which also has the data files and folders to bundle (`;`-separated, bundled at the same relative place, so the program finds them under `sys._MEIPASS`), extra PyInstaller options and the interpreter. Building leaves the run widget's selection as it was and is not what Run repeats with nothing selected (`runConfig(cfg, {remember: false})`), so Shift+F10 still runs the program.
- The build runs `python -u -m PyInstaller --noconfirm --clean --onefile|--onedir --name N --distpath <project>\dist --workpath <project>\build\N --specpath <project>\build\N [--windowed] [--icon …] [--add-data src;dest …] … script` in the Run tool window (`exe-builder:prepare` checks the paths, finds PyInstaller's version and builds the command line). The program lands in `dist` (`dist\N.exe`, or `dist\N\N.exe` for one folder) and the .spec file and work files in `build\N`, not in the project root.
- PyInstaller missing from the interpreter: "Install and Build" installs it into the interpreter the configuration builds with (`ide.dependencies.installSpecs(specs, {interp})`) with the project's tool (the Python Packages console), without adding it to the requirements, then builds; another package job still running cancels the build with that reason. A finished build prints the program's path and size (the .exe, or the whole folder for one folder: `exe-builder:size`), adds Show in Explorer to the Run tab and posts a notification with Show in Explorer and, in a Git repository whose .gitignore does not cover them, "Add build/, dist/ to .gitignore" (appended under a `# PyInstaller (Build Executable)` line, into the editor when .gitignore is open with unsaved edits). A failed build posts an error with Show Output; one the user stopped (`session.stopRequested`) posts nothing. Local interpreters only; Safe Mode builds nothing.
- Test: `verify/exe-builder.mjs` (port 9797, in CI) with a stand-in PyInstaller wheel installed offline: the dialog defaults, the install offer, the arguments, the saved configuration and untouched run widget, one folder with bundled data, a failed build, a name typed with .exe, a stopped build, the .gitignore offer, Run with nothing selected after a build, and the editor menu entry. `verify/exe-builder-live.mjs` (port 9799, opt-in, not in CI) installs the real PyInstaller into `verify/out/exe-builder-real-venv`, builds one file and one folder from a script that reads a bundled file, and runs both programs.

## Sync Python Requirements (requirements-sync)

`renderer/features/requirements-sync` and `main/features/requirements-sync.js` (plugin `requirements-sync`, depends on `packages` and `import-install`) are PyCharm's Sync Python Requirements: Tools › Sync Python Requirements… (action `SyncPythonRequirements`, also in the Python Packages window's toolbar).

- The project interpreter reads every .py file of the project with `ast` (folders of tools and environments skipped: dot folders, venvs with pyvenv.cfg, node_modules, build, dist), keeps absolute imports that are not the standard library (`sys.stdlib_module_names`, or the stdlib folder before 3.10) nor a module of the project (any .py file or package folder in it), and maps each to the installed distribution that provides it (`importlib.metadata.packages_distributions`, `top_level.txt` before 3.10): bs4 → beautifulsoup4. An import that is not installed gets import-install's name for it (yaml → PyYAML).
- The dialog lists, against requirements.txt (else a pyproject.toml with [project], else a new requirements.txt): the packages the code imports that no dependency file declares, checked, with their installed version and where they are imported; and, for requirements.txt, the declared packages no import uses, unchecked, since a command-line tool or a plugin is used without an import. The version form is Compatible (~=, the default), Greater or equal, Exact or none (`requirementsSync.version`); ~= and >= drop a local version label (2.3.0+cu121 → 2.3.0) and ~= becomes >= for a one-part version, which pip would otherwise reject. A namespace several installed distributions share (google) is matched to the one named for an imported submodule (google.cloud.storage → google-cloud-storage).
- Apply removes the chosen lines (with their backslash continuations; a line that moved since the check, the file edited during the scan, is found again by its text) and adds the chosen requirements through the packages plugin's `packages:depsAdd`; a file open with unsaved edits is changed in its editor, otherwise on disk. The dependency check runs again afterwards, and a package added without a version (not installed in the project interpreter) is offered for install in a notification ("Install '<name>'" or "Install N packages", through `ide.dependencies.installSpecs`).
- Test: `verify/requirements-sync.mjs` (port 9798, in CI) with offline wheels: metadata mapping, a submodule import, an import that is not installed, the standard library and project modules left out, an unused requirement, Apply with == into an unsaved editor, a second sync, and ~= with a local label and a one-part version.

## Rerun on Save (rerun-on-save)

`renderer/features/rerun-on-save` (plugin `rerun-on-save`, depends on `run`) adds a Rerun on Save toggle (↻) to the Run tool window's toolbar of each Run tab (through `ide.run.registerSessionAction`, a toolbar action for every run of a tab) and the action `ToggleRerunOnSave` (Run menu). While it is on, the tab is marked "On save" and saving a file of the project (outside venvs, caches, .git, node_modules, build and dist) stops the run if it is still going and starts the configuration again 800 ms after the last save; saves during the wait or the restart fold into one run, so runs never overlap or pile up; a run of the tab started any other way (Rerun, Shift+F10, whose Save All saves files too) drops a pending rerun (event `runSessionStarting`, emitted by the run plugin once that Save All is done). It is off by default, not kept across restarts, and turned off in every tab when another project opens. Test tabs keep their own Toggle Auto-test, and a PyInstaller build or a debug session gets no toggle. Safe Mode reruns nothing. Test: `verify/rerun-on-save.mjs` (port 9796, in CI).

## Minecraft servers, plugins and mods (minecraft)

`renderer/features/minecraft` and `main/features/minecraft.js` (plugin `minecraft`, depends on `run`; uses the Ports plugin and the Java debugger when they are on; `main/minecraft/` holds the sources, the templates and a small zip reader and writer) keep Minecraft servers in a project: `servers/<name>` (described by `pyide-server.json`: kind, version, loader or build, Java version, how to launch, the mod template), plugin projects in `plugins/<name>` (`pyide-plugin.json`) and mod projects in `mods/<name>` (`pyide-mod.json`), each naming its server. The Minecraft tool window (right, order 80) lists the servers with Start/Stop, Debug, Share, New Plugin…, New Mod…, Client Pack, Resource Pack, Send to Players and Open Folder, and under each server its plugin and mod projects with Build and Deploy (and Add Library… for plugins); Tools has New Minecraft Server/Plugin/Mod….

- Sources (`main/minecraft/sources.js`): Paper and Velocity from PaperMC's Fill v3 API (release versions, the newest STABLE build, its sha256; a User-Agent naming PyIDE, which Fill requires), Purpur from its API (its MD5), Vanilla from Mojang's version manifest (sha1), Fabric from its meta API (the stable loader and installer's server launcher jar), Forge from its promotions and Maven (the recommended build's installer, with the .sha256 Maven publishes) and NeoForge from its Maven metadata (its versions map to Minecraft's: 21.1.x is 1.21.1, 26.3.0.x is 26.3). The Java a version needs comes from Mojang's own version file (`javaVersion.majorVersion`: 8, 16, 17, 21, 25); Velocity's, whose versions are its own, from its jar (the main class's class file version: 4.x is built for Java 25). `PYIDE_MC_SOURCES` replaces the base URLs in tests.
- New Server asks for the type, a version from its service, name, memory and port, and creates nothing until the user ticks that they agree to the Minecraft EULA (linked; a Velocity proxy runs no Minecraft server and asks nothing); `eula.txt` records the agreement with its date. A download whose checksum does not match removes the half-made server. Downloads and the Forge/NeoForge installer (which fetches Minecraft's server and libraries itself) are tried three times, a few seconds apart: a 59 MB download from Mojang broke off mid-file in testing ("terminated"), and so did the NeoForge installer's own fetch; a checksum mismatch or a 404 is not retried. Forge and NeoForge run their installer (`--installServer`) with the right Java and start from the @argument files it leaves (`user_jvm_args.txt`, `libraries/…/win_args.txt`), or the forge jar of older versions. A "Minecraft Server" run configuration is saved for the server without taking over the run widget.
- Java: a JDK of this computer of exactly the needed version (the toolchains' detection, which also finds IntelliJ's ~/.jdks), else a Temurin JRE (JDK for Gradle) from Adoptium's API into `~/.pyide/java/temurin-<version>-<jre|jdk>`, its sha256 checked; plugin builds take any JDK at least that version (`javac --release`). `PYIDE_MC_JAVA` names one Java home for every version in tests.
- Running: `java -Xmx<memory> <GC options> -jar <jar> nogui` (or the @argument files) in the server folder, its console taking server commands (op, whitelist, say). Stop sends `stop` (`end` to a proxy) so the server saves the world and quits, and kills it only if it is still there 30 s later. A start waits up to 30 s for the server's port to be free (a Rerun of a running server, which is still saving), and a restart after a build waits for the old process to exit: starting at once failed to bind the port. The game's port is hinted as TCP to the Ports window (`ide.ports.hint`), so it is announced as host:port with no browser and Share uses bore.
- GC options: a Minecraft server starts with Aikar's flags, which PaperMC's documentation recommends (G1 tuned for a game loop: fewer, shorter pauses), and a Velocity proxy with Velocity's. Both leave out `-XX:+AlwaysPreTouch` and `-Xms` = `-Xmx`, so on a development machine the heap grows as the server needs it instead of being taken and touched in full before the server starts. `jvmArgs` (an array) in `pyide-server.json` replaces them, `[]` for none; Forge's and NeoForge's `user_jvm_args.txt` still follows them. Class data sharing (`-XX:+AutoCreateSharedArchive`) is not added: its effect on server start was not measured.
- Share (a running server's card): the Ports plugin's bore tunnel. The card then shows the address players join with (`bore.pub:<port>`) with Copy Address and Stop Sharing, "Making a public address…" while bore starts, and why it failed if it did; the card follows `ports:changed`. A server with account checks off (`online-mode=false`, as the proxy sets its Paper and Purpur servers) is not shared as asked: Share offers the project's proxy instead, which checks the accounts (it must be running), or, with no proxy, shares only after a warning that anyone could play under any name.
- Debug (the server's run configuration's `debug`, a Debug button on the card) starts the server with `-agentlib:jdwp=…,suspend=y` and attaches PyIDE's Java debugger (`ide.debugger.debugJvm`) with the source folders of the server's plugin and mod projects (`minecraft:sourceRoots`): the server waits until the breakpoints are set, so a breakpoint in a plugin's onEnable is hit.
- Plugins: New Plugin (Paper, Purpur, Velocity) writes a plugin for the server's API: for Paper a JavaPlugin class (a join message and a /hello command) and plugin.yml (api-version as major.minor, 1.21 or 26.3, which every Paper version accepts); for Velocity a @Plugin class (initialize and login handlers) and velocity-plugin.json, written directly because javac of JDK 23 and later runs no annotation processor unless asked. It also writes `.project`, `.classpath` and `.settings/org.eclipse.jdt.core.prefs` (sources, the server's API jars, `libs/`, the server's Java level), which the Java language server imports as an Eclipse project: completion and errors in the editor, verified with the real JDT LS on Paper 1.21.11. They are written again (only when they change) when the server stops after a start, a library is added or the plugin is built, since Paper unpacks its API at its first start. Build and Deploy compiles with `javac --release <server Java> -proc:none -g` against the server's API (Paper's `libraries/` and `versions/`, Velocity's own jar) and `libs/` (until Paper has started once it says to start the server once), packs the classes and resources with the JDK's `jar`, copies the jar to the server's `plugins/`, and restarts the server if it runs. Add Library… downloads a group:artifact:version jar from a Maven repository (PaperMC, Maven Central, JitPack, CodeMC or an https address) into `libs/`, checked against the repository's .sha1, compiled against but not packed (the plugin that provides it must be on the server), and with "Requires plugin" adds that plugin to plugin.yml's depend (velocity-plugin.json's dependencies). Plugins run on the server: players join with plain Minecraft.
- Mods: New Mod (Fabric, Forge, NeoForge) asks for a mod id, a display name and a package, unpacks the official template for the server's version (Fabric: the branch of `FabricMC/fabric-example-mod` named after the version; Forge: `forge-<mc>-<build>-mdk.zip` from its Maven; NeoForge: `NeoForgeMDKs/MDK-<mc>-ModDevGradle`, or for a version without one yet the nearest older of the same year with `minecraft_version`, `minecraft_version_range` and `neo_version` set to the server's, which may need changes) into `mods/<id>`, and renames it (`main/minecraft/templates.js` renameMod: the template's package, display name, class and id in the texts under src/ and the build files, the package folders, and the files and folders named after the id or class, such as `assets/examplemod` and `modid.mixins.json`; checked on the real Forge 1.20.1 MDK and Fabric's 1.20.1 template). Fabric's `loader_version` and NeoForge's `neo_version` become the server's. It saves "Deploy" (gradlew build, then the jar that is not -sources/-dev/-slim/-javadoc into the server's `mods/` and a restart) and "Test client" (runClient) configurations and opens the main class. gradlew runs as `.\gradlew.bat` (cmd finds a program in the current folder only so when NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath is set) with JAVA_HOME the JDK of the server's Java version.
- Test Client with HotSwap: a mod project's row has Test Client, which debugs its "Test client" configuration (Gradle's runClient). An init script (`build/pyide/init.gradle`, passed with `-I`, with `--no-configuration-cache`) gives the runClient JavaExec task of Loom, ForgeGradle and ModDevGradle the JDWP agent on the debugger's port (suspend=y; the attach waits up to 30 minutes while Gradle prepares Minecraft), and a pyideClasspath task writes each source set's sources, compile class path and output to `build/pyide/classpath.json`. Breakpoints in the mod (src/main/java, src/client/java) stop the game. While it runs, saving a .java file of the mod compiles it with javac (`--release` of the server's Java, against its source set's class path and the outputs), writes the classes over the source set's output, and redefines the loaded ones in the game through JDWP RedefineClasses; the Debug console says how many classes were reloaded and how long it took. The JVM swaps method bodies only: a new or removed method or field, or a changed signature, is refused with the reason and a note to restart, and the game keeps the last code that swapped. Breakpoints are set again after a swap.
- Game View (`renderer/features/minecraft/game-view.js`, `main/minecraft/game-window.js`): while a test client is debugged, its game window is found through the JVM that owns the debug port (PowerShell's Get-NetTCPConnection, so Gradle's argument files and daemon do not matter), then followed by its process. "Beside the IDE" (the default) gives the game the right 40% of the IDE's screen work area and the IDE the rest, once per window, so a window the user moves stays put; Always on top keeps it above the IDE. "Inside the IDE" keeps the game behind the IDE and shows its picture in the Game View tool window (right side) through desktopCapturer and getUserMedia; `main/security.js` allows that capture to the IDE page for 10 s after the IDE asks for one window's source, and never a camera or microphone. A click on the picture brings the game to the front. Reload Resources sends F3+T to the game and gives the keyboard back to the window that had it. Saving a file under the mod's `src/<set>/resources/assets/` copies it over the source set's resources output (pyideClasspath now writes resources and resourcesOutput) and reloads the game's resources; `data/` is copied and the console asks for /reload; other resources (fabric.mod.json, mods.toml) wait for the next start, since Gradle fills them in. A HotSwap the JVM refuses shows in the Game View with Restart highlighted, which debugs the client again. Debug of the test client writes `pauseOnLostFocus:false` to `run/options.txt`, so the game keeps running while the user types in the editor. Win32 calls (SetWindowPos, SetForegroundWindow with AttachThreadInput, keybd_event) come from one PowerShell helper started on first use, per-monitor DPI aware; Electron's DIP rectangles are converted with screen.dipToScreenRect.
- Velocity: a proxy server whose `velocity.toml` (written at creation with `bind`, modern forwarding and `forwarding.secret`; Velocity takes its defaults for the rest) gets, before each start, a `[servers]` table and `try` list of the project's Paper and Purpur servers, each of which gets `proxies.velocity` (enabled, the secret) in `config/paper-global.yml` and `online-mode=false` in server.properties, since the proxy checks the players' accounts.
- Resource pack: Resource Pack makes `resourcepack/` with a pack.mcmeta whose format comes from version.json in the server's Minecraft jar (the jar itself, Paper's `cache/`, the installers' `libraries/net/minecraft`, Fabric's `.fabric`; `pack_format`, or `min_format`/`max_format` from format 65 on, as 1.21.9 changed it: 75 for 1.21.11 and [97, 1] for 26.3 from the real jars). Send to Players zips it into `client-pack/resourcepack.zip`, serves that folder with the project's Python (`http.server` on loopback) shared through the Ports plugin (a Cloudflare https address), writes `resource-pack`, `resource-pack-sha1` and `require-resource-pack=true` into server.properties and restarts the server: every player's game downloads the pack when joining. The address lasts while PyIDE shares it (a quick tunnel's address changes each time).
- Drag and drop (`main/minecraft/imports.js`): files and folders dropped from Explorer on the Minecraft window are told apart by what they hold, and each goes where it belongs (the drop is taken before the window's own handler, which would open a folder as the project). A folder with `level.dat` is a world: copied into the server and made its `level-name`. A folder with `pack.mcmeta`, or a zip holding one, is a resource pack: copied or unpacked into `resourcepack/`. A jar with `plugin.yml`/`paper-plugin.yml` is a Paper plugin, with `velocity-plugin.json` a Velocity plugin, with `fabric.mod.json` a Fabric mod, with `META-INF/neoforge.mods.toml` a NeoForge mod and with `META-INF/mods.toml` a Forge (or older NeoForge) mod: copied into the server's `plugins/` or `mods/` when the server runs that API or loader, refused with the reason otherwise. On a server's card these go to that server; on the window, to the one server they fit (else the user is told to drop them on one). A folder that looks like a server (`server.properties`, `eula.txt`, `run.bat` or `velocity.toml`, and a jar or `libraries/`) is imported: copied into `servers/` and recognized from what it runs (Forge's and NeoForge's installer libraries with their win_args.txt; else the jar: Paper's or Purpur's launcher by META-INF/versions.list, Velocity by its main class, Fabric's server launcher, Mojang's server by version.json), with its Java from Mojang's data; its eula.txt is left as it was. A `.mrpack` opens New Server from Modpack; a CurseForge modpack zip is refused with the way that works (its mods come only from CurseForge's own service: download the pack's Server Pack and drop that folder). The results are listed in a "Minecraft: Import" Run tab.
- Modpacks: From Modpack… (and Tools › New Minecraft Server from Modpack…, and a dropped .mrpack) takes a Modrinth modpack file or a Modrinth address (the newest version's .mrpack from Modrinth's API, its SHA-1 checked), shows its name, version, Minecraft, loader and how many files are for the server, and with the EULA ticked makes a server of that Minecraft and exact loader version (Fabric, Forge or NeoForge; Quilt is refused), downloads the files the pack lists for servers (not `env.server: unsupported`) checking each against its SHA-512 or SHA-1, and writes `overrides/` and `server-overrides/` (not `client-overrides/`) into it. The .mrpack is kept as `client-pack/<file>` and becomes the server's Client Pack, since players need the client mods the server leaves out.
- Market (`main/minecraft/market.js`, `renderer/features/minecraft/market.js`): Market in the Minecraft window (and on a server's card, and Tools › Minecraft Market…) opens the Minecraft screen's Market page, which browses Modrinth like a store. Its tabs are Modpacks, Mods, Plugins and Resource Packs, with a search box (Modrinth's full-text search, typed with a short pause), sorting by relevance, downloads, follows, newest or recently updated, and Show More for the next 20. Each card shows the icon, title, author, description, downloads, follows and categories, and Modrinth Page opens the project in the browser. Mods, plugins and resource packs are searched for the server chosen at the top: mods for its Minecraft version and loader and only those that run on a server (`server_side` required or optional), plugins for Paper, Spigot and Bukkit (with Purpur's own on a Purpur server) and its Minecraft version, or for Velocity on a proxy (any Minecraft version, since Velocity's version is its own). Modpacks are only those that run on a server, filtered by loader and Minecraft version, and Create Server… downloads the newest .mrpack into New Server from Modpack. Install takes the newest release for the server (a beta when there is none), with its required dependencies (recursively; one that is client-only is left out and named, one already in the folder is not downloaded again), each file checked against Modrinth's SHA-512 or SHA-1 (a mismatch leaves no file), into `mods/` or `plugins/`; a resource pack is unpacked into `resourcepack/` (refused when the server has a resource pack of its own there) for Send to Players. What the Market installed is recorded under `market` in pyide-server.json, so its card shows Installed with the version, Update (the newest version, its old file removed once the new one is in place) and Remove (its files and the dependencies nothing else installed needs; files the user put there are left alone). A server that is running is offered a restart.
- Minecraft screen (`renderer/features/minecraft/screen.js`): Tools › Minecraft Screen, Open Screen in the tool window, and the Market open a Minecraft editor tab laid out like New Project: a list on the left (Servers and Market, then Create: New Server…, From Modpack…, New Plugin…, New Mod…, all keyboard-navigable) and the chosen page on the right. Servers shows the tool window's server cards side by side with the same actions, and takes the same drops.
- New Project (`renderer/features/minecraft/project-types.js`): Minecraft Mod, Minecraft Plugin and Minecraft Server are project types. Each asks its type or loader (Fabric, Forge, NeoForge for a mod; Paper, Purpur, Velocity for a plugin; any kind for a server), the version from that service, memory, and the EULA (not for a Velocity proxy); a mod also its id, name and package, a plugin its class name and package, all named after the project until edited, and a server its port. The wizard makes the folder (with a .gitignore for what servers download and Gradle writes); once the project is open, the Minecraft screen shows and the server is made, then the mod or plugin on it (as New Mod… and New Plugin… do), its main class opened.
- Players of a modded server need the same mods, and Minecraft cannot install them when joining. Client Pack writes `client-pack/<server>-<mc>.mrpack`, a Modrinth modpack (modrinth.index.json naming Minecraft and the loader with its version, the server's mods as overrides), which Prism Launcher, the Modrinth App and ATLauncher import into a ready instance in one step. Share Link serves that folder the same way as the resource pack.
- Auto Sync (AutoModpack, Modrinth's `automodpack`, LGPL): New Server ticks "Players get the server's mods when they join" for a Fabric, Forge or NeoForge server, and a modded server's card without it has Auto Sync (not one made from a modpack, whose own client pack has client-only mods AutoModpack would not send). Both install it through the Market, so it shows there as installed and Remove takes it out. With AutoModpack in `mods/` the Client Pack holds it alone: at a player's first join it downloads the server's mods into its own folder (over the game's port, so a bore share carries it), after the player confirms the server's certificate, and later joins bring their changes; the server's mods in the instance's `mods/` as well would load twice. The card says AutoModpack is on.
- Delete… (a stopped server's card) asks, then moves the server folder, worlds included, to the Recycle Bin (`ide.api.trash`), wires each proxy again without it (`minecraft:rewireProxies`), removes its run configuration (`ide.run.removeConfigs`) and closes tabs of its files. Its plugin and mod projects stay, and the question names them.
- Settings… (every server's card) opens the server's settings tab (`renderer/features/minecraft/server-settings.js`, `main/minecraft/server-settings.js`; one tab per server, closed when the server is deleted). A list on the left jumps to the sections (General, Gameplay, World, Players, Performance, Mods on a Fabric, Forge or NeoForge server, Other properties) and a search box shows only the settings whose name, description or key match. Each setting has a description; booleans are switches, choices are lists, numbers are checked against their range (red, Save off), and a line missing from server.properties shows the value the server uses for it. The message of the day takes two lines with a live preview of its § color and format codes. Players: Check accounts (online-mode), whitelist on and enforced, the whitelisted players and the operators with their level (a name is looked up in Mojang's profile API for its UUID when accounts are checked or the project has a proxy, which forwards real accounts; otherwise it gets its offline UUID), the level /op gives, idle kick, hiding who is online. Performance adds the JVM options: the recommended ones (Aikar's, Velocity's for a proxy) or the user's, one a line, `-Xmx` refused in favor of Memory. Other properties lists every other line of the file. Changed settings are marked; Save checks everything, then writes only what changed (server.properties lines in place, other characters as `\uXXXX`, which servers reading the file as ISO-8859-1 or as UTF-8 both decode; whitelist.json and ops.json; memory and jvmArgs in pyide-server.json), and a new port rewires the proxies. On a running server, whitelist and operator changes are typed into its console (`whitelist add/remove`, `op`/`deop`), since the server keeps its own lists, and the rest offers Restart. Revert drops the draft; Reload reads the files again. A proxy's tab has its memory and JVM options.
- Korean translation (`main/minecraft/translate.js`; the Mods section of a Fabric, Forge or NeoForge server's settings tab): each jar in `mods/` keeps its text in `assets/<namespace>/lang/en_us.json` and maybe its Korean in `ko_kr.json`; the dialog counts the lines that neither the jar's Korean nor the server's resource pack has, and Translate to Korean sends them, in batches of at most 120 lines or 6,000 characters, to Claude through the Claude Code CLI (`complete()` in `main/ai/claude.js`, sonnet, the user's own subscription) with a JSON object in and one out. A Korean line is kept only when it has the English line's format codes (`%s`, `%1$d`, `§7`, line breaks), since the game fills those in; the others are counted and left out. The lines go into `resourcepack/assets/<namespace>/lang/ko_kr.json` (the pack made first, which needs the server to have downloaded Minecraft once), which Minecraft merges line by line over the mods' own Korean; lines already there, an earlier run's or the user's edits, are never asked for again. The run shows in a Run tab, a balloon cancels it (what is done stays), and the result offers Send to Players and Open File.
- IPC: `minecraft:kinds`, `minecraft:versions(kind)`, `minecraft:servers(root)` (servers with their projects, port, resource pack and autoSync), `minecraft:create(opts, job)`, `minecraft:launch(root, dir, job, {debugPort})`, `minecraft:start(root, spec)`, `minecraft:portFree(port)`, `minecraft:sourceRoots`, `minecraft:wireProxy`, `minecraft:newPlugin`, `minecraft:syncPlugins`, `minecraft:buildPlugin`, `minecraft:addDependency`, `minecraft:newMod`, `minecraft:modTask`, `minecraft:deployMod`, `minecraft:clientPack` -> {file, mods, autoSync, modpack?}, `minecraft:rewireProxies(root)`, `minecraft:settings(root, dir)` -> {proxy, memory, jvmArgs, defaultJvmArgs, schema, props, whitelist, ops}, `minecraft:saveSettings(root, dir, {memory?, jvmArgs?, props?, whitelist?, ops?})`, `minecraft:player(name, online)` -> {name, uuid}, `minecraft:langStatus(root, dir)` -> {mods: [{jar, ns, total, missing}], skipped, missing, pack}, `minecraft:translateMods(root, dir, job)` -> {translated, rejected, failed, files}, `minecraft:cancel(job)`, `minecraft:newResourcePack`, `minecraft:packResourcePack`, `minecraft:applyResourcePack`, `minecraft:drop(root, serverDir, paths, job)` -> {done, skipped, modpacks}, `minecraft:modpackInfo(root, {file | url}, job)`, `minecraft:createFromModpack(opts, job)`, `minecraft:marketSearch({type, query, sort, offset, serverDir | loader, mc})` -> {hits, total}, `minecraft:marketInstall(root, dir, {id, type}, job)` -> {installed, skipped}, `minecraft:marketRemove(root, dir, id)`, `minecraft:modTask(root, dir, task, job, {debugPort})`, `minecraft:hotswapCompile(root, dir, files, job)` -> [{name, file}]; job output arrives as `minecraft:log`. Untrusted projects: nothing runs.
- Test: `verify/minecraft.mjs` (port 9793, in CI) against stand-ins, offline and without accepting the real EULA: a local web server plays Mojang, PaperMC, Purpur, the Forge Maven and a Maven repository, a small Java program plays the server (it also loads the plugins' jars and calls onEnable, so a breakpoint there is hit under Debug for real) and the proxy, stub Bukkit classes the API, a stand-in cloudflared the tunnel; drops are real file drags through CDP (a plugin, a Fabric mod refused, a world, a resource pack zip, a server folder, a modpack with server, client-only and override files); a stand-in Modrinth for the Market (the search facets for a Forge server and a Paper server, a mod installed with its required library and without its client-only and optional ones, updated and removed, a plugin whose checksum fails, a modpack opened in New Server from Modpack, Auto Sync installing AutoModpack and the client pack holding it alone); the settings tab (the file's values and defaults, an out-of-range number refused, the changed rows marked, the colored MOTD preview, the search, a player whitelisted through a stand-in Mojang profile API and an unknown name refused, Save writing server.properties with \u escapes, the memory, the JVM options and whitelist.json, and on a running server an operator added through /op in its console), and Translate to Korean against a stand-in Claude Code CLI (a test mod's lines without Korean translated into the resource pack, an answer that dropped a %s refused, a second run asking only for what is still missing and keeping the user's edit); Delete (the folder moved aside in place of the Recycle Bin, the proxy wired without it, its run configuration and settings tab gone); Test Client under Debug with a stand-in Gradle and game (a real JVM printing the mod's greeting(): the saved change shows in its output, a new method is refused); the Minecraft screen's servers and New Server, and New Project › Minecraft Plugin (the EULA asked, then a project with a Paper server and the plugin, opened on the screen). `verify/minecraft-live.mjs` (port 9792, opt-in, not in CI) runs against the real services: it creates Paper 1.21.11, Purpur, Velocity 4.3.1, Vanilla 26.3, Fabric 26.3, Forge 1.20.1 and NeoForge 1.21.1 servers through the IDE (Java 17, 21 and 25 downloaded into a temporary install root), starts each Minecraft server with eula.txt set back to false so it gets as far as Mojang's EULA check (nothing is agreed to), runs the Velocity proxy for real wired to the Paper server with a Velocity plugin built in the IDE, compiles the Paper plugin and makes resource packs from the real jars; MC_LIVE_LSP=1 opens the plugin with the real JDT LS, MC_LIVE_GRADLE=1 builds renamed mods from the real Forge MDK and Fabric template with Gradle and deploys them, MC_LIVE_MODPACK=<slug> makes a server from that Modrinth modpack and starts it as far as the EULA check, MC_LIVE_KINDS limits the kinds. `verify/port-sharing-bore-live.mjs` (port 9784, opt-in, not in CI) shares a stand-in of a Minecraft server (`verify/fixtures/port-sharing/mcping.py`, the server list ping) through the real bore.pub and pings it the way a client does.

### Playing through the official launcher (`main/minecraft/launcher.js`)
- Instances (`instances/<name>`, `nano-instance.json`) for Vanilla, Fabric, Quilt, Forge and NeoForge. Fabric and Quilt profiles come from their meta services. Forge and NeoForge run their official installers headlessly (`--installClient`) into `.launcher`, the official Minecraft Launcher's work folder, on the managed Java. An installer without a headless client install is a clear error.
- Play writes the instance's own version (`nano-<name>`, inheriting from the loader's) with the server to join: Quick Play on 1.20+, `--server/--port` on 1.13-1.19, the full argument line before 1.13. It merges one custom profile into `launcher_profiles.json`, keeping the launcher's own profiles and settings, then starts the official launcher detached with `--workDir`. The user signs in and presses Play there; Nano Studio never handles Microsoft accounts. Without the launcher, Play answers `noLauncher` with Mojang's download page.
- Servers to join (`servers-list.json`) and join codes (`nanostudio:join?addr=…&pack=…&name=…`, pasted, no protocol handler). A server's client pack (.mrpack) is synced at Play when its SHA-1 changes: files hash-checked before anything changes, pack files added, updated and removed, user-added mods kept, disabled pack mods kept disabled, Minecraft and the loader following the pack.
- Untrusted input: a join code's pack must be https, and a pack's files download only over https from the hosts the .mrpack format allows (cdn.modrinth.com, github.com, raw.githubusercontent.com, gitlab.com); the origin of a `PYIDE_MC_SOURCES` stand-in is the one exception, for tests. Only release Minecraft versions are accepted for instances and packs. JSON files with a UTF-8 BOM are read; a `launcher_profiles.json` that cannot be kept is backed up as `.broken-<time>` first.
- Modpacks as instances, from a .mrpack file, a direct address or a Modrinth project. An instance's mods can be listed and turned on or off (`.jar.disabled`).
- Tests: `verify/minecraft-launcher.mjs` with stand-ins only; `MC_LAUNCHER_LIVE=1` installs the real Fabric, Quilt, Forge and NeoForge client loaders into a temporary folder.

### The Craft screen (`renderer/features/minecraft/craft.js`, `screen.js`)
- In craft mode the Minecraft screen is the whole window, with Play, Servers to Join, My Servers and Market on the left. In code mode the same pages are in the Minecraft editor tab. Everything is in Korean and uses the IDE's look.
- **Play**: a card for each instance (cover in the loader's colour, Minecraft and loader version, mod count, last played) with a big Play button, plus Mods, Open Folder and Delete.
  - Mods lists the instance's mods. A switch turns each one on or off (`.jar.disabled`); mods the server's pack installed are marked and can only be turned off, and the user's own can be removed.
  - New Instance takes a name (Korean works), the loader (Vanilla, Fabric, Quilt, Forge, NeoForge), the Minecraft version, the loader version (recommended one chosen) and memory.
  - From Modpack opens the Market on Modpacks, whose cards offer **Install as Instance** in craft. A `.mrpack` file can be installed too.
- **Servers to Join**: one field takes a join code (`nanostudio:join?…`, found anywhere in pasted text) or an address. An address without a pack asks for the Minecraft version and loader.
  - Each server card shows its address, "모드 자동 동기화" when it has a pack, and the instance it is played with.
  - **Join** syncs the server's pack and plays its instance with the server to join (Quick Play). It makes the instance first if there is none.
  - Copy Join Code and Remove.
- **My Servers**: the existing server cards. In craft, a shared server gets **Copy Join Code**: its public address plus, for a modded server, its client pack's share link.
- Play and Join show progress in a balloon that follows the job's log, a status bar task and a Run tab. When the launcher opens they say "런처에서 로그인하고 플레이를 누르세요".
- Without the official Minecraft Launcher, a banner says "공식 Minecraft Launcher가 필요해요" and offers Mojang's download page (opened in the browser), and Play offers it too. Nothing is ever installed.
- An editor tab opened in craft (a server's settings, a file) covers the app until it is closed.
- The craft nav ends with Settings… and About. In craft, Settings shows only the pages flagged `craft: true` (Appearance, Language, Plugins, Updates), and About leaves out the Python rows.
- Progress balloons show Korean phases matched from the job log (checking the pack, downloading mods n/m, opening the launcher), and only one launcher-opened balloon stays on screen. Network failures read as a Korean cannot-connect message.
- `verify/i18n-static.mjs` checks each mode separately: a plugin's strings against core plus the dictionaries of the plugins loaded in that mode.
- Tests: the UI part of `verify/minecraft-launcher.mjs` runs a real craft window (`--mode=craft`, `PYIDE_CRAFT_HOME`) against stand-ins only, plus a second window without the launcher. Screenshots: `craft-play.png`, `craft-servers.png`, `craft-no-launcher.png`.

## Call parentheses in completion (lsp)

Completing a function or method (Pyright kinds Method/Function) adds `()` as PyCharm does: the caret goes inside and the parameter info opens when the callable takes parameters (every signature from signatureHelp is checked), after `()` when it takes none. The completion item carries a command (`pyide.lsp.callParens`) that types the `(` through Monaco with `autoClosingBrackets: 'always'` for that key, after tokenizing the line (Monaco does not auto-close on a line it has not tokenized, as right after an auto-import edit), so Monaco closes it and knows the `)` as its own: a `)` typed before it steps over it, Backspace in the empty pair deletes both, and strings, brackets and inner calls typed inside close themselves (`print("hi")`, `print(len(items))`, also with `len` completed inside). If Monaco still does not close it, the plugin inserts the `)`. Not after `@`, in an import (one line or a parenthesized list), in a def/class header, for a class, or where `(` or the rest of a word follows. Each added pair (a stack per editor) is also followed with two decorations until the caret leaves it, for what Monaco does not do: a `(` typed into the empty pair or right after it steps into it (`(shapes)` gives `total_area(shapes)`), and text arriving at once that leaves the call one `(` short (counted outside string literals) replaces the added `)`, and those of enclosing added pairs that directly follow. A pair whose `)` Monaco stepped over is forgotten. There is no setting, as in PyCharm. Test: `verify/completion-parens.mjs` (port 9375; in CI).

## Coverage and profiling

An executor runs a configuration another way, as PyCharm's Run with Coverage and Profile do. `ide.run.registerExecutor({id, title, label(name), iconName, glyph, prepare(cfg, interp, session)})` adds it to the run widget's More Actions (⋮, next to Run and Debug), the gutter run menu and Rerun. `runConfig(cfg, {executor: id})` calls `prepare` before the type's `start`. `prepare` returns the interpreter to start with, or null to cancel. An executor can append arguments to `interp.args` that launch a wrapper script, which then runs the program's own command line (`-u script`, `-m pytest …`, or `-c` probes, which it runs unwrapped). This way scripts, pytest and the unittest runner all work without changes. `prepare` may push callbacks to `session.exitHooks`, which run once when that run's process exits. Remote interpreters (`interp.target`) are refused, since the wrapper exists only locally.

Coverage (`renderer/features/coverage`, `main/features/coverage.js`): `pyide_coverage_run.py` runs the program under coverage.py with branch coverage, measuring the project root as the source, so files that were never imported show 0%. It then writes a summary with executed, missing and partial lines (lines with a branch that never ran), and statement and branch counts. When coverage.py is missing, a prompt installs it into the project interpreter through the Install Manager's pip job (`pip:coverage`). The Coverage tool window (bottom, order 65) shows the folder tree with Line, % and Branch, % columns, using coverage.py's rounding, and double-click opens a file. Editors get 4px stripes in the line-decoration gutter: green for covered lines, red for missed ones and yellow for partial ones. Clicking a stripe shows "Covered 1/2 branches" and offers Hide Coverage. The project tree shows "N% lines covered". Hide Coverage / Show Coverage in Editors (setting `coverage.showInEditor`) toggles the stripes and badges. A new coverage run replaces the data.

Profiler (`renderer/features/profiler`, `main/features/profiler.js`): `pyide_profile_run.py` runs the program under cProfile and writes each function's calls, own and total time, and callers as JSON. Frames of the wrapper, runpy and the profiler are left out. The program's `<module>` becomes a root instead of a callee of runpy's `exec`, and calls from outside the program are kept as `root` entries. The Profiler tool window (bottom, order 66) has three tabs. Statistics is a table sortable by Name, Call Count, Time (ms) and Own Time (ms), with a filter. Call Tree expands the hot path. Flame Graph draws callers below callees; click zooms, Reset Zoom undoes it, and the tooltip shows time, share and calls. cProfile keeps caller and callee totals, not stacks, so the tree splits each function's callees across its callers in proportion to time, and recursion folds into the outermost call. Double-click, Enter or F4, or Navigate to Source in the context menu, opens the function's line. Tests: `verify/coverage-profile.mjs` (ports 9700-9701). It keeps a venv with pytest in `verify/out/coverage-profile-venv` and uninstalls coverage.py first, so every run installs it through the prompt. Set `COVERAGE_KEEP=1` to keep it.

## Data View and Plots (SciView)

`renderer/features/sciview` (plugin `sciview`) and `main/features/sciview.js` add PyCharm's scientific view. The Python side is two helpers in `renderer/features/sciview/python/`, which main puts on `PYTHONPATH` for every process the IDE starts (`unpackedDir`, so packaged builds run them from `app.asar.unpacked`).

- Data View: "View as DataFrame" / "View as Array" links next to pandas DataFrames and Series, NumPy arrays, polars DataFrames and lists of dicts in the debugger's Variables and Watches, the Python Console's Variables pane, notebook results (`Out[n]`) and the Jupyter Variables tool window (bottom, order 66). Each opens an editor tab (`dataview:<source>:<expression>`). A *source* evaluates Python where the object lives: the debugger's selected frame (DAP `evaluate`, `clipboard` context), the console (a `\x05` query between statements, answered as `\x02q[id, ok, text]\x03`), or a notebook kernel (bridge op `evaluate`: a silent execute with a user expression). `pyide_sciview.call(obj, request)` answers in base64 JSON with one block of 200 rows × 40 columns, so a million-row frame is never copied; sorted and filtered row orders are cached per object and refresh generation. The grid is virtualized in both directions (proportional scrolling past 8M px), with dtypes under the column names, header-click sort (ascending, descending, off; missing values last), a Python filter expression (`DataFrame.eval` for pandas, `x` for NumPy arrays, `pl.sql_expr` for polars, row keys for lists of dicts), a slice field for arrays with more than two dimensions, column resize (double-click a header edge to fit), dimmed None/NaN/NaT/<NA>, Colored (numeric heatmap: per column for frames, whole slice for arrays), Ctrl+C / Copy with Headers / Copy as CSV, and Export to CSV… (written by Python). Views refresh on `debugContextChanged`, `pyconsoleChanged` and `notebookExecuted`.
- Extension points used: `ide.debugger.addVariableDecorator(fn(row, variable, expression))`, `ide.debugger.evaluate(expr, context)`, `ide.debugger.isSuspended()`; `ide.pyconsole.evaluate(expr)` and the console's Show Variables toggle (`pyconsole.showVariables`); `ide.notebook.addOutputDecorator(fn(wrap, output, {host}))`. The plugin exposes `ide.dataView` (`open({source, expr, title})`, `variablesView(source)`, `call`, `sources`) and `ide.plots` (`list`, `select`, `setZoom`, `save(i, 'png'|'svg', file?)`, `copy`, `delete`, `clear`).
- Plots: while Settings › Python Plots › "Show plots in tool window" is on (default), main sets `MPLBACKEND=module://pyide_sciview_backend`. Its `plt.show()` renders each figure to PNG (2× DPI) and SVG, sends it over a local socket with a per-session token, closes it and returns, so scripts never block. In the Python Console matplotlib is interactive and the console flushes new or changed figures after each statement (a changed figure replaces its plot). The Plots tool window (right, order 40, between Notifications and Minecraft) has history thumbnails, zoom (Ctrl+wheel, 1:1, fit), Save as PNG/SVG, Copy Image and delete. Notebook kernels drop that backend and keep inline plots. A run configuration that sets `MPLBACKEND` wins.
- numpy, pandas, matplotlib and polars are listed in the Install Manager (Python Tools).
- Tests: `verify/dataview.mjs` (ports 9720-9721; keeps a venv in `verify/out/dataview-venv` and installs the four packages through the Install Manager when missing).

## Python Console and Debug console input

- `renderer/features/pyconsole/pyide_repl.py` reads stdin in a thread and hands lines to the REPL loop and, while a statement runs, to `sys.stdin` (`ConsoleInput`: `input()`, `readline`, `read`, `.buffer`). On Windows the thread waits with `PeekNamedPipe` and reads only what is there: a read left pending on the pipe blocks other threads that query it (`GetFileType`), which a DLL's C runtime does when numpy's OpenBLAS loads. Stdlib modules cannot reproduce that hang, so `verify/dataview.mjs` (numpy and pandas in its venv) covers it, not `verify/pyconsole.mjs`. A line holding only `\x03` is Interrupt: SIGINT to the main thread while a statement runs (`signal.pthread_kill` on POSIX, `signal.raise_signal` on Windows, so `time.sleep` wakes too), and at a prompt it drops the block being typed; the session keeps its state. Stop still ends the process.
- The console sends what is typed before the first prompt, and what is submitted while earlier entries wait, one entry per prompt, so each echo sits right above its own output. While a statement runs the prompt is hidden (`.pyc-running`, after 150 ms, so quick statements do not flicker) and a typed line is the program's input: echoed as program input (`.stdin`), not as a `>>>` command and not into the history. Interrupt: Ctrl+C in the console input with no selection (with a selection it copies), the tool window's Interrupt button (`data-action="pyconsoleInterrupt"`) and `ide.pyconsole.interrupt()`.
- Its output goes to `ide.ui.console` as the program wrote it, so `\r` progress lines update in place and ANSI colors show, on stdout and stderr (`verify/pyconsole.mjs`).
- Debug (Shift+F9): the Debug console's stdin field (`#dbgIn`) takes the keyboard as the session starts, so an answer to `input()` never lands in the source file. Lines typed before debugpy has started the program are queued and sent once the process is attached; if the user moved the keyboard outside the Debug console meanwhile, it stays there. At a stop the editor gets the keyboard back from the hidden field. The Frames header names the stopped thread from the DAP `threads` response (`worker-1 · breakpoint`), not always MainThread.
- A console start that has not reached its first prompt ends when its process does, also when Stop or Rerun let go of it (`ended()`, which both run, settles it), so Rerun clicked twice quickly or Stop while the console starts never leaves the console unable to start.
- Debug start: main sends `debug:event {startKey, event: 'process', body: {id}}` as soon as it has spawned the debuggee, and the Debug console follows that process from then on, so its output shows live. A program that ends before debugpy listens (a Python debugpy cannot run on, an import error in sitecustomize) shows its own output and "Process finished with exit code N" instead of "Could not connect to debugpy … ECONNREFUSED"; main's own "The program exited before the debugger could connect" is `expected` and normally not shown. A startup the user stopped or replaced drains the output too, so nothing stays buffered. A Stop during a launch, an attach or Attach to Process rejects with an `expected` error ("Debugging stopped", `stoppedError()` in main/features/debug-attach.js), not an IDE internal error. The debugger matches debugpy's connection messages on the raw text, so a local ECONNREFUSED is never shown as "Cannot reach the server".
- Tests: `verify/pyconsole.mjs` (ports 9190-9191, in CI: lines before the banner with a project `.env` that slows the interpreter's start, `input()` in English and Korean, Ctrl+C in `time.sleep`, the toolbar button on a busy loop, Ctrl+C in `input()`, Ctrl+C with a selection, at a continuation prompt, Stop, `\r` progress lines on stdout and stderr and an ANSI color, 1008x681), `verify/debugger-input.mjs` (ports 9192-9193, in CI: Korean 철수 typed right after Shift+F9 reaches the program and the file is unchanged, focus moved away stays, a worker thread's name), `verify/run-debug-edges.mjs` (ports 9286-9287, in CI, about 3 minutes: a double Rerun, Stop then Rerun and four Rerun-then-Stop rounds while a slow sitecustomize keeps the console starting, a second Run during Before launch tasks, StopDebug at 0, 100 and 400 ms, a debuggee that exits before debugpy listens, Run and Debug in a 307- and a 562-character folder, and Pyright killed six times).

## Remote interpreters (WSL, SSH, Docker)

`renderer/features/remote-interp` (plugin `remote-interp`), `main/features/remote-interp.js` and the core library `main/remote/` add PyCharm's Add Interpreter › On WSL…, On SSH…, On Docker… and On Docker Compose… (Settings › Python Interpreter). A target is `{id, kind: 'wsl'|'ssh'|'docker'|'compose', python, distro | host, user?, port?, keyFile?, remoteDir? | image | composeFile, service, mountPath?}`, kept in the `interpreters.remote` setting after the form has connected and found Python. It joins `ide.interps` through `ide.interpreterProviders` with `executable` `<kind>://…#<id>` and a `target` field (plus the project root); its `cmd`/`args` are for display only.

- Routing: main features call `require('../remote')` for an interp with `target`: `startPython(ctx, interp, pyArgs, {cwd, env, forwards, onExit})` returns a process id like `ctx.startProcess`, `execPython` captures output, `debugEndpoint`, `terminal` and `pyrightPython`. Run, Debug, the test runner, the Python Console, Python Packages, the terminal and Pyright route through it. Not routed yet (local interpreters only): notebook kernels, the search-nav AST helper, viewers, workspace scripts, Install Manager pip items, venv creation and the AI/semantic setup.
- Every command is a POSIX `sh -c` script run by `wsl.exe -d <distro> --exec`, `ssh … <host>` or `docker exec` into the interpreter's dev container. `PathMap` (`main/remote/target.js`) maps Windows paths in arguments, `PYTHONPATH` (`;` → `:`) and the working directory to the target; a path the target cannot see is kept, so the program reports it instead of running something else. Paths the target prints map back for console traceback links through `ide.pathMappers` / `ide.toLocalPath(p)`.
- WSL sees every drive under its automount root (asked with `wslpath`), so the project is used in place; other Linux paths open as `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\…`. The IDE's Python helpers and debugpy are copied into the distribution like for SSH, since importing them over WSL's `/mnt` file sharing takes many seconds per process. SSH: before each run the project is uploaded rsync-style to `~/pyide-projects/<name>-<hash>` (or the form's remote folder): a size+mtime manifest from the target, then only changed files as a gzip stream unpacked by the target's own Python (`main/remote/pyide_remote.py`); the IDE's Python helpers and debugpy (without Windows binaries) go to `~/.cache/pyide/helpers/<version>` once. Docker and Compose: one long-lived container per interpreter and project (`docker run -d` / `docker compose run -d`, named `pyide-<hash>`, label `dev.pyide.target`) bind-mounts the project at `/opt/project` and the helpers at `/opt/pyide` and publishes ports 5678-5680; pip installs last until the container is removed, and the containers stop when the IDE quits.
- Debugger: debugpy listens in the target; the IDE reaches it through WSL's localhost forwarding, an `ssh -L` tunnel or a published container port. `ready()` waits until the port listens in the target (`/proc/net/tcp`, never a probe connection, which would take debugpy's one client), and `attach` gets `pathMappings` so breakpoints and frames use local paths.
- Tests: with `PYIDE_TEST_REPORT=fd:3` and `PYIDE_TEST_REPORT_MARK`, both reporters write marked lines to descriptor 3, a copy of stderr that pytest's capture leaves alone (written directly: WSL refuses to reopen it as `/dev/fd/3`); `startProcess`'s new `opts.filter(text, stream)` takes them out of the output and writes them, with paths mapped back, to the local report the tests feature polls.
- Stop: the script prints its PID as a marker first; `ctx.killProcess` calls `proc.remoteKill` (set by the remote layer) so the program in the target ends, not only the local client.
- Terminal: with a remote project interpreter, a plain new session (not one opened with a chosen shell or command) is a login shell in the target, in the project folder.
- Pyright stays on this machine. A WSL-side Pyright would need Node in the distribution and would not cover SSH or Docker, so instead the target's library folders (site-packages and the like; only `.py`, `.pyi` and `py.typed`) are copied to `<userData>/remote-interp/libs/<id>` with the same manifest diff, and Pyright's `pythonPath` becomes a `python.cmd` there that answers Pyright's `sys.path` and version queries with the copies and the target's version. The standard library comes from Pyright's bundled typeshed for that version.
