The copy refreshes when the interpreter is selected, after pip install or uninstall in it, and from Settings › Remote Interpreters. Pyright still evaluates `sys.platform` checks for Windows.
- Secrets: SSH passwords and key passphrases are encrypted with safeStorage in `<userData>/remote-interp/secrets.json` and never reach the renderer or settings. ssh gets them through `SSH_ASKPASS`: the IDE's executable run as Node with a `--require`d script that reads the answer for a one-time token from a named pipe the IDE serves. Hosts without a secret run with `BatchMode=yes`.
- Detection: `wsl -l -v`, `docker version` / `images` / `compose version`, and the Host entries of `~/.ssh/config` (`PYIDE_SSH_CONFIG` overrides it). A kind that cannot work shows the reason in its form and in Settings › Remote Interpreters, which also syncs a target's libraries again or removes it with its password and caches. Main-side messages are translated by the plugin (`main/remote/messages.js`).
- Tests: `verify/remote-interp.mjs` (ports 9730-9731) puts stand-in `wsl.exe`, `ssh.exe` and `docker.exe` first on PATH (`verify/remote-fake`: pip's console-script launcher around `fakeremote.py`, a small sh interpreter over a path-mapped root that rejects Windows paths, running a venv in `verify/out/remote-interp-venv` as the targets' python3) and covers adding, Run, tracebacks, a breakpoint hit, pytest, pip list, the console, the terminal, Pyright, Stop, password hosts, unavailable kinds and Korean for each kind. `REMOTE_REAL=wsl`, `REMOTE_REAL=docker` or `REMOTE_REAL=ssh:<host>` runs the checks against what is installed (`REMOTE_WSL_DISTRO`, `REMOTE_DOCKER_IMAGE`) and skips with a message when it is not; `REMOTE_KINDS=wsl,ssh` limits a fake run.

## Claude Code IDE integration

`main/features/claude-ide.js` and `renderer/features/claude-ide` connect Claude Code to PyIDE the way the official VS Code and JetBrains extensions do. The main process listens on a random 127.0.0.1 port for MCP over WebSocket (a minimal RFC 6455 server, text frames only) and writes `~/.claude/ide/<port>.lock` (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` moves it) with `{pid, workspaceFolders, ideName: 'PyIDE', transport: 'ws', runningInWindows, authToken}`; the token is 128 random bits and a connection without it in `X-Claude-Code-Ide-Authorization` is refused. Every PyIDE terminal gets `CLAUDE_CODE_SSE_PORT` (`claudeIde.ideEnv()` in `main/features/terminal.js`), which makes Claude Code started there connect to this window on its own; the lock file follows the open project and is removed on quit. While connected the status bar shows ✳ Claude Code. The renderer sends `selection_changed` (debounced) and answers the tools: openFile (project-relative paths, startText/endText selection), openDiff (a side-by-side diff tab; Accept writes the right side, which can be edited, and returns FILE_SAVED with the text; Reject or closing the tab returns DIFF_REJECTED), getCurrentSelection, getLatestSelection, getOpenEditors, getWorkspaceFolders, getDiagnostics (all problem sources, 0-based ranges), checkDocumentDirty, saveDocument, close_tab and closeAllDiffTabs. Tests: `verify/claude-ide.mjs` (ports 9810-9812) plays Claude Code's side; `CLAUDE_IDE_REAL=1` also starts the real CLI in a PyIDE terminal and waits for it to connect (no prompt is sent).

## Port sharing

The `port-sharing` plugin ("Ports"; `renderer/features/port-sharing`, `main/features/port-sharing.js`; depends on installer) works like VS Code's Ports view. Main polls `netstat -ano` every 3 s for listening TCP sockets on any address of this computer and keeps those whose process descends from the IDE's main process (Run, Launchers, setup steps and programs started in the terminal), reading the process tree (`Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`) only when the listeners change. Electron's own helpers, cloudflared, debugpy, language servers and a JVM's JDWP port are not servers and are left out. Add Port… lists a port whatever started it (a game server started by its own launcher); added ports are kept in `ports.added` (for every project), wait in the list as "Not listening" until their server starts, and have a × to remove them. The Ports tool window (bottom, order 66) lists port with its protocol, process (its command line as the tooltip), local address, the LAN addresses and public address; a new port is announced in a balloon with Open in Browser (web servers only) and Share.

Each server (port and PID) is probed once, and only while the Ports window is shown (the renderer asks with `ports:probe` when the window opens and when the list changes while it is open) or when Share needs it: a probe is a connection, and a program that accepts a single client would otherwise lose it to the probe. Until then the protocol is unknown and the row is treated as a web server. It is HTTP when it answers `HEAD / HTTP/1.0` with an HTTP status line (a development server logs that one request), HTTPS when a TLS handshake completes, TCP otherwise. A web server's local address is `http(s)://localhost:<port>/` and opens in the browser; a TCP server's is `localhost:<port>`, copied on click. A server on IPv4 loopback or every interface is named localhost (probed and tunnelled for bore on 127.0.0.1); one on `[::1]` or `[::]` alone (Vite and Node 17+ dev servers on Windows) is named `[::1]` and reached on ::1; one bound to a single LAN address is reached, probed and tunnelled on that address. A server on every interface (or on a LAN address) also shows this computer's private IPv4 addresses as `LAN` lines, for other computers on the same network (the Windows firewall may still ask once).

A tool Windows will not start (spawn UNKNOWN: Smart App Control or an antivirus blocking bore.exe, which has no publisher's signature, or a damaged file) leaves no tunnel "starting": Share says so, points at Windows Security's Protection history, and offers Install Again (uninstall, install, share again) and Show in Explorer.

Share on a web server runs a Cloudflare quick tunnel, `cloudflared tunnel --no-autoupdate --url http://localhost:<port>` (`https://…` and `--no-tls-verify` for an https server, whose development certificate is self-signed): no account, no router, firewall or other system-wide change, and the `https://<words>.trycloudflare.com` address it prints becomes the row's public address, announced with Copy Address and Open in Browser, once cloudflared has also logged "Registered tunnel connection": opened before that, the new name is not served yet and a resolver caches "no such name" for a minute or more. The address is kept from the chunk it arrives in, since cloudflared prints a long connectivity report before it registers. Share on a TCP server runs bore, `bore local <port> --local-host <host> --to bore.pub`, a raw TCP tunnel through the public bore.pub server (no account, no time limit); its "listening at bore.pub:<port>" line (colour codes stripped) gives the `bore.pub:<port>` address, announced with Copy Address and a note that the program's own client (a game's server address) connects to it. Before the first share a dialog says anyone with the address reaches the server without signing in (Don't ask again: `ports.confirmShare`). Stop Sharing, the server no longer listening, and quitting the IDE end the tunnel. "Share new ports automatically" (`ports.autoShare`, off by default) asks with the same warning before it turns on, then shares each new port the IDE started by itself. cloudflared 2026.10.0 (Apache 2.0; a 55,365,048-byte program, sha256 `86aee401…` as GitHub lists it) and bore 0.6.0 (MIT; a 673,531-byte zip holding bore.exe, sha256 `01709c64…` as GitHub lists it) are Install Manager items (`tool:cloudflared`, `tool:bore`, System Tools) installed like the built-in toolchains under `~\.pyide\cloudflared\cloudflared-2026.10.0` (`single`: one program, no archive) and `~\.pyide\bore\bore-0.6.0`; Share offers the download of the one it needs when it is missing. IPC: `ports:state`, `ports:refresh`, `ports:setAdded(ports)`, `ports:share(port)`, `ports:unshare(port)`, event `ports:changed` (ports carry `proto`, `host`, `lan`, `added`; tunnels `kind`); `ports:probe`; `ide.ports` = `{state, share, unshare, setAutoShare, addPort, removePort}`. `PYIDE_CLOUDFLARED` and `PYIDE_BORE` (JSON argvs) replace the tools in tests. Test: `verify/port-sharing.mjs` (ports 9782-9783, servers on 18631-18637) with stand-ins that print a quick tunnel's banner, a long report and the registration, and bore's coloured log: no probe (no connection) while the window is closed, a web server, a TCP server on every interface (protocol, host:port address, LAN address, bore arguments and address), an https server with a self-signed certificate (`verify/fixtures/port-sharing`, test-only), a server on [::1] only, added ports, and a bore.exe Windows will not start; `verify/port-sharing-live.mjs` (port 9785, opt-in, not in CI) installs the pinned cloudflared, shares a server answering one test sentence through Cloudflare and fetches it from the public address.

## HTML tag closing (html-autoclose)

HTML tag closing (plugin `html-autoclose`), as in PyCharm and VS Code: in files with the `html` language id (`.html`, `.htm`, `.xhtml`, `.jsp`), typing the `>` that ends a start tag puts its end tag after the caret (`<h1>|</h1>`), keeping the name's case. Typing `</` completes the end tag of the innermost open element (`</h1>|`), or of `script`/`style`/`textarea`/`title` inside their content. Nothing is inserted for void elements (`area`, `base`, `br`, `col`, `embed`, `hr`, `img`, `input`, `link`, `meta`, `param`, `source`, `track`, `wbr`), `<x/>`, a `>` in an attribute value, a comment, `<!DOCTYPE …>`/CDATA, or script, style, textarea and title content, or when the same end tag already follows the caret. `</` adds nothing when no element is open or a name already follows. A small scanner over the text before the caret works out the state and the open elements. Only typed text counts (`onDidType`): pastes and undo insert nothing. Each insertion is its own undo step, and several carets each get theirs in one step. Django and Jinja2 templates (`django-html`, `jinja2`) are left alone. Settings › Editor › Smart Keys › HTML: "Insert closing tag on typing >" (`editor.htmlCloseTag`) and "Complete the end tag on typing </" (`editor.htmlCloseOnSlash`), both on by default. Other plugins can add sections to the Smart Keys page through `ide.smartKeysSections` (`{defaults?, keywords?, mount(host, ctx)}`). Test: `verify/html-autoclose.mjs` (ports 9555-9556, in CI).

XML files (`xml` language id: `.xml`, `pom.xml`, `.fxml`, `.xsd`, `.xsl`, `.svg` opened as text, `.csproj`…) get the same, as in IntelliJ IDEA, with no void or raw-text elements (`<br>` gets `</br>`). Monaco's XML editing pairs `<` with `>`, so there `</` is followed by that `>`: only the element's name goes before it and the caret moves past it (`endTagNameFor` returns `{text, skip}`). The Smart Keys section is "HTML and XML"; the two settings cover both. Test: `verify/language-support.mjs`.

## HTML preview

The viewers plugin opens `.html`, `.htm` and `.xhtml` files with an editor and a live preview side by side (Editor / Editor and Preview / Preview, like Markdown). The page renders in a sandboxed iframe (scripts on, no `allow-same-origin`) served by `main/features/viewers.js` over `pyide-preview://<site>/<path>`, a scheme declared privileged in `main.js` before the app is ready. A site is the project folder (or the file's own folder outside a project), so relative and root-relative URLs for stylesheets, scripts, images and fonts resolve as on a web server; paths outside the site get 403, and so do paths a symlink or junction leads out of it; hidden files and folders (`.env`, `.git`) get 404 unless a previewed page lies in one. The unsaved text of every open document in the site (`viewers:previewOverrides`) stands in for the file on disk, so typing in the page or in an open stylesheet reloads the preview at once (debounced), as do saves and outside changes. HTML responses get a small helper script that keeps the scroll position across reloads, reports script errors (counted in the toolbar with their messages) and hands links that leave the site to the system browser. The toolbar has Reload, preview widths (Responsive, 1280, 768, 375 px) and Open in Browser for the saved file. Test: `verify/html-preview.mjs` (ports 9850-9851).

- JavaScript apps: a built page (`build/index.html`, `dist/index.html`) asks for its files from the root of the server it was built for (`/static/js/main.js`). A root-relative path the site root does not have is looked up from the folders of the pages previewed in that site (`pageDirs`), with the same symlink and outside-the-site checks, so the built app runs in the preview. A page only an app's dev server fills in (Create React App's `public/index.html` with `%PUBLIC_URL%`, or Vite's `index.html` whose module script is `/src/…`) gets a notice over the preview (`.vw-html-notice`): which app it is (`viewers:appInfo`: the nearest `package.json` at or above the page within the project, its scripts, its dev tool such as react-scripts or vite, its built page, whether `node_modules` exists), "Run npm start" (or `npm run dev`/`serve`) and "Open build/index.html" when there is a built page. Run opens a terminal tab in the app's folder running `npm.cmd` (PowerShell would run npm.ps1, which a Restricted execution policy refuses), with `BROWSER=none` and `npm install` first when `node_modules` is missing, watches the tab for the first `http://localhost:<port>` it prints, and opens that address in the frameworks plugin's Browser tab (the system browser without it). `verify/html-preview-app.mjs` (port 9795, in CI) checks a built page's `/static/` files, the notice and both buttons, with a `start` script running a small Node server.

## Conditional breakpoints

The debugger plugin's line breakpoints carry PyCharm's extra settings: `{line, condition, hitCondition, logMessage, enabled}`, saved with the other breakpoints in the `debugger.breakpoints:<project root>` setting and sent unchanged as DAP `SourceBreakpoint` fields in `setBreakpoints`, so debugpy does the work. `condition` is a Python expression evaluated in the frame. `hitCondition` follows debugpy: `3` suspends on the third hit only, `>= 3` from the third on, `% 3` on every third. A non-empty `logMessage` makes a logpoint: debugpy evaluates each `{expression}` in it, sends the text as an `output` event (shown in the Debug console) and never suspends there.

- Editing: right-click a breakpoint's glyph, or press Ctrl+Shift+F8 with the caret on its line, to open an inline popup under the glyph (`#dbgBpPopup` in `#dialogHost`, so global keys stay quiet while it is open): Enabled, Condition, Hit count, and "Log message instead of suspending" with the message. Enter, Esc, Done or a click outside apply the changes; Remove deletes the breakpoint; More or Ctrl+Shift+F8 again opens the Breakpoints dialog on it. Ctrl+Shift+F8 anywhere else opens the dialog, whose list shows each breakpoint's condition, hit count and log message and whose detail pane edits the same fields (`bpFields` builds both).
- Glyphs: a red dot; a dot with "?" when there is a condition or a hit count (`.dbg-bp.cond`); a diamond for a logpoint (`.dbg-bp.log`), which does not tint its line. The hover lists the settings.
- Test: `verify/cond-breakpoints.mjs` (ports 9900-9901) sets all three through the popup on `verify/fixtures/cond-breakpoints/loop.py`, runs it under debugpy, and checks that `i == 3` suspends once with i = 3, that hit count 4 suspends on the fourth pass only, that the logpoint prints one interpolated line per iteration with exactly two suspensions in the run, and that everything is restored after a restart with the same user data.

## Python exception breakpoints

The Breakpoints dialog (View Breakpoints, Ctrl+Shift+F8) lists "Python Line Breakpoints" and then "Python Exception Breakpoints", as in PyCharm. "Any exception" is always there and suspends on termination (uncaught) by default, which is debugpy's `uncaught` filter. "+" (`#dbgExcAdd`) takes a class in a field above the list, either a builtin name (`ValueError`) or a module path (`myapp.errors.AppError`); a name that is no Python class name is refused ("Not a Python class name: …"). "−" or Delete removes the selected one, Space toggles it, and Up/Down move through the list, which has the keyboard when the dialog opens. Each has Enabled, Activation policy (On raise, On termination) and a Condition: a Python expression evaluated in the frame that stops, where `__exception__` is (type, value, traceback). "Ignore library files" is shown checked and disabled, because debugpy runs Just My Code: an exception raised in a library suspends where it reaches project code. They are saved in `debugger.exceptionBreakpoints:<project root>` as `{type (null: Any exception), enabled, onRaise, onTerminate, condition}`.

- To debugpy: one DAP `exceptionOptions` entry per mode (`always` for On raise, `unhandled` for On termination). "Any exception" is the path `Python Exceptions` alone (BaseException). debug:start and debug:attach take them as `opts.exceptions` (main defaults to `{filters: ['uncaught']}`), and changes during a session are sent again. pydevd resolves each class by evaluating its name before the program runs, and a name that is not an importable exception class (`json.NoSuch`, `os.sep`, `True`) would end the debug session. So each name goes as an expression that gives the class or None. Known limit: a class defined in the script being debugged (`__main__`), or a nested class (`mod.Outer.Inner`), cannot be named.
- The renderer filters exception stops (`wantExceptionStop`). pydevd stops on a raised exception again in each caller it propagates through, so a stop for the same exception whose stack is the previous stack minus its top frame is passed over: On raise suspends once, where the exception is raised. pydevd takes no conditions through DAP, so the condition of the breakpoint pydevd matched (the most derived class in the exception's MRO) is evaluated at the stop, with the builtins taken from `__import__('builtins')` so a frame's own `bool` or `len` cannot shadow them; a false one continues before the UI shows anything, and an error suspends and prints the error to the Debug console. A stop passed over during a step goes on with the step (a step over that ended up deeper first steps out). A propagation stop that a step brought is shown, so F8 never turns into a run.
- At an exception stop the Variables pane shows `#dbgException`: a red lightning glyph, the type and message (`ValueError: empty text`, program text) and On raise or On termination.
- Test: `verify/debugger-exceptions.mjs` (ports 9050-9051, in CI). It covers the default group; a caught ValueError that does not suspend without a breakpoint and suspends at the raise with one (once, not again in its caller), and not with the breakpoint disabled; an `errors.AppError` condition that passes over n == 3; names debugpy cannot use as classes (`json.NoSuch`, `nosuch.Missing`, `os.sep`, `True`) leaving the session working; an uncaught KeyError with "Any exception" on and off; Space to disable, and a disabled breakpoint not sent; a breakpoint enabled during a session; F8 at the raise, and F8 over a call whose exception is passed over; 1008x681; a restart; and the dialog and strip in Korean.

## Variables: Set Value, Copy Value, Copy Name, Add to Watches

Rows of the Variables and Watches lists have PyCharm's context menu (right-click, or the context-menu key for the selected row): Set Value… (F2, `SetValue`), Copy Value (Ctrl+C, `CopyValue`), Copy Name and Add to Watches. F2 and Ctrl+C act only while the Variables or Watches list has the keyboard and a row is selected (by click or Up/Down), and Ctrl+C stands back while text is selected, so lsp's F2 and copying elsewhere are unchanged. Delete in the Watches list removes a watch only when the list itself has the keyboard, not from a field inside it.

- Set Value turns the value into a field (`#dbgSetValue`) holding the whole value. Enter assigns the Python expression typed there, and Enter with the text unchanged assigns nothing. Esc or leaving the field cancels, and the keyboard stays in the list. A row with an expression (`evaluateName`, or the watch) uses DAP `setExpression`, because debugpy's `setVariable` silently ignores a value that fails. A variable shown under Globals (also inside its groups) is set with DAP `setVariable` on that scope after the value is evaluated (so a bad value shows its error): pydevd runs setExpression's assignment with the frame's locals, which inside a function makes a local of that name. Other rows without an expression use `setVariable` on their container. A failure keeps the field and says why ("Cannot set the value: NameError: …"). Afterwards the frame is read again, so Variables, Watches and inline values show the new value, and the row stays selected.
- Set Value prefills, and Copy Value copies, the row's expression evaluated with the DAP `clipboard` context. The tree shows debugpy's shortened text (a string inside a dict is cut at 128 characters into text that is still valid Python), so Enter never assigns the shortened value. Collections over 60 items stay shortened even there, because pydevd caps them.
- A file that a run configuration type with a `debugTarget` claims (pytest for a test file, a visual script; `claimingType(path)` in the debugger) is debugged through that type by the toolbar Debug button and the `Debug` action. The project tree hides the plain-script "Debug '…'" entry for such a file and keeps the type's own "Debug 'pytest in …'".
- Test: `verify/debugger-values.mjs` (port 9052, in CI). It covers F2 on a loop variable and Enter; an error and Esc; Ctrl+C on an int and on a dict holding a 400-character string (the tree's text is shorter, while the clipboard and the Set Value field get all 412 characters); F2 and Ctrl+C keeping their editor meaning in the editor; the menu by mouse and by the context-menu key; Copy Name; Add to Watches; Set Value on a watch (Delete in its field edits the text) and on a dict item; a global set while stopped in a function (`gv.py`: a bad value shows its error, `99` changes the Globals row and adds no local, the program prints `counter 99`); the program finishing with the values set (`sum=(220, 5, 400)`); a test file's tree menu, and the toolbar Debug button on it (`-m pytest`); 1008x681; and Korean.

## .env files

Like PyCharm's EnvFile and VS Code's `python.envFile`, programs the IDE starts get the variables of `.env` files. `main/dotenv.js` owns the parsing and loading, in the main process only, so values never reach the renderer.

- Syntax (python-dotenv's): `KEY=value` (trimmed; ` #` after whitespace starts a comment, `a#b` keeps its `#`), an optional `export ` prefix, `# comment` lines, single quotes (literal), double quotes (escapes `\n \t \r \" \\ \$` and the rest of python-dotenv's set), quoted values spanning lines, and `${VAR}` / `${VAR:-default}` in unquoted and double-quoted values, resolved from keys earlier in the same or an earlier file, then the IDE's process environment (case-insensitively on Windows); unknown names expand to nothing. Lines that are not entries, and an unterminated quote's first line, are skipped and logged by file and line number only. `parse(text, lookup)`, `loadFiles(files, base)` (later files win; a missing file is skipped) and `withEnvFiles(env, envFiles, root)`.
- Run configurations: Python and pytest configurations have an Env files field (`envFiles`, `;`-separated, relative to the project root), defaulting to `.env`; configurations saved before the field existed get the default, and an empty field loads nothing. A listed file that does not exist (other than the default `.env`) prints "Env file not found" in the run console. The configuration's own environment variables override the files. Other types (Django, Flask, FastAPI) get the project's `.env`. `ide.run.envFiles(cfg)` gives the absolute paths, `ide.run.envFilesField` the field for other types.
- Launchers: `run:start` (7th argument), `tests:start` (`envFiles`), `debug:start` (`opts.envFiles`, forwarded by `ide.debugger.debugFile` from `cfg.envFiles`) take absolute paths; `undefined` means the project root's `.env` (the first root), which is also what the Python Console and a debug session started without a configuration get. New terminals load the project's `.env` while Settings › Terminal › "Load the project .env file into new terminals" (`terminal.loadEnvFile`, default on) is on; explicit `pty:create` `env` still wins, and remote sessions do not get it.
- Secrets: every loaded value of four or more characters (except booleans and short numbers) is added to the log scrubber (`addSecretValues` in `main/diagnostics/redact.js`), so it is masked in the IDE log, internal errors and Collect Logs even where a launch line carries it (remote interpreters put the environment on the command line).
- Highlighting: the `dotenv` plugin registers a Monarch language for `.env`, `.env.*` and `*.env` files (keys, `export`, values, escapes, `${VAR}`, comments).
- Test: `verify/dotenv.mjs` (port 9910) checks the parser and the scrubber as pure Node, then on `verify/fixtures/dotenv` Run, explicit overrides, several and missing files, Debug with the default and a configuration's own file, a test, the Python Console, new terminals with the setting on and off, the settings page, highlighting, and that no value reaches the IDE log.

## Previews of closed files (Peek, Ctrl+hover)

Monaco's Peek Definition, the Ctrl+hover definition link and preview, and the references peek open their targets through Monaco's model service, which only knows models that exist; a target in a file with no tab used to reject with "Model not found". `ide.previewModel(path)` (`renderer/core/editors.js`) reads such a file from disk into a preview model, which is disposed two minutes after no editor (a peek) shows it, or taken over by `loadDoc` when a tab opens the file: an unedited preview is brought up to the disk text, one edited inside a peek keeps those edits as unsaved changes. The LSP plugin's definition, type definition and references providers load the target files first (at most 100 files per result) and give their uris as `Uri.file` of the path, as tabs do; the frameworks plugin's template definitions do the same. Test: `verify/peek-definition.mjs` (port 9260; in CI) first shows the rejection with the preview models turned off, then Peek Definition, Ctrl+hover and the references peek into a closed file with no rejection, and a tab opened after the file changed on disk showing the disk text, not modified.

## Breadcrumbs and call hierarchy

`renderer/features/navigation-bar` (plugin `navigation-bar`, depends on `lsp`) adds PyCharm's breadcrumbs and Call Hierarchy, both from Pyright through `ide.lsp`; main only declares the `callHierarchy` client capability in `main/features/lsp.js`.

- Breadcrumbs: every editor group gets a 24 px bar between its tab bar and its body (found through `ide.groups()` in an `ide.onEditor` hook, so the core needs no mount hook; editors that viewers embed get none). It shows the file's path relative to the project (the file alone outside it), then the classes, functions, methods and properties containing the caret, from `ide.lsp.documentSymbols` (the lsp plugin's shared document-symbol request, see Runtime performance), cached per model version. Caret moves re-render after 120 ms and edits after 400 ms, and only when the chain changed. The bar's height is fixed and it scrolls sideways instead of wrapping, so updates never resize the editor; it is hidden (and the editor takes the space back) only while the group shows no text model (a non-text viewer tab or no tab) or the setting is off. Clicking a path segment lists the entries of its folder with `ide.fileIcon` icons (a folder opens its own list, a file opens); clicking a symbol jumps to its name, and its ▾ lists the scopes beside it with kind icons. A request made before Pyright was ready is asked again on the next `problemsChanged`.
- Setting `editor.breadcrumbs` (default on): Settings › Editor › Breadcrumbs, and the action `EditorToggleShowBreadcrumbs` (Show Breadcrumbs).
- Call Hierarchy (`CallHierarchy`, Ctrl+Alt+H, and Call Hierarchy in the editor context menu): `textDocument/prepareCallHierarchy` at the caret, or, when the caret is not on a name, at the innermost scope's name, as PyCharm takes the enclosing function. The Hierarchy tool window (bottom, order 19) shows the tree with Caller Hierarchy (`callHierarchy/incomingCalls`, the default) and Callee Hierarchy (`callHierarchy/outgoingCalls`) toggles. A node asks for its calls only when it is expanded, and a node that repeats an ancestor is a leaf. Double-click or Enter opens a caller at its call site in the caller and a callee at the call in its parent (the root at its name); Up/Down/Left/Right move and expand. `ide.navigationBar` has `showCallHierarchy()`, `mode()` and `setMode('callers'|'callees')`.
- Test: `verify/breadcrumbs.mjs` (ports 9930-9931) on `verify/fixtures/breadcrumbs` (nested classes and functions in `pkg/shapes.py`, callers in `app.py`): the crumbs at several carets and the debounce, that the bar and editor heights stay put, symbol clicks and both dropdowns, split groups, an image tab, the setting, callers from the other file, callees, lazy loading, double-click jumps, the context menu entry, no ResizeObserver loop notice or renderer exception, and the Korean UI.

## Go to Implementation, Super Method and Test; Comparison Failure

- Go to Implementation(s) (action `GotoImplementation`) and Go to Super Method (`GotoSuperMethod`) are in the type-hierarchy plugin, the editor context menu and Navigate; their keys (PyCharm's Ctrl+Alt+B and Ctrl+U) come from core's keymap table (`KEYMAP_DEFAULTS`). Pyright has no implementation request, so the implementations of a class are its subtypes at every level (found as Type Hierarchy finds them, at most 100) and those of a method the same-named methods of those classes. Go to Implementation takes what the name at the caret refers to (a call `s.area()` too), else the method or class around the caret; Super Method takes the method around the caret and opens, on each path up through the bases, the nearest class that defines it (`object` left out), or on a class line its superclasses. One target opens; several are listed by name ("Choose Implementation of area (2 found)"); none gives an editor hint (No implementations found, No super method found, No super class found).
- Gutter markers (left glyph lane): an up arrow on a method that overrides a base's method (green when every one it overrides is abstract: Implements method in …), a down arrow on a method overridden in subclasses (green when it is abstract: Is implemented in …) and on a class with subclasses (Is subclassed by …), both arrows on a method that does both. A click opens the one target or lists them in a menu by group, and never sets a breakpoint. Markers are worked out for the classes in view (and 20 lines around it, at most 40 classes) 0.3 s after a file shows or scrolls and 1.5 s after typing stops, kept per class and document version until a save or file event, and not at all in Power Save Mode; files that are not open are read and their symbols asked for once until a file event. Subclasses are not found with references as in the Type Hierarchy window (Pyright parses and binds every project file that mentions the name, and cannot stop such a request: 40 classes of a 2,000-line module cost 35 s of Pyright time on open and 60 requests after one typed line): the candidates are the files whose class statements spell the name in their bases, from code vision's word index (`type-hierarchy:subclassFiles(root, names, epoch)` in `main/features/type-hierarchy.js`, a plugin main module that uses `main/features/code-vision.js`'s worker whether or not code vision is enabled), at most 100, plus the open documents that mention it (their unsaved text), and each such base is checked with Pyright's definition. A subclass that names its base through an `import ... as` alias gets no marker, and overrides of `object`'s methods get none, as in PyCharm. Measured on H1's project (medium.py, 3 launches each): Pyright CPU opening the file 43.6 s -> 17.0 s, typing one line 47.6 s -> 4.9 s, working set 690 -> 550 MB, median completion in the 25 s after the open 210 ms -> 3 ms, 0 requests in Power Save Mode (60-62 before). `ide.typeHierarchy.gutterMarks(editor)` lists them (`{line, kind: up|down|updown, implements, hover}`).
- Go to Test (goto-test plugin, action `GotoTest`; PyCharm's Ctrl+Shift+T comes from core's keymap table) goes from a module, class or function to test_<module>.py or <module>_test.py anywhere in the project (nearest folder first), at TestSquare for Square, test_area for a function area and TestSquare.test_area for Square.area, and from a test file back to <module>.py at the matching class or function ("No test subjects found" when there is none). With several tests, or none, a list offers Create New Test… first (`CreateTest`), as PyCharm does. The Create Test dialog has Target directory (the project's tests/ or test/ folder when there is one, else the module's folder), Test file name, Test class name (Test<Class> for a class at the caret, empty for the module) and the methods, or module functions, to write stubs for (public ones checked). OK writes pytest code after `from <module> import <names>`: a class with `def test_x(self): assert False` methods, or `def test_x(): assert False` functions. The module path is the file's path from the deepest Sources Root that holds it, or from the project root; with no root marked it follows the packages above the file, from below a `src` folder in the src layout. An existing file gets the import after its imports (a statement over several lines, in parentheses or after a backslash, stays whole) and the code at its end; a class or test function of a name the file already defines is refused, and such functions start unchecked. The file opens at its first test. `ide.gotoTest`: `go`, `create`, `outline`, `moduleOf`.
- Comparison Failure: the pytest plugin keeps the operands of the running test's last failed `==` (`pytest_assertrepr_compare`) and of a failed `TestCase.assertEqual(first, second)` (`pyide_test_diff.capture_assert_equal`, also used by the unittest runner), and the result event of a test that failed on that assertion carries `diff: {expected, actual}` (expected is the right side of `==` and assertEqual's second argument; two strings as they are, other values `pprint.pformat`, each at most 200,000 characters). The pair counts only when the test failed at that assertion: the same frame and line for `==`, the same exception for assertEqual, so a comparison the test caught does not count. The values are formatted only then, and a value of more than 66,666 elements is cut before `pprint`, so a large comparison costs a fraction of a second. The test's output then ends with `<Click to see difference>`, which, like Ctrl+D in the Test Results tree, opens Comparison Failure (<test>) in a compare tab (`ide.compare.texts`): Expected left and Actual right, read-only, with the number of differences (two texts that differ only in line separators say Contents have differences only in line separators), F7 / Shift+F7 and the viewer switch; opening it again for the test replaces that tab. Without the compare plugin it is a dialog with Monaco's diff editor. Run and Debug test results both show it.
- Known limits: Create Test writes pytest code only (no unittest TestCase option) and has no folder browse button. Comparison Failure formats the values when the test call has ended, so a value changed after the failing assertion (in a `finally` or a `with` exit) shows its changed state; past the 66,666-element budget a dict or set is cut in insertion order before `pprint` sorts it, so the shown part can differ.
- Test: `verify/navigation-tests.mjs` (ports 9550-9551, in CI); it also checks that the markers send no references request and nothing in Power Save Mode. `verify/goto-test-edges.mjs` (port 9890, in CI; it makes its own project in the temp folder): Create Test after a parenthesized and a backslash-continued import (the file compiles and pytest collects it), a test function the file already has, the module path from a Sources Root, a caught comparison followed by another failure (also for assertEqual and a re-raised helper), a value of 3,000,000 elements, Contents have differences only in line separators, and the test helpers kept in LF.

## Dependency management

The packages plugin checks what the project declares against what the project interpreter has, like PyCharm's "Package requirements are not satisfied". `main/packages/deps.js` is pure (parsing, PEP 440, markers); `main/features/packages.js` does the I/O; the renderer half is in `renderer/features/packages/index.js`.

- Sources, in the project root only: `requirements*.txt` (options such as `-r`, `--find-links`, `-e`, paths and URLs are not packages; `\` continuations and `--hash` work), `pyproject.toml` (`[project].dependencies`, and `[tool.poetry.dependencies]` with `^`, `~`, `*` and `||` turned into PEP 440, `python` and `optional = true` left out; a pyproject.toml with neither is a tool config, not a dependency file) and `Pipfile` (`[packages]`). The manager is `uv` when `uv.lock` exists, `poetry` for `poetry.lock`, otherwise `[tool.poetry]` picks Poetry and `[tool.uv]` uv, otherwise pip. uv and poetry are looked for next to the interpreter (the Install Manager's uv item installs it there), on PATH, in `~\.local\bin`, `~\.cargo\bin` and `%APPDATA%\Python\Scripts`.
- Check (`packages:deps(root, interp, {texts, fresh})`): installed distributions and marker variables come from `importlib.metadata` run in the interpreter (not pip, so uv's pip-less venvs work; remote interpreters too), cached per interpreter until an install or `fresh`. Names compare as PEP 503 (case, `-`, `_`, `.`); specifiers follow PEP 440 including `~=`, `==1.2.*`, `===`, local versions and the exclusive `<`/`>` rules, and pre-releases that are installed count. A requirement whose marker is false (`; python_version < "3"`, `extra == "dev"`) is not required; a marker the parser cannot read counts as true. Unsaved editor text of open dependency files is checked, not the disk copy. It runs when the project opens, the interpreter changes, a dependency file is saved, edited (600 ms), created or deleted through the IDE, after every install from the Packages window, and when the window gets focus (re-reading the installed packages at most every 20 s).
- Results: a `warning` per unsatisfied line in problems source `dependencies` ("Package 'x' is not installed", "Package requirement 'x>=2' is not satisfied: 1.0 is installed"); a banner above the editor of the file, "N packages not installed / outdated" plus the names and Install (it sits in the editor group's body and moves the editor host down, so it never covers text; embedded editors get none); the same line with Install in the Python Packages window (`#depsSummary`) and the count as the window's badge. Alt+Enter (`ShowDependencyActions`, only while a dependency file has focus; Python keeps the lsp feature's) and the lightbulb offer "Install <pkg>" on a flagged line.
- Install (`packages:depsInstall`) runs in the Python Packages console with the command line shown: pip `install -r <file>` (cwd: the file's folder) for requirements.txt and `pip install <unsatisfied requirements>` for pyproject.toml / Pipfile; uv `sync --inexact` into the interpreter's venv (`UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT`) for pyproject.toml, `uv pip install --python <interp> -r <file>` otherwise; `poetry install --no-root` with `VIRTUAL_ENV` set for a Poetry pyproject.toml. A quick fix installs only its requirement (`uv pip install` in a uv project, pip otherwise). When the project's tool is missing the banner says so and offers "Install uv…" (opens the Install Manager, which lists uv under Python tools) and "Install with pip"; PyIDE never installs a tool system-wide itself. Afterwards the package list and the check refresh. In that console stderr lines are red unless they start with WARNING or DEPRECATION (per line, not per message: a line split over two messages keeps the color its start got).
- "Add to requirements" (Packages window, for an installed package no dependency file declares) adds `name~=version` to requirements.txt, else to `[project].dependencies` (created when missing), else to a new requirements.txt; an open file is edited in its editor when it has unsaved changes, otherwise written and reloaded (`packages:depsAdd(kind, text, requirement)` returns the new text).
- `ide.dependencies`: `check({fresh})` -> the state, `state()` -> `{manager, lock, tool: {name, path} | null, files: [{path, kind, editable, reqs: [{name, spec, raw, line, col, endCol, status: 'ok'|'missing'|'outdated', installed}]}]}`, `install(path, {specs, pip})`.
- Test: `verify/deps.mjs` (port 9920) builds three tiny wheels with zipfile, a temp project from `verify/fixtures/deps` with a `.venv` holding pyide-deps-other 1.0, and runs pip offline (`PIP_NO_INDEX`, `PIP_FIND_LINKS`): missing and outdated (with `_`/case-different names) are flagged with squiggles, banner and summary; the quick fix installs one; Install runs `pip install -r` and the banner, squiggles and summary go; Add to requirements; then a pyproject.toml with `uv.lock` (uv detected, marker-skipped item, banner per uv's presence, quick fix) and a Poetry pyproject.toml (`^`/`~` constraints).

## AI chat and commit messages

`renderer/features/ai-chat` and `main/features/ai-chat.js` add an AI Chat tool window and ✨ commit messages. Both call the Claude Code CLI through `complete()` in `main/ai/claude.js`, exactly like ai-edit (same flags, no tools, single turn; models from `aiChat.model`, default `sonnet`), so `PYIDE_CLAUDE_PATH` swaps in a stand-in CLI for tests.

- Right-side tool windows: `registerToolWindow({side: 'right'})` puts the window in `#rightPanel` with its button on `#stripeRight`, which only shows while it holds a button. The right splitter grows leftwards (`data-grow="left"`). AI Chat is the only right-side window (order 10).
- AI Chat (Ctrl+Shift+L: opens and focuses the input, hides it when the input has focus, and asks about the selection when the editor has one; also the editor context menu's Ask AI about Selection and Open AI Chat, and Alt+Enter › Ask AI about Selection, a `quickfix` code action offered only on explicit requests so a selection never lights the bulb). A chat is a list of messages; the reply streams in (`ai-chat:stream` {reqId, text}) and is rendered with the bundled `marked` through an allowlist sanitizer (no images; links open in the browser). Fenced code gets Copy, Insert at Cursor (replaces the active editor's selection as one undo step) and Apply to File. Apply opens the target file (the fence's `path=…`, else the asked-about selection's file, the current file, the active tab) and hands `ide.aiEdit.review(editor, {start, end, lines})` the proposal, so it is reviewed in ai-edit's inline diff, hunk by hunk. The region is the asked-about selection, else the `def`/`class` the block redefines (its decorators when the block has some, re-indented for a method), else the whole file when the block is at least half of it, else an insertion after the caret's line.
- Context chips: the current file (follows the active tab; × leaves it out until the tab changes), the selection, and `@file` mentions completed from the project files (`fs:walk`, ranked by name prefix, name, path). A fully typed `@rel/path` counts too. Contents are read when the message is sent (an open document's text wins) and capped at 40,000 characters per file; the message keeps only paths and line ranges. Older turns are dropped first beyond 60,000 characters of conversation.
- History: `<userData>/ai-chat/<sha1 of the project path>.json` (`ai-chat:loadHistory` / `ai-chat:saveHistory`, written 200 ms after a change through a temp file), up to 50 chats; the tool window's ＋ starts a chat and 🕘 lists them. Stop (the Send button while generating, or Esc in the input) calls `ai-chat:cancel`, which kills the CLI's process tree; the partial reply stays, marked Stopped.
- Commit messages: the vcs plugin exposes `ide.vcs.addMessageAction(node)`, `commitMessage()` (the textarea) and `includedFiles()`. ✨ saves all, then `ai-chat:commitMessage` builds the diff in main: `git diff --cached`, or when nothing is staged the files ticked in the Commit window (`git diff HEAD -- <paths>` in batches, untracked ones as all-added diffs of their first 64 KB, binaries named only). A large diff keeps each file's header and its first 3,000 characters, cut at a line, within 16,000 characters, then names the files left out. The system prompt asks for a Conventional Commits message (`type(scope): summary`, ≤ 72 characters, optional 1-3 bullets) in `aiChat.commitLanguage` (`ko` default, or `en`; Settings › AI › AI Chat). The text streams into the box (`ai-chat:commit-stream`, fences and quotes stripped) and stays editable; typing into the box while it streams cancels the request and keeps what the user typed.
- `ide.aiChat`: `open`, `askAboutSelection(editor?)`, `send(text)`, `stop()`, `newChat()`, `generateCommitMessage()`, `state()`, `saved()`.
- Test: `verify/ai-chat.mjs` (ports 9940-9941) with a stand-in CLI: Ctrl+Shift+L and the right panel, Alt+Enter › Ask AI about Selection, @ completion, streaming, the request's context, Markdown and code buttons, Insert at Cursor, Apply through the ai-edit review (selection and method regions), history across a page reload, Stop, then a temp Git repo: staged diff, ticked changes with nothing staged, a 20,000-line diff, Korean and English, and the Korean UI.

## Live templates and postfix completion

`renderer/features/live-templates` adds PyCharm's live templates and postfix completion for Python, as Monaco snippets.

- Templates use PyCharm's syntax: `$NAME$` variables (the caret visits them in order of first appearance; a repeated name is mirrored), `$END$` for the final caret, `$SELECTION$` for the text Surround With wraps, `$$` for a literal dollar. Bodies indent with a tab, which Monaco turns into the file's indentation, and lines after the first get the current line's indentation. Built-ins: `main`, `iter`, `itere`, `compl`, `compli`, `prop`, `super`, `defm`, `defs`, `defc`, `with`, `try`, `pt`, `dataclass`, `fstr`, `log`, with default variable values in their `vars`. `dataclass` and `log` add `from dataclasses import dataclass` / `import logging` after the imports at the top of the file when it lacks them. The core's fallback provider (`renderer/core/python.js`) no longer offers `main` and `try`; `def`, `class` and `for` stay there.
- Expansion: Tab right after an exact abbreviation expands it, through the `liveTemplateAtCaret` context key the plugin keeps per editor and a Tab keybinding rule that also wins over the suggest widget, but not over visible ghost text or an active snippet. Templates are also completion items (kind Snippet, with a preview). Nothing expands or is offered inside a string or comment: `contextAt` scans the file from the top to the caret for `#`, quotes and triple quotes. A word that only ends in an abbreviation (`xmain`) or follows a dot is not a template.
- Postfix completion: `expr.if`, `.ifn`, `.nn`, `.not`, `.for`, `.enum`, `.print`, `.return`, `.len`, `.par`, `.with`, `.try`, `.main`, `.f`. `exprStart` scans back from the dot on the same line over names, attribute access, calls, subscripts, bracketed groups and string literals (bracket- and string-aware), so `foo(a, b).print` wraps the whole call. Statement templates (`if`, `ifn`, `nn`, `for`, `enum`, `return`, `with`, `try`, `main`) apply only when the expression starts the line. `.for` and `.enum` name the loop variable from the expression (`items` → `item`, `range(…)` → `i`). Tab expands an exact key; the completion list shows them after a dot with a preview, replacing only the key and deleting `expr.` with an additional edit, so they rank by the key like Pyright's members and sort after them.
- Surround With Live Template (Ctrl+Alt+J, action `SurroundWithLiveTemplate`) picks a template that contains `$SELECTION$` (`main`, `iter`, `itere`, `with`, `try`) and wraps the selected lines (the caret's line when nothing is selected), keeping their relative indentation.
- Settings › Editor › Live Templates lists the templates with a switch each, edits the abbreviation (user templates only), description and text, adds and removes user templates, restores a modified built-in, and switches each postfix template. Settings: `liveTemplates.custom` (`{abbr: {description?, body?, enabled?}}`: overrides of built-ins and user templates) and `liveTemplates.postfixDisabled` (keys).
- `ide.liveTemplates`: `templates()`, `postfix()`, `expand(editor)`, `surround(editor, abbr?)`, `toSnippet(template, selection)`, `exprStart(line, dot)`, `contextAt(model, pos)`, `candidateAt(editor)`.
- Test: `verify/live-templates.mjs` (port 9970): `main`, `iter`, `pt`, `itere`, `log` and `prop` with Tab and their first tab stops, the completion list, `items.for` picked from the list with its preview, `x.ifn`, `foo(a, b).print` and other postfix cases, strings, comments and docstrings, Surround With, and a user template, a disabled built-in and a disabled postfix template set in Settings that hold after a reload.
- Other languages, in groups (`GROUPS`: `python`, `java`, `js` for JavaScript and TypeScript, `c` for C and C++), each with its built-in templates, postfix templates and its own list on the settings page (a language list at the top of the page; a group without postfix templates hides that section). Java has IntelliJ IDEA's `psvm`/`main`, `sout`, `souf`, `soutv`, `serr`, `fori`, `iter`, `itar`, `ifn`, `inn`, `thr`, `psf`, `psfs`, `psfi`, `prsf`, `St` and the postfix `.sout`, `.if`, `.nn`, `.null`, `.not`, `.for`, `.fori`, `.while`, `.var`, `.return`, `.par`; JavaScript/TypeScript WebStorm's `log`, `fori`, `iter` (for…of), `itin` (for…in), `imp`, `fn`, `af`, `try` and `.log`, `.if`, `.not`, `.for`, `.const`, `.let`, `.return`, `.await`, `.par`; C/C++ `main`, `fori`, `inc`, `incl`, `guard`, `pf`, and for C++ only (`langs: ['cpp']`) `cout` and `forr`. Outside Python, string and comment are told by Monaco's token at the template's first letter (the postfix template's dot). Their edits and switches are stored in the same settings as `group:name` (`java:sout`), so Python's stay as they were. The completion provider is registered for every group's languages; next to JDT LS or Monaco's TypeScript service its items sort after theirs. `ide.liveTemplates.templates(group)` / `postfix(group)` take a group id (default `python`), `groups()` lists them. Test: `verify/language-support.mjs`.

## Language basics (language-basics) and editing in every language

What PyCharm, IntelliJ IDEA and VS Code give every language, offline. `verify/language-support.mjs` (ports 9870-9871, in CI) checks each in the app.

- Languages by file name (core `editors.js` `languageFor`): the extension table first, then the language Monaco registers for the file name or extension, so `.tsx`, `.jsx`, `.mts`, `.hpp`, `.hh`, `.cc`, `.cxx`, `.scss`, `.less`, `.rb`, `.php`, `.swift`, `.dart`, `.r`, `.scala`, `.kts`, `.xsd`, `.svg` (as text), `.properties`, `Dockerfile` and more open with highlighting, folding, brackets and comments instead of as plain text. `.tsx`/`.jsx` get JSX (`jsx: Preserve` in Monaco's TypeScript and JavaScript compiler options): elements are not errors.
- Keyword and word completion (plugin `language-basics`) for every language Monaco has a grammar for and no completion service of its own (not Python, JavaScript/TypeScript, CSS/SCSS/Less, HTML, JSON or Markdown): C, C++, Java while JDT LS is not running (`ide.lsp.clientFor(path).status() !== 'ready'`), Go, Rust, Kotlin, C#, SQL files, shell, PowerShell, Lua, Ruby, PHP, YAML and the rest. The words are the grammar's `keywords`, `typeKeywords`, `builtinFunctions`, `builtinVariables`, `builtins` and `constants` (loaded with the grammar), and the file's own identifiers, since Monaco's word completion stays out while a provider of the language answers. Nothing in a string or comment token; only for `file:` models (database consoles keep their own completion).
- Markdown: a folding range provider folds each heading's section (up to the next heading of the same or a higher level, trailing blank lines left out), fenced code blocks and `<!-- #region -->` … `<!-- #endregion -->`. Enter in a list item past its marker continues the list as PyCharm's Markdown editor does: the same bullet, the next number (`1.` → `2.`, `1)` → `2)`), an unchecked `[ ] ` after a task item, the item's indentation; the text after the caret moves to the new item. Enter in an empty item removes the marker and ends the list. Not inside a fenced code block.
- Lua and shell: Enter after a line that opens a block (VS Code's patterns: `function`, `then`, `do`, `repeat`, `else`, `{` in Lua; `if … then`, `while/until/for/select … do`, `else`, `case … in`, `{`/`(` in shell) indents the new line (an `onEnterRules` rule added over Monaco's configuration once the language loads; Monaco's editor runs `autoIndent: 'advanced'`, which does not use indentation rules on Enter). Typing the closing word on a line of its own (`end`, `else`, `elseif`, `until`, `}`; `fi`, `done`, `esac`, `else`, `elif`, `}`) moves the line back to the indentation of the block's opening line.
- Comment with Block Comment (Ctrl+Shift+/, core action `CommentByBlockComment`, Monaco's `editor.action.blockComment`) as in PyCharm, in every language with a block comment.
- Reformat Code (Ctrl+Alt+L, tooling plugin) in a file Monaco formats itself (JavaScript, TypeScript, JSON, HTML, CSS, SCSS, Less) runs Monaco's Format Document, or Format Selection when text is selected, in the editor showing the file, with the same "Reformatted code: N lines changed" hint as for Python. Python keeps ruff, Java its language server; a file with no formatter says "Reformat Code has no formatter for this file type".
- Indentation (settings plugin): "Detect and use existing file indents for editing" (Settings › Editor › Code Style, `editor.detectIndent`, on by default, as in PyCharm): an open file takes the indentation it is written with (Monaco's `detectIndentation`: a 2-space JavaScript or YAML file indents 2 spaces on Enter and Tab, a tab-indented file uses tabs), and a file without indented lines gets the tab size setting (YAML files 2). Off, every file gets the tab size setting with spaces, as before.
- Language servers for the other languages: see "Language servers (language-servers)" below.
- Checked in the app and left as they were: Python (Pyright: completion, hover, definition, symbols, rename, signature help; ruff: format; folding; Enter after `:`); Java with JDT LS (completion, hover, definition, format, rename); JavaScript/TypeScript, JSON, HTML and CSS through Monaco's language services (completion, hover, definition where it applies, format, symbols); brackets and quotes pair in every language, and Enter between brackets or tags puts the closing one on its own line.

## Language servers (language-servers)

Plugin `language-servers` (renderer) with `main/features/language-servers.js`: code insight for C/C++, Rust, Go, Kotlin, C#, Ruby and PHP through the lsp plugin's client (`ide.lsp.createClient`, one per server, channel `lsp-<id>`): completion, errors as you type (Problems and the editor), hover, Go to Declaration (Ctrl+B), Find Usages, Rename, quick fixes and Reformat Code, as CLion, RustRover, GoLand, Rider, RubyMine and PhpStorm give them. A client may now serve several Monaco languages (`language: ['c', 'cpp']`; didOpen sends the model's own language id).

- Servers, pinned in `main/installer/toolchains.js` and installed by the Install Manager's Language Servers category (`category: 'languages'`) into `<install root>/<kind>/<folder>-<version>` like the toolchains: clangd 23.1.0 (`clangd-windows-23.1.0.zip`, 29.7 MB, GitHub's sha256), rust-analyzer 2026-10-12 (17.6 MB), JetBrains' Kotlin Language Server 263.6379.0 (363 MB, with its own Java runtime; sha256 from JetBrains' `.sha256`; started as `kotlin-lsp.cmd --stdio`), clang-format 23.1.3 (PyPI's win_amd64 wheel, 1.5 MB, PyPI's sha256). Servers that ship as packages of their language's package manager are installed with it at a pinned version (`via` in the catalog), which checks what it downloads: gopls v0.23.0 (`go install golang.org/x/tools/gopls@v0.23.0` with `GOBIN` the install folder and `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`), Ruby LSP 0.26.10 (`ruby -S gem install … --install-dir`), csharp-ls 0.27.0 (`dotnet tool install --tool-path`), Intelephense 1.18.5 (`npm install --prefix --ignore-scripts`, run with the IDE as Node). A `via` row whose package manager is not on PATH is shown unavailable ("Needs Go on PATH."); `findTool` looks for `go.exe`, `dotnet.exe`, `ruby.exe` and `node.exe` with its `npm-cli.js`, so no `.cmd` runs through a shell.
- Nothing is downloaded until the user asks: opening a file of the language shows the status item ("C/C++: not installed") and, once per session, a notification offering the download with its size (or, for a `via` server whose package manager is missing, saying to install it first, without a Download button). Download runs the Install Manager item, then the client starts. Each server starts only once a file of its language is open, only in a trusted project (Safe Mode shows "C/C++: Safe Mode"), and only from the install folder; main refuses with a code (`untrusted`, `missing`, `noruntime`).
- clangd runs with `--background-index --header-insertion=never` and fallback flags `-Wall` (a `compile_commands.json`, `compile_flags.txt` or `.clangd` of the project takes over). Reformat Code (Ctrl+Alt+L) in C and C++ uses clang-format when it is installed (`--style=file` so the project's `.clang-format` applies, else LLVM style with the file's indentation), through the tooling plugin's `ide.tooling.addFormatter(fn)` (a formatter hook asked before the language server's and Monaco's formatters; fn(tab) -> {text} | {error} | null), and clangd's own formatting otherwise.
- `ide.languageServers`: `servers()`, `client(id)`, `install(id)`. IPC: `language-servers:clangFormat(text, path, {tabSize, insertSpaces})`, `language-servers:tools`, `language-servers:installed`. Test hooks: `PYIDE_LSP_STANDIN` (a script run with the IDE as Node in place of every server), `PYIDE_CLANG_FORMAT_STANDIN`, and `tools` in `PYIDE_TOOLCHAINS` (stand-in package managers).
- Tests: `verify/language-servers.mjs` (ports 9880, 9882-9883, in CI), offline with stand-in archives, a stand-in `go` and a stand-in server: the category's rows (Ruby LSP and csharp-ls unavailable without their package managers), the offer and nothing downloaded before it is accepted, clangd installed with its pinned checksum and then completion, errors, hover, Ctrl+B and Reformat Code through it, clang-format installed and used with the fallback style, rust-analyzer, gopls installed with the pinned `go install`, Ruby's "install Ruby first", Safe Mode in an untrusted project, Korean. `verify/language-servers-live.mjs` (port 9885, opt-in, not in CI) installs the real servers into a temporary install root and checks completion, hover, errors and formatting with each (`LS_LIVE_ONLY`, `LS_LIVE_KOTLIN=1`); it has not been run in this environment (running downloaded servers was refused here).

## Emmet (emmet)

Emmet abbreviations (plugin `emmet`), as in PyCharm and VS Code, with its own expander (no npm dependency, nothing from the network). The abbreviation right before the caret expands with Tab into a Monaco snippet, with tab stops in empty attribute values and element bodies, indented with the file's indentation. HTML (`html` language id, also in the HTML viewer's embedded editor): element names, `#id`, `.class` (several), `[attr=value attr2 "quoted value"]`, `>`, `+`, `^` (climb one level per `^`), `*N`, `$` numbering (`$$` pads, `$@3` starts at 3, `$@-` counts down) by the nearest repeated element or group, `{text}`, `(groups)`, implicit tag names (`li` in `ul`/`ol`/`menu`, `tr` in `table`/`tbody`/`thead`/`tfoot`, `td` in `tr`, `option` in `select`/`optgroup`/`datalist`, `span` in inline elements, `div` elsewhere, taking the element the caret is in as the parent), `!` and `html:5` (the HTML5 boilerplate with "Document" as the first tab stop), aliases (`a` → `href=""`, `img` → `src`/`alt`, `input:text`/`checkbox`/`radio`/`password`/`email`/`hidden`/`submit`…, `link:css`, `script:src`, `meta:vp`, `meta:utf`, `form:post`, `label`, `select`, `textarea`, `bq`, `btn`, `sect`…) and `lorem`/`loremN` (deterministic placeholder text, N words). Void elements are written HTML style (`<br>`). A few inline elements and text stay on one line (`<li><a href="">x</a></li>`); block elements and three or more inline siblings get a line each. In HTML text content, as in PyCharm (VS Code expands any word), a plain word expands only when it is a known element, alias or `lorem`, or when the abbreviation starts with `#`/`.` or has operators (`> + ^ * [ { (`): "hello" or "file.txt" in a paragraph stay text. Nothing expands in a tag, an attribute value, a comment, `<!DOCTYPE>` or script content; the content of `<style>` takes CSS abbreviations. In `.jsx` and `.tsx` files (`javascript`/`typescript` models) markup expands only where JSX is expected: after `return (`, `=> (`, `? (`, `: (`, `&& (` or `|| (` (on the line or the line above) or after a JSX tag, outside strings and comments, for a lower-case HTML element, an implicit one (`.card`) or a component with operators; it writes `className` and `htmlFor`, and void elements self-close (`<br />`). A live template of the same name keeps its Tab. CSS, SCSS and Less: property abbreviations (`m`, `p`, `mt`…, `w`, `h`, `maw`, `d`, `pos`, `t`/`r`/`b`/`l`, `z`, `c`, `bg`, `bgc`, `fz`, `fw`, `lh`, `ta`, `td`, `jc`, `ai`, `fd`, `fxw`, `gap`, `gtc`, `bd`, `bdrs`, `bxsh`, `op`, `ov`, `cur`, `trs`, `trf` and more, or the full property name) with numbers (`m10` → `margin: 10px;`, `m10-20` → `10px 20px`, `m-10--20` → `-10px -20px`, `w100p` → `100%`, `1.5` → `1.5em`, `e`/`r`/`x` → em/rem/ex, `a` → auto, unitless for `z-index`, `opacity`, `font-weight`, `flex`, `order` and float line heights), colours (`c#f` → `#fff`, `#3a` → `#3a3a3a`), keywords after a colon (`d:f` → `display: flex;`, `pos:a`, `fw:b`, `ta:c`, `jc:sb`, `ov:h`, `cur:p`…) and `!` for `!important` (`m10!`). A property alone gives `name: |;` or a default (`bd` → `border: 1px solid #000;` with tab stops). Only where a declaration may start: inside a rule block (not at the top level or directly in `@media`/`@supports`/`@layer`/`@container`), after `{`, `;` or `}`, not in a selector, a value, a string or a comment (`//` too in SCSS and Less). Tab expands through a context key (`emmetAtCaret`) and `monaco.editor.addKeybindingRules`, not while the completion list shows (Tab accepts its item then), ghost text is visible, a snippet is active or Tab moves focus; Ctrl+Z gives the abbreviation back. The expansion is also a completion item (`CompletionItemKind.Snippet`, "Emmet abbreviation", preselected, a preview in its details, re-requested as the abbreviation grows); a plain word (`p`, `div`, `bd`) is listed only on Ctrl+Space, so typing prose or property names does not keep offering it. Settings › Editor › Smart Keys › Emmet: "Expand Emmet abbreviations with Tab" (`editor.emmet`, on by default). `ide.emmet.expand(abbr, {syntax: 'html' | 'jsx' | 'css', parent})` returns the snippet text for any valid abbreviation, or null. Test: `verify/emmet.mjs` (ports 9872-9873).

## Prettier and ESLint (js-tools)

`renderer/features/js-tools` and `main/features/js-tools.js` (plugin `js-tools`) use a JavaScript/TypeScript project's own Prettier and ESLint, as WebStorm and VS Code do: the packages in `node_modules/prettier` and `node_modules/eslint`, found from the file's folder up to the project root (the CLI is the package.json `bin` entry, which must stay inside the package). PyIDE never downloads them. They run with the IDE's own Electron as Node (`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, as Pyright is run), so the user needs no Node on PATH. They run only in trusted projects: the main process answers `{skipped: 'untrusted'}` otherwise, and the renderer does not ask in Safe Mode. Each run has a 30 s limit.

- Prettier: Reformat Code (Ctrl+Alt+L) in a .js/.jsx/.ts/.tsx/.mjs/.cjs/.mts/.cts/.css/.scss/.less/.json/.md/.yaml/.yml/.html/.vue file of a project that has Prettier sends the buffer on stdin with `--stdin-filepath <file>`, so its .prettierrc and .prettierignore apply. This goes through tooling's `ide.tooling.addFormatter`, asked before the language server and Monaco's formatters. The result is one undoable edit that keeps the file's own line separators, with tooling's usual hint ("Reformatted code: N lines changed" / "No lines changed"). A file Prettier cannot parse keeps its text, and the hint shows Prettier's first `[error]` line as an error ("Prettier cannot format the file: SyntaxError: … (1:17)"). With no Prettier in the project, in Safe Mode, or with the setting off, Ctrl+Alt+L falls back to the language server or Monaco's formatter as before. "Run Prettier on save" (off by default) is a save action (order 45, after ruff's) on explicit and auto saves.
- ESLint: open .js/.jsx/.ts/.tsx/.mjs/.cjs/.mts/.cts files of a project that has ESLint and an ESLint configuration are checked with `--format json --stdin --stdin-filename <file>`. Checks run when the file opens, 800 ms after typing stops (files over 1 MB only on open and save) and on save. A configuration is a flat `eslint.config.{js,mjs,cjs,ts,mts,cts}` (ESLint runs in its folder) or a legacy `.eslintrc*`/package.json `eslintConfig` (then `ESLINT_USE_FLAT_CONFIG=false`). A project with ESLint installed but no configuration is not linted. Findings go to `ide.problems` under the source `eslint`, which gives editor markers and rows in the Problems tool window: `message (rule)`, severity 2 as an error and 1 as a warning, at ESLint's line, column and end. "File ignored" notices are dropped. Quick fixes (Alt+Enter, the lightbulb): "Fix with ESLint: <message> (<rule>)" applies the message's `fix`, each of ESLint's suggestions is offered as "ESLint: <desc> (<rule>)", "Suppress <rule> for line with // eslint-disable-next-line" adds the comment above the line (or the rule to an existing one), and "Fix all ESLint problems in file" (also action `ESLintFixAll`) applies `--fix-dry-run`'s `output` as one undo step. If ESLint cannot lint at all (exit code 2: a configuration it cannot load, a crash), the findings of that file are cleared and one error notification ("ESLint cannot check files", group ESLint, with ESLint's message without its stack) is shown. It is not repeated per file or keystroke until ESLint works again or reports a different error.
- Settings › Prettier and ESLint: "Run Prettier on Reformat Code" (`jsTools.prettierOnReformat`, on), "Run Prettier on save" (`jsTools.prettierOnSave`, off), "Check JavaScript and TypeScript files with ESLint" (`jsTools.eslint`, on). `ide.jsTools` = `{lint, fixAll, prettierFormat, keys, state(path)}`.
- Test: `verify/js-tools.mjs` (ports 9876-9877, in CI). The fixture's `node_modules` hold small stand-ins for both CLIs that act like them for the flags used and log each run, so the suite runs offline. It covers .prettierrc (semi: false, tabWidth: 4), .prettierignore, one undo step, the syntax error hint, the setting off, on save, the no-unused-vars error and prefer-const warning as markers and in Problems, re-linting after typing, the fix, suppress and fix-all quick fixes, a broken eslint.config.mjs as one notification, the Monaco fallback in a project without Prettier, Safe Mode starting neither CLI, and Korean. `JS_TOOLS_LIVE=1` runs the same checks once against the real prettier@3 and eslint@9 (`JS_TOOLS_PRETTIER`/`JS_TOOLS_ESLINT` choose versions), installed with npm into `verify/out/js-tools-live` and removed afterwards. Not in CI, since it needs the network.

## Data formats (data-formats)

Data formats (plugin `data-formats`), all offline with compact hand-written schemas (JSON Schema subsets of 6-12 KB, written with small helpers in `index.js`: `str`, `en`, `obj`, `closed`, `map`, `arr`, `any`, `ci` for case-insensitive values). JSON goes through Monaco's JSON service (`monaco.json.jsonDefaults` in Monaco 0.57; `setDiagnosticsOptions` merges in its schemas, `fileMatch` against the model's `file:///` URI). package.json gets name, version, scripts with the lifecycle names, dependencies/devDependencies/peerDependencies as name→range maps, engines, bin, files, exports, workspaces, private, license, author, repository and `type` (`commonjs`/`module`). tsconfig.json, tsconfig.*.json, jsconfig.json and jsconfig.*.json get compilerOptions (target, module, moduleResolution and lib with their values, case-insensitive like tsc, plus jsx, the strict flags, paths, baseUrl, outDir…), include/exclude/files/extends/references. Comments and trailing commas are allowed through the schema's `allowComments`/`allowTrailingCommas`, so the files stay `json` with no errors for them. .prettierrc/.prettierrc.json and .eslintrc/.eslintrc.json get their options too. `.prettierrc` and `.eslintrc`, which have no extension, open as JSON when they start with `{` and as YAML otherwise. Monaco has no YAML service, so `yaml.js` reads YAML itself, line by line: block mappings and sequences by indentation, with plain, quoted and block (`|`, `>`) scalars and flow collections each kept whole. That reading drives completion, hover and validation for GitHub Actions workflows (`.github/workflows/*.yml|yaml`: on and its events and filters, permissions, env, concurrency, jobs with runs-on, needs, if, strategy/matrix, container, services, steps with uses, run, with, shell…), Docker Compose files (`compose.yaml`, `docker-compose.yml`, `compose.*.yaml`…: services with image, build, ports, volumes, environment, depends_on, networks, command, healthcheck, deploy, restart…, top-level networks/volumes/configs/secrets, `x-` extension keys) and YAML .prettierrc/.eslintrc. Completion works out the key path at the caret from indentation and `- ` items. It lists the keys allowed at that level, minus the ones already there, with their descriptions, inserting `key: `, `key:` plus an indented line, or `key:` plus `- `. After `key: ` it lists enum values and suggestions (runner labels, common actions), quoting words YAML would read as booleans (`restart: "no"`). Hover shows a key's description, allowed values and default. Validation adds warnings (owner `data-formats`) for keys a closed mapping does not have and for values outside an enum or pattern. `${{ }}` expressions, block scalar text and `<<` merge keys are never checked. Reformat Code (Ctrl+Alt+L, through Monaco `DocumentFormattingEditProvider`s for `yaml` and `xml`): YAML indentation is normalized to the model's indent size, including list items under a key and `-   x` → `- x`. The formatter writes `key: value` with one space after the colon and none before it (`key:value` only where a key must be and the line is otherwise invalid YAML), drops trailing blanks and leaves one final newline. Comments, quoted and flow values, document markers and block scalar content keep their text exactly, including trailing blanks and relative indentation; block content moves to one level under its key. Files it cannot handle safely are left untouched with the reason: tab indentation, `? ` complex keys, explicit indentation indicators that would move, or any result that does not read back as the same keys and values. XML is printed one element per line with the model's indentation, keeping the declaration, processing instructions, DOCTYPE, comments, CDATA, text-only elements, start tags exactly as written (attributes on the line, in their order), self-closing tags, and the whole content of mixed-content and `xml:space="preserve"` elements. One blank line between siblings is kept, and XML that is not well-formed is refused with its line. Test: `verify/data-formats.mjs` (ports 9878-9879).

## Auto save and actions on save

The core save path (`renderer/core/editors.js`) runs save actions on every save and offers `ide.autoSave(paths?)`; the `autosave` plugin decides when to call it, as PyCharm's System Settings › Autosave does.

- Triggers (Settings › Appearance & Behavior › System Settings, all on by default): the window losing focus to another application or focus entering a built-in terminal (`autosave.onFrameDeactivation`; a blur only counts when `document.hasFocus()` is false, so an iframe taking focus does not, and test instances, which run with CDP focus emulation, never auto-save on a real OS blur); switching to another editor tab or pane saves the tab left behind (`autosave.onTabSwitch`); no key, mouse or text change for `autosave.idleSeconds` (default 15) saves everything (`autosave.onIdle`); Run, Debug and tests (which go through `ide.run.runConfig`) save all first (`autosave.beforeRun`).
- `ide.autoSave` skips untitled documents (`doc.untitled`, or a model that is not `file:`), read-only ones (`doc.readOnly`), documents open only in a viewer tab, a version whose auto save already failed, and files it cannot read or whose text on disk changed since PyIDE read or wrote it. Each document keeps `diskHash`, a fingerprint of that text, updated on load, save, `ide.reloadFromDisk`, a save from another window and Reload in an encoding. A skipped file emits `autoSaveSkipped({path, reason: 'changedOnDisk' | 'unreadable' | 'failed', error?})`, and the plugin shows it in the status bar (`#autosaveStatus`), whose menu offers Save (overwrite) or Reload from disk. Auto save never shows an error dialog; an explicit save still writes a changed file and still alerts on failure. Saves of one document are serialized.
- Save actions (Settings › Actions on Save): `ide.registerSaveAction({id, order, run(text, ctx) -> text | undefined})` with `ctx = {doc, path, auto, eol, modifiedLines, caretLines}`. They run on explicit and auto saves, in order, on the text; the result goes into the model as line-level edits (a Myers line diff) between two undo stops, so one Ctrl+Z reverts all of them and carets outside changed lines stay put. If the text changes while they run, an auto save is dropped and an explicit save writes the text as it is. Built in: strip trailing whitespace on modified lines (order 10, `save.stripTrailingSpaces`, on; lines edited since the last save are tracked as whole-line decorations; caret lines keep their spaces), ensure a final line break in the model's EOL (20, `save.ensureFinalNewline`, off), and from the tooling plugin optimize imports (30, `tools.optimizeImportsOnSave`, off) and ruff reformat (40, `tools.formatOnSave`, off). A Python file ruff cannot parse is saved unformatted.
- Settings pages from plugins may now use `group: 'appearance'` to sit under Appearance & Behavior.
- Test: `verify/autosave.mjs` (ports 9960-9961): defaults on the System Settings page, a window blur, a tab switch and a 1 s idle timer each write the file (and not with their setting off), untitled, read-only and in-memory buffers stay unwritten, a file changed on disk is not overwritten and shows in the status bar, each save action applies only when on (and also on auto save), Ctrl+S keeps the caret and one Ctrl+Z reverts all actions, and the Korean pages and warning.

## Crash recovery (hot exit)

`renderer/features/recovery` + `main/features/recovery.js`, like VS Code's hot exit. Every window (the plugin is
`detached: true`) backs up each modified document 2 s after the last edit (10 s for documents over 2 million
characters) and drops the backup when the document is saved, reverted, closed without saving or undone back to its
saved text; a 5 s sweep also catches a document marked saved without an edit (another window saved it, a plugin set
`tab.savedVersion`). Documents are found through Monaco's model events and `ide.getDocument`, so documents held by
viewer and compare tabs are covered too. Each PyIDE process writes to its own session folder,
`<userData>/backups/<pid>-<start ms>/`, made again on the next backup or within 10 s if something deletes it (the
windows are then sent `recovery:lost` and write every backup they hold again). Three failed backup writes in a row show
one error notification ("Unsaved changes are not being backed up", group Crash Recovery; counted per window), and a
successful write resets the count. `session.json` (`{pid, beat}`, written atomically every 10 s) is kept only for
PyIDE 0.2.6 and older running beside it on the same user data folder, which judge session folders by it. A backup file is
named by the SHA-1 of the document's identity (its lower-case path, or `untitled:<name>`) and holds one JSON line
(`path, name, untitled, project, language, encoding, lineSeparator, diskHash, asText, time`), a line break and the text;
it is written to `<file>.tmp` and renamed over. Windows editing the same document write the same file, and the main
process removes it only when no window holds it any more, so closing a detached window without saving keeps the
backup the main window still needs. A clean quit (`will-quit`, after every save prompt was answered) removes the
session folder; backups of a window whose renderer crashed are kept through it.

A session folder whose process is gone (pid not running, the same pid as this process, or a session that started more
than 10 min before this computer booted, which covers a reused pid) is a crash; a running process's folder is never
offered or removed, however old its heartbeat. With Windows Fast Startup a shutdown is a hibernation that keeps the boot
time, so a pid reused after one counts as running until that program exits (its backups are kept, not lost). Listing
reads only each backup's metadata line (the first 64 KB; `recovery:text` reads the text on Restore), and deletes dead
sessions' backups older than 30 days or of a project folder that no longer exists (a project on a drive that is not
connected, or on a UNC share, keeps them). A crashed session's backups are keyed by project (`project` is the main window's root when the
backup was written): the main window offers those of the project it opens (at startup once boot finished, then on
every `projectOpened`) plus those made with no project open; other projects' backups stay until their project opens.
A document backed up by several dead sessions is offered once, newest text. The dialog ("Restore Unsaved Changes",
`#recoveryDialog`) lists the files: Restore opens each with the backed-up text as an unsaved change on top of the disk
version (`ide.openFile(path, {text})`), keeps its line separator, and when the file changed on disk since the backup
puts the backup's `diskHash` back and sets `doc.conflict`, so an explicit save asks (File Cache Conflict) and auto save
skips it. Untitled documents and files that no longer exist come back in a new scratch file. The restored documents
are backed up by the new session before the old files are deleted. Discard deletes the backups; Esc keeps them for
the next start. After Restore or Discard the keyboard goes to the active tab, or to the Project view when that tab
cannot take it (the Workspace page). Settings › Appearance & Behavior › Crash Recovery (`recovery.backup`, on by default) turns backups off
