(and drops the current ones). IPC: `recovery:hello`, `recovery:project`, `recovery:put`, `recovery:drop`,
`recovery:pending` (metadata and a `ref` per document, no text), `recovery:text(ref)`, `recovery:consume` (refs checked
against `<session>/<sha1>.backup`), and the event `recovery:lost`. Test: `verify/recovery.mjs` (ports 9060-9064, 9105):
debounce, metadata, save/undo/close remove the backup, one backup across a detached window, hard kill
(`taskkill /F /T`), Restore, a conflict after a disk change, Discard, focus after both, the setting, a clean quit,
Korean, then at 1008x681 the session folder deleted and made unwritable while running (backups written again, one
notification). `verify/recovery-main.mjs` (plain Node with a stand-in `ipcMain`, in CI): folder recreation, which
sessions are over, aging out, the metadata-only listing (four 40 MB backups of another project), `recovery:text` and
its ref check.

## Find in Files and Replace in Files

`renderer/features/search-nav` and `main/features/search.js` (matching runs on a worker). A regex is matched line by line unless it can match a line break (`\n`, `\r`, `\W` or a typed line break, Monaco's `isMultilineRegexSource`), on text whose `\r\n` are `\n`, so the preview and Replace in Files find what the editor's Ctrl+R finds (`\s+$` no longer eats a CRLF file's line breaks). Replacing copies the text between matches from the original unchanged (mixed separators survive), writes a line break the replacement adds with the file's own separator (CRLF when most of its line breaks are CRLF), and keeps a UTF-8 BOM out of reach of `^\s+`; unsaved buffers (`search:replaceText`) go through the same code. A regex with no match costs ~23% more than before on large projects (slicing lines). `search:replace` answers `{changed, count, skipped}`: `skipped` lists the files the preview showed but Replace left as they were (`reason` `tooLarge`, `binary`, `encoding` when the file's encoding cannot hold the new text, checked before writing, or `error` with the fs `code` and message), so one read-only file no longer aborts the files after it, and the Find window's summary names them ("Not replaced in N files: kr949.py (CP949 cannot store the new text), ro.py (Access is denied …)").

Files are read 64 ahead of the one being matched, on a libuv thread pool of up to 16 threads (main.js sets `UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE` from the CPU count before any file work and takes it out of the environment again, so programs the IDE starts do not inherit it; one the user set is kept): opening a file costs 250 µs with a busy virus scanner, and Find in Path over perf.mjs's 20,000 files went from 8.6 s to 0.6 s, the TODO scan from 0.68 s to 0.45 s.

Find in Files keeps the text it read on the search worker (`textCache` in main/features/search.js: path -> mtime, size and text, oldest dropped past 32 M characters): every search stats each file and reads again only those whose mtime or size changed, so a change the project watcher misses is still found, and `search:invalidate` drops named paths or everything. Misses are read 16 at a time ahead of the file being matched (callback `fs.open`/`fs.read`; inside Electron's main-process worker `fs.promises.readFile` made a first search take 5-15 s) and decoded once. On a 6,136-file project a first search takes ~0.35 s and later ones and the dialog's live previews ~0.15 s (before: ~0.9 s inside the app and ~1.4 s from typing to the count in the dialog). Folders marked Excluded are not walked unless one is chosen as the search directory (see Source roots (Mark Directory As)). Test: `verify/search-cache.mjs` (port 9431).

- Test: `verify/tool-failures.mjs` (ports 9278-9279; fixture `verify/fixtures/tool-failures/`) runs two instances, one normal and one with a PATH without Git and an OSV server that answers 500; each item is its own section. It covers Replace in Files on CRLF files and the skipped files, a project folder denied with `icacls` (removed in `finally`), launchers in folders named `R&D^up%PATH%x` and `R&D 100% ^up 'q' 한글`, missing Git and a damaged repository, an unreadable ruff.toml, and the OSV error kinds. The no-Git part needs `git` on the test process's own PATH (it runs `git init`).

## Search Everywhere

`renderer/features/search-everywhere` (plugin `search-everywhere`, depends on `search-nav`) replaces the core `SearchEverywhere` action, which without the plugin falls back to Go to File. Double Shift (two Shift keydowns less than 350 ms apart by the events' own timestamps, with no other key between them; core's keymap detects it), the toolbar search button and Navigate › Search Everywhere open a popup in `#dialogHost`, so the global keymap is suspended while it is open. Tabs: All, Classes, Files, Symbols, Actions, Settings; Tab / Shift+Tab cycle them, Up/Down move, Enter opens the file, jumps to the symbol, runs the action or opens the settings page, and Esc or a click outside closes it. The last query comes back selected. `ide.searchEverywhere.open({tab, query})` opens it from code.

- Providers are reused, not copied. search-nav exposes `ide.searchNav`: `match(name, q, {fuzzy})` (its Go to Class matcher, with exact, prefix, CamelHumps/snake_humps, substring and `*`, returning `{score, ranges}` for highlighting; `fuzzy` adds every word of a multi-word query, then an in-order subsequence from 3 characters), `rankSymbols(list, q, limit)` and `symbols()` (the Go to Class/Symbol index, including `pkg.Name` scoping), `recentFiles()`, `kindIcon`, and `goTo`. Files come from the core project walk (`ide.api.walk`, the same as Go to File), actions from `ide.actions` (titles and shortcuts), and settings pages from the settings plugin's `ide.settingsPageList()` (built-in pages and every page in `ide.settingsPages`, with their group and `keywords`; a keyword match ranks below a title match and shows the keyword).
- All lists Classes, Files, Symbols (non-class), Actions and Settings groups, 5 rows each, plus a "more" row that switches to that tab with the query kept. An empty query shows Recent Files (without the active file). `name.py:12[:5]` opens a file at a line.
- Large projects: the walk loads once per popup, input is debounced 120 ms, each keystroke drops the answers to older text, file matching yields every 4,000 files, tabs show at most 100 rows, and groups appear as their providers answer, so a symbol index that is still being built never holds back files. An Enter typed before the debounced search runs waits for that search. The symbol index lists files on a worker of its own (not behind the TODO scan or Find in Files) and parses up to 4 batches of 400 files at once; a batch Python could not finish is not cached, so its files are parsed again at the next build instead of being remembered as having no symbols. The window keeps the index between popups: `search:symbolIndex(interp, root, since)` answers `{version, full, files: [[path, symbols]], removed}` with only the files whose symbols changed after the window's version (none for a second popup or a save inside a function body; a deleted file comes back in `removed`), so a later popup waits for the walk (~60 ms) instead of 48,000 symbols over IPC (~300 ms); `ide.searchNav.symbols()` still returns the flat list. Each project's index is kept in `userData/symbol-index` (compact, checked per file by mtime and by the Python that parsed it, implementation and major.minor, so a file that an older Python could not parse is read again under a newer one; ignored when the index script changes; 20 projects kept; each process writes through its own temporary file), so a reopened project has its symbols without Python: 3,000 modules 0.45 s instead of 1.5 s. On `interpreterChanged` a window that holds the project's index asks for it again at once. The index script reads each batch on 16 threads: a project whose files are new (just cloned or generated) waits at each file's first read for the virus scanner, and the threads overlap those waits (first open 7.0 s -> 4.2 s). Go to Class/Symbol shows "Indexing…" instead of "Nothing found" until the index answers (`ide.searchNav.indexing()`), and the TODO list is scanned only while its window is shown (its first show takes ~650 ms instead of ~60 ms). 3,000 modules: classes 4.2 s after a project of new files opens (0.2.6: 18.9 s), 0.45 s after a reopen, 1.2 s for a later rebuild (before: 3.2 s). While `ide.searchNav.indexing()` is true and Classes or Symbols has not answered yet, Search Everywhere shows a non-selectable "Indexing…" row (`.se-indexing`, 색인 중…) where those groups will appear. Test: `verify/perf-index.mjs` (ports 9184-9185, not in CI: it times things), which also checks the Search Everywhere row during a rebuild and times a later popup (< 150 ms) and a reopen (< 1 s); `verify/search-nav.mjs` checks that a failed batch is not cached (through `search:symbols`, the flat list, kept for that check). `verify/symbol-index.mjs` (port 9066, in CI, no timing): the first popup receives the whole index, a second popup and Search Everywhere receive nothing, a saved class comes back as its file alone, a body-only save sends nothing, a deleted file is removed, the store holds the saved class and not the deleted file, a reopened project lists its classes with an interpreter that does not exist, and a module changed while closed is parsed again; a file in 3.12 syntax indexed by a stand-in Python 3.11 is parsed again when the index is asked with the current Python, and after a restart Go to Class finds its class; `--lang ko --viewport 1008x681` also checks the popup's kind column and missing translations.
- Test: `verify/search-everywhere.mjs` (ports 9950-9951): a single Shift, two Shifts 500 ms apart and Shift, Shift+A, Shift do not open it, Double Shift does, every tab finds and acts on its kind (file, class by humps, function, an action that runs, a settings page by keyword), All's groups and "more", recent files, the toolbar button and menu entry, 12,000 files (capped, debounced, no long task), and Korean.

## HTTP client

`renderer/features/http-client` (plugin `http-client`) and `main/features/http-client.js` add JetBrains' HTTP Client for `.http` and `.rest` files.

- Format: requests are separated by `###` lines (the rest of the line is the request's title). Before a request line come `#` / `//` comments, `# @name x` and `@var = value` lines; the request line is `METHOD URL [HTTP/1.1]` (a URL alone means GET), optionally continued by indented `?a=1` / `&b=2` lines, then headers, an empty line and the body. `< ./file.json` sends a file as the body (`<@ file` also substitutes variables in it, and `< file` lines inside a text body are spliced in). Response handler scripts (`> {% %}`), `>> file` and `<> file` lines are skipped. Multipart bodies have no special support. `ide.httpClient.parse(text)` returns `{vars, requests}`.
- Highlighting: a Monarch language `http` (states root, headers, body) colors separators, comments, `# @name`, `@var` lines, methods, header names, `{{var}}` everywhere and JSON bodies; Monarch never sees empty lines, so the first line after the request line that is not a header starts the body. Response JSON is colored by the same rules (language `http-json`) through `monaco.editor.colorize`.
- Running: a ▶ glyph (left lane) on every request line and Ctrl+Enter (the request containing the caret) send it to `http-client:send`. Main resolves the variables and sends with Node's `fetch`, so there are no CORS limits, following redirects, with `httpClient.timeoutSeconds` (default 60). Failures come back as `{ok: false, error: {kind: refused | dns | reset | timeout | url | file | other, target, detail}}`, which the renderer turns into a translated sentence ("Connection refused: nothing is listening at 127.0.0.1:9997.").
- Variables, resolved in main: `@var` lines above the request (their values may use other variables), then the selected environment, then `{{$uuid}}` (also `$random.uuid`), `{{$timestamp}}`, `{{$isoTimestamp}}`, `{{$randomInt}}` (0-999) and `{{$random.integer(a, b)}}`, then `{{name.response.body.field}}` / `{{name.response.body.$.items[0].id}}` / `{{name.response.headers.Location}}` from the last response of the request named `name` in this project (kept in main memory). Unknown names are sent as written and listed above the response as unresolved.
- Environments: `http-client.env.json` and `http-client.private.env.json` in the nearest folder from the request file up to the project root; private values override public ones, and `$shared` applies to every environment. Every private value is added to the log scrubber (`addSecretValues`) as soon as the file is read, and requests are never logged, so private values never reach the IDE log. Only environment names reach the renderer (`http-client:envs`); values appear there only inside a sent request shown in the panel. The environment is chosen per project (setting `httpClient.env.<root>`) in the tool window's selector, the status bar item "Env: …" shown for `.http` files, or the action HttpClient.SelectEnvironment.
- The HTTP Client tool window (bottom, order 68, Services icon) lists the last `httpClient.historySize` (default 50) responses of the session, newest first, and shows the selected one: method and URL, status, time, size and environment, then Body (JSON pretty-printed and colored, HTML as colored source with a sandboxed preview, images previewed, XML colored, other text as is, binary as its size), Headers and Request (the request as sent, with variables resolved). Double-clicking a history row or Go to Request jumps to the request.
- File › New HTTP Request (`NewHttpRequest`, also in the project tree's context menu) creates a `.http` file with an example request. The core already gives `.http` and `.rest` the HTTP file icon.
- Frameworks: `ide.frameworks.routeActions` (new) adds items to every route menu (view gutter icons, Endpoints); the HTTP client adds Generate Request in HTTP Client, which appends `### METHOD path` and `METHOD <route URL>` (with a JSON `Content-Type` and `{}` for POST, PUT and PATCH) to `generated-requests.http` in the project root and opens it there.
- Test: `verify/http-client.mjs` (IDE ports 9990-9991; local servers on 9995 and 9996 stand in for the dev and prod environments, nothing listens on 9997) checks highlighting, the gutter icons, GET through the gutter and POST with a JSON body through Ctrl+Enter, the status/time/size and pretty JSON in the panel, headers, `{{host}}` from the env file and switching dev to prod, a private env value, `{{$uuid}}` and `{{$timestamp}}`, named responses in a later request, `< ./payload.json`, HTML and image responses, a refused connection's message, the history, the log scrubber, New HTTP Request, the frameworks route action, and the Korean tool window.

## Spell checker

`renderer/features/spellcheck` and `main/features/spellcheck.js` (plugin `spellcheck`) add PyCharm's Typo inspection, offline.

- Dictionary: Hunspell en_US from `dictionary-en` 4.0.0 (SCOWL, "(MIT AND BSD)", 0.55 MB) read by `nspell` 2.1.5 (MIT, 42 KB). Both are plain dependencies, so electron-builder packs them inside `app.asar`. They run on a worker thread (`main/worker-host.js` `fileWorker`, role `pyide-spellcheck`) that reads them through Electron's asar support. The dictionary loads on the first check (about 120 ms, on the worker). IPC: `spellcheck:check(words)` returns the typos in a batch of lower-case words (a word the dictionary only knows capitalized, such as english, is accepted), `spellcheck:suggest(word, limit)` returns nspell's suggestions re-ranked so a swapped pair or a missing double letter comes first (nmae → name, coment → comment), and `spellcheck:read/writeProjectDictionary(root)`.
- What is checked: Python comments, docstrings and string literals (Monaco's comment and string tokens), and the names a Python line defines (def and class names, parameters, assignment targets including `self.x`, `for` and `as` names), not every name it uses. Also Markdown text outside fenced blocks, inline code, link targets and HTML tags (also in the Markdown viewer's embedded editor), and the Git commit message. Identifiers and prose tokens are split on snake_case, camelCase and digits, and each part is checked.
- Never flagged: parts under 3 letters, ALLCAPS, hex (`0x1f`, `deadbeef42`), URLs, e-mail addresses, paths, `%s` placeholders, any token holding a non-ASCII letter (Hangul and other scripts, accented words), Python keywords and builtins, a list of programming words (args, kwargs, json, http, utf, stdout…), and names that open Python files import or read as attributes of something other than `self`/`cls` (numpy's `linspace` and the like). These are harvested from the whole file when it is first checked and again on save.
- Checking: only lines in view (plus 10 lines above and below) of editors showing a Python or Markdown model, in `requestIdleCallback` slices of at most 8 ms, 300 ms after typing stops (150 ms after scrolling). Verdicts are cached per word, so only new words cross IPC. Each model keeps every checked line's typos. An edit shifts the lines below and rechecks only the changed ones, and a token change (an opened `"""`) marks lines dirty, keeping their typos until they are rechecked. Group editors and editors embedded by viewers are both tracked, since `ide.editors()` lists only the former.
- Display: Monaco markers with owner `spellcheck`, `MarkerSeverity.Hint` and code `typo`, message "Typo: In word 'x'". Problems sources use error, warning and info, and Pyright's hints carry the Unnecessary or Deprecated tags, so only typos are plain hints. style.css turns `.squiggly-hint` into a green wave (#6a9f62). Typos are not listed in Problems. An unchanged marker set is not published again, because a marker change restarts an open Alt+Enter request in Monaco.
- Quick fixes (Alt+Enter, the lightbulb): "Change to 'x'" for up to 5 suggestions (one undo step), "Save 'x' to project dictionary" and "Save 'x' to user dictionary". The action `Spelling.ShowActions` takes Alt+Enter only while the caret is on a typo in a `file:` editor or in the commit message box. In the editor it opens Monaco's quick-fix menu, so Python's Pyright actions appear too. In the commit box it opens a context menu. Notebook cells keep their own Alt+Enter.
- Commit message: the vcs textarea gets an overlay with the same metrics (`.spell-overlay`, pointer-events none) that draws wavy underlines under the typos. A replacement goes through `execCommand('insertText')`, so the textarea's undo keeps working.
- Dictionaries: the project dictionary is `<project>/.pyide/dictionary.txt` (one lower-case word per line, sorted; saving that file in the editor reloads it), and the user dictionary is the setting `spellcheck.userWords`. Settings › Editor › Spelling has the switch (`spellcheck.enabled`, default on; off clears every marker and underline) and both word lists. The project list goes through the transient setting `spellcheck.projectWordsEdit`, so OK/Apply/Cancel cover it; it is written to the file and cleared. `ide.spellcheck`: `enabled()`, `markers(model)`, `suggest(word)`, `saveWord(word, 'project'|'user')`, `commitTypos()`, `checkedLines(model)`, `at(model, line, col)`, `stats()`, `idle()`.
- Test: `verify/spellcheck.mjs` (ports 9980-9981; fixture `verify/fixtures/spellcheck`) checks the typo comment and `def get_usr_nmae()` with their suggestions and the green wave, Alt+Enter › Change to and its undo, zero markers on correct code with Korean comments, URLs, e-mails, hex, paths and ALLCAPS, Markdown (code blocks, inline code and link targets skipped; Alt+Enter in the viewer), Save to project and user dictionary, both persisting after a reload, the Settings page (switch off clears markers, project list written to the file), the commit message underline and fix, and typing 200 characters (on this machine the longest checking slice was 1.7 ms, with no long tasks and at most 7 ms of timer lag). A second instance checks the Korean marker message and Settings page. Monaco can restart an Alt+Enter request as an automatic one when Pyright publishes diagnostics mid-request. The test then presses Alt+Enter again.

## Auto-test

The tests plugin adds PyCharm's Toggle Auto-test (`renderer/features/tests/index.js`). Each test tab in the Run tool window has a ↻ toggle in the window's toolbar (`data-action="autoTest"`, pressed while on), and the action `ToggleAutoTest` (Find Action) flips it for the active test tab. It is off by default, kept on the session object only, so it never survives a restart, and closing the tab ends it. While it is on, the tab shows an "Auto-test" mark (`.run-tab-autotest`), and saving a `.py`/`.pyw` file under the project root (any `fileSaved`, explicit or auto save) re-runs that tab's last run (the same targets, e.g. only the failed tests after Rerun Failed Tests, and the same executor) `tests.autoTestDelay` ms after the last save (default 1000; Settings › Python Integrated Tools › Testing). `fileSaved` fires only for documents that were really written, so idle and focus-loss auto saves without changes run nothing, and several saves inside the delay make one run. A run that is still starting or running in the tab is stopped and waited for (its exit hook), then one run starts; saves during that wait fold into it, so runs never overlap or queue more than one. Saves made by the auto-test run's own Save All are ignored, any run that starts in the tab cancels a pending timer, saves while a debug session is active (`debugSessionChanged`) do not trigger (any debug session counts, also a debugged test run), and the editor keeps the keyboard. Opening or closing a project turns auto-test off in every kept Run tab (and clears pending timers), and nothing auto-runs while `ide.trusted === false`. `ide.tests.autoTest`: `enabled(session?)`, `set(on, session?)`. Test: `verify/auto-test.mjs` (ports 9150-9151, default interpreter's pytest as in run-tools; the fixture's `conftest.py` logs each session and notes overlapping ones): off by default, toggle and tab mark, break → failure, fix → pass, six rapid saves → one run, an idle auto save → one run, a save during a 5 s run stops it and runs once, no overlaps, no trigger during a debug session, for a non-Python file, after toggling off through Find Action or after closing the tab, after opening another project (toggle off, a save there runs nothing) or in an untrusted one, after Close Project, the settings default, and the Korean UI.

The tests plugin also adds the project tree's Run 'pytest in <name>' ('<name>의 pytest' 실행) and, beside it, Debug 'pytest in <name>' (see Debugging tests), offered on test files (`test_*.py`, `*_test.py`) and on folders with a test file at any depth, the project folder included; it runs `pytest in <name>` with that folder or file as the target. The set of such folders comes from a project walk (`ide.api.walk`, on a worker) when the project opens and 0.5 s after each `filesChanged`, so a new test file adds the entry to its folders and deleting the last one removes it. The tree reads an action's `title` right after its `when`, so `title` is a getter naming the entry `when` just saw. Test: `verify/tests-tree-menu.mjs` (ports 9106-9107, in CI; fixture `verify/fixtures/tests-tree-menu`: labels, folders without tests, created and deleted test files, the run's target, Korean, the label inside a 1008x681 window); `verify/run-tools.mjs` and `verify/i18n-run-tools.mjs` check the entry's label too.

## Debugging tests

The pytest configuration type has a `debugTarget` (renderer/features/tests/index.js): `main/features/tests.js` `tests:debugTarget(interp, opts)` prepares the same run as `tests:start` (report file, `PYIDE_TEST_REPORT`, the helpers, the project and its source roots on PYTHONPATH, the .env files merged) without starting it and returns `{id, script, params, cwd, env, command}`: `-m pytest -p pyide_pytest_plugin …`, or the unittest runner when the interpreter has no pytest. The debugger starts it (`ide.debugger.debugFile`), so breakpoints in test files and in the code under test are hit and the output is in the Debug window. Entry points: the test gutter's Debug item, the project tree's Debug 'pytest in <name>' ('<name>의 pytest' 디버그, on the same entries as Run), the toolbar Debug button on a test file, Shift+F9 on a test file with Current File selected or with a pytest configuration selected (`DebugTests`, registered on `ready` ahead of the debugger's `Debug`, only there), Alt+Shift+F9, Run Anything's pytest commands, and Rerun (the Debug window's Rerun) and Rerun Failed Tests, which debug again (Rerun Failed Tests only the failed tests). The Test Results show in a Test Results sub-tab of the debug session in the Debug window, after Threads & Variables and Console, as in PyCharm (filled from the report every 250 ms, `tests:poll` with the `debug-N` id; ◷/✓/✗/⊘ on the sub-tab; Rerun Failed Tests above the tree). The debugger's API for it: `ide.debugger.debugFile(file, {…, tabs: [{id, title, select?, mount(host, tab) -> {dispose?}}]})`; a tab belongs to that session, stays after it ends and is removed (dispose) when the next session starts (debugFile or Attach to Process), `select` shows it at the start (a breakpoint still switches to Threads & Variables), and `tab` is `{show(), setIcon(glyph, cls)}`. `run.debugConfig` hands the target's `tabs` to `debugFile`, so the pytest type's `debugTarget` returns `{script, cwd, params, env, tabs, started}` like the other types (`rerun` debugs the configuration again), and `started(p)` gets debugFile's promise. When its own debug session ends (`{ended}`, a promise of `{replaced}`) the results run ends: final poll and release of the report, the exit status from the report's `done` event, the final state and the gutter icons, and the Debug window shows the Test Results; a session that another one replaced takes its sub-tab with it. A plain test run keeps its Run window tab; the Run window's `Rerun` action does not rerun a debug test run (the Debug window's Rerun does). Contract: `debugTarget(cfg, interp, opts)` -> `{script, cwd, params, env, interp?, interpOptions?, tabs?, started?}` or null to cancel; `debugConfig(cfg, opts)` passes `opts` (`{targets}`).

- A test run whose report has no exit status from the runner (pytest never got to its end: Stop, or for a debug run another debug session started in its place) reads "Stopped. …" (중지됨. …) with the Terminated icon on the tab and the status (class `stopped`), unless a test failed, and the tests that did not run show as ignored. This holds for plain test runs too, which showed Passed before.
- Known limit: remote interpreters should get the report on fd 3 as for a plain run (`remote.startPython` maps `PYIDE_TEST_REPORT`); not tested on a remote target.
- Test: `verify/tests-debug-run.mjs` (ports 9053-9054, in CI): breakpoints with Ctrl+F8 in a test and in the code under test; the gutter's Debug, Shift+F9 on the file, the Debug window's Rerun and Stop, the Test Results sub-tab's Rerun Failed Tests (all in the Debug window, no Run window tab), the tree's Run and Debug entries; results, failed gutter icons, Stop; a stopped run shown as stopped, and a run replaced by another debug session (`slow.py`) removing its sub-tab while the Debug window stays on the new session; the Module name and rerun-question checks below; 1008x681; and Korean. `verify/tests-debug-target.mjs` (pure Node, no window, in CI): the `tests:start` command line unchanged, `tests:debugTarget`'s script, params, env, .env files and report, a real pytest run's report through `tests:poll` with pytest's exit status, the report folder released, and the unittest runner's target and report.

## Python configurations: Script path or Module name

The Python type's form starts with Run: (`runMode`: Script path or Module name, a `kind: 'select'` field; fields may have `shown(cfg)` and `placeholderFor(cfg)`). Module name (`module`) runs `python -u -m <module> <params>` through `run:start` (the script slot takes `-m`), debugs the same way, and defaults the working directory to the project root (Script path keeps the script's folder); PYTHONPATH and .env handling are those of scripts. Configurations without `runMode` are scripts. `ide.run.resolvePython` returns `{script: '-m', params: [module, …]}` for modules and `missing` when the script or module is empty (Run prints, Debug shows, "… is not specified. Edit the configuration first."). Test: `verify/tests-debug-run.mjs` (the form, the field swap and cwd placeholder, the stored `runMode`/`module`, `-m pkg.hello` run from the project root with quoted params, debugging it with Alt+Shift+F9, the empty-module message).

## Source roots (Mark Directory As)

PyCharm's Mark Directory As. `main/project-roots.js` keeps per project `{sources, tests, excluded}` (folders relative to the project root) in `<userData>/project-roots.json`, keyed by the lower-cased root (user data, like run configurations; re-read when another instance wrote it, written atomically). Without a record a project gets the src layout's root: `src/` without `__init__.py` that holds Python code within its first 200 folders, breadth first: a module, a package or a namespace package such as `src/acme/tools/helper.py` (`*.egg-info`, `__pycache__`, `node_modules` and dot folders do not count). The required plugin `project-roots` ("Project Structure", `renderer/features/project-roots`, `main/features/project-roots.js`) shows it: when a project without a record opens with such a `src`, `project-roots:get` writes it down as the record (`detected: 'src'`) and PyCharm's notification "'src' has been marked as source root" offers Ok, Revert (unmarks it, and the record keeps it unmarked) and Don't show again (hides the group "Python source root detection"). The project tree's context menu has Mark Directory As › Sources Root, Test Sources Root, Excluded, right after New ›; a marked folder offers Unmark as Sources Root / Unmark as Test Sources Root / Cancel Exclusion instead of its own kind, a folder has one kind at a time, and the project root cannot be excluded (`project-roots:mark(root, folder, kind | null)` refuses it and anything outside the project). The tree draws PyCharm's New UI folder icons for them (blue sources root, green test sources root, orange excluded; types `sourceRoot`, `testRoot`, `excludeRoot`, added to the file icon table by the plugin) through a tree decorator's `iconType`.

Who reads the roots: `withPath` in `main/features/run.js` puts each root it is given (the project root) followed by its sources roots and then its test sources roots (`projectRoots.withSources`) on PYTHONPATH, so Run, Run Anything, frameworks, visual scripts and the test runner (`tests:start`, `tests:debugTarget`) import from them; Debug of a file (`debug:start`) and the Python Console (`pyide_repl.py`'s sys.path) do the same. Pyright gets `autoSearchPaths: false` and `extraPaths` = the same folders, so the editor resolves imports exactly as the runtime does (after Revert both report `mypkg`), and when folders are excluded `exclude` = Pyright's defaults (`**/node_modules`, `**/__pycache__`, `**/.*`) plus those folders and the project's virtualenvs (`projectRoots.venvDirs`: folders holding pyvenv.cfg, conda-meta, Scripts/activate or bin/activate, four levels down, not inside excluded folders), which Pyright leaves out by itself (autoExcludeVenv) only while `exclude` is unset; a change reaches the running server as `workspace/didChangeConfiguration`. Excluded folders also leave Go to File (`fs:walk`), the semantic index, Find and Replace in Files, the TODO window, the symbol index of Go to Symbol / Go to Class and code vision's word counts (main passes `excludedDirs(root)` to the search and code vision workers with each scan; Find in Files still searches an excluded folder chosen as its directory). The shown TODO window rescans on `projectRootsChanged`. `ide.projectRoots`: `get()` (relative), `sourceRoots()`, `testRoots()`, `excludedRoots()` (absolute), `kindOf(path)`, `isExcluded(path)`, `mark(path, kind | null)`; event `projectRootsChanged(roots)`.

- Known limits: PyCharm's Resource Root and Namespace Package items are not offered; a virtualenv created after Pyright got its settings stays in its files until the next settings change or server start; Run configurations have no "Add source roots to PYTHONPATH" checkbox (always on, PyCharm's default).
- Test: `verify/run-roots.mjs` (ports 9590-9592, in CI): pure Node checks of the src layout rule, the PYTHONPATH order and the record; the notification and its actions, the sources root icon and the saved record; Run, pytest and Pyright (definition of `add` in `src/mypkg/core.py`) on a src layout; Revert (ModuleNotFoundError in Run and Pyright's unresolved import); Mark Directory As with the mouse (Sources Root, Unmark as Sources Root, a second folder `lib2` on PYTHONPATH, Test Sources Root, Excluded and Cancel Exclusion, an excluded folder's symbols leaving Pyright's workspace symbols); the roots and icons after a restart with no notification; Korean (확인/되돌리기/다시 표시 안 함, 디렉터리를 다음으로 표시 › 소스 루트, 테스트 소스 루트, 제외됨, 소스 루트 표시 해제). `verify/run-roots-wiring.mjs` (port 9593, in CI): Debug of a file and the Python Console import from `src`; Go to File, Find in Files, TODO (the tool window too) and the symbol index leave out an Excluded folder, and Cancel Exclusion brings it back. `verify/run-roots-edges.mjs` (port 9633, in CI): a namespace package under src detected, run and resolved by Pyright; venv/ staying out of Pyright's files after a folder is excluded; Redirect input from releasing the file of a program that reads none of it; a terminal run and its child ended by a page reload; Debug with -E and -I.

## Run configuration options: Interpreter options, Redirect input from, Emulate terminal

The Python type's form has PyCharm's Interpreter options (`interpOptions`, e.g. `-X dev -W error -O`), Redirect input from (`stdinFile`, a browse button for any file: a field's `browse` may be a filter list, `[]` for every file) and Emulate terminal in output console (`emulateTerminal`, a checkbox). `ide.run.resolvePython(cfg)` adds `interpOptions` (parsed like the parameters) and `stdin` (absolute, or ''). Interpreter options go before the script: `run:start(…, envFiles, {interpOptions, stdin})` runs `python <options> -u <script> <params>` (local and remote), and `debugConfig` adds them to the interpreter's arguments, ahead of `-m debugpy`; with -E or -I (PYTHONPATH ignored) debugpy starts through debug.js's `-c` launcher (`DEBUGPY_LAUNCHER`), which puts the bundled debugpy and the long names on sys.path itself (an exception that ends the program then shows pydevd's "execution is paused at: <module>" note, since the outermost frame is the launcher's) (as with Run, the project and source roots still leave PYTHONPATH: that is what those options mean). With Redirect input from, `run:start` streams the file into the program's stdin and closes it (the program reads to EOF; `stream.pipeline`, so the file is closed too when the program exits without reading it all), the console has no input field, and a missing file prints "Input file doesn't exist: <path>" and starts nothing.

Emulate terminal in output console runs the program in a ConPTY pseudo-console (node-pty, as the Terminal): `run:startTerminal(interp, file, cwd, params, env, roots, envFiles, {interpOptions, cols, rows})` -> `{id, cwd}`, with the environment, PYTHONPATH, .env files and working directory of `run:start`; output goes to the starting window as `run:terminalData {id, data}` (coalesced per tick), then `run:terminalExit {id, code}`; `run:terminalWrite` and `run:terminalResize` (sent), `run:terminalKill` (taskkill /T on the program, then the pty; also when the window closes or its page reloads, and before quit). The Run tab shows it in an xterm.js terminal (`session.term`; xterm and its fit addon are loaded on first use), so `sys.stdout.isatty()` and `sys.stdin.isatty()` are True: colors, `\r` progress lines and prompts behave as in a terminal, typed keys go to the program, and the IDE's shortcuts still work there (Ctrl+F2 stops it), as in PyCharm's Run console (the box is no `[data-terminal]`). The tab ends with "Process finished with exit code N"; a session's `kill(id)` is the terminal's. A remote interpreter runs with the output console and a note; with Redirect input from set as well, the terminal run says the redirect is not used.

- Known limits: Debug runs with pipes, without Redirect input from or Emulate terminal.
- Test: `verify/run-roots.mjs`: `-X dev -O` (dev mode and optimize 1, shown in the command line), Debug with `-X dev`, a redirected file read to EOF with no input field, a missing input file, the terminal (isatty True True, RED-TEXT in red, a line rewritten with `\r`, the keyboard in the terminal, 민수 typed into input(), the exit line, Ctrl+F2 in the terminal stopping a program, another configuration running beside it), the form's three fields saved, 1008x681, and the Korean labels (인터프리터 옵션, 다음 위치의 입력 리디렉션, 출력 콘솔에서 터미널 에뮬레이션).

## Stop and rerun

`runConfig` asks "Process 'x' Is Running" / "'x' is running. Stop and rerun?" (Stop and Rerun, Cancel, Don't ask again) when the configuration's Run tab is still running or starting, for every type including tests, from Run, Rerun, the Run tab's ⟳, Rerun Failed Tests, the gutter and the tree; Cancel keeps the running process. Don't ask again sets `run.confirmRerun` false; Settings › Run/Debug (page `run-debug`) "Confirm rerun with process termination" turns it back on. Auto-test's reruns and before launch dependencies pass `confirm: false`: Run again while a before launch configuration still runs starts it over without asking, since a Cancel there would drop the launch. Debug tabs never ask (the debugger replaces its own session). Run tab toolbar actions carry `data-action="rerun"` / `"stop"`. Tests: `verify/tests-debug-run.mjs` (the question, Cancel, Enter, the tab's ⟳, Don't ask again, the settings page, a running test configuration) and `verify/run-tools.mjs` (Rerun while running answers the question; a before launch dependency starts over without one, and Cancel on the running Main starts no before launch task).

## Renaming and moving modules from the project tree

Renaming or moving a Python module or package in the project tree rewrites its imports, as PyCharm's Rename and Move with Search for references do. The core tree offers `ide.treeMoveHandlers` (`handler({kind: 'rename'|'move', entry, dir})` resolves true when it handled the request, else the plain rename or move runs) and `ide.moveTreeEntry(entry, to)`, the plain rename or move. A plain rename or move renames on disk and then carries the open documents at or under the old path, unsaved text included, to the new path in every window (`ide.documentsMoved`); nothing is saved first, so a read-only file, a file in File Cache Conflict or a folder holding one keeps its edits. Delete closes the deleted documents' tabs in every window. Rename runs from the context menu, Shift+F6 while the tree has focus, and moves from dragging a row onto a folder or Ctrl+X then Ctrl+V on a folder (or a file in it), also Cut and Paste in the context menu. The refactor plugin takes over for a `.py` file other than `__init__.py`, a package and a namespace package (a folder without `__init__.py` that holds `.py` files) inside a trusted project; other files and untrusted projects keep the plain prompt and confirmation.

- Rename shows "Rename module 'geo.shapes' to:" (or package) with the file name, the part before `.py` selected, and Search for references on, with Preview, Refactor and Cancel. Moves open the Move dialog with the drop target as the destination and the same checkbox (also the tree's Move… item). With it on, `refactor_server.py` runs rope's `Rename` on the module or package resource (`renameModule`) or `MoveModule` (`move`), so `import a.b`, `from a import b`, `from a.b import f`, aliases and relative imports are rewritten in every project file. The result is applied like every refactoring: open editors get one undoable edit each (their unsaved text is what rope read), closed files are written, the open files under every moving path are saved first (`saveMoving`; if one stays modified nothing changes and the user sees "{name} has unsaved changes that could not be saved, so it was not moved.", also before an undo or redo replays a move) and their documents then follow to the new place in every window (`ide.documentsMoved`), Local History gets "Before Refactoring", and one Ctrl+Z undoes it all. With it off, or for a module renamed to a name without `.py`, only the file or folder is renamed.
- rope's output is adjusted: a namespace package gets a temporary empty `__init__.py` while rope analyses it (rope refuses or fails on namespace folders), changes that only touch blank lines are dropped (MoveModule strips them in the moved module), and the imports MoveModule rewrites keep their place in the import block instead of moving to its end. A move keeps each importer's style, as PyCharm does: rope turns relative imports into `import tools.figures` with qualified uses (and fails on `from ..pkg import mod`), so before rope runs, the relative from-imports that name the moved module (or its name) are made absolute in memory (`absolutize_importers`), and afterwards those imports, now naming the new place, become relative again when the new place is in the importer's top-level package (`from ..views.price import total`, `from . import price`), else stay absolute (`from tools import figures`) (`relativize_importers`). Dotted names are taken relative to rope's source folders (`project.get_source_folders()`, deepest first), so in a src layout `src/shop/models/price.py` is `shop.models.price`; an absolute name is used only when rope's `find_module` resolves it to the module the relative import names (other source folders holding the importer are tried next), and an importer without one is left to rope unchanged. Call sites stay unqualified; `import a.b` (also `as c`) importers keep the qualified style with the new path. Rope may still merge two from-imports of the same new package into one statement.
- Without rope the Install rope offer has a second button, Rename Without References or Move Without References. Move on a folder from the tree no longer reads the folder as a file (it failed before).
- Test: `verify/rename-module.mjs` (ports 9110-9112, the third instance a src-layout project with `src/shop` and `tests/`, rope from `verify/out/refactor-venv` as in verify/refactor.mjs) renames a module with Shift+F6 (unsaved text in the module and an importer, absolute, from, dotted, aliased and relative imports, open and closed files, undo and redo), renames a package through the preview, moves a module with Ctrl+X / Ctrl+V, a namespace package by drag-and-drop and a module with relative (`from .x import y`, `from ..pkg.x import y as z`, `from . import x as a`), `from pkg import x` and `import a.b [as c]` importers with Cut / Paste in the context menu (exact resulting text), renames with the checkbox off, checks that a `.txt` file and an untrusted project keep the plain prompt, uses the rope offer's fallback, and checks the Korean dialogs; the fixture program's output must not change. It also checks that the moved module's unsaved text survives undoing the rename. `verify/rename-unsaved.mjs` (port 9275) renames from the tree a read-only file, a file in File Cache Conflict, a folder holding a read-only modified file and a normal file, each with unsaved text, and in a detached window follows a rename (a save there does not bring the old file back) and a delete. `verify/tree.mjs`'s move check expects the tab to follow with its unsaved edit.

## Keymap editing

Settings › Keymap (`renderer/features/settings`) edits the keymap like PyCharm's: select an action, then Add Keyboard Shortcut… (a capture box records the next key press with its modifiers, Shift twice is Double Shift, and *Second stroke* adds a two-stroke shortcut such as `Ctrl+K C`, shown as "Ctrl+K, C"), Remove Shortcut (a menu when the action has several), Reset Shortcuts and Reset All; double-click or the row's context menu does the same. The capture box warns when the chord is taken (the same key, or one is the other's first stroke, by an action that can fire in the same place: actions with different `context`s are not conflicts) and OK then asks PyCharm's question: Remove (the other assignments), Leave (both keep it) or Cancel. Modified actions are blue, and *Show modified only* filters to them. Changes wait for OK / Apply.

- Storage: only the user's changes, `keymap.overrides` = `{actionId: [keys]}` (an empty list means no shortcut). The core (`renderer/core/ide.js`, user keymap block) keeps each action's `defaultKeys` and sets `keys` from the override, at registration (so plugin actions registered later, also on `ready`, get theirs) and live on `settingsChanged`, then emits `keymapChanged`. Defaults of untouched actions can change in later versions. Overrides for ids that are not registered stay in the setting; values that are not lists of keys, and keys that are not `comboOf` strokes (`Ctrl+Alt+Shift+K`, two strokes, `Shift Shift`), are ignored. API: `ide.keymap.comboOf(e)`, `isValid(key)`, `parse(raw)` -> Map, `isModified(id)`, `format(key)`, `label(id)`.
- Dispatch: the global keydown handler also takes two-stroke shortcuts: a first stroke that starts an enabled action's two-stroke key is swallowed and waits up to 1.5 s. When the wait runs out or the second stroke completes nothing, the first stroke's own single-stroke action runs (Ctrl+K Commit… beside a `Ctrl+K C` chord, as in PyCharm and VS Code) and the second stroke is then handled as an ordinary key; Escape just cancels.
- Where shortcuts show: Find Action, Search Everywhere and the main menu read `action.keys` when they open (`menuHint` only for actions without keys, such as Undo). A tooltip follows the keymap when its element has `data-keymap-action="<id>"`; the title's trailing "(keys)" is replaced on hover (toolbar main menu, Search Everywhere, Hide, Run, Stop, every tool window stripe button). Tool window `tw.keys` comes from `tw.keyAction` (`Activate<id>ToolWindow`, or AI Chat's `AiChat.Toggle`). Find Action also lists the main editor's Monaco actions that no IDE action runs (`monacoActions` in ide.js: Sort Lines Ascending, Transform to Title Case…), without a shortcut; a Monaco command an IDE action carries as `editorCommand` (Join Lines) is listed once, as the IDE action.
- Monaco: an action whose `run` (or the action) carries `editorCommand` (core's editor actions: Duplicate Line, Delete Line, Comment with Line Comment, Go to Line, Find, Replace, Extend/Shrink Selection, Move Line, Collapse/Expand) keeps Monaco's own keybindings while unmodified. Once modified, the settings plugin removes every default Monaco keybinding of that command (`{keybinding: 0, command: '-cmd'}`) and binds the action's keys to it with `monaco.editor.addKeybindingRules`, so embedded editors (viewers, notebook cells, consoles) and Monaco's context menu follow the keymap and the old key no longer runs it anywhere.
- Test: `verify/keymap-edit.mjs` (ports 9120-9122): rebinds Tool Window: Project (old key dead, new key works, tooltip, More list), the Leave and Remove conflict choices, Remove and Reset, a two-stroke Duplicate Line, Comment with Line Comment in the editor and in an embedded Monaco editor, Find Action and main menu hints, persistence across a restart, malformed and `__proto__` overrides, a late-registered action, Reset All, and the Korean page and dialogs.

## Scratch files

`renderer/features/scratches` and `main/features/scratches.js` add PyCharm's scratch files. File › New Scratch File (`NewScratchFile`, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Insert, also in the Scratches nodes' context menu) opens a filterable language picker (Python first, then Markdown, JSON, SQL, HTML, Plain Text, YAML, TOML, JavaScript, TypeScript, CSS, XML, HTTP Request, Shell Script, PowerShell, Batch, INI, CSV, reStructuredText), creates an empty `scratch.<ext>`, `scratch_1.<ext>`, … (the first free name, created exclusively) in `<userData>/scratches/` and opens it. The folder is outside every project and shared by all of them, so scratches survive restarts and show in every project.

- Project tree: core's `ide.registerTreeRoot({id, order, entry: {name, path, dir: true, label?, iconType?}, list?(dir) -> entries | null, menu?(entry) -> items})` adds a top-level node after the project root. Its rows' entries carry `treeRoot` (the id) and their rows the class `tree-extra`; they are not saved in `projectTree.expanded`, cannot be dragged or dropped on, get only the node's `menu` items plus Rename…/Delete (not the project's New › menu or other plugins' tree actions), and a `fixed: true` entry cannot be renamed or deleted (also not with the Delete key). `list` returns the children of a virtual folder, or null to list the folder on disk. `ide.fileIcon(name, {type})` picks an icon type directly. The scratches plugin registers Scratches and Consoles (스크래치 및 콘솔, the `scratches` icon; its path is the user-data folder, which only identifies it) with one child, Scratches (스크래치), the folder itself. A scratch row offers New Scratch File, Run '…' and Debug '…' for Python, Show in Explorer, Rename… and Delete (core's confirmation, then the Recycle Bin). A watcher on the folder (`scratches:changed`) refreshes the tree when files appear, go or are renamed, also from outside PyIDE.
- Running: a Python scratch runs and debugs with the project interpreter in the project root, which is also on PYTHONPATH, as in PyCharm. The run plugin's Python `forFile` and the debugger's Debug current file (Shift+F9) ask `ide.scratches?.isScratch(path)` and then use `cwd: ide.root`; the gutter, Shift+F10, Ctrl+Shift+F10 and the tree's Run all go through `forFile`.
- Other features: Pyright opens a scratch like any open file and reports its problems (it is not in the workspace, so it only checks open scratches); ruff lints it with the project's settings; completion works. Auto save saves scratches like other files. Find in Files, the semantic index, Go to File and Local History walk or record only the project, so scratches are never in them. Search Everywhere's Files tab adds `ide.scratches.list()` to the project walk, so scratches can be found by name.
- API: `ide.scratches = {ready, dir(), isScratch(path), list(), create(ext), newScratch(), run(path), debug(path)}`. IPC: `scratches:dir`, `scratches:list`, `scratches:create(ext, text?)`.
- Test: `verify/scratches.mjs` (ports 9140-9142; lib.mjs's temporary `PYIDE_USER_DATA` keeps the user's own scratches untouched) creates Python and JSON scratches from the action and the shortcut, checks the numbering and the tree node, saves, runs a Python scratch from Shift+F10 and the tree (output, project-root cwd, a project import) and debugs it with Shift+F9, checks Pyright and completion, that Find in Files and the semantic index leave it out and Search Everywhere finds it, renames and deletes from the tree, adds a file from outside, restarts with the same user data, opens another project, and repeats the surfaces in Korean.

## Attach to Process

Run › Attach to Process… (`AttachToProcess`, Ctrl+Alt+F5, as in PyCharm) debugs a Python program that is already running outside the IDE. `main/features/debug-attach.js` (a main module of the debugger plugin) lists the processes and injects debugpy; `main/features/debug.js` (`debug:processes`, `debug:attach`) runs the DAP session; `renderer/features/debugger/index.js` has the dialog and the session.

- The dialog lists local `python*` processes with their pid, command line (without the interpreter) and interpreter (version and path; a venv's launcher stub is folded into the real interpreter it starts), filtered as you type (pid, script, command line, interpreter); Up/Down/PageUp/PageDown move, Enter or a double click attaches, Refresh lists again. Processes whose command line (or, on Linux, working directory) is inside the project come first under "In this project", then the rest, newest first. Windows has no cheap way to read another process's working directory, so there only the command line counts. Listing is one `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "Name like 'python%'"` (about 0.7 s, 10 s timeout) plus one cached `python -c` version probe per interpreter; elsewhere `ps`. Left out: PyIDE's own Python (anything whose command line points into the app folder, `app.asar.unpacked`, `vendor/`, user data or `~/.pyide`: the REPL, semantic and AI servers, refactor, notebook kernels, coverage/profiler bootstraps), debugpy adapters and processes already run under `-m debugpy`.
- Attaching runs `<target's python.exe> -X frozen_modules=off -m debugpy --listen 127.0.0.1:<port> --pid <pid>` with `PYTHONPATH=vendor` (the target's own interpreter, so Python 3.14's `sys.remote_exec` matches its version; debugpy falls back to its DLL injector otherwise), then connects to the port and sends the same `initialize`/`attach` (`redirectOutput: true`, so the program's output reaches the Debug console)/`setBreakpoints`/`configurationDone` as a launched session. Breakpoints, stepping, frames, variables, watches, evaluate and inline values all work unchanged; stdin is not connected. A process can call `debugpy.listen()` only once, so a process attached before (remembered per pid, or found from its debugpy adapter child's `--port`, which also covers programs that call `debugpy.listen()` themselves; marked "debugpy listening") is connected to directly, in about 0.3 s instead of 3-5 s.
- Stop/Ctrl+F2 (titled Detach while attached) and Rerun: Stop sends `disconnect` with `terminateDebuggee: false` and never kills the process; Rerun attaches to the same process again. The session ends on its own when the target exits ("Process N finished with exit code X", or "Process N finished" when debugpy reports no exit code: the adapter `debugpy.listen()` started outlives the program and keeps the connection open, so main closes it 300 ms after `terminated` and waits up to 3 s for the pid to go). The Python processes an attached program starts are debugged too (see Debugging subprocesses); Stop detaches from them as well and kills none.
- Errors are shown in the Debug console as "Cannot attach to process N: …", never as a hang: the process is gone (checked before injecting and while waiting), access denied (an elevated or other user's process; restart PyIDE as administrator), Python older than 3.10 (debugpy 1.8's minimum), the injector failing (its last output lines) or running over 30 s, and the target not starting debugpy within 20 s (`PYIDE_ATTACH_TIMEOUT_MS`), e.g. a program blocked in one long native call, which runs injected code only when it executes Python again.
- Test: `verify/attach-process.mjs` (port 9130) checks the error wording as pure Node, then starts `counter.py` (a 0.2 s counting loop) outside the IDE, sets a breakpoint, opens the dialog with Ctrl+Alt+F5 (project process first, no sidecars, version shown, filtering), attaches with Enter, checks the hit, the counter in Variables, frames, evaluate, F9 to the next hit, F8, program output, detaches with Ctrl+F2 and checks the process is alive, attaches again (direct connect) and detaches with the Stop button, kills only that process tree, then attaches to a process that has just exited and to one blocked in `time.sleep(300)` (8 s timeout) and checks both messages and that no session is left.

## Debugging subprocesses

As with PyCharm's "Attach to subprocess automatically while debugging" (on by default), the Python processes a debugged program starts (`subprocess` with `sys.executable`, `multiprocessing` with Windows' spawn) are debugged too: their breakpoints suspend them, stepping, Variables, Watches and evaluation work in them, and their output is in the Debug console. Settings › Python Debugger (page `python-debugger`, setting `debugger.attachSubprocess`) turns it off.

- How: debugpy patches the debuggee's process creation (its CLI's default `subProcess`) and holds each Python child until a client attaches, announced by the adapter's `debugpyAttach` event. `main/features/debug.js` answers it with a second DAP connection to the same adapter port (`attachChild`), configured with the event's attach arguments (debugpy copies the parent's) and the session's current breakpoints. Before 0.2.9 nobody answered and the session hung as soon as a child started (the `subProcess: false` PyIDE sent in its attach arguments is read only by debugpy's launcher, so it changed nothing). Off, debug:start passes `--configure-subProcess False` (children run plain); for Attach to Process each child is let go (attach, configurationDone, disconnect), since the injected debugpy patches the target too.
- Sessions: a debuggee and its children form a group in main (`{root, port, members, bps, exceptions}`); a child's session id is `<root>/<pid>` and its `debug:event`s carry `root`, after a `subprocess {pid}` event. setBreakpoints and setExceptionBreakpoints sent to any member go to all and are kept for children that start later. A child's grandchildren come through its connection.
- The renderer keeps `sess.procs` and `sess.cur` (the process the Frames, Variables, Watches and stepping act on; `window.__debugger.state()` reports both). Once a child exists the Frames header shows a process list (`#dbgProcesses`: "child.py (pid 456) · breakpoint", "parent.py (pid 123) · Running"; "Subprocess" for a `-c` or `-m` child); choosing a suspended one shows its stop. A stop while another process's stop is shown waits in the list; Resume shows the next waiting stop, a step stays with its process. A child that exits leaves the list. The console says "Attached to subprocess N". Exception pass-over state is per process.
- Stop ends the debuggee and every child (disconnect with terminateDebuggee, then `taskkill /T` on each child pid, since a Popen the parent does not wait for survives it); an attached program and its children are only detached from. When the debuggee ends by itself, children still running go on without the debugger, as under Run. Pause pauses the process shown (a parent blocked in `subprocess.run` pauses in library code, hidden by Just My Code); choose the child in the list to pause it.
- Remote interpreters: children run without the debugger (`--configure-subProcess False` whatever the setting). Their debugpy would reach the adapter through the forward and need the target's path mappings, which the fake targets cannot test. Before, a remote program that started a Python child hung the same way.
- A child inherits the debuggee's PYTHONPATH, so in an 8.3 working folder it gets the long names too (see Long working folders); a child in a folder whose long form is past MAX_PATH is not tested.
- Test: `verify/debugger-subprocess.mjs` (ports 9370-9371, in CI, about 70 s): subprocess.run with a breakpoint set while running, the process list, F8 and F9 in the child, its output, the list after it exits; multiprocessing spawn; Stop ending a Popen child; Attach to Process with a child and a program that ends by itself; Korean, and the setting unchecked on its page.

## Java debugging

The debugger plugin (now named Debugger) debugs Java in the same Debug tool window as Python, as close to IntelliJ IDEA as a JDWP client allows. Nothing is downloaded: `main/features/debug-jvm.js` speaks JDWP, the protocol of the JVM's own `-agentlib:jdwp` agent, and answers the DAP requests the renderer already sends (threads, stackTrace, scopes, variables, evaluate, setExpression, setVariable, continue, next, stepIn, stepOut, pause, setBreakpoints, disconnect) with the DAP events it handles (stopped, continued, output, terminated). `main/features/debug-jvm-eval.js` parses and evaluates Java expressions on JDWP values. HotSwap: `ide.debugger.jvmRequest('redefineClasses', {classes: [{name, file}]})` redefines the loaded classes in the JVM being debugged (JDWP VirtualMachine.RedefineClasses) -> {redefined, notLoaded}; their cached line tables are dropped and the session's breakpoints set again; a change the JVM refuses comes back as its JDWP error with the reason (a method was added, a field was added or removed, ...).

- Starting: Debug (Shift+F9, the run widget's debug button, the main method's gutter icon, the project tree's Debug '…') on a Java Application configuration, or on a `.java` file with Current File selected. Shift+F9 debugs the selected configuration when its type has its own `debug(cfg, opts)` (run plugin's `debugConfig` calls it; Python types keep their behaviour). The type builds exactly as Run does (`javac -encoding UTF-8 -g`, compiler errors in Problems, Stop ends the build) into the Debug console, then starts `java -agentlib:jdwp=transport=dt_socket,server=y,suspend=y,address=127.0.0.1:<free port> …` through `toolchains:start`. A single source file runs as `java File.java`; the source launcher keeps local variables.
- `ide.debugger.debugJvm({launch({con, port, cancelled}) -> {procId, sourceRoots?, suspended?, timeoutMs?} | null, rerun?, tabs?})` is the generic entry: "debug a JVM listening on a JDWP port". `launch` starts whatever runs the JVM with that port and returns the IDE process id (`procId`), whose output and exit the session follows like a run; `timeoutMs` (default 20 s, at most 30 min) gives a build tool that forks the JVM (Maven's spring-boot:run with `-Dspring-boot.run.jvmArguments=-agentlib:jdwp=…`) longer to start listening. `procId: null` attaches to a JVM running elsewhere; Stop then detaches (JDWP Dispose) and the JVM keeps running. Main: `debug:freePort`, then `debug:jvm({port, procId}, breakpoints, {startKey, sourceRoots, suspended, timeoutMs})` connects with retries while that process runs, makes the session (id `procId`, or `jvm-<n>` for an attach) in the same sessions map as debugpy's, so `debug:request` and `debug:stop` work unchanged, sets the breakpoints and resumes the VM. Stop on a launched JVM sends VirtualMachine.Exit and kills the process.
- Console: "Connected to the target VM, address: '127.0.0.1:N', transport: 'socket'" once connected and "Disconnected from the target VM, …" when the JVM goes, as in IntelliJ IDEA (Korean: 대상 VM에 연결되었습니다…), then the usual "Process finished with exit code N". Program output and stdin work as under Run.
- Breakpoints: a `.java` file's breakpoints go to every class compiled from it (nested, anonymous and local classes, lambdas: every method whose line table has the line) that is loaded, and to classes loaded later (a ClassPrepare request per source file name with SourceNameMatch, then the file's package read from its `package` line). A breakpoint suspends every thread (IntelliJ's default). Conditions are Java expressions (the field says "Java expression" for a `.java` file); one that cannot be evaluated suspends and the console says why. Hit counts take the Python debugger's forms (`3`, `>= 3`, `% 3`); a log message prints its `{expression}` parts and goes on. Run to Cursor works through a temporary breakpoint. Exception breakpoints are Python-only.
- Stepping: Step Over (F8), Step Into (F7), Step Out (Shift+F8) step by line in the suspended thread and skip the JDK's classes (`java.*`, `javax.*`, `jdk.*`, `sun.*`, `com.sun.*`, IntelliJ's default step filters). Pause suspends every thread and shows `main`. A breakpoint hit while another stop is shown waits and is shown after Resume.
- Frames: `Class.method, File.java:line`; a frame's file is the one a breakpoint names, else `<source root>/<package>/<File>.java` from the configuration's source root. JDK frames have no file and are shown dimmed.
- Variables (IntelliJ's renderers): `this`, then the method's parameters and the locals live at the stopped line (`javac -g`). A primitive shows its type and value (`{int} 42`, `{char} 'a' 97`), a String `{String} "…"`, an object `{Person@123}` with its toString() when its class overrides it (records, enums unquoted, boxed values as numbers), an array `{int[3]@45} [3, 1, 4]`, a Collection or Map `{ArrayList@46} size = 2`. Expanding shows an object's instance fields (superclasses first), an array's items (first 1000), a collection's elements (through `toArray()`) and a map's entries as `key = value` (through `entrySet()`). Each row has an expression (`p.name`, `numbers[2]`, `names.get(1)`, `ages.get("kim")`) for Watches, Copy Value and Set Value. Calls made to show values run only in the suspended thread (INVOKE_SINGLE_THREADED), one at a time, give up after 3 s, are skipped after a Pause (the thread is not suspended by an event), and a breakpoint they hit is passed over. Resume and the steps wait for a call in progress (at most 3 s), so a Resume pressed while the Variables view is still loading does not let the next breakpoint be taken for one the call hit (the suite resumes at that moment on purpose). Objects created for the views are kept from garbage collection until the program resumes; frame ids are fetched again after a call, which invalidates them.
- Evaluate (console input line, Alt+F8, Watches, Set Value, conditions): names (locals, fields of `this`, static fields of the frame's class, loaded classes by simple name in the frame's package, as nested classes or in `java.lang`, or by full name), `this`, fields, `array.length`, method calls (instance and static, overloads chosen by the arguments' types, with boxing, unboxing and widening; strings created in the program when a parameter needs one), array elements, literals (int, long, float, double, char, String, boolean, null), unary and binary operators with Java's numeric promotion and int/long overflow, string concatenation with toString(), `==` (by identity, by value for primitives and boxed values next to a primitive), `&&` `||` `?:`, casts and assignment (`x = 5`, `+=`, to a local, a field, a static field or an array element). `new`, lambdas, `instanceof` and `.class` are refused with a message. Errors are translated in the renderer (MAIN_ERRORS: "Cannot find symbol 'x'", "Method threw 'java.lang.IllegalStateException' exception.").
- Safe Mode: Debug, like Run, is an explicit action and works in an untrusted project; nothing starts by itself.
- Known limits: no exception breakpoints, field watchpoints, method breakpoints, Drop Frame, HotSwap or Force Return; threads other than the stopped one are not listed in Frames; Evaluate cannot create objects or compile code.
- Test: `verify/java-debug.mjs` (ports 9774-9775; a JDK 17+ on this computer, else skipped; fixture `verify/fixtures/java-debug`): breakpoints from the gutter, Ctrl+F8 and a condition in the popup; Shift+F9 on the selected Java Application configuration (javac -g and the agent's command line, "Connected to the target VM"), Frames, the current-line highlight, Variables (an int array, an ArrayList, a HashMap, a record, a Korean String) and their expansion; console evaluation (calls, Map.get with a String key, a static JDK method, an unknown name, an assignment), Alt+F8 and a watch; Step Into, Over and Out; the conditional breakpoint in a loop; Stop (no java process left); Rerun to the end ("Disconnected from the target VM", exit code 0); a compile error that stops before the JVM starts; then in Korean the console line, tabs, stop reason, an evaluation error, the exit line, no English UI text and no missing translations.

## CI

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on pushes and pull requests to `master` on `windows-latest` with Node 24.11.0 and Python 3.13 (`PY_PYTHON3=3.13`, so the IDE's default `py -3` interpreter is that one): `npm ci`, `npm run vendor`, pytest, the rope venv in `verify/out/refactor-venv`, then one step per suite, in order, each with its own timeout and run even after an earlier one failed, among them: core, editors, keyboard, tree, settings, settings-search, search-nav, symbol-index (also in Korean at 1008x681), workspace, i18n-static, security, live-templates, autosave, recovery, recovery-main, rename-module, scratches, keymap-edit, auto-test, tests-tree-menu, tests-debug-run, tests-debug-target, appearance-modes, vulnerabilities, settings-sync, github, vcs-daily, report-update, import-install, venv-prompt, ui-inspect-dialogs, dialog-keyboard, debugger, debugger-input, debugger-exceptions, debugger-values, debugger-subprocess, pyconsole, and the failure suites core-lifecycle, encodings, multi-window, settings-instances, tool-failures, rename-unsaved, terminal-pipes, env-failures, history-instances, interpreter-health, run-debug-edges, and from 0.2.9 case-sensitive, plugin-load-errors, vcs-force-push, vcs-log-actions, search-cache, run-roots, run-roots-wiring, code-generation-sidecar, code-generation, editor-keys, console-output, pyright-budget, completion-parens, inlay-hints, navigation-tests, goto-test-edges and run-roots-edges (some share ports; steps run one after another). These need no network, AI models, databases, Docker or WSL. The runner has an interactive desktop, so they run with plain `node` (not `verify/on-desktop.ps1`). When a step fails, `verify/out` (without the venv) is uploaded as the `verify-out` artifact. A suite added there must pass serially on a fresh machine.

## Shortcut labels

Every shortcut the UI shows comes from the keymap, so a rebinding in Settings › Keymap (or `keymap.overrides`) shows everywhere at once, without a reload, and an action whose shortcut was removed shows none. Never write a chord into UI text or a `tr()` string for a keymap action; translations stay chord-free (keys that are not keymap actions, such as Enter/Esc inside a dialog or Ctrl+C in the terminal, may still be literal). The `ide.keymap` helpers (`renderer/core/ide.js`, user keymap block):

- Menus: a context menu item `{label, keyAction: '<action id>', run}` shows that action's current keys as its hint, computed when the menu opens. A project tree action (`ide.registerTreeAction`) takes the same `keyAction`. `ide.keymap.hint(id)` formats keys as the main menu, Find Action and Search Everywhere do: two strokes as "Ctrl+K, C", alternatives separated by commas.
- Tooltips: `ide.keymap.tooltip(el, text, id)` sets `data-keymap-action` and the title "Text (keys)"; tool window actions take `{title, keyAction}`. Titles of `data-keymap-action` elements are refreshed on `ready` and `keymapChanged` (and on hover), so they are right before the first hover.
- Text: `ide.keymap.withKeys(text, id)` gives "Find Usages (Alt+F7)", or just the text without a shortcut; put it into a sentence through a placeholder (`tr('Use {action} on a symbol to find its usages', {action})`). `ide.keymap.text(el, fn)` fills an element now and again on `keymapChanged` and `ready` (for texts made before a plugin registered the action); give it its own element (a `<span>`) when the parent's text is replaced later.
- Converted: editor tab and split menus, the split button and empty editor, toolbar (main menu, Search Everywhere, Run, Stop, Debug, Claude Code), Debug window buttons, the breakpoint popup's More link and View Breakpoints key, terminal new-session menu and Claude Code action, Git gutter menu and Commit/Git tool window actions, the "no remote" note, project tree actions (Reformat Code, Optimize Imports, Run, pytest, Find/Replace in Files), Find / Find in Files / Call Hierarchy / Bookmarks / Run / HTTP Client / query console texts, HTTP gutter hover, AI Chat, AI edit status and settings, Actions on Save note, live template help, Semantic Search model description, and the New Project welcome script (a comment about an action without a shortcut is left out).
- Test: `verify/keymap-labels.mjs` (ports 9160-9161) rebinds actions from each of those places while the IDE runs (one-stroke, two-stroke and removed shortcuts) and checks the menus, tooltips, texts, Find Action and Search Everywhere, a second rebinding of the tab menu, and the Korean texts.

## Appearance modes (Distraction-Free, Zen, Full Screen, Presentation)

`renderer/features/appearance-modes` (plugin `appearance-modes`) and `main/features/appearance-modes.js` add View › Appearance › Enter Presentation Mode, Enter Distraction-Free Mode, Enter Full Screen and Enter Zen Mode (actions `TogglePresentationMode`, `ToggleDistractionFreeMode`, `ToggleFullScreen`, `ToggleZenMode`; no default keys, as in PyCharm's Windows keymap). The same action leaves the mode, and its title then reads Exit … (menu, Find Action, Search Everywhere). Esc keeps its usual job.

- Distraction-Free Mode hides the main toolbar, both tool window stripes, every open tool window, the status bar, the editor tab bars and the breadcrumbs (body classes `appearance-chromeless` and `appearance-centered`, `data-appearance-mode` on body). Group editors get `lineNumbers: 'off'`, no glyph margin, no folding and no minimap (per editor, so viewers' embedded editors keep theirs), and the editor is centered at `appearance.distractionFreeColumns` columns (default 100) of its font plus its gutter and scrollbar (`--appearance-editor-width`). Tool windows still open by their shortcuts (Alt+1, Alt+6…), docked as usual; hiding them returns to the mode.
- Zen Mode is Distraction-Free in full screen. Full Screen is only `BrowserWindow.setFullScreen` (`appearance-modes:setFullScreen(on)`, `appearance-modes:isFullScreen`). Presentation Mode is full screen with the chrome hidden like Distraction-Free, the gutter kept, and the editor font at `appearance.presentationFontSize` (default 24; the line height keeps the editor's ratio). Settings › Appearance & Behavior › Presentation and Distraction-Free Modes holds both settings; changing them, or the editor font, while a mode is on applies at once.
- One mode at a time: entering another leaves the first (staying in full screen between two full-screen modes). Leaving restores exactly what was there: which tool window each area showed (also for a hidden area), which areas were open, their sizes, the group editors' own options and the current editor font, and the focus. If the window leaves full screen some other way (`appearance-modes:fullScreenChanged`), a full-screen mode ends too. Entering shows a notice for 5 s with the exit: the action's current shortcut, or View › Appearance and Find Action. Modes are not saved across restarts (PyCharm keeps Distraction-Free Mode; PyIDE does not, to keep it simple).
- Reload and edits during a mode: after a renderer reload no mode is on, so a window left in full screen leaves it. On exit only the gutter options the mode changed, and that still hold the mode's value, are restored (a line-number style the user picked during the mode stays); entries for editors disposed during the mode are dropped.
- `ide.appearanceModes`: `mode()` -> `'distractionFree' | 'zen' | 'fullScreen' | 'presentation' | null`, `toggle(mode)`, `exit()`; event `appearanceModeChanged(mode)`.
- Test: `verify/appearance-modes.mjs` (ports 9230-9231) enters Distraction-Free from the main menu and checks the hidden chrome, Monaco options, the centered 100-column editor, the notice and Esc; opens Project and Problems by shortcut inside the mode, changes sizes and opens other windows, leaves through Find Action and checks the layout is restored exactly; then Zen, Full Screen and Presentation (full screen through the main process, font size 24, live setting changes), switching Zen → Presentation, leaving full screen from outside, split editors, the settings page, and the Korean menu, labels, notice and page.

## Vulnerable packages (OSV)

`renderer/features/vulnerabilities` (plugin `vulnerabilities`, depends on `packages`), `main/features/vulnerabilities.js` and the pure `main/packages/osv.js` flag packages with known vulnerabilities, like PyCharm's vulnerable dependency inspection, from the [OSV](https://osv.dev) database. Nothing extra is installed: the main process uses `fetch` against `https://api.osv.dev` (`POST /v1/querybatch` for every package@version in one request, then `GET /v1/vulns/{id}` for each advisory). `PYIDE_OSV_URL` replaces the base URL; `off` turns lookups off (only cached answers show, and the manual check reports it). `verify/lib.mjs` sets `off` for every suite, so tests never reach osv.dev; `verify/vulnerabilities.mjs` points it at a fake server.

- What is checked: the installed packages of the project interpreter (the Python Packages window's list) and, for each requirement of `ide.dependencies` (requirements*.txt, pyproject.toml, Pipfile), the version it stands for: an exact pin (`==1.0`, `===1.0`), else the version `uv.lock` / `poetry.lock` resolves, else the installed version when it satisfies the requirement. A range with no known version is not checked.
- Where it shows: in the Python Packages window a ⚠ on the row (`.pkg-vuln-icon`, `data-vulnerable` = count) and, for the selected package, the advisories (`#pkgVulns`: id linking to the ADVISORY reference or osv.dev, aliases such as the CVE, the database's severity label or the CVSS vector, the summary, the fixed versions) with "Upgrade to <version>" (`#pkgVulnUpgrade`), which runs the window's ordinary `pip install <name>==<version>`. The version is the first one above the installed one that none of its advisories affects, from the versions they name as fixed (OSV's range rules: `introduced`, `fixed`, `last_affected`, explicit `versions`). Requirement lines get a warning (problems source `vulnerabilities`, so also a Problems entry and a marker hover listing the advisories) and the quick fix "Upgrade to <version>" (Alt+Enter or the lightbulb), a Monaco edit that pins `==<version>` when the line pinned with `==`, otherwise sets `>=<version>` and keeps the upper bounds and `!=` clauses the version still meets, the extras and the marker. The fix is offered only while the line still holds the text that was checked, and not for Poetry or Pipfile tables, whose entries are not PEP 508 strings.
- When: after every package listing (`packagesListed`) and dependency check (`dependenciesChecked`), so also after installs, the interpreter changing and dependency files being edited. Answers are cached in `<userData>/vulnerabilities/osv-cache.json` for 24 hours per package@version and per advisory, so each is asked at most once a day, also across restarts; expired answers still show while offline. Check for Vulnerabilities (Find Action `CheckForVulnerabilities`, and the 🛡 button `data-action="checkVulnerabilities"` of the Python Packages window) asks again for everything. Errors (offline, timeout after 20 s, an HTTP error) never block anything: the window shows a quiet note (`#vulnStatus`, `data-kind="error"`) with Retry, and the IDE log a warning. A failed lookup carries `errorKind` (`network`, `timeout`, `server`), and the note shows a translated sentence for each ("Could not check for vulnerabilities: Cannot reach the vulnerability database (osv.dev). Check your internet connection or proxy settings."), with Node's message in its tooltip and the log; an unknown error still shows its message (`verify/tool-failures.mjs`).
- Trust: in Safe Mode (`ide.trusted === false`) nothing is sent automatically, and the note says so with a Check for Vulnerabilities button; cached answers still show. The request runs nothing from the project, but its content (package names, some of them possibly private, read from files the project ships) would leave the machine just because a folder was opened, which is what Safe Mode avoids; the manual check is the user's decision. The same rule lets the manual check run while the setting is off.
- Setting: `packages.vulnerabilityCheck` (Settings › Vulnerable Packages, default on). Off clears the marks and stops the automatic checks.
