- `ide.packages` (packages plugin), the extension points this plugin uses: `list()`, `busy()`, `toolWindow`, `notes()` (a host under the window's search field), `install(spec)`, `select(name)`, `render()`, `decorateRow(fn(row, pkg))`, `addDetails(fn(pkg, {busy}) -> element | null)`, `addAction({icon, title, id, run})`. `ide.vulnerabilities`: `check({manual})`, `state()`. IPC `vulnerabilities:check({root, installed, files, lock, network, force})` and `vulnerabilities:clearCache`.
- Quick fix "Upgrade to x.y.z" (IPC `vulnerabilities:upgrade`, `upgradeSource` / `upgradeRequirement` in `osv.js`): re-reads the requirement from the editor (through `` continuation lines) and changes only its specifier, keeping name, extras, marker, comment, options and spacing. Clauses the fixed version satisfies stay (`~=`, `==X.*`, `<`, `!=`), a pin becomes `==fixed`, a lower bound becomes `>=fixed`, otherwise `,>=fixed` is added. No quick fix when a written clause excludes the fix (e.g. `~=3.1.0` with 3.2.25), on `--hash` lines, URL requirements, Pipfile and Poetry tables. The cache file is validated entry by entry on load and written atomically (temp file + rename).
- Test: `node verify/vulnerabilities.mjs --unit` runs only the pure-Node part (requirement rewrites, cache loader, IPC over a damaged cache). `verify/vulnerabilities.mjs` (ports 9220-9221 for CDP, 9228 for a fake OSV server in the test process, node `http`, which counts requests) builds pyide-vuln-demo 1.0 and 1.1 wheels offline as `verify/deps.mjs` does and installs 1.0 into a temp project venv; the fixture pins `pyide-vuln-demo==1.0`. It checks the squiggle, its message and hover, the Problems entry, the row icon and the details, that each package@version and advisory is asked once and a later listing or dependency check asks nothing, the quick fix rewriting the pin to `==1.1`, Upgrade installing the 1.1 wheel and clearing the marks, the server stopped (a note, no exception), the setting off (no requests), Safe Mode (no automatic request, the note's button asks once), and in Korean after a restart with the same user data: nothing is asked again, the manual check asks once, and the texts and the settings page are Korean.

## Terminal with the project's venv

A terminal opened while the project's interpreter is a venv (`terminal.activateVenv`, on by default) starts with that venv active, as in PyCharm (`main/features/terminal.js`): its `Scripts` first on PATH and `VIRTUAL_ENV` set for every shell, and in Windows PowerShell, PowerShell 7 and Command Prompt the venv's own activate script run after the user's profile, so the prompt shows the venv's name and `deactivate` works. PowerShell reads `Activate.ps1` as a script block (`. ([scriptblock]::Create((Get-Content -Raw …))) -VenvDir …`): the execution policy, Restricted on many computers, applies to script files and would refuse running the file, and changing it would be a system change. Command Prompt runs `call …\Scripts\activate.bat` (`/k`). A terminal that runs a command (Run Anything) or opens in a remote target keeps the environment only. Test: `verify/terminal-venv.mjs` (port 9794, in CI) under a Restricted policy for its own processes: the (.venv) prompt, the venv's python, deactivate, Command Prompt, and the setting off.

## Split terminals

`renderer/features/terminal` splits a Terminal tab the way PyCharm's Terminal tool window does. A tab holds a tree of panes (a leaf is one pane, a split is `{dir: 'row'|'column', ratio, a, b}`, so splits nest), and each pane is its own PTY session (`pty:create`, unchanged in `main/features/terminal.js`).

- Split Right / Split Down: the tab's and the pane's context menus and the tool window toolbar; actions `Terminal.SplitRight`, `Terminal.SplitDown`, unbound by default as in PyCharm (menus and the toolbar tooltip show a user binding through `keyAction`). The new pane starts in the split pane's folder with the same shell (a Claude Code or installer pane splits into a plain shell). The folder is the one the pane started in, or the last one its shell reported with OSC 7 (`file://host/path`) or Windows Terminal's OSC 9;9 (`"path"`); PowerShell reports nothing unless the user's prompt prints it.
- Layout: the panes reuse the editor's `.split` / `.split-handle` classes. A divider keeps 80 px (side by side) or 40 px (stacked) on each side and resets to half on double-click; where a nested divider meets its parent's, the parent's takes the mouse. Every pane fits its own xterm to its size (`ide.util.observeSize` per pane, the fit addon), and xterm's resize reaches the PTY, so `$Host.UI.RawUI.WindowSize` / `mode con` follow the divider. A pane in a hidden tab keeps its size until it shows.
- Focus: clicking a pane, `Terminal.NextSplitter` / `Terminal.PreviousSplitter` (Next/Previous Terminal Split, unbound, in reading order of the tree, wrapping). The active pane has a 1px outline while the tab is split, and the tab title (and its rename) is the active pane's; a split tab shows its pane count.
- Closing: Close Session (pane menu, `Terminal.CloseSession`) closes one pane and gives its room to its neighbour; the last pane closes the tab; the tab's × and its menu's Close Tab close every pane. `exit` in a split pane closes it; a tab's only pane keeps its output with "Press Enter to close this tab", as before. Each close kills the pane's PTY (`pty:kill`: node-pty kills every process attached to the pane's console, so a running `ping` or `python` goes too). Closing the project, or opening another project in the window, closes all terminal tabs; if any session is still running, it first asks "Terminate Processes" through `ide.beforeProjectChange`, and Cancel keeps the project and every session. Installer sessions (`projectless`) are not tied to a project and keep running across a switch. A renderer reload (Trust Project) kills the window's sessions in main on its `did-navigate`; quitting kills the rest (`before-quit`, window `destroyed`).
- Folders from terminal output: OSC 9;9 and OSC 7 only set a pane's folder to a local drive path (OSC 7 only from an empty host, localhost or this computer's name); UNC and other network paths are ignored, and `pty:create` starts any non-local `cwd` in the home folder, so output can never make PyIDE open an SMB connection.
- Persistence: terminal tabs are not restored when a project reopens, so layouts are not either.
- Shells: the default shell is Command Prompt (also when the chosen one is unknown), chosen in Settings › Terminal › Default shell (`terminal.shell`, from the shells `pty:shells` found); with the project's interpreter a venv it opens with the venv's activate.bat run, as PowerShell runs Activate.ps1. Its name is translated (명령 프롬프트 in Korean); Claude Code's sessions and other commands written for PowerShell name PowerShell themselves. Windows PowerShell starts by its full path (`%SystemRoot%\System32\WindowsPowerShell\v1.0\powershell.exe`), so a PATH without its folder does not matter. A shell that cannot be found answers `pty:create` with `{error: 'shellNotFound', shell, shellName, file}` (not an IPC error); the tab says "The terminal could not start: PowerShell 7 was not found (<file>)." and points to New Session with… (▾) and the setting. Each session's ConPTY input pipe, output pipe and output-reader thread are released when it ends (`releasePty(proc)` in `main/features/terminal.js`: @lydell/node-pty 1.1 never destroys the input socket, and keeps the output-reader worker of a shell that exits by itself). Tests: `verify/terminal-pipes.mjs` (ports 9284-9285, the main process over `--inspect`: 25 terminals closed from the tab and 25 ended by `exit`, PipeWrap and thread counts flat), `verify/env-failures.mjs` (a missing shell, the picker).
- `ide.terminal`: `sessions()` lists every pane (`{id, name, kind, exited, pid, cwd, tab}`), `tabs()` gives `{name, active, panes, layout}`, `split('right'|'down', id?)`, `focus(id)`, `close(id?)`; `text(id)`, `send(text, id)` and `active()` (now with `pid`) work per pane. Panes are `.term-pane[data-session=<id>]` around the old `.term-session`.
- Test: `verify/terminal-split.mjs` (ports 9210-9211; in CI) splits right from the toolbar and down from the pane menu (nested), checks each pane's own shell, folder and output, drags the divider and compares `$Host.UI.RawUI.WindowSize.Width` with xterm's columns, the minimum size, Next/Previous and click focus, a user binding in menus, the tooltip and inside the terminal, closing a pane, `exit`, closing the last pane and a tab, closing the project and quitting the app with a `ping` running in each pane (the shell PIDs and their child processes, from `Win32_Process`, must be gone), and the Korean menus and action names.

## Settings export and import

File › Manage IDE Settings › Import Settings… / Export Settings… (`ImportSettings`, `ExportSettings`; plugin `settings-sync`: `renderer/features/settings-sync`, `main/features/settings-sync.js`, `main/settings-sync/archive.js` and `unzip.js`) move the user's IDE-level state between computers as one `.zip`, as in PyCharm.

- Export: a dialog lists the categories with what each holds (all ticked; Recent projects list unticked; Scratch files unticked when the folder is over 20 MB; empty ones disabled), then the Save dialog (default `settings.zip` in the home folder). Import: the Open dialog, then the archive is checked and its categories are listed (ticked); Import backs up the current state to `<userData>/settings-backups/<yyyy-mm-dd_hh-mm-ss>.zip` (same format, every category except scratch files, which an import never overwrites), applies the chosen ones and offers Restart / Not Now (language and plugin state need a restart; the rest applies at once).
- Archive: `pyide-settings.json` (`{format: 1, product: 'PyIDE', version, created, categories: {id: {count}}}`), `settings/appearance.json`, `settings/code-style.json` (`{key: value}`), `keymap.json` (the `keymap.overrides` object), `live-templates.json` (`{custom, postfixDisabled}`), `plugins.json` (`{disabled}`), `database/sources.json` (`{version, sources}`), `scratches/<path>`, `recent-projects.json` (`{projects}`). Written with `main/diagnostics/zip.js` (deflate); read with `main/settings-sync/unzip.js`, a ~90-line reader (central directory, stored and deflate, CRC and size checks) that reads entries by name. `open()` refuses an archive before inflating anything when it has more than 5000 entries, when two entries share or overlap bytes (a ZIP bomb pointing many entries at one stream), or when declared sizes exceed the limits in `archive.js` `LIMITS` (archive 512 MB, 512 MB unpacked in total, 64 MB per entry, manifest 1 MB, each category JSON 16 MB, at most 1000 scratch files and 256 MB of scratches; export keeps to the same scratch limits). Entries are inflated asynchronously (`readAsync`, `maxOutputLength` = the declared size), and one import runs at a time. No ZIP library ships at run time (yauzl and unzipper are electron/electron-builder build dependencies), and the `tar` on PATH may be Git's GNU tar (see `zip.js`), so nothing was added to package.json.
- Refused, with a translated message and nothing changed (no backup either): a file that is not a zip or is damaged, a zip without `pyide-settings.json` or with an invalid one, a newer `format` ("exported by a newer PyIDE (version, format N). Update PyIDE…"), and any entry name that is absolute, has a drive letter, a backslash, a `.`/`..` segment, `:` (alternate data streams), a reserved device name or control characters (zip-slip). Unknown but safe entries are ignored. Every chosen entry is read and checked before anything is written; scratch files are written only under `<userData>/scratches` (resolved path checked again), with `wx`.
- Applying: settings keys merge (imported wins, the user's other keys stay); keymap overrides are replaced; live templates merge per abbreviation, imported wins (the result names the replaced ones; the backup holds the old ones), postfix switches are replaced; plugin state replaces `disabled` in `plugin-state.json` (other fields kept); data sources go through `ide.database.addDataSource` (same id: the local encrypted password is kept), or straight into `sources.json` when the Database plugin is disabled; scratch files are added, an identical file is skipped and a different one with the same name is kept as `name_1.ext`; recent projects merge by path (newest 15). Imported recent projects are not trusted (`trusted-projects.json` is never touched). The renderer applies settings.json keys, because it owns that file (`ide.settings`), and calls `ide.settings.flush()` before export and import so main reads the current file.
- IPC (`main/features/settings-sync.js`): `settings:chooseExportFile(title)` / `settings:chooseImportFile(title)` show the dialogs and remember the path returned; `settings:exportInfo()`, `settings:export(file, categories)`, `settings:inspect(file)`, `settings:import(file, categories, {databaseLoaded})` accept only a path the matching dialog returned in this session (otherwise `{ok: false, code: 'notChosen'}`). Errors are `{ok: false, code: notZip | noManifest | badManifest | newerFormat | unsafeEntry | badEntry | tooLarge | tooManyEntries | overlap | busy | readFailed | failed, detail, version?, format?, size?, limit?, count?}`. `PYIDE_SETTINGS_SYNC_DIALOG` (tests only) names a JSON file `{save?, open?}` read at each dialog instead of showing it.

What is exported. A settings.json key is classified by `classify()` in `main/settings-sync/archive.js`, on export and again on import (a crafted archive cannot set interpreter paths or the update feed): its own categories first, then never a key matching the diagnostics secret pattern (`SECRET_KEY`: password, token, api key, secret, credential…), never a per-project key (one holding a path: `[\/:]`), never the excluded keys below, never a key outside the preference groups, and never a value holding an absolute Windows path. A new feature's keys are exported only if their group prefix is in `PREFERENCE` there; add the prefix or an `EXCLUDED` entry when adding settings.

| settings.json key | Category / reason |
| --- | --- |
| `appearance.*` (theme, language, presentation font size, distraction-free columns), `editor.*` (font size, line height, whitespace, soft wrap, preview tab, tab limit, breadcrumbs, inlay hints), `tools.formatOnSave`, `tools.ruffOnSave`, `tools.optimizeImportsOnSave`, `autosave.*`, `save.*`, `terminal.fontSize`, `terminal.activateVenv`, `terminal.loadEnvFile`, `tests.autoTestDelay`, `httpClient.timeoutSeconds`, `httpClient.historySize`, `viewers.*`, `dataView.colored`, `plots.showInToolWindow`, `coverage.showInEditor`, `pyconsole.showVariables`, `searchNav.find`, `searchNav.structureSort`, `sidebar.pinned`, `spellcheck.enabled`, `spellcheck.userWords`, `ai.idleUnloadMinutes`, `aiComplete.*` (enabled, model, delay, multiline), `aiEdit.*`, `aiChat.*` (model, commit language), `semantic.*` (data folder limits, confirm chunks), `localHistory.*`, `diagnostics.logLevel`, `diagnostics.logSizeMB`, `database.resultView`, `newProject.git`, `updates.checkOnStartup`, `workspace.collapsed`, `packages.vulnerabilityCheck`, `vcs.shelf.*`, `vcs.commitChecks.*` (commit options), `vcs.autoFetchMinutes`, `fileTemplates.custom` (File and Code Templates), `clipboardHistory.*` (Paste from History options, not the copied text) | Appearance and editor settings |
| `editor.tabSize`, `editor.detectIndent`, `editor.ruler`, `tools.ruffLineLength` | Code style |
| `keymap.overrides` | Keymap |
| `liveTemplates.custom`, `liveTemplates.postfixDisabled` | Live templates (user templates and edits of built-ins live here; the plugin has no other store) |
| `recentProjects` | Recent projects list (unticked by default) |
| `interpreters.extra`, `interpreter.<project>` | not exported: interpreter paths of this computer |
| `interpreters.remote` | not exported: this computer's WSL / SSH / Docker targets |
| `terminal.shell` | not exported: a shell installed here, or a path to one |
| `updates.feedUrl`, `updates.skippedVersion` | not exported: this installation's update state (and an imported feed could redirect updates) |
| `aiComplete.snoozeUntil` | not exported: transient |
| `pyconsole.history` | not exported: commands the user typed (may hold secrets) |
| `database.openConsoles`, `spellcheck.projectWordsEdit` | not exported: session state |
| `newProject.location`, `workspace.recent`, `projectTree.expanded`, `projectTree.rootsExpanded` | not exported: paths |
| `bookmarks.<project>`, `debugger.breakpoints:<project>`, `debugger.watches:<project>`, `runConfigs:<project>`, `searchNav.recentFiles.<project>`, `httpClient.env.<project>`, `workspace.args:<project>`, `frameworks.autoConfigured:<project>`, `vcs.commitChecks.testConfig:<project>`, `runAnything.history:<project>` | not exported: per-project state |

| `<userData>` file or folder | Exported? |
| --- | --- |
| `settings.json` | by key, above |
| `plugin-state.json` | Plugins (enabled state): only `disabled` |
| `plugins/` (installed plugins) | no: code; reinstall from the Marketplace |
| `database/sources.json` | Data sources without `secret` (safeStorage, readable only on this computer), password fields, URL credentials or connection state; `hasPassword: false` |
| `database/consoles/` | no: query console text per data source |
| `scratches/` | Scratch files (unticked over 20 MB) |
| `trusted-projects.json` | no: this computer's trust decisions |
| `interpreters.json` | no: interpreter probe cache |
| `installer/servers.json` | no: safeStorage passwords of local database servers |
| `remote-interp/` | no: safeStorage SSH secrets and remote caches |
| `semantic/` | no: semantic index |
| `local-history/`, `ai-chat/` | no: per-project history and chats |
| `logs/` | no: IDE log |
| `workspace/run-ps1.ps1`, `plugin-zip-*`, `settings.json.bak`, `settings.json.lock`, `*.tmp` | no: generated or temporary |
| `settings-backups/` | no: earlier backups |
| `clipboard-history.bin` | no: copied text (Paste from History, only with "keep after a restart" on; safeStorage) |
| Chromium's own profile (Local Storage, Cache, GPUCache, Cookies, Preferences…) | no |

HTTP client environments (`http-client.env.json`, `http-client.private.env.json`) are project files, not IDE settings, so they are not exported; only the HTTP client's IDE-level settings are (timeout, history size). Window bounds are not saved by PyIDE at all.

- Test: `verify/settings-sync.mjs` (ports 9200-9202; in CI). Pure Node: the key table and unsafe names. Instance A changes the font size, theme, code style, keymap (Go to Line), adds live templates, disables spellcheck, adds a PostgreSQL data source with a password, a scratch file, and machine-specific settings (interpreter path, skipped version, update feed, shell, console history); exports through File › Manage IDE Settings › Export Settings… (category ticks and labels), and the zip is read back: manifest, entries, no password or encrypted blob, no machine-specific keys, `hasPassword: false`; paths no dialog returned are refused. A crafted archive's interpreter path and update feed are dropped, and with the Database plugin off its source is written without secrets. Instance B (fresh user data, with a conflicting template, its own template, keymap change, setting and scratch) imports: the result dialog (replaced template, scratch, backup, Restart / Not Now), every value, the replaced keymap, merged templates, `plugin-state.json`, the data source without a password in the Database plugin and on disk, `scratch_1.py` beside B's `scratch.py`, the dated backup holding B's earlier state; then a damaged file, a truncated zip, a zip without a manifest, format 99 and three zip-slip names are refused with their messages, write nothing anywhere and change nothing. B restarts in Korean with the same user data: spellcheck stays unloaded, settings persist, the menu, both dialogs and the error are translated, no missing translations.

## GitHub (Share Project, Pull Requests)

`renderer/features/github` (plugin `github`, depends on `vcs`) and `main/features/github.js` work through the GitHub CLI, like PyCharm's GitHub plugin but without its own OAuth: gh holds the login. Git › GitHub has Share Project on GitHub… (`Github.Share.Project`) and View Pull Requests (`Github.View.Pull.Request`); no shortcuts.

- Finding gh: `PYIDE_GH` names the executable to use (`none` simulates a missing one); otherwise `where gh`, then the MSI, winget-links, scoop and Chocolatey locations, so a gh installed after PyIDE started is found by Refresh. `github:status` runs `gh --version` and `gh auth status` (account parsed from both the 2.40+ and older wording). A `.cmd`/`.bat` gh runs through `cmd.exe /d /s /c` with every argument quoted and its metacharacters escaped twice (cross-spawn's rule), so descriptions with `"`, `&`, `%`, `^`, `!` or Korean reach gh unchanged. gh runs with `GH_PROMPT_DISABLED`, no update notifier, no color, and a 60 s limit for reads (10 min for create/checkout).
- Trust: every channel refuses a project the user has not trusted (`main/trust.js`), and the renderer does not call them while `ide.trusted === false`; the tool window and Share explain that GitHub features are off in Safe Mode. Nothing runs gh until the tool window is shown or Share is used.
- Share Project on GitHub: needs gh logged in (else the dialog explains install or login), a repository with a commit (else it offers the Commit tool window, running `git init` first if needed), and no GitHub remote yet. The dialog asks for the repository name (folder name, made URL-safe), Private (on), remote (`origin`, or `github` when `origin` is taken) and description, then runs `gh repo create <name> --private|--public [--description <text>] --source <root> --remote <remote> --push`. Its output streams into the Git console (`ide.vcs.appendConsole`, added for this), and the notification shows the new URL. Names and remotes are validated on both sides.
- Pull Requests tool window (left, order 40): the state area explains a missing gh (Install GitHub CLI… opens the Install Manager's System Tools, where `system:gh` installs `GitHub.cli` with winget; a cli.github.com button), a logged-out gh (Log In in Terminal runs `gh auth login` in a PowerShell terminal session), and a project without a GitHub remote (Share button). Otherwise a filter (Open / Closed / Your pull requests: `gh pr list --json number,title,author,headRefName,baseRefName,state,isDraft,updatedAt,reviewDecision --limit 50 --state open|closed [--author @me]`) and the list (title, number, author, branches, time, Draft / review decision / Merged / Closed badges; keyboard via `ide.ui.listKeys`). Selecting one runs `gh pr view <n> --json title,body,author,commits,files,reviews,comments,statusCheckRollup,number,url,state,isDraft,baseRefName,headRefName,headRefOid` and shows the header ("{author} wants to merge {head} into {base}"), Open in Browser, Checkout, the description as plain text (never rendered: it is the author's Markdown), files changed with line counts, checks (check runs and commit statuses summarized as passed / failed / pending / skipped, each with a Details link), commits, reviews and comments.
- A changed file opens `ide.vcs.openTextDiff` of the head commit against its merge base with `<remote>/<base>` (else the local base branch), from the local repository only (`github:fileDiff`). When the head commit is not local the note says to use Checkout; nothing is fetched. Checkout (button or the list's context menu) is the only command that fetches: it saves all, puts a Before Checkout label in Local History, runs `gh pr checkout <n>` into the Git console and reloads like a branch checkout. Open in Browser and Details open only https URLs; `PYIDE_FAKE_OPEN_EXTERNAL=1` records them instead (`github:openedUrls`).
- Test: `node verify/github.mjs --unit` runs only the pure-Node gh lookup checks (PATH walking that skips the working folder and relative entries). `verify/github.mjs` (ports 9240-9241) with the fake gh in `verify/fixtures/github` (`gh.cmd` runs `fake-gh.js`, which answers from `FAKE_GH_STATE` and logs each argv to `FAKE_GH_LOG`; `repo create` adds the remote with git and pushes nothing). It checks that opening a project runs no gh; logged-out, missing (cli.github.com, Install Manager card) and no-remote states; Share while logged out; the exact `gh repo create` argv with Private by default and a metacharacter-laden description, the remote in the temp repository and the Git console; list argv, rows and the three filters; details (literal Markdown/HTML, header, files, reviews, comments, checks, commits); base/head diffs of a changed and an added file; Open in Browser and a check's Details; a PR whose head is not local; the Checkout argv; the Git › GitHub menu; refusals of a commit-less repository, an already shared project and an option-like name; the initial-commit offer; then in Korean an untrusted project in Safe Mode (no gh call at all, every channel refuses) and a trusted one with the list and details in Korean, no English leaks and no missing translations.

## Shelf (Shelve Changes, Unshelve)

PyCharm's shelf, in the vcs plugin (`renderer/features/vcs`, `main/features/git.js`, channels `git:shelve`, `git:shelves`, `git:unshelve`, `git:shelfDiff`, `git:shelfRename`, `git:shelfDelete`). Git › Shelve Changes… (action `ChangesView.Shelve`, also a Commit window toolbar button) lists the changed files with checkboxes, preselecting the files selected in the Commit window or else those ticked for commit, with a name (default "Changes from <date>") and "Keep changes in working tree" (off; remembered in `vcs.shelf.keep`). Whole files only: the Commit window has no per-hunk selection to reuse. The Commit tool window has Commit | Shelf tabs (`.vcs-ctab`; Git › Show Shelf, `Vcs.Show.Shelf`): shelves newest first with their file count and date, expanded to their files (status colors, Ctrl+click selects several, keyboard via `ide.ui.listKeys`, Delete deletes), double-click shows the shelved diff in the usual diff tab (a binary file says it cannot be shown), and Unshelve, Unshelve Selected Files, Rename… and Delete… (asks first) from the bar and the context menu. "Remove successfully applied files from the shelf" (`vcs.shelf.removeApplied`, default on) drops the files that came back, with or without conflicts; a shelf with no files left is deleted.

- Storage: `<userData>/shelf/<sha1 of the project folder's real path, lower-cased>/<shelf id>/changes.patch` plus `shelf.json` (`{name, time, branch, head, files: [{rel, orig?, status, binary, staged}]}`). Not `.idea/shelf` as in PyCharm: PyIDE has no `.idea`, and a patch inside the work tree would show up as a change, could be committed, and `git clean` would delete it.
- Shelving builds the patch in a private index (`GIT_INDEX_FILE` in a temp folder): `read-tree HEAD`, `add -A` of exactly the chosen paths (and a rename's old path), then `diff --cached --binary --full-index -M` with fixed prefixes and no external diff or textconv. So untracked files are new files, deletions and renames (staged or found by `-M`) are kept, binary files are base85, CR line ends stay content, and the user's own index is never touched. The patch is one `diff --git` section per file, in the order of `--name-status`, which is how a subset is applied later. Unless kept, the files are then rolled back only after `git apply -R --check` of the patch succeeds: paths in HEAD are restored (`restore --source=HEAD --staged --worktree`), the others are unstaged and deleted (and folders left empty). A file is deleted only when the patch holds all of it (a new file, or the new name of a rename whose old name is in HEAD); if git cannot say which paths are in HEAD, or any step fails, the rollback stops before touching anything and the shelf is kept. A file that was staged as new (or a staged rename) is staged again when it comes back.
- Unshelving applies to the work tree without the index (`git apply`, `--whitespace=nowarn`), all chosen sections at once when they apply cleanly, else each that does. Each remaining file goes through `git apply --3way` against the blob it was shelved from; that needs the index to match the work tree, so its current state is staged first and its old index entry is put back unless git left it unmerged. A conflicted file has markers and index stages 1-3, so the vcs-merge Conflicts dialog and merge tool resolve it like a conflicting unstash, and it stays on the shelf (only cleanly applied files are removed) so "Accept Yours" never loses the shelved change; a file that cannot be applied at all (for example deleted since) is reported with git's reason and stays on the shelf. With `core.autocrlf=true`, files git rewrites come back with git's checkout line ends, as with `git stash`.
- Test: `verify/shelve.mjs` (ports 9250-9252): a repository with modified, CRLF, binary, deleted, renamed, staged-new and untracked files; shelve a subset rolling back (work tree, open editor, the patch's contents, nothing in the repository), a shelved text, rename and binary diff, a second shelf kept in the work tree, Unshelve Selected Files (binary byte for byte, removed from the shelf), Unshelve all over a conflicting edit (Conflicts dialog, CRLF kept, Accept Theirs; staged-new staged again, untracked untracked), a file that cannot be applied staying on the shelf with the index untouched, rename with a Korean name, persistence across a restart, delete; then in Korean the dialog, tab, notifications, menu and confirmation without English leaks, the remove option off, and no missing translations.

## Background tasks and notifications

The core part is `renderer/core/notifications.js` (with `core/notifications.css`), loaded right after `ide.js` in every window, so both APIs exist before any plugin runs. The required `notifications` plugin (`renderer/features/notifications`, `main/features/notifications.js`) is the Notifications tool window and the event log.

- `ide.tasks.start({title, text?, fraction?, cancellable?, onCancel?})` -> `{id, update({fraction?, text?, title?}), done(), fail(err), active, cancelled}`. `fraction` is 0..1; leave it out (or `null`) for an indeterminate bar. A task with `onCancel` is cancellable unless `cancellable: false`. Cancel calls `onCancel` once and removes the task; later `update`/`done`/`fail` calls are ignored. `fail(err)` ends the task and, when given an error, posts an error notification "{title} failed"; `fail()` only ends it. Also `ide.tasks.list()`, `ide.tasks.cancel(id)`, `ide.tasks.showPopup()` and the event `tasksChanged(list)`.
- Status bar (order 5, `#bgTasks`): one task shows its title and progress bar; several show "N processes running" and the newest task's bar. Clicking opens the Background Tasks popup (`#bgTasksPopup`): every task with its title, bar and text, and a Cancel button for cancellable ones. Main window only.
- `ide.notifications.notify({title, text?, kind?: 'info'|'ok'|'warn'|'error', group?, actions?: [{label, run(note), valid?()}], raw?, timeout?, balloon?, onClick?, balloonClass?})` -> note `{id, entry, balloon, expire(), remove(), expired}`. It shows a balloon in the lower right (`.ide-balloons` / `.ide-balloon`; errors stay 20 s, others 10 s) and records the notification for the session. Pass translated `title`/`text`; `raw: true` marks the text as user data (program output, paths). An action stays usable in the tool window until the note expires (`note.expire()`, typically from the action itself) or its `valid()` returns false. Error notifications get a Show Log action (`OpenLogInEditor`, the IDE log). `balloon: false` records only. Also `list()`, `unread()`, `markAllRead()`, `clear()`, `hiddenGroups()`, `setGroupHidden(group, hidden)` and the event `notificationsChanged({type: 'added'|'expired'|'read'|'cleared'|'groups', entry?})`.
- Groups: a balloon of a notification with a `group` has a ⋮ menu with Don't Show Again, which adds the group to the setting `notifications.hiddenGroups`; that group's notifications are still recorded, without a balloon. The tool window's ↺ action (Show Balloons Again) clears the list. Groups in use: `Git` (vcs, vcs-merge, github, ai-chat through `ide.vcs.notify`), `Install Manager`, `Background Tasks`. Group names are English and translated where shown.
- Notifications tool window (right, order 20, pinned by default, `Notifications` / `ActivateNotificationsToolWindow`): this session's notifications newest first with time, text, kind and group, and their usable actions; an unread badge on the stripe button until the window is shown (notifications posted while it is open count as read); Mark All as Read; Clear All (`ClearAllNotifications`, also empties the event log). Below them, under Earlier sessions, the notifications the event log held at startup, without actions.
- Event log: every notification of the main window is appended to `<userData>/notifications/log.json` (title, text, kind, group, raw, time; the last 200; written at once, so a killed IDE keeps it). Channels `notifications:append`, `notifications:previous`, `notifications:clear`.
- `ide.vcs.notify(title, text, kind, raw)` is now a wrapper over `ide.notifications.notify` (group Git, extra class `vcs-note`, clicking the balloon still opens the Git console); the vcs tests look for `.ide-balloons` / `.vcs-note`.
- Migrated to `ide.tasks`, with the old status items kept as they were: semantic indexing ("Updating semantic index", progress and time left; Cancel pauses indexing, as the tool window's Pause does), Install Manager jobs ("Install: <name>", the job's progress and last log line; Cancel cancels the job; a finished install, update or removal posts an `ok` notification and a failure an error notification, both with Open Install Manager), the update download ("Downloading PyIDE <version>", not cancellable), and notebook kernels ("Starting Jupyter kernel: <file>" while starting or restarting; Cancel stops the kernel) plus the notebook's Install ipykernel. Runs are not tasks (they have their own tool window). PyIDE has no venv creation outside the New Project wizard, which shows its own progress.
- Test: `verify/tasks-notifications.mjs` (ports 9330-9332; in CI): the tasks API (progress, several tasks, popup, Cancel calling `onCancel`, done/fail), balloons and actions, the unread badge, the tool window, Show Log, Don't Show Again, a fake Install Manager item (progress, cancel from the popup, finished and failed notifications), Clear All, the event log across a restart in Korean, and semantic indexing as a task with the fake backend slowed by `PYIDE_SEMANTIC_FAKE_PAGE_MS` (Cancel pauses it).

## Periodic update checks and Report on GitHub

`renderer/features/updater` and `main/features/updater.js` check for updates again while PyIDE runs; `renderer/features/diagnostics`, `main/features/diagnostics.js` and `main/diagnostics/redact.js` (`redactPaths`) turn an internal error into a prefilled GitHub issue.

- Periodic checks: Settings › Updates › "Check for updates" (`updates.checkOnStartup`, now the switch for every automatic check) with On startup only / Daily / Weekly (`updates.checkInterval`, default `daily`). The renderer schedules: 3 s after startup, then whenever the interval has passed since the last check that read the feed. Main keeps `{lastCheck, lastAttempt, failures, lastError}` in `<userData>/updater.json`, so a restart soon after a check does not check again (On startup only checks at every start, as before, and does not retry). After failed checks the next one comes sooner but backs off: 1/24 of the interval (an hour for daily), doubling up to the interval; a check that reads the feed resets the count. Automatic checks fail quietly (logged). Nothing is checked while a check runs, while an update downloads or waits for its restart, in Power Save mode (`ide.powerSave.on()`; turning it off checks again), or while `navigator.onLine` is false (the `online` event checks again). A found update behaves as from the startup check (NSIS: background download, then Restart to Update; MSI: Download the new installer); an automatic check does not open a second Update Available dialog for a version whose dialog is still open. Settings › Updates also shows when the last check ran. `ide.updater.status()` gives the last scheduling decision (`reason`: check, recent, backoff, off, noFeed, startupOnly, checking, downloading, downloaded, powerSave, offline), its `due` time, the automatic checks of the session and the interval.
- Test variables: `PYIDE_UPDATE_INTERVAL_MS` replaces the length of a day (weekly is seven of it); `PYIDE_UPDATE_TEST=1` adds `updater:testPhase(phase)`, since a source run cannot download.
- Report on GitHub: the IDE Internal Errors details have Report on GitHub… for the selected error, and Help › Report a Problem… (`ReportProblem`) reports without one. `diagnostics:reportDraft({errorId, plugins, root})` builds the issue in main: a title from the error message, "What happened" for the user to fill in, the environment (PyIDE version and install kind, Windows edition and version, Electron and Chromium, the number of enabled plugins that are not bundled), the error's plugin, source, kind and stack, and the last 30 lines of the IDE log. Everything goes through `redactPaths`: secrets as in the log (`scrubText`), the open project and the recent projects as `<project>`, the home folder as `~`, any other `C:\Users\<name>` as `<user>`, in either slash style and in `file:///` URLs. The new-issue link (`<bugs.url>/new?title=…&body=…`, from package.json `bugs` or `repository`, https only) stays under 8,000 characters: the oldest log lines go first, then the whole log, then the stack's last frames, each with a note in the text. A preview dialog shows exactly that text with Open in Browser, Copy and Cancel; Open in Browser asks main (`diagnostics:openReport({title, body})`), which rebuilds the link itself and calls `shell.openExternal`, so the renderer cannot send the browser elsewhere. Nothing is submitted: the user does that on GitHub. With `PYIDE_DIAGNOSTICS_TEST=1` the link is recorded (`diagnostics:testOpened`) instead of opened.
- Test: `verify/report-update.mjs` (ports 9360-9362; in CI): with a local folder feed and a short interval, the startup and periodic checks, no second dialog, no check while downloading or in Power Save mode, back-off after failures and recovery, the Settings page, a recent check not repeated at startup, checks turned off, the preview (version, OS, error, `~` for the home folder, `<project>`, no token, the log tail), the link limit with a huge stack, the stubbed openExternal URL, Help › Report a Problem…, and Korean.

## Install missing packages and the virtual environment prompt

Two plugins on top of the packages plugin, like PyCharm's "Install package" quick fix and its "Create a virtual environment" notification.

- Quick fix (plugin `import-install`, depends on `lsp` and `packages`; `renderer/features/import-install`, `main/features/import-install.js`): on an import Pyright cannot resolve (`reportMissingImports`, or `reportMissingModuleSource` when only stubs exist), Alt+Enter and the lightbulb offer "Install package '<dist>'" and, when the project has a requirements.txt or a pyproject.toml with `[project]`, "Install package '<dist>' and add to <file>". The imported name is the text under the diagnostic (Pyright's message is the fallback); its distribution comes from the `DIST` table for names that differ (cv2 → opencv-python, PIL → Pillow, sklearn → scikit-learn, yaml → PyYAML, bs4 → beautifulsoup4, dateutil → python-dateutil, dotenv → python-dotenv, attr → attrs, jwt → PyJWT, serial → pyserial, Crypto → pycryptodome, docx → python-docx, magic → python-magic, win32api/win32con/… → pywin32, google.protobuf → protobuf; dotted keys match first, the bare `google` namespace gets nothing), else the top-level module with `_` as `-`. No fix for relative imports, the standard library (a built-in list: 3.14's `sys.stdlib_module_names` plus modules removed since 3.8) or a module of the project (`import-install:isLocal`: a `name.py`/`.pyi`/`.pyd`, or a folder `name` with Python files, anywhere in the worker's project walk), and none in Safe Mode. `ide.importInstall.distFor(module)` exposes the mapping.
- Installing goes through `ide.dependencies.installSpecs([dist])` (new): the packages plugin's requirement quick-fix path, so `uv pip install --python <interp>` in a uv project, pip otherwise (also for Poetry), in the Python Packages console with the command line. The add variant then reads the installed version (`import-install:version`, `importlib.metadata` in the interpreter) and calls `ide.dependencies.addRequirement({name, version})` (new; "Add to requirements": `name~=version`, or the bare name). Every successful job in the Packages console now emits `packagesChanged(interp)`, and the lsp plugin answers with `workspace/didChangeConfiguration`, so Pyright resolves imports again and the squiggle goes. Other additions: `ide.lsp.diagnostics(path)` (raw LSP diagnostics with `code`), `ide.lsp.refreshImports()`, `ide.dependencies.requirementsFile()`, `ide.packages.run(header, start)` (any process in the Packages console; resolves with the exit code), `installDeps` and `ide.dependencies.install` resolve with the exit code.
- The same installs where the error shows (`verify/import-install-run.mjs`, port 9302, offline like the quick fix test): the hover on the squiggle adds the quick fix titles as links (`command:` links to the install command, a cloud icon each), so one click installs. In Run, Debug and console output, `No module named '<name>'` (a `ModuleNotFoundError`) becomes a link with an "Install package '<dist>'" button after it (`.console-install`, drawn with CSS `::after` from `data-install`, so the console's text and copies keep only what the program printed). The button installs into the project interpreter, then reads "'<dist>' installed. Run again."; names `distFor` rejects (standard library) get no button, and a module of the project (run from the wrong folder) loses its button once `import-install:isLocal` answers.
- Virtual environment prompt (plugin `venv-setup`, depends on `packages`; `renderer/features/venv-setup`, `main/features/venv-setup.js`): a bar above the editors (`#venvBar`, `data-state` prompt / working / done / error) when a trusted project has requirements*.txt, pyproject.toml or Pipfile in its root, no virtual environment (none two levels deep, none among the listed interpreters; a project venv from which no working interpreter is listed counts as none) and no usable interpreter: `interpreter.<project>` unset, or set to an interpreter that is gone or does not start (it stays selected as invalid, see Renderer API). For a broken venv the bar says "The virtual environment … does not work" and offers Recreate .venv (`ide.venvSetup.recreate(dir)`: confirm, move the venv to the Recycle Bin, create it again in place); invalid entries never count as interpreters. Create .venv runs `<newest base interpreter> -m venv .venv` (base: a local interpreter whose folder has no pyvenv.cfg, highest version), or `uv venv .venv` in a uv project when uv is found, in the Python Packages console; then sets `interpreter.<project>`, reloads and selects the interpreter (adding it to `interpreters.extra` only when the scan would not find it), and installs each dependency file that is not satisfied through `ide.dependencies.install`. Choose Interpreter… opens Settings › Python Interpreter, × hides the bar for the session, Don't Ask Again sets `venvPrompt.ignore:<project>` (a per-project key, never exported). Tools › Create Virtual Environment… (`CreateVirtualEnvironment`, no shortcut) asks for the location (`.venv`, else `venv`, else `.venvN`), the base interpreter (or uv) and whether to install the dependencies. Safe Mode: no bar, the action explains, and `venv-setup:create` refuses an untrusted project.
- Tests (offline, in CI): `verify/import-install.mjs` (ports 9300-9301) builds pyide-imp-demo and a stand-in PyYAML wheel with zipfile and installs with `PIP_NO_INDEX`/`PIP_FIND_LINKS` into a temp project `.venv`: the mapping table, the fix and its add variant on `import cv2`, none for a module in `tools/`, a package in `lib/` or distutils, the install in the console and the squiggle gone, `PyYAML~=6.0.99` appended to requirements.txt, and the Korean titles. `verify/venv-prompt.mjs` (ports 9305-9308): the bar for a missing configured interpreter, Create .venv (venv made, selected, saved, requirements installed offline, importable), no interpreter configured, Choose Interpreter…, close, Don't Ask Again across a restart, the dialog without installing, Korean bar and dialog, and Safe Mode (no bar, no install fix, main refuses). `verify/interpreter-health.mjs` (port 9274): a saved interpreter that is missing or does not start (invalid entry, status bar, notification, Choose Interpreter…, Recreate .venv), one that starts but is not listed, a Python 2.7 provider entry. uv is not exercised by the tests.

## Commit checks and auto-fetch

Commit checks (plugin `vcs-checks`: `renderer/features/vcs-checks`, `main/features/vcs-checks.js`; depends on `vcs`) are PyCharm's commit options: the gear after the Commit buttons (`#vcsCommitOptions`) opens a Before Commit popup with Reformat code, Optimize imports, Analyze code, Check TODO and Run tests: plus a run configuration of the project. They run before every commit, Commit and Commit and Push alike, through `ide.vcs.addBeforeCommit(fn)` (the vcs plugin calls each hook once the files and message are valid; `false` stops the commit; it saves all and refreshes afterwards, committing the same files) and show a progress strip after the buttons ("Analyzing code… 3/4", `#vcsChecksProgress`) whose Cancel stops the step in progress and the commit; the same run is an `ide.tasks` task when that API exists. Order: Optimize imports, Reformat code (imports first, so the reformat tidies what removing one leaves), Analyze code, Check TODO, Run tests.

- Optimize imports / Reformat code: the tooling plugin's `tooling:optimizeImports` and `tooling:format` (ruff, `tools.ruffLineLength`) on each Python file being committed and nothing else; an open document is edited as one undo step and saved, a closed file is rewritten in its own encoding. A file ruff cannot parse is left as it is (a warning lists it). Disabled without the tooling plugin.
- Analyze code: `vcs-checks:analyze` runs the bundled Pyright CLI (`node_modules/pyright/index.js --outputjson --pythonpath <project interpreter>`, as Node through Electron) on the committed Python files as saved, since the language server only analyzes open files; plus ruff's lint as the editor shows it (warnings; a syntax error is an error) while `tools.ruffOnSave` is on. When ruff cannot run (`tooling:check` answers `{error}`, for example a broken ruff.toml) the check warns once, "ruff could not check the files being committed" with ruff's error, instead of treating the files as clean; Pyright failing to run is reported the same way. Errors stop the commit with "Code analysis found problems…: N errors, M warnings" and Review / Commit Anyway / Cancel; warnings alone do not. Review opens a tab listing the problems by file (`#vcsChecksResults`, a row opens the file at its line). `vcs-checks:cancel` kills a run.
- Check TODO: TODO and FIXME comments (PyCharm's default patterns, after `#` or `//` by file type) on lines the changes add that the replaced lines did not already have (`ide.vcs.diffLines` against HEAD). Same dialog and Review.
- Run tests: the chosen configuration runs through `ide.run.runConfig`, as Run would start it; it failed when its test status says failed (`.tr-status[data-state]`) or, for another type, its tab ended failed. Review shows the Run window with that session. A missing configuration skips the check with a warning.
- Settings: `vcs.commitChecks.reformat`, `.optimizeImports`, `.analyze` (default on), `.todo` (default on), `.runTests` per IDE, as in PyCharm; `vcs.commitChecks.testConfig:<project>` per project (a run configuration id). Choosing a configuration turns Run tests on. `ide.vcsChecks`: `checks()`, `testConfig()`, `running()`, `cancel()`, `openOptions()`. Other vcs extension points added: `ide.vcs.addCommitOption(node)`, `committing()`.

Auto-fetch (vcs plugin and `main/features/git.js`): every `vcs.autoFetchMinutes` minutes (default 10, fractions allowed, 0 = off; Settings › Git) the renderer asks `git:backgroundFetch`, first within 20 s of opening the project. Main runs `git fetch --all --prune --quiet --no-recurse-submodules` with `gc.auto=0` and `maintenance.auto=false` only when the project is trusted, is a repository with remotes, no IDE git operation is running for it (every `git:` channel that is not a read counts; push and pull wait for a fetch in flight, since two fetches writing remote refs fail on ref locks) and no `index.lock` is held. It never prompts: `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, empty `GIT_ASKPASS`/`SSH_ASKPASS` and `-c core.askPass=` (no askpass program, including one the user configured), `GCM_INTERACTIVE=never` and `-c credential.interactive=never` (credential helpers answer from their stores only; git then fails with "unable to get password from user"), `ssh -o BatchMode=yes` unless the user set an ssh command, and a 120 s limit (`PYIDE_GIT_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS`). A failure goes to the IDE log (`#git`) only, and the next try waits twice as long each time, up to an hour; a success resets it and refreshes the status. A busy repository is tried again within 30 s. The toolbar shows the current branch's incoming and outgoing commits after the branch widget (`#vcsSync`, "↓3 ↑1", hidden when both are 0 or there is no upstream), with a tooltip (counts, upstream, last fetch) and a menu: Update Project…, Push… (when ahead), Fetch (a fetch the user asked for reports its errors). `ide.vcs.autoFetch`: `state()` (`{attempts, failures, skipped, lastFetch, lastError, nextDelay, running}`), `now()`.

- Test: `verify/commit-checks.mjs` (ports 9310-9312 for CDP, 9317 a local server answering 401, 9318 nothing listening; in CI). Pure Node first: `backgroundFetch` against the 401 server with a recording askpass and credential helper in the test's git config (no askpass, helper asked with `GCM_INTERACTIVE=never`), again with the machine's system credential helper (Git Credential Manager) when there is one, an unreachable https remote, a held index lock, no remote, an untrusted project. Then a project cloned from a bare repository in a temp folder: the popup and its defaults, Esc; Optimize imports + Reformat code on a closed new file and an open document with unsaved edits (committed content equals ruff's output, a file not being committed untouched, progress in order); Analyze code (2 errors, 1 warning: Cancel, Review with rows that open the file, Commit Anyway); Check TODO (2 new items, an unchanged one not counted; Review; Commit and Push stopping, then committing and pushing to the bare remote); Run tests (a failing pytest configuration stops the commit, Review shows the Run window, a slow run is cancelled from the strip, passing tests commit); a restart keeps the options and the project's configuration; a second clone pushes three commits and auto-fetch at 0.05 min shows ↓3 ↑1 with its tooltip, whose Update Project pulls them; an unreachable remote at 0.02 min backs off (1.2 s × 2ⁿ, no try before then) with no notification or dialog and a WARN line in the log; the 401 remote fails quietly (no askpass, dialog, notification or window); 0 stops it; then the Korean popup, tooltip, menu, analysis dialog, review tab and Git settings page without English, and every commit-checks string translated.

- Safety: Reformat code and Optimize imports run ruff on the text in memory; only after the last rewrite step is one Local History checkpoint "Before Commit Checks" saved with every file's old text, then open documents get one undoable edit and other files are written. A notification ("Reformatted N files before commit", a warning when the commit then stopped) offers Undo, which restores every file in one step, and Show Local History. Pyright runs in chunks of at most 30,000 characters of command line (Windows allows 32,767), relative paths, results merged; `PYIDE_PYRIGHT_CLI` swaps in a stand-in for tests. Pyright and background fetch process trees are killed on Cancel, when their window reloads or closes, and on quit; the fetch time limit kills git-remote-https too. Test ports 9313 (quit instance) and 9316 (a silent server).
## Before launch tasks and File Watchers

Before launch (run plugin, `renderer/features/run/index.js` + `main/features/run.js`), PyCharm's "Before launch" list in Run/Debug Configurations:

- A configuration's `beforeLaunch` is `[{kind: 'config', id} | {kind: 'tool', name?, program, args?, cwd?}]`, kept in the configuration JSON (`runConfigs:<project>` in the user's settings, never in the project). Configurations saved before the field existed have none; malformed entries are dropped when the project opens. The form's Before launch section adds (＋: Run Another Configuration, listing the other configurations, or Run External Tool), removes, and moves tasks up and down; a tool's Name, Program, Arguments and Working directory are edited below the list. "Save all files" stays the `autosave.beforeRun` setting (saves before every launch), and Activate tool window is not offered.
- `runConfig` and `debugConfig` (Alt+Shift+F9, gutter Debug, frameworks, scratches) run the tasks in order first. Run Another Configuration runs it in its own Run tab (with its own before launch tasks) and waits for its process to exit; Run External Tool runs in a Run tab named after the tool, with the command line and output there (`run:startTool`, the tab's Rerun runs the tool again). A non-zero exit, a Stop, a missing configuration, a program that cannot start or a missing working directory stops the launch: later tasks and the configuration do not run, and an error notification ("'Main' was not started", the task and the exit code) is posted. Progress is an `ide.tasks` background task ("Before launch: 'Main'", the running task as its text; cancelling it stops the running task). Run or Debug pressed again while a configuration's tasks run starts them over: `runBeforeLaunch` registers the newest launch of each configuration, and the earlier launch ends quietly when its current task returns (the newer launch reuses that task's Run tab and stops it), with no "was not started" notification; a dependency configuration replaced the same way is handled alike (`verify/run-debug-edges.mjs`).
- Cycles (A before B before A, or longer) are refused when added in the dialog (an error under the list, nothing added), on OK (the dialog stays open on the configuration in the cycle), and at launch (a hand-edited settings file: notification naming the cycle, nothing runs).
- Sessions: `session.finished` resolves with the exit code of the session's current run (-1 when it did not start, was replaced by a rerun or the tab closed); `exited(id, code)` takes the code (a type that leaves it out counts as success, a killed process, `null`, as failure). New `ide.run` members: `runBeforeLaunch(cfg)`, `findCycle(id, configs)`, `outputSession(key, title)` (a Run tab that is not a configuration's, kept with its console; a new one is selected when no tab is, without showing the window), `show(session)`, `notifyError(title, text)`.
- Macros (main/features/run.js `expandMacros`, shared with File Watchers), written `$Name$` or `${Name}`, case-insensitive, unknown ones left as written: `ProjectDir` (`ProjectFileDir`), `ProjectName`, `FilePath`, `FileName`, `FileNameWithoutExtension`, `FileNameWithoutAllExtensions`, `FileExt`, `FileDir`, `FileRelativePath`, `FileDirRelativeToProjectRoot`, `FileParentDir`, `PyInterpreterPath`, `PyInterpreterDirectory`. For before launch tools the file is the active editor's. Arguments are split like the script parameters (quotes group) before expansion, so a path with spaces stays one argument. A bare program name is looked up in the absolute PATH folders with PATHEXT (never the working folder); a `.cmd`/`.bat` (npm shims such as `sass.cmd`) runs through `cmd.exe /d /s /c`: arguments are quoted for the C runtime and their cmd metacharacters escaped once, or twice for npm shims (`node_modules.bin`, or a cmd-shim forwarding `%*`, `isNpmShim`), with `NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath=1` so cmd never runs a program from the project folder in place of one on PATH. Before launch tools, like File Watchers, are refused in a project that is not trusted. A tool's working directory defaults to the project folder. My Workspace's .bat launchers (`workspace:start`) and launch.json programs that resolve to a `.cmd`/`.bat` go through the same `toolCommand` (cmd metacharacters in the path escaped, no `call`, so a project path holding `&`, `^` or `%` works); a .bat's extra arguments are passed as typed (cmd syntax), and a bare launch.json program name is resolved on PATH with PATHEXT (`resolveProgram`), so `npm` runs `npm.cmd` with the npm-shim escaping and an .exe is spawned directly. Every `.cmd`/`.bat` started through `toolCommand` (launch.json programs, My Workspace .bat launchers, before launch tools, File Watchers) runs after `chcp 65001>nul` when the file is UTF-8 (plain ASCII included), so cmd reads it and writes its own messages in UTF-8 (in English, under that code page); a batch file that is not UTF-8 keeps the console code page and its output is read as CP949 (`verify/tool-failures.mjs`).

File Watchers (plugin `file-watchers`, depends on `run`; `renderer/features/file-watchers/`, `main/features/file-watchers.js`):

- Settings › File Watchers (a top-level page next to Tools; the Tools page is not a group here) lists the project's watchers with an Enabled box, ＋ (templates: Custom, Black `$PyInterpreterPath$ -m black $FilePath$`, pyside6-uic and pyuic5 for `*.ui` → `ui_<name>.py`, Sass for `*.scss;*.sass`), − and Copy, and edits the selected one: Name, File type (`*.ui;*.scss`; a pattern with a slash such as `src/**/*.py` matches the project-relative path), Scope (Project Files, or a folder relative to the project), Program, Arguments, Working directory (default `$FileDir$`), "Auto-save edited files to trigger the watcher" (off), "Trigger the watcher on external changes" (off), Show console (Always, On error (default), Never). Values follow the dialog's OK/Apply/Cancel. ruff format on save is Actions on Save, not a template.
- Storage: `fileWatchers:<project>` in the user's settings. The key holds a path, so Settings export/import and sync skip it (main/settings-sync/archive.js); a cloned repository cannot define watchers.
- Triggers: `fileSaved` for a matching file under the project (explicit and auto saves), an edit of a matching open file when the watcher auto-saves (saved after 1 s), and, with the external option, `watch:changed` from main/project-watch.js for files that exist (events within 2.5 s of the IDE's own save of that file or of a watcher run on it are those writes and are ignored). `.git`, `__pycache__`, `node_modules`, `venv`/`.venv` and tool caches never trigger.
- Running: per file, triggers wait 400 ms without another one (rapid saves give one run); while a file's watchers run, new triggers for it queue and run once after, so runs for one file never overlap (other files run in parallel). `fileWatchers:run` expands the macros with that file and starts the program; afterwards the open editor reloads the file if the watcher rewrote it. Output goes to the "File Watchers" tab of the Run window (command line, output, exit line). A non-zero exit or a program that cannot start posts an error notification ("File watcher 'Black' failed", group File Watchers, Show Console action) and shows the console unless Show console is Never.
- Trust: in Safe Mode (`ide.trusted === false`) nothing triggers and the settings page says why; `fileWatchers:run` refuses a project `main/trust.js` does not trust and a file outside the project.
- `ide.fileWatchers`: `list()`, `history()` (`{watcher, file, code, error?}` per finished run), `busy()`, `templates()`.

Long working folders (run plugin, `workingDir(cwd, fallbacks)` in main/features/run.js): Windows starts no process in a folder longer than 258 characters (259 and more give spawn ENOENT), whatever the long path setting. `run:start`, `debug:start` and `tests:start` (local interpreters) replace such a folder with its 8.3 short form (the same folder, from `cmd /c for %I in ("…") do @echo %~sI`, about 35 ms, only for long folders) or, when the volume keeps no short names or that is still too long, with the project root (else the home folder), so every caller (frameworks, Run Anything, visual scripts) starts. The Run console (Python configurations) and the Debug console ask `run:workingDir` first (`ide.run.workingDir(cwd, write)`) and say when another folder stands in: "The working directory is longer than Windows allows (N characters): <path>" / "The program runs in <root> instead." (`verify/run-debug-edges.mjs`). A test run (`tests:start`) falls back the same way but prints no such line; a pytest run from such a stand-in folder gets `--rootdir=<project root>` (unless its arguments set one): from an 8.3 folder pytest otherwise picks a rootdir above the project, such as the temp folder, and collects what else is there. Debug gives a local debugpy the script and breakpoint paths in long form (`longPath` in main/features/debug.js, also for `setBreakpoints` sent later through `debug:request`) and names the script's folder (for `-m`, a Module name or test run, the working folder's long form) as pydevd's auto-reload folder: pydevd cannot resolve an 8.3 path whose long form is longer than MAX_PATH and printed "unable to get real case" tracebacks for the script and for an 8.3 working folder (`verify/run-debug-edges.mjs`, which also debugs a Module name configuration and pytest in a 310-character folder; CI's TEMP is an 8.3 path). Remote interpreters keep the IDE's form, which their path mappings use. Before launch tools and File Watchers (`startTool` in main/features/run.js, behind `run:startTool` and `fileWatchers:run`) fall back the same way, with no line about it, and report the folder they ran in as `cwd`. When a local Debug's working folder is in 8.3 form, `main/features/debugpy-long-paths` goes first on PYTHONPATH and its `sitecustomize.py` installs long names (`pyide_long_paths.py`) before `python -m debugpy` starts, then runs the interpreter's own sitecustomize if there is one: modules imported through the working folder (`''` and its absolute path, which debugpy puts first on sys.path) or through entries built from it (pytest's `<folder>\tests`, partly resolved) had 8.3 file names pydevd cannot resolve. A path hook maps every sys.path entry with a short name to an import finder rooted at its long form (GetLongPathNameW on the `\\?\` form), when Python can read it there (long paths enabled), so those modules get long names and breakpoints in them stop, test files in subfolders included (`verify/run-debug-edges.mjs`). It does not change to the long folder: a process whose current folder is past MAX_PATH cannot start a child. The first version (0.2.9 development) started debugpy through a `-c` launcher instead, and pydevd then described an exception that ends the program with "(note: full exception trace is shown but execution is paused at: <module>)", because the outermost frame was the launcher's; with an 8.3 TEMP (as on CI) that broke the exception strip and Visual Scripting's exception marks (`verify/debugger-exceptions.mjs`, `verify/visual-script-debug.mjs`). Known limit: with long paths disabled the 8.3 names stay, and pydevd still prints "unable to get real case" for such a module.
- Test: `verify/run-automation.mjs` (ports 9350-9351, in CI). The external program is `verify/fixtures/run-automation/tool.js` run with the test's own Node (logs its start/end, cwd and arguments as JSON lines, sleeps, exits with a given code). It adds Run Another Configuration and Run External Tool in the dialog and checks the saved JSON, progress task, order (dependency finished → tool → configuration), macro expansion, Run tabs and tool output, Move Up and the new order, a failing tool stopping the rest with a notification, cycle refusal (dialog, launch, OK), Debug running the tasks and a failure stopping it; then File Watchers set up in Settings (templates, defaults, stored in settings.json), a save with all file macros and `$FileDir$`, five quick saves → one run, saves during a slow run → one queued run and no overlap, external change only with the option on and the IDE's own save not counted twice, an error notification with the console shown, a disabled watcher, and Safe Mode in the renderer. A second instance opens an untrusted copy in Safe Mode in Korean: no watcher runs on save or disk change, the main process refuses `fileWatchers:run`, and the File Watchers page, templates menu, Before launch section, Add Task menu and failure notification have no English UI text; every `tr()` string of both plugins has a Korean entry.

## Font zoom, Sync with OS theme, Power Save Mode and tool window layouts

- Font size (settings plugin): Ctrl+Mouse Wheel over any Monaco editor changes the editor font size by 1 (a 100 px notch, or small trackpad deltas adding up to 50 px), as PyCharm's "Change font size with Ctrl+Mouse Wheel in: All editors" (the only scope here): every editor follows and the size is stored as `editor.fontSize` (6-48), so it survives a restart. Settings › Editor › General › Mouse Control has the switch (`editor.wheelZoom`, default on). Actions `EditorIncreaseFontSize`, `EditorDecreaseFontSize`, `EditorResetFontSize` (unbound, as in PyCharm's Windows keymap) and `EditorIncreaseFontSizeGlobal` / `EditorDecreaseFontSizeGlobal` / `EditorResetFontSizeGlobal` (Alt+Shift+. and Alt+Shift+,, PyCharm's keys) all change every editor. Reset goes back to the default size (14). A hint at the editor's top right (`#fontZoomHint`) shows "Font size: 15pt" with "Reset to 14pt" for 2.5 s. During Presentation Mode the wheel and the actions change `appearance.presentationFontSize` (8-72, default 24) instead, so leaving the mode still restores the editor font. `ide.fontZoom`: `size()`, `set(n)`, `reset()`.
- Sync with OS: `appearance.theme` takes `'system'` besides `'dark'` and `'light'` (Settings › Appearance, the gear's Theme menu and View › Appearance list Dark, Light, Sync with OS). A new install (no settings file and no old localStorage settings, `renderer/core/i18n.js`) starts with `'system'`; existing settings keep their value, Dark when it was never chosen. The OS mode is Electron's `nativeTheme.shouldUseDarkColors`: core channel `theme:system` and the broadcast `theme:systemChanged({dark})` on nativeTheme `updated` (`main/system-theme.js`), so the theme switches live. `ide.theme`: `current()` ('dark' | 'light'), `preference()`, `systemDark()`, `menuItems()`; event `themeChanged(theme)` when the shown theme changes (also when only the OS changed); code that recolors for the theme (terminal, viewers' canvases, Git gutter marks, the rainbow-brackets sample plugin) listens to it rather than to the `appearance.theme` setting.
- Tests and the OS theme: CDP `Emulation.setEmulatedMedia` (prefers-color-scheme) does not reach nativeTheme in the main process, so it cannot drive "Sync with OS". `PYIDE_OS_THEME=dark|light` starts the app with `nativeTheme.themeSource` set to that mode and adds `theme:testSetOs(mode)`, which changes it later through the same `updated` event a real OS switch sends. `verify/lib.mjs` sets `PYIDE_OS_THEME=dark` for every suite, so new-install test instances stay dark whatever the machine uses.
- Power Save Mode (plugin `power-save`; state in the core): File › Power Save Mode (action `TogglePowerSave`, checked while on), kept across restarts in `powerSave.enabled`. `ide.powerSave.on()` / `set(on)` and the event `powerSaveChanged(on)` let any feature respect it. While on, the status bar shows a 절전 모드 / Power Save Mode indicator (`#powerSaveStatus`, its menu turns it off) and these pause: semantic indexing (`semantic:powerSave`; runs wait before the walk and between batches, apart from the user's Pause; `semantic:indexInfo` reports `powerSave`), automatic AI completion (`ide.aiComplete.mode()` is `'powerSave'`, the explicit Trigger AI Completion still works), next edit suggestions (`ide.aiEdit.nesActive()`; the setting stays as it was), spell checking (markers cleared, `ide.spellcheck.enabled()` false), inlay hints (hidden, no Pyright requests), automatic vulnerability checks going online (`ide.vulnerabilities.autoCheck()`; cached answers still show, the manual check still runs) and git auto-fetch (the manual fetch still runs). Turning it off resumes each of them. Pyright already analyzes open files only (`diagnosticMode: openFilesOnly`) and has no lighter background mode, so it is left as is.
- Tool window layouts (plugin `layouts`): Window › Layouts lists Save Current Layout As… (`StoreNewLayout`, a Name dialog), Save Changes in Current Layout (`StoreDefaultLayout`), Restore Current Layout (`RestoreDefaultLayout`, Shift+F12 as in PyCharm), Restore Default Layout (`RestoreFactoryDefaultLayout`), then each saved layout as a submenu with Restore, Rename… and Delete; the current one is checked. A layout is, per area (left, bottom, right), whether it is open, the window it shows (kept when closed) and its size, plus the pinned set (`sidebar.pinned`, which decides the stripe buttons). Layouts are IDE-level: setting `toolWindowLayouts` (a list of `{name, layout}`, read field by field) and `toolWindowLayouts.current`. The default layout is the first-run one: left and bottom open with their lowest-ordered windows (Project, Run) at the CSS sizes, the right side closed, the default pinned set (`ide.sidebar.defaultPinned()`). Restoring leaves an appearance mode first. `ide.layouts`: `capture()`, `apply(layout)`, `defaultLayout()`, `list()`, `current()`, `save(name)`, `saveAs()`, `restore(name)`, `restoreDefault()`, `rename(name)`, `remove(name)`, `menu()`.
- Test: `verify/ui-comfort.mjs` (ports 9320-9323; in CI; fixture `verify/fixtures/ui-comfort`). A new install on a dark OS starts on Sync with OS; the OS switching to light and back through nativeTheme changes the theme and Monaco at once, an explicit Dark ignores the OS, the View › Appearance items and the Settings options. Ctrl+wheel (CDP `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` with the Ctrl modifier) up and down in two split editors, trackpad deltas, no Ctrl, the switch off; the actions, their keys and Alt+Shift+. / Alt+Shift+,; Presentation Mode (the wheel changes its size, leaving restores the editor font). Power Save Mode from the File menu: the indicator and check mark, no AI completion request or ghost text while typing, next edit suggestions, spell check markers gone, vulnerability auto check off, a new file not indexed, no git auto-fetch to a local bare remote; turning it off resumes each. Layouts: save through the menu and dialog, Restore Default Layout equal to the first-run layout (areas, sizes, pinned, stripe buttons), Restore from the submenu equal to the saved one, Shift+F12, Delete. A restart keeps the font size, Power Save Mode (everything starts paused) and the layouts. A Korean new install on a light OS starts light and shows the menus, indicator, dialog, hint and pages in Korean; an existing install without a theme stays Dark on a light OS.

## Paste from History, Run Anything, File and Code Templates

Three plugins with small hooks in the core (`renderer/core/ide.js`): `clipboardWritten(text)` (emitted for every `clipboard:writeText` through `ide.api.invoke`, whose wrapper is now `ide.api`), the Double Ctrl key `'Ctrl Ctrl'`, `ide.newFileHooks`, `ide.treeNewItems` and `ide.trustProject()`.

- Double Ctrl (`'Ctrl Ctrl'`, shown as "Double Ctrl"): Ctrl pressed and released twice with no other key, mouse button, wheel or window blur in between, the second press within 350 ms of the first release (event timestamps). It fires on the second release, so Ctrl+C, Ctrl+S, Ctrl+click or a held Ctrl never count, and Double Shift is untouched. It is a keymap key like `'Shift Shift'`: `ide.keymap.isValid`, the Keymap page's capture box records it, Remove Shortcut unbinds it.
- Paste from History (plugin `clipboard-history`, `main/features/clipboard-history.js`; action `PasteMultiple`, Ctrl+Shift+V, Edit menu and the editor's context menu): the last `clipboardHistory.limit` (30) texts copied or cut in PyIDE, newest first, a text copied again moves to the top. Sources: `copy`/`cut` events anywhere in the page (Monaco's data, including its per-caret texts and whole-line copies, text fields, DOM selections) and `clipboardWritten` (Copy Path, terminal, consoles, viewers). Text copied in other applications is read from the system clipboard when the window gets focus only with `clipboardHistory.captureSystem` (off: PyCharm does it, but it would also keep passwords that password managers clear a few seconds later). The popup (in `#dialogHost`) filters by words as you type, shows a preview, pastes with Enter or a double click into the editor it was opened from (`editor.trigger('keyboard', 'paste', {text, pasteOnNewLine, multicursorText})`: a copy with N carets pastes one piece per caret into N carets; Monaco spreads a text with one line per caret; otherwise every caret gets the text), puts the text back on the system clipboard, and Delete at the end of the filter removes the selected item. The history is session-only; `clipboardHistory.persist` (off) keeps it in `<userData>/clipboard-history.bin`, encrypted with safeStorage, and turning it off deletes the file. Settings › Editor › Clipboard holds the three options and Clear History. `ide.clipboardHistory`: `items()`, `add(text, {multicursor, wholeLine})`, `remove(text)`, `clear()`, `open()`, `paste(text, editor)`.
- Run Anything (plugin `run-anything`, depends on run and terminal; action `RunAnything`, Double Ctrl, Run menu): a popup that runs run configurations (Enter; Shift+Enter debugs when the configuration can be debugged), `python <file> [args]` (an unsaved Python configuration named after the command, cwd the project root), `python -m …` / `python -c …` (the new `pythonCommand` configuration type, Python Command, whose arguments go to `run:start`; `-m` can be debugged), `pytest …` (the tests plugin's pytest type, else `python -m pytest`), all in the Run tool window with the project interpreter, and any other command (`pip install x`, `git status`, `echo`) in a new Terminal session (`ide.terminal.open({command})`, so the project venv is on PATH). Holding Shift shows Debug in the popup. Suggestions as you type: the typed command, run configurations, recent commands, the project's Python files (`python <file>`, test files after `pytest `), and pip / `python -m` / pytest templates; Tab completes the input with the selected row, Delete removes a recent command. History: the last 50 commands per project in `runAnything.history:<project>` (not exported). In Safe Mode nothing runs: a "Trust Project?" dialog offers core's Trust Project (`ide.trustProject()`, the trust bar's button, which reopens the page), and the command comes back in the popup afterwards (`sessionStorage`), waiting for Enter. `ide.runAnything`: `open({query})`, `execute(cmd, {debug})`, `classify(cmd)`, `history()`, `suggest(text, files)`.
- File and Code Templates (plugin `file-templates`, `main/features/file-templates.js` for `${USER}`): Settings › Editor › File and Code Templates lists Python Script (empty by default, PyCharm's file header place), Python Package (the new package's `__init__.py`), Python Unit Test, HTML File, JSON File, Markdown File, Text File and user templates (+, −, Reset to Default, name, extension, text, the variable list). Stored IDE-level in `fileTemplates.custom` (`{name: {ext?, body?}}`: edits of built-ins and user templates; exported with the appearance and editor settings). Variables: `${NAME}`, `${USER}`, `${DATE}` (yyyy-mm-dd), `${TIME}` (HH:mm), `${YEAR}`, `${MONTH}`, `${DAY}`, `${HOUR}`, `${MINUTE}`, `${PROJECT_NAME}`, `${PACKAGE_NAME}` (the folder's dotted path from the project root), `${PRODUCT_NAME}`, `${DS}`; `$NAME` without braces for these, `\$` for a literal dollar; any other `${VAR}` is asked for in a dialog (Cancel creates nothing). The project tree's context menu has New › File, Directory (core's), Python File, Python Package (`a.b` makes nested packages), the other templates and Edit File Templates…; core's New Python File (Alt+Insert, toolbar, File menu) takes the Python Script template through `ide.newFileHooks` (`async (path) => text | false | undefined`). `ide.fileTemplates`: `templates()`, `find(name)`, `render(template, path, {name, vars})`, `create(name, dir, fileName?)`.
- Test: `verify/editing-comfort.mjs` (ports 9340-9342; in CI; fixture `verify/fixtures/editing-comfort`). Instance A: Ctrl+C / Ctrl+X in Monaco and Copy Path recorded newest first, de-duplication, the limit, other applications only with the option on, Ctrl+Shift+V (filter, Enter at the caret, Delete, Esc), a three-caret copy pasted into three carets, a text at every caret, a whole-line paste; Double Ctrl opens Run Anything while Ctrl+C/Ctrl+V, a Ctrl tap then Ctrl+S (which still saves), slow taps and a click between taps do not, Double Shift still opens Search Everywhere, removing and rebinding the shortcut; a run configuration, `python app.py extra`, `python -m modx` and `echo marker-RA1` with their output in the Run tool window and the Terminal, the history, file suggestions, Tab, the Debug mode, pip suggestions; New Python File with the empty and a header template, New › Python File in a sub-package (every variable), Python Package, HTML File, a user template with a custom variable (Cancel, then Korean text), edit and Reset to Default in Settings, and the settings export snapshot holding the templates. Instance B restarts in Korean with the kept, encrypted history and checks the Korean popups, New submenu and settings pages (no English, no missing translations) and that turning the option off deletes the file. Instance C opens a Safe Mode project: Run Anything asks to trust it, Cancel runs nothing, Trust Project reopens the page with the command waiting, Enter runs it.

## Main window layout rules

- Status bar (`ide.registerStatusItem`): widgets stay on one line (`white-space: nowrap`). When they do not all fit, the file path shrinks with an ellipsis (down to 120px), then the widgets with the highest order get `.status-overflow` (hidden) until the rest fit; the caret position, line separator (`#statusLineSeparator`, placed before the encoding rather than in `#statusItems`), encoding and interpreter always show. Give an important widget a low order.
- Toolbar: the project and branch names end in an ellipsis (`clamp()` widths); the branch widget's tooltip holds the full name.
- The editor area keeps `min(320px, 35vw)` of width beside open side panels (the panels shrink to their 140px minimum first) and some height under a tall bottom panel.
- Popups (Search Everywhere, Run Anything, Paste from History, Find Action) and context menus never run past the window: their lists shrink and scroll. Balloons stack from the status bar and drop the oldest once the stack would reach the toolbar (they stay in Notifications).
- Plugin stylesheets share one page: never define a bare class rule another plugin also defines (prefix classes with something unique to the plugin).
