Theme colours: `--gray1`..`--gray12` including `--gray6`, `--gray8` (secondary text, `.muted`) and `--gray10`, defined for Dark, Light and the Light theme's dark toolbar.
- `verify/ui-inspect-main.mjs` measures all of this in English and Korean, Dark, Light and Sync with OS, at 1600x1000, 1280x800 and 900x560 (CDP `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride`), and checks the stylesheets statically (every `var(--x)` defined, no class defined by two plugins).

## Dialog and Settings layout rules

- Footer buttons: the primary button comes first, then Cancel or Close, then extra actions (OK, Cancel, Apply; Attach, Cancel, Refresh; Evaluate, Close, Add Watch). Find in Files and File Cache Conflict keep PyCharm's own order.
- Keyboard focus inside a dialog shows the IDE's blue ring (`.dialog :focus-visible` in `renderer/core/styles.css`), never the browser's default (orange on Windows in the Light theme).
- Settings rows (`ctx.ui.fieldRow`): a 190px label, a control of 120-240px that is the same width in every row, and the hint after it; in windows under 940px wide the hint goes under its control. Plain inputs in dialogs use the `input` class.
- Lists inside dialogs take the keyboard with `ide.ui.listKeys` (the Conflicts dialog: Up/Down choose the file, Enter merges it); a list that re-renders must not take its focused row out of the page.
- The Settings category list takes the keyboard with `ide.ui.listKeys`: Up/Down/Home/End switch pages, Left/Right collapse or expand a group or go to it (a group chosen by keys is drawn selected instead of the page row). `verify/dialog-keyboard.mjs` (ports 9100-9101, in CI) walks a generic dialog with a focused scroller and Settings (search, categories, page controls, buttons, back to search; Shift+Tab; the arrow keys) by keyboard only, in English and Korean.
- Settings search reads every page's labels as rendered in the session's language. On the first non-empty query it mounts each page off screen once per dialog and collects its rows (`legend`, `label`, `.field`, `.settings-check`, `.settings-note`, buttons, headings; list choices and field values are left out). Pages with `index: false` (Keymap, Plugins, Python Interpreter) are found by title and keywords only; a plugin page should set it when mounting lists data or starts slow work. A query's words match in any order; a row matches when it holds every word, or one of them while its section title holds the rest ("글꼴 크기" finds 크기: in the 글꼴 section). Pages are ranked by matching rows (a title match first), the best one opens, and its matching rows are highlighted (`.settings-hit`) and scrolled into view. Test: `verify/settings-search.mjs` (ports 9194-9195, in CI; English and Korean, 1008x681).
- `verify/ui-inspect-dialogs.mjs` (port 9494, in CI) opens every Settings page and the common dialogs in Korean, Light, 900x560 and checks that they fit the window, line up, keep Tab inside, show the blue ring, put the primary button first and close on Esc.

## End-to-end tool flow

`verify/flow-tools.mjs` (ports 9570-9576: IDE 9570 and 9571, the shop API 9575, a fake OSV server 9576; in CI) runs one continuous session the way a user works through the tools, offline: Create Git Repository from the Commit window, a first commit with every commit check, a branch and a conflicting merge resolved in the merge tool, a rebase, a stash, a shelf whose unshelve conflicts, the log, Annotate, Commit and Push defining a local bare remote, auto-fetch showing ↓2 after another clone pushes and Update Project (also over a conflicting change); Share Project on GitHub, the pull request list, details and Checkout with the fake gh (`verify/fixtures/github`, whose `realCheckout` state makes `pr checkout` fetch and check out the branch; the test's git config rewrites `https://github.com/` to a local folder, so nothing reaches GitHub); split terminals and the project-switch question; a notebook with Jupyter Variables and the Data View (when `verify/out/dataview-venv` has ipykernel and pandas); a SQLite data source with completion and Copy as CSV; the HTTP client; Create Virtual Environment, install, uninstall and Upgrade with offline wheels (`PIP_NO_INDEX`, `PIP_FIND_LINKS`) and an advisory from the fake OSV server (`PYIDE_OSV_URL`); background tasks and the Notifications tool window; Report a Problem (`PYIDE_DIAGNOSTICS_TEST`); an update check against a folder feed; Settings export (`PYIDE_SETTINGS_SYNC_DIALOG`). It then closes the project, checks that no branch widget, run configuration, pull request, problem or background task of it stays and that the kernel and shells are gone, quits with File › Exit and checks that no process the IDE started is left, and imports the settings into a second instance with a fresh profile. `FLOW_ONLY=git,github,…` runs only some phases while debugging.

What the flow fixed along the way:

- `vcsBranchChanged({root, branch, isRepo})` (vcs plugin): emitted when the repository appears or goes or its branch changes (Create Git Repository, checkouts, rebases, a PR checkout). The Workspace home's branch chip follows it; it used to say "No Git repository" after Create Git Repository and kept the old branch after a checkout.
- Closing the project now clears the vcs plugin's state (branch widget, ↓↑, Commit window, log, auto-fetch timer), the run widget's configurations (Run could start the closed project's configuration) and the Pull Requests window (it kept listing the closed project's pull requests).
- Commit… (Ctrl+K) switches the Commit window from its Shelf tab (where shelving leaves it) back to the changes; the message box was focused while hidden.
- `ide.ui.console` follows the end of its output by the user's last scroll, not by measuring at each append: a console written to while hidden (the Git console behind the Log tab) opened at its first line. Since 0.2.9 following stops on the user's input (wheel up, a scrollbar drag or touch scroll, PageUp/Up/Home within 500 ms after a click in the console), not on the scroll position alone, so the wheel works during a flood that trims the console every frame; scrolling back to the end (as measured at the last frame) follows again.
- Background tasks: git Push, Update Project and a manual Fetch (`Pushing…`, `Updating project…`, `Fetching…`), and every job of the Python Packages console (its header as the title, Cancel stops the process; this includes Create Virtual Environment) show in the status bar.
- Conflicts from an unshelve or unstash name the incoming side: "Theirs (shelf <name>)" / "Theirs (stash@{n})" in the Conflicts dialog and the merge tool, where git has no operation to name it.
- Inlay hints: commas inside a generator argument (`sum(p * q for p, q in items)`) are loop targets, not call arguments; the plugin put a `start:` hint in the middle of the generator.

## Coding flow (verify/flow-coding.mjs)

`verify/flow-coding.mjs` (ports 9560-9563; in CI) is one continuous session of everyday Python coding: New Project with a `.venv`, a package and its tests written with New › Python Package / Python File / Python Unit Test and the `dataclass`, `prop` and `pt` live templates, completion with auto-import and the Alt+Enter import fix, Ctrl+B, Peek Definition, Ctrl+hover and Ctrl+click into closed files, Find Usages, Shift+F6 on a function, renaming and moving the module from the tree (rope installed through its offer), Extract Method, Reformat Code, Optimize Imports, the run widget, the gutter and Run Anything (Double Ctrl), a conditional breakpoint with Variables, Alt+F8 and F8, Attach to Process, pytest installed from the requirements.txt banner, Ctrl+Shift+F10 and Auto-test on save, Run with Coverage (installs coverage.py) and Profile, a scratch file, the Python Console, the unresolved-import Install quick fix, a before launch tool, a File Watcher, Local History revert, a numbered bookmark, Search Everywhere, Paste from History, Power Save Mode, font zoom and a saved layout. After every stage it checks the IDE's internal errors, renderer exceptions and background tasks; at the end the IDE log, that Close Project ends every Python process of the project, and that nothing PyIDE started outlives File › Exit. It is offline: pytest, rope and coverage.py come from wheels repacked from distributions installed in local interpreters (the default Python, `verify/out/refactor-venv`, `verify/out/coverage-profile-venv`), a tiny `flow-units` wheel stands in for a PyPI package, and pip runs with `PIP_NO_INDEX` / `PIP_FIND_LINKS`. Without coverage.py anywhere the coverage step is skipped. `FLOW_STOP=<stage>` stops after a stage while debugging.

What the flow fixed:

- Alt+Enter right after an edit that moved code (an auto-import line, a paste) offered no import for the name under the caret: Pyright's diagnostics still had the old line numbers. The lsp plugin now keeps a decoration per Pyright diagnostic of an open file, so `ide.lsp.diagnostics(path)` and the quick fixes see where each one is now, and Show Context Actions waits (up to 2.5 s) for Pyright's diagnostics of the current text before Monaco asks for actions.
- The import fix leads the list (it names Pyright's marker, so Monaco no longer puts ruff's "Suppress F821 … # noqa" above it), joins an existing `from module import …` of the same module instead of adding a second line, and a name used twice on a line gives one action (also one `# noqa` and one `# pyright: ignore` action).
- A requirements.txt (pyproject.toml, Pipfile, lock file) written outside the IDE (`pip freeze > requirements.txt` in the terminal) was not checked until a restart in a project that had none before; the packages plugin now follows the project watcher.
- Close Project left the Python Console's interpreter and the rope sidecar running in the closed project's venv (its folder could not be deleted); both stop now, the sidecar also when another project opens.
- The breadcrumbs kept naming the previous file until Pyright answered with the new file's symbols (seconds while Find Usages runs); the path changes at once.
- Node's MaxListenersExceededWarning (eleven main modules listen for will-quit) was logged as an ERROR at every start.

## Runtime performance (0.2.4)

Measured with CDP (Performance.getMetrics, Profiler, heap snapshots) and per-process CPU (Win32_Process) on a 2,000-line Python file and perf.mjs's 20,000-file project; fixes and their check in `verify/perf-runtime.mjs` (ports 9580-9581, numbers in `verify/out/perf-runtime/results.json`; not in CI, like perf.mjs and soak.mjs, since it times things).

- Inlay hints asked Pyright for a signatureHelp per visible call after every pause in typing. Pyright 1.1.406's signatureHelp also looks up documentation, which for an overloaded builtin takes seconds every time (`open(`: 5-7 s), and Pyright answers one request at a time, so typing in a large file kept it at 1.5 cores and completion waited ~10 s behind queued hint requests (19 s of hint work after the last key). Now the plugin asks for the callee's hover first (quick; it prints the signature with its parameter names) and asks signatureHelp once per printed signature and argument count, reusing the names at every call with the same signature, in any file and after edits; requests go at most two at a time, and a provide call Monaco cancels drops its queued requests and cancels the sent ones. Typing two lines: Pyright ~12 s CPU instead of ~50 s, quiet ~2-4 s after the last key, completion answers in ~30 ms.
- `ide.lsp.request` takes a cancellation token (above); the lsp plugin's own providers (completion, resolve, hover, signature help, definition, document highlight, document symbols) pass Monaco's, so a stale request queued in Pyright is dropped (`lsp:cancel` sends `$/cancelRequest` and answers null at once). Pyright's file-based cancellation (`--cancellationReceive=file:`) is not used: in 1.1.406 it stops the background analysis from publishing diagnostics.
- Pyright lists the project's source files after answering initialize (it logs "Found N source files"); a references, rename, workspace/symbol or callHierarchy/incomingCalls request asked before that answers from the open files only (Type Hierarchy right after opening a project showed no subtypes in closed files). `main/features/lsp.js` holds these until that log, or 20 s after the start; a renderer cancel while held answers null and sends nothing.
- Monaco keeps an editor's `addAction` registrations (a global command whose handler holds the editor) after the editor is disposed. Plugins add actions to every editor through `ide.onEditor`, so each embedded editor (a Markdown or HTML viewer, a notebook cell) stayed in memory with its DOM: +22 DOM nodes and +23 listeners per Markdown file opened. The core now disposes them with the editor (`renderer/core/editors.js`, `ownActions`).
- A notification's closed balloon stayed in memory through the session's history (up to 1,000); `note.balloon` is now held weakly and is null once the balloon is gone.
- The core `createDiffEditor` wrapper's `dispose` calls `setModel(null)` first: each closed compare or Local History diff kept +46 DOM nodes and +11 listeners before. main/project-watch.js adds one 'destroyed' listener per webContents (a WeakSet) that closes whatever watcher it has then, instead of one per project switch (7 -> 18 listeners over 12 switches before, 3 -> 3 now). Both are measured by `verify/core-lifecycle.mjs`.
- Pyright's file watcher (`main/features/lsp.js`) ran `existsSync` for every file event on the main process; it now batches like project-watch and checks existence asynchronously when the batch goes (main-process JS during a 14,000-file external change: ~420 ms -> ~210 ms).
- Document symbols (0.2.7): the breadcrumbs, Monaco's symbol provider (sticky lines, outline), editor-assists and type-hierarchy share one textDocument/documentSymbol request per open file (`sharedSymbols` in the lsp plugin; `ide.lsp.documentSymbols` and the provider both go through it). It is sent once the text has been unchanged for 150 ms (1,000 ms above 5,000 lines), for the text at that moment. An edit cancels one in flight and its callers get the next answer. A cancelled Monaco token answers null without cancelling the shared request, and a null answer is not cached. 20,000-line file: 19 -> 0.5 requests per typed line, Pyright CPU while typing 30.4 s -> 25.8 s (medians of 5 interleaved runs). Known limit: completion there still takes seconds (median 3.2 s on a quiet machine), bounded by Pyright's check of the open file after each edit (65% of its CPU in one profile), which runs on the thread that answers requests and cannot be interrupted. Since 0.2.8 Pyright checks files on its background-analysis thread, so requests (completion, hover, signature help, document symbols) no longer wait for the check of the open file after each edit: main/features/lsp.js starts Pyright with `--cancellationReceive=file:<folder>` (a folder under the temp folder, removed when the server exits; folders of IDE processes that are gone are removed at the next start) and a short `-e` bootstrap that starts Pyright's worker from the language-server entry (the npm build starts it from a webpack chunk that only defines modules, so the thread never ran before; checked in a packaged build too). Open files are bound again on the request thread 1 s after an edit or a project file change, since auto-import completion reads only files bound there. 20,000-line file: completion while typing 3.1 s -> 0.18 s, now bound by Pyright parsing and binding the whole file after each edit (~200 ms); diagnostics follow ~6 s after the last key there (0.8 s at 2,000 lines); the worker costs ~110 MB. Pyright 1.1.414 measured no different, so 1.1.406 stays. `verify/lsp.mjs` checks that the thread runs. Test: `verify/perf-bigfile.mjs` (ports 9180-9181, not in CI: it times things; budgets of 2 requests per typed line, completion under 1.5 s at 2,000 lines and under 15 s at 20,000).
- Document symbols (0.2.9): `ide.lsp.documentSymbols` decorates each shared answer once (a WeakMap keyed by the answer) and every caller gets the same tree, read-only. 20,000-line file, one typed line + 3 s: `decorate` 133 ms of renderer self time -> under 7 ms (one profile each).
- Consoles (0.2.9): a program printing 100,000 lines (5.2 MB) took about 100 s in the Run console (median of 3, 59-127 s) and froze the window, because each chunk's scroll to the end laid out the whole growing output. `append` now extends the last span and scrolls once per animation frame, the console keeps the last 1024 KB (PyCharm's console cycle buffer, `console.cycleBufferKB`, Settings › Editor › General › Console), and main batches process output (`main/output-batch.js`: one message per 10 ms or 64 K characters, the first after a pause at once, stdout and stderr in order): 0.74 s (median of 5, 0.73-0.97 s), 79-94 messages, renderer 314-459 MB after five floods instead of 751-913 MB after three. The terminal batches the same way (about 1,500 `pty:data` messages per 100,000 lines instead of one per ConPTY chunk; renderer CPU 10.2 s -> 6.3 s). Its main process CPU (about 17 s per 100,000 lines) did not change: reading ConPTY inside Electron's main process costs about 11 s even without any IPC (4.7 s in plain Node), so it is bound by where node-pty runs, not by the messages. Test: `verify/console-output.mjs` (port 9290; also a stray OSC, an escape sequence split by the other stream, the wheel and PageUp during a flood, and the Python Packages console's stderr lines).
- Commit window (0.2.9): a refresh (every save, file event and window focus) rebuilds the Commit window's rows only when what they are drawn from changed (`changesKey()` in renderer/features/vcs/index.js: the status answer, selection, ticks and rows shown); with 1,540 changes an unchanged refresh costs ~10-15 ms of renderer work instead of ~70-110 ms.
- Measured and left as they are: idle with a project open is ~0.2 s CPU per 20 s for all processes (renderer script ~1 ms; the caret blink is most of it); per-keystroke plugin work is under 0.2 ms per key (keydown to paint ~10 ms median); opening the 20,000-file project draws the tree in ~105 ms with main-process stalls under 60 ms; settings writes take 2-3 ms in main (the renderer waits longer only while main is busy spawning processes).

## Editor assists: sticky lines, f-strings, PyCharm editing keys, docstring stubs (editor-assists)

`renderer/features/editor-assists` (plugin `editor-assists`, also loaded in detached windows).

- Sticky Lines (PyCharm's name; Monaco's `stickyScroll`): the classes and functions around the code at the top of a group editor stay pinned at its top, at most `editor.stickyLinesMax` lines (default 5, 1-20); `editor.stickyLines` (default on) turns them off. Both are in Settings › Editor › General › Appearance (rows the settings plugin draws only while this plugin is loaded; their defaults live in this plugin's settings page). Clicking a sticky line puts the caret on it (Monaco's own handler). Only group editors (split panes included) get them: the plugin wraps `monaco.editor.create` and `createDiffEditor` so every other editor (notebook cells, query consoles, viewers, diff views, the Python Console) defaults to `stickyScroll: {enabled: false}`, and `ide.onEditor` turns them on per the setting in group editors only. Monaco's peek editors copy their pane's option. Model: `outlineModel`, i.e. Pyright's document symbols through the lsp plugin's DocumentSymbolProvider; Monaco puts each sticky line on the symbol's `selectionRange` (the name), so a decorated function sticks as its `def` line and a signature over several lines as its first line. Monaco's indentation model, compared in the test, sticks the `) -> int:` line of a Black-style signature instead; the outline model does not show `if`/`for`/`with` blocks, which PyCharm does. Until Pyright has symbols (and for languages without a symbol provider) Monaco falls back to indentation; once `ide.lsp.documentSymbols` answers for a shown file the plugin registers and drops an empty symbol provider, which makes the sticky scrolls ask again. Colors: the editor background with a separator (#393b40 Dark, #ebecf0 Light) and a soft shadow, set as Monaco's `--vscode-editorStickyScroll-*` variables in `style.css`, since the PyCharm themes do not define them. Distraction-Free Mode hides their line numbers with the editor's; Presentation Mode gives them its line height.
- Automatic f-string conversion (PyCharm's Smart Keys › Python "Convert to f-string automatically", setting `editor.autoFString`, default on, Settings › Editor › Smart Keys): typing the `}` that closes `{expr}` inside a plain string literal adds the `f` prefix as its own undo step (Ctrl+Z removes only the prefix). It runs on Monaco's `onDidType` (typed text and IME, not pastes or other plugins' edits) with a small Python scanner over the file (`ide.editorAssists.fstringInsertAt(text, braceOffset)`), never Pyright, so it is instant. Conservative: only prefix-less strings (not bytes, raw, `u`, already f/t); not docstrings (a triple-quoted string starting its line at the top of the file or after a `…:` line), strings followed by `.format(`/`.format_map(` or `%`, strings with `%s`-style placeholders or `{{`/`}}`, or messages passed to `log…/logger/logging.debug/info/warning/error/exception/critical/log(`; every replacement field must be a simple expression (names, attributes, calls, subscripts, operators, `=`, `!r`, `:spec`; no backslash or the string's own quote) whose first name the file uses as code (not only as an attribute or keyword argument) or is a common builtin. Unicode (Korean) names work.
- PyCharm editing actions the keymap runs while the main editor has the keyboard (`context: 'editor'`): Start New Line (Shift+Enter: each caret to its line end, then Monaco's Enter), Start New Line Before Current (Ctrl+Alt+Enter, Monaco's `insertLineBefore`), Complete Current Statement (Ctrl+Shift+Enter), Join Lines (Ctrl+Shift+J), Toggle Case (Ctrl+Shift+U: upper case unless the selection, or the word at the caret, has no lower case), Add Selection for Next Occurrence (Alt+J), Unselect Occurrence (Alt+Shift+J, Monaco's cursor undo), Select All Occurrences (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+J), Move Statement Up/Down (Ctrl+Shift+Up/Down), Collapse All / Expand All (Ctrl+Shift+- / =), Quick Definition (Ctrl+Shift+I, Monaco's peek) and Type Info (Ctrl+Shift+P, Pyright's hover). The Edit, Code, Navigate and View menus list them. Enter-like keys accept the selected completion first. Complete Current Statement (`ide.editorAssists.statementCompletion(text, offset)` -> `{at, insert, end, indent, block}`) closes the statement's unterminated string and open brackets, adds `:` to a compound statement without a depth-0 colon (`():` for `def name`), puts it before a trailing comment and opens a line after the comment, one level deeper after a colon, as one undo step. Inside brackets the statement continues over lines of any indentation, except at a line not indented past it that starts with a statement keyword (def, class, return, import, if, for…) or puts an operand right after an operand (`print("a"` then `x = 1`), where PyCharm's parser recovers. Outside Python, or inside a string over several lines, it is Start New Line. Move Statement (`statementMove(text, line, dir)` -> `{from, to, lines, upper, lower}`, false at a block edge, null on a blank or comment line) swaps the statement at the caret (decorators, the comment lines right above it, its body, its elif/else/except/finally clauses, continuation lines) with its neighbour in the same block; blank lines between them stay. Every caret moves with its own line (one in the other statement or the blank lines between moves with them, carets elsewhere stay). Blank and comment lines and other languages fall back to Monaco's Move Line.
- Triple quotes and docstring stubs (PyCharm's smart quotes and "Insert documentation comment stub", setting `editor.docstringStub`, default on, Settings › Editor › Smart Keys): typing `"""` or `'''` where a string may start, with only Monaco's paired quotes after the caret, adds the closing quotes (not when they close an open string or sit in a comment, decided by the tokenizer on the text before them). Typing the closing `"""` of that string steps over the added quotes (they are removed when the third closing quote is typed), so `"""Summary"""` typed in full stays as typed. Enter between fresh quotes on the first line of a def or class body replaces the line with a stub in `python.docstringFormat` (Auto-detect: NumPy or Google when the file has such docstrings, else reStructuredText): reST `:param x:` lines and `:return:`; Google `Args:` (stars kept) and `Returns:`; NumPy `Parameters`/`Returns` with underlines; plain, or nothing to list, an empty summary line. `self`/`cls`, `/` and `*` are left out; a function gets a return entry unless it is `__init__` or annotated `-> None`. The header may span lines; a docstring that is not the body's first statement gets no stub. The stub is its own undo step.
- Brackets in strings and comments (core, `renderer/core/ide.js` next to the Python language configuration): as in PyCharm, `(`, `[` and `{` typed in a string literal or a comment get no closing bracket (`print("(")` typed key by key stays `print("(")`), while in an f-string `{` opens a replacement field and is closed, and brackets inside a field are code. A `[` right after a string (`"abc"[`) is closed too. Monaco's `notIn` would judge by the character before the caret and get both of these wrong, so each typing is judged on `onWillType`: the line as it will read is tokenized from its start state (`tokenizeLinesAt`) and, when a typed bracket lands in text, `autoClosingBrackets` is `never` for that one typing. Text typed at once (an IME commit) is judged bracket by bracket, since Monaco closes its brackets one character at a time. The lsp plugin's completion `(` goes through the same check and, after a name, is code.
- Known limits: Move Statement does not move a statement into or out of a block at its edge (PyCharm does). Lines after the first of a triple-quoted f-string count as plain text for bracket pairing (`{` not closed there). A quote typed right after a word character gets no closing quote, so `f"`, `rf"` and `b"` are not paired (PyCharm pairs them).  Auto-detect reads only the current file (AI Write Documentation also reads the project); there are no `:type:`/`:rtype:` placeholders (PyCharm's "Insert type placeholders", off by default there).
- Test: `verify/editor-assists.mjs` (ports 9385-9386; in CI): sticky lines with nested classes and a decorated method, the indentation-model comparison, click to jump, the max lines and on/off settings, Dark/Light colors, split panes, breadcrumbs, Distraction-Free and Presentation modes, Peek Definition, off in embedded/diff editors and notebook cells; a 27-case f-string table, real typing with the auto-closed brace, undo, bytes, docstring, an edit that is not typing, the setting off, the settings pages, and Korean.
- Test: `verify/editor-keys.mjs` (ports 9510-9511; in CI): the key-to-action table in a Python editor, keys bound by id (Rerun; Go to Implementation, Super Method, Test and Override Methods when their plugins are loaded), Open Folder… and Reopen Closed Tab without keys, each action with real key events, a 21-case Complete Current Statement table (continuations at any indentation inside brackets), a 5-case Move Statement table, its edges and three carets, peek and hover, Ctrl+F5, Find Action's Monaco actions, the Edit/Code/View menus, triple quotes and the four stub formats with real typing, the edge cases and the setting, `"""Summary"""` typed in full, brackets typed in strings, comments, f-strings and after a string (13 cases key by key, 3 at once), and Korean.

## AI Actions: Write Documentation and Generate Unit Tests

Plugin `ai-generate` (depends on ai-edit; `renderer/features/ai-generate`, `main/features/ai-generate.js`, analysis and text assembly in `main/ai/generate-text.js`, pure functions) adds PyCharm AI Assistant's two actions for the function, method or class at the caret. Both call the Claude Code CLI through `complete()` in `main/ai/claude.js`, like ai-edit and ai-chat (model `aiChat.model`, default `sonnet`), so `PYIDE_CLAUDE_PATH` swaps in a stand-in CLI.

- Entry points: Alt+Enter on a def/class header (decorators to the colon; a `quickfix` code action offered only on explicit requests, so the lightbulb stays off), the editor context menu's AI Actions… (a popup with both actions; Monaco's menu has no submenus), and Find Action (`AiGenerate.WriteDocumentation`, `AiGenerate.GenerateTests`, `AiGenerate.Menu`). No shortcut.
- Context sent: the target's source (cut at 12,000 characters at a line), the module's top-level imports, for a method its class header, and small helpers from the same file (≤ 40 lines, ≤ 4,000 characters together) that it calls: module-level functions/classes, sibling methods through `self.`/`cls.`, and for tests of a method the class's `__init__`. The prompt also carries a JSON signature (parameters with annotations and defaults, return annotation, raised exceptions, generator, decorators; for a class its bases, `__init__` parameters and attributes) and the language (Korean when the file's comments or strings have Hangul). The whole prompt goes to the IDE log at DEBUG level only (`#ai-generate`).
- Write Documentation: the format is Settings › Python Integrated Tools › Docstrings › Docstring format (`python.docstringFormat`: `''` Auto-detect, `restructuredtext`, `google`, `numpy`, `plain`); Auto-detect counts the section markers of the current file's docstrings, then of up to 150 project files (no venvs or caches), and falls back to reStructuredText, PyCharm's default. The answer is cleaned (fences, quotes, indentation), indented like the body (tabs too) and put in the file's docstring quotes (`'''` when its docstrings use them; a replaced docstring keeps its own), then shown with `ide.aiEdit.review` as an inline diff with Accept (Ctrl+Enter, hunk Accept) and Reject (Esc). A def/class that already has a docstring asks "Replace Docstring?" first; Cancel sends nothing. The def line is tracked while Claude writes, so edits elsewhere do not misplace it. A one-line `def f(): ...` is refused.
- Generate Unit Tests: `tests/test_<module>.py` (an existing `test/` folder when there is no `tests/`). The module path is relative to the project, or to `src/` in a src layout (`from shop.cart import total`); a method is imported through its class, a nested function through its top-level function. The framework is the test file's own style, else pytest when pytest is configured (pytest.ini, conftest.py, `[tool.pytest…]`, `[tool:pytest]`, tox `[pytest]`), else unittest only when most of the project's test files use `unittest.TestCase`; a new unittest folder gets an `__init__.py`. A src layout without pytest's `pythonpath = ["src"]` (or a conftest naming src) gets a note in the preview. The answer is merged without touching existing tests: new imports after the existing ones, new tests appended before a trailing `if __name__ == "__main__":` block, clashing names renamed (`test_area_2`, `TestArea2`), the file's line separator kept (its most frequent one: LF, CRLF or CR); a new file is the answer as is, with the new-file line separator (`editor.lineSeparator`). A test file that is not open is read in its own encoding (detected like the editor: UTF-8, UTF-8 BOM, UTF-16, CP949) and written back in it, so a CP949 file keeps the bytes of its old lines; it is refused, with Open <file> in the notification, when it is over 4 MB (never cut), cannot be read as text, would not come back byte for byte from its encoding, or the new tests hold characters its encoding cannot (an emoji in CP949). An open test document is merged from the editor, which saves it in its encoding. The result opens in a preview tab (read-only inline diff, Append to File / Create File, Cancel); an open test document is merged from its unsaved text and edited as one undo step and saved, otherwise main writes the file only if it is still what the preview was made from (Local History label "Before Generating Tests"). A notification then offers Run Tests (`pytest in test_<module>.py` through the tests plugin).
- Progress is an `ide.tasks` task ("Writing documentation for …", "Generating tests for …") whose Cancel kills the CLI's process tree; failures are error notifications (group `AI Actions`) with Open Install Manager or Sign in to Claude Code where they help.
- Trust: in Safe Mode (`ide.trusted === false`) the actions are disabled with a note (context-menu popup items, code actions' `disabled` reason) and running one posts a warning with Trust Project…; every main channel that reads the project, calls Claude or writes refuses a project `main/trust.js` does not trust.
- `ide.pythonToolsSections` (tests plugin): functions `(host, ctx)` that add sections to Settings › Python Integrated Tools; without the tests plugin ai-generate registers its own page.
- `ide.aiGenerate`: `writeDocumentation(editor?)`, `generateTests(editor?)`, `showMenu(editor?)`, `runTests(path)`, `state()` (`{running, last, preview, written}`).
- Fake backend: `verify/fake-claude-generate.mjs` is a stand-in Claude Code CLI (run through a `claude.cmd` the test writes; `FAKE_CLAUDE_DIR` holds `scenario.json` with `delayMs`/`error`/`reply` per kind and receives `log.jsonl`). Its answers come from the prompt alone: docstrings in the requested format from the signature line, tests from the code's doctest examples (`assert expr == expected`, `pytest.raises`/`assertRaises` for a traceback), pytest or unittest.
- Test: `verify/ai-generate.mjs` (ports 9010-9012; in CI): pure Node checks of the outline, signature, context limits, format and quote detection, formatting, placement, merging and the test plan (unittest projects, src layout with and without pythonpath); then Alt+Enter › Write Documentation with Google detected from the project, the prompt's content and limits, Accept; a reST class docstring from the context menu; Replace Docstring? (Cancel sends nothing, Replace reviews the old docstring's lines); tabs and `'''` quotes; Esc; a Korean docstring; Cancel from the Background Tasks popup (the CLI process ends); an auth error notification; tests appended to an existing file with a renamed clash, the preview tab and nothing written before Append; an open test file getting a new import and pytest.raises tests; Cancel in the preview; a new Korean test file run from the notification's Run Tests, and all appended tests passing with pytest; the settings field; the DEBUG log; then a src-layout project in Korean (preview, write, pytest passes, translations, nothing logged at INFO) and an untrusted project in Safe Mode (disabled items, the note, main refuses, nothing reaches the CLI).

## Class diagrams (Show Diagram)

`renderer/features/diagrams` (plugin `diagrams`), `main/features/diagrams.js` and `main/diagrams/model.js` add PyCharm Professional's Diagrams › Show Diagram for Python (action `ShowUmlDiagram`, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+U): Show Diagram in the project tree's context menu on a `.py` file or a folder/package, and in the Python editor's context menu, where the class at the caret shows with its bases and subclasses (all levels) and the classes it is associated with in either direction (no class at the caret: the file). The shortcut takes the class at the caret in an editor, the selected tree row while the tree has focus, else the current Python file.

- Model: `renderer/features/diagrams/python/diagram_model.py` runs in the project interpreter (a local one when the project interpreter is remote) with `-I` and reads files with `ast` only; user code is never imported or run. Per class: bases, methods (visibility from leading underscores, static, class, property with its setter folded in, abstract, async, overloads folded), fields (class attributes, annotated attributes, `ClassVar`, `self.x = …` / `self.x: T = …` in `__init__`/`__post_init__`, taking a parameter's annotation for `self.x = x`), dataclass and attrs fields, Enum members, nested classes, and the class names in attribute annotations (strings and `X | None` too; inside `list[…]`, `Dict[…]`… they count as to-many). `model.js` resolves names as Python binds them: classes of the module, `import a.b`, `import a.b as x`, `from m import n as x` (absolute and relative), `from m import *`, module-level aliases and re-exports through `__init__.py`. A file is known by its package-chain module name and by its path from the project root and from `<root>/src` (src layout, namespace packages). Modules a scope's classes import from outside the scope are parsed on demand so their bases resolve (shown classes' project bases are included). Parsed files are cached per path, mtime and size for the session; unsaved editor text is read instead of the file. The walk skips caches, `node_modules`, virtual environments (`pyvenv.cfg`, `conda-meta`, `venv`/`.venv`/`env`) and dot folders and stops at `diagrams.maxFiles` (default 5000) files. Files with syntax errors are skipped and counted in the status line (their messages in the ⚠ tooltip). Parsing runs as an `ide.tasks` task ("Building class diagram", files done/total); Cancel kills the Python process.
- View: an editor tab with an SVG diagram. Class boxes have a header («enum», «dataclass», «abstract» or «protocol», the name in italics when abstract, the module) and field and method sections with PyCharm-like icons (f field, e enum member, m method, s static, c class method, p property; static members underlined, abstract methods italic, `#`/`−` for protected/private). Toolbar toggles Fields, Methods and Private (settings `diagrams.showFields`, `diagrams.showMethods`, `diagrams.showPrivate`), zoom in/out, 1:1, Fit, Add Class… (a search over the project's classes; the first use parses the whole project, from the cache), Export SVG and PNG, Refresh. Inheritance arrows have hollow triangles and are routed orthogonally into the base's bottom centre; associations are straight lines with an open arrow, the attribute names and `*` for to-many. Clicking a class selects it and highlights its relations (others dimmed); double-click or Enter opens the class in the editor; Delete removes it (and its context menu has Jump to Source and Remove from Diagram). Pan by dragging the background, middle button, Space+drag or the wheel (Shift+wheel horizontally); Ctrl+wheel zooms around the cursor, `+`/`-` zoom. Saving a Python file under a diagram's scope refreshes it, keeping added and removed classes.
- Layout (`ide.diagrams.layout(nodes, edges)`, no library): per inheritance component, longest-path layering (a class without bases then moves down next to its highest subclass), dummy nodes on long edges, 24 alternating barycenter sweeps keeping the order with the fewest crossings (counted with a Fenwick tree), then x coordinates by repeated least-squares placement towards the neighbours' mean under the order and gap constraints (pool-adjacent-violators); components and single classes are shelf-packed into rows. 300 classes lay out in ~10 ms (~30 ms with text measuring) and draw in ~70 ms.
- Panning moves a composited wrapper (`.dg-layer`), not the `<svg>`: a transform on the `<svg>` itself made Chrome lay out all ~10,000 SVG elements on every move (~30 ms per frame). A zoom first scales that wrapper (one composited frame), then resizes the `<svg>` 150 ms after zooming pauses, so text is crisp again at the new scale: resizing a large diagram's `<svg>` on every step took 300-550 ms a step.
- Theme colours are the `--dg-*` variables in `style.css` (Dark and Light); the SVG rules live in `index.js` (`svgCss`), injected into the page and, for an export, copied with the current theme's values. SVG export is a standalone file with its own `<style>`; PNG export draws that SVG on a canvas at twice the size (capped at 16,000 px per side and 120 MP). Both go through a save dialog, and `diagrams:write` writes only a path that dialog returned in this session (like Export Settings); `PYIDE_DIAGRAMS_SAVE_DIR` (tests) answers `<dir>/<name>` without it and also allows files directly in that folder. The diagram itself is marked `data-user-text` (class names are user data).
- `ide.diagrams`: `show()`, `showPaths(paths)`, `showAt(path, line)`, `showModel({key, title, load})` (a model another plugin builds, in the shape main gives: classes with `fields` of kind `key` drawn with a k and no path, edges `assoc` or `extends`; such a tab has no Methods and Private toggles, adds tables, counts tables, and Refresh calls `load` again), `get(tabId?)`, `state(tabId?)` (classes with boxes, routed edges, options, view, timing, parse stats), `layout`, `measure`, `exportSvg(file?)`, `exportPng(file?)`, `addClass(id?)`, `findClass(qualname)`, `slowMs` (tests: delays each parsed file so a run can be cancelled).
- Test: `verify/diagrams.mjs` (ports 9040-9042; in CI; fixture `verify/fixtures/diagrams`): pure Node resolution checks (aliases, star imports, re-exports, src layout names); A: cancelling a parse from the task, the tree's Show Diagram with its shortcut hint, classes, inheritance across modules (relative imports, `import a.b`, aliases, `TYPE_CHECKING`, src layout, multiple inheritance), associations and to-many, stereotypes, members and their marks, the syntax-error file, that `danger.py` never ran, no overlaps and bases above subclasses, toggles, selection highlight, double-click navigation, Delete, Add Class… (another source root, a package re-export), Ctrl+wheel, Fit, pan, the editor action and Ctrl+Alt+Shift+U, the cache (second run parses nothing, a changed file alone is parsed again), unsaved text, SVG content and colours, PNG size, the Light theme; B: a generated 300-class project (layout under 1 s, no overlaps, bases above subclasses, pan frame times at the fitted scale and at 1:1, zoom and relayout times); C: Korean toolbar, status, menu and no missing translations. `DIAGRAMS_ONLY=fixture|big|ko` runs one part.

## File compare and line separators

File compare (plugin `compare`, `renderer/features/compare` + `main/features/compare.js`), as in PyCharm:

- Entry points: select two files in the Project tool window (Ctrl+click adds a row to the selection or takes it out; a plain click or arrow key selects one row again) and use the context menu's Compare Files or Ctrl+D; Compare With… (`CompareTwoFiles`, View menu, Ctrl+D in the tree, the tree menu of one file) compares the selected or current file with a file picked from the project (or Browse…); Compare with Clipboard (`CompareClipboardWithSelection`, View menu and the editor's context menu) compares the clipboard with the whole file, or with the selection when there is one; Compare with Branch… (`Git.CompareWithBranch`, Git menu, and Git: Compare with Branch… in the tree menu of a file) picks a local or remote branch, one of the last 50 commits or any revision typed into the picker (a tag, `HEAD~2`) and shows `git show <rev>:<file>` (an empty side titled "does not exist there" when the file is missing in it).
- The diff tab is Monaco's diff editor with the vcs plugin's look (blue changed blocks on both sides, PyCharm Light diff colours), a toolbar with Previous / Next Difference (Shift+F7 / F7, `PreviousDiff` / `NextDiff`; they also work in the vcs plugin's diff tabs through `host._vcsDiff`), the number of differences ("Contents are identical") and a Side-by-side / Unified viewer switch (setting `diff.unified`). It opens at the first difference with the keyboard in the right side. The left side is a read-only snapshot. The right side, when it is a file, is that file's own document: the tab holds it through `ide.openCustomTab({doc})`, so edits show the modified mark, Save All (Ctrl+S) writes them with the file's encoding and line separator, the status bar shows its encoding and separator, and closing the tab with unsaved edits asks first. A selection compared with the clipboard is read-only.
- A compare tab's path is only its title: Open in New Window / Move to New Window (also from Find Action), Copy Path, Select in Project View and Show in Explorer act only on file and viewer tabs. Deleting a file closes every tab holding its document in every window, compare tabs included, so nothing saves it back; a renamed file's modified document shown only in a compare tab gets a file tab at the new path, so its text is not lost. A document loaded for a comparison that was opened meanwhile is released (`ide.releaseDocument(doc)`). While the debugger is suspended, F7 in a focused diff tab is still Next Difference (`ide.compare.diffFocused()`).
- Binary files, files over 5 MB (`ide.compare.MAX_DIFF_BYTES`) and unreadable files open a message tab that says why, and for two files whether their bytes are the same (`compare:sameBytes`, read in 1 MB slices).
- Core hooks: `ide.loadDocument(path)` (a document without a tab; hand it to `ide.openCustomTab({doc})` at once), `ide.openCustomTab({..., doc})`, `ide.selectedTreeEntries()` and tree actions' `when(entry, selection)` / `run(entry, selection)`. `ide.compare`: `files(left, right)`, `with(path)`, `clipboard(editor)`, `branch(path, rev?)`, `view(tab?)` -> `{diff, go(dir), count(), ready(), setUnified(on)}`, `texts({id, title, left: {title, text}, right: {title, text}, language?})` (two texts as a read-only diff tab; an open tab of that id is replaced; the tests plugin's Comparison Failure). Its two sides are raw texts, so when they differ only in line separators the count says Contents have differences only in line separators (PyCharm's text); Compare Files, Clipboard and Branch compare documents whose separators the model already normalized.

Line separators (core `renderer/core/editors.js`; the status bar widget is the `line-separators` plugin):

- A document keeps its file's separator: `doc.lineSeparator` (`'\n'`, `'\r\n'` or `'\r'`, the most frequent one; ties go to LF) and `doc.mixedSeparators`. Monaco knows only LF and CRLF and normalizes a model to one of them, so a CR file's model is CRLF and saving turns it back (`ide.lineSeparators.diskText(doc)`); a mixed file is saved with its main separator everywhere. Reload from disk, Load File System Changes and Reload in another encoding detect it again (event `lineSeparatorChanged(doc)`). Before this, saving already kept CRLF (Monaco's model kept the file's majority EOL and `getValue()` used it), but CR files were saved as CRLF and a file without line breaks got LF.
- New files: Settings › Editor › Code Style › Line separator for new files (`editor.lineSeparator`: `system` (CRLF on Windows), `lf`, `crlf`; exported with the code style settings). New Python File and the file templates write their text with it (`ide.lineSeparators.forNewFile(text)`), and a file without any line break (an empty new file) uses it too.
- The widget (`#statusLineSeparator`) sits between the caret position and the encoding and always shows for a tab with a document, like the encoding (it is not an ordered `#statusItems` widget, so a crowded status bar never hides it). Its tooltip names the separator and says when the file has mixed ones; its menu (`#lineSeparatorMenu`) converts the whole file to LF, CRLF or CR (also the actions `ConvertToUnixLineSeparators`, `ConvertToWindowsLineSeparators`, `ConvertToMacLineSeparators`). A conversion is one undo step and marks the file modified; CR <-> CRLF, which changes nothing in Monaco's model, does it by rewriting the first character. The plugin remembers the separator per model version, so Undo and Redo bring the right one back (Monaco reports an undone EOL change without `isUndoing`). `ide.lineSeparators`: `detect(text)`, `newFile()`, `forNewFile(text)`, `diskText(doc)`, `convert(doc, sep)`, `name(sep)`.

Tests: `verify/compare-files.mjs` (ports 9380 and 9383; in CI; fixture `verify/fixtures/compare-files`, a temporary Git repository with its own `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`) and `verify/line-endings.mjs` (ports 9381-9382; in CI; fixture `verify/fixtures/line-endings`; the LF, CRLF, CR, mixed and Korean files are written by the test, so git's line ending handling cannot change them), both also in Korean.

## Database Export Data and number fidelity

Export Data… (PyCharm's data extractors; plugin `database`: `renderer/features/database/export.js`, `main/db/export.js`, IPC in `main/features/database.js`) writes a query result or a whole table to a file. It is in the console's result bar (`.db-export`), the result grid's context menu, the context menu of a table or view in the Database tool window, and Find Action (`Database.ExportData`, no shortcut).

- Formats: CSV (value separator `,` `;` Tab `|`, column header, quote values when needed / always / never), TSV (CSV with Tab), JSON (array of objects, or JSON Lines), SQL INSERT statements (one per row; the table's own quoted name, or a typed name used as written, `MY_TABLE` for a query), Markdown table (`|` escaped, line breaks as `<br>`), and Excel `.xlsx`. Text formats are written in UTF-8, UTF-8 with BOM (Excel opens UTF-8 CSV correctly only with it) or UTF-16 LE with BOM. CSV/TSV use CRLF; NULL is an empty field (an empty string is `""` when quoted), blobs are `0x…` hex in CSV, Markdown and xlsx, `{"$base64": "…"}` in JSON, and the dialect's literal in SQL (`kind.sqlDialect`: postgres `'\x..'::bytea`, mysql `X'..'`, mssql `0x..` with `N'…'` strings, oracle `HEXTORAW('..')`, duckdb `'\xAA'::BLOB`, clickhouse `unhex('..')`, standard `X'..'`); numbers keep the database's text everywhere (`{$number}`), bigints stay exact (SQL Server DECIMAL/MONEY only to 15 digits, a tedious limit). The options are remembered in `database.export`.
- `.xlsx` is written by `main/db/export.js` itself, no dependency: a ZIP written front to back (small parts deflated in memory, the sheet deflated as it streams, with a data descriptor), `[Content_Types].xml`, `_rels/.rels`, `xl/workbook.xml` (sheet named after the table), its relationships, `xl/styles.xml` (bold header) and `xl/worksheets/sheet1.xml` with inline strings (no shared string table, so rows stream). Numbers Excel holds exactly (15 significant digits) are numbers; bigger integers are text; booleans are booleans; NULL is no cell; text is cut at 32,767 characters and rows at 1,048,576 (the notification says so).
- What runs: a table exports `SELECT * FROM <qualified name>` (with a `COUNT(*)` first for the progress bar); a console result runs again (read-only) only when `isReadOnlyStatement` (renderer/features/database/export.js) proves it only reads: plain `SELECT`/`WITH`/`VALUES`/`TABLE` with no `INTO`, data-modifying CTE, `FOR UPDATE`/`LOCK`, sequence use, known side-effecting functions or extra statements; `EXPLAIN` without `ANALYZE`; `SHOW`/`DESCRIBE`; reading PRAGMAs (comments and strings are removed first). Otherwise, when the grid holds every row they are exported; else a dialog asks: Export Shown Rows (default), Run and Export, or Cancel. MongoDB and Redis results export the rows the grid holds.
- Streaming: every SQL kind exports through a driver cursor (`adapter.cursor(sql, {readOnly})`, helpers in `main/db/cursors.js`), 1000 rows at a time, each batch written before the next is read, so a million-row table never sits in memory and every row is read exactly once in the statement's own order: SQLite `stmt.iterate()` on its worker; PostgreSQL/CockroachDB a `DECLARE … NO SCROLL CURSOR` + `FETCH` inside `BEGIN TRANSACTION READ ONLY` (a statement that cannot be declared, like `SHOW`, runs whole, capped at 1,000,000 rows and reported as `capped`); MySQL/MariaDB query events with pause/resume in `START TRANSACTION READ ONLY`; SQL Server `request.stream` with pause/resume; Oracle `resultSet` + `getRows` in a read-only transaction; DuckDB `prepared.stream()`; ClickHouse the HTTP response stream. Network kinds use a connection of their own (so the export cannot see the console's session state such as temp tables), push drivers are paused with at most ~2000 rows waiting, and Cancel closes the cursor at once (`db:exportCancel`). A kind without a cursor answers `EXPORT_UNSUPPORTED`. Errors cross IPC with a `code` (`NO_ROWS`, `NOTHING_TO_EXPORT`, `FILE_NOT_CHOSEN`, `UNKNOWN_FORMAT`, `EXPORT_UNSUPPORTED`) that the renderer translates. The file is written as `<name>.pyide-export` next to the target and renamed at the end; a cancelled or failed export removes it and leaves an existing file alone.
- Progress: an `ide.tasks` task "Exporting data to <file>" with "N of M rows" (a fraction for tables) and Cancel (`db:exportCancel`); then an `ok` notification with Show in Explorer, or an error notification.
- The Save dialog runs in main (`db:chooseExportFile({title, name, format})`, default `<Documents>/<name>.<ext>`), and `db:export({job, sourceId, format, options, file, table? | sql? | columns+rows?})` writes only a path that dialog returned in this session, like Export Settings. `PYIDE_DB_EXPORT_DIALOG` (tests only) names a JSON file `{save}` read at each dialog instead of showing it.
- Number fidelity: SQLite rows are read with BigInts, so a JS number is always a REAL. A REAL JS would print differently (2.0 as `2`, 1e20 as `100000000000000000000`, infinities) and an INTEGER past 2^53 now reach the renderer as `{$number: '2.0'}` / `{$number: '9007199254740993'}` (`fidelity()` in `main/db/kinds/sqlite.js`); the grid shows them right-aligned as numbers (`ide.database.cellText(v)`, `ide.database.isNumber(v)`), and Copy as CSV/TSV and every export use that text. `ide.database.query` results may therefore hold `{$number}` cells.
- Test: `verify/db-export.mjs` (ports 9395-9396; in CI): a SQLite fixture with REAL/INTEGER/TEXT/NULL/BLOB/Korean values; 2.0, 1e20 and 9007199254740993 in the grid and Copy as CSV; each format's exact bytes (CSV defaults and `;`/always/no header/BOM, TSV in UTF-16, JSON array and lines, SQL INSERT run back into a fresh database, Markdown); the `.xlsx` read back with a ZIP reader in the test (parts, CRCs, inline strings, numbers, header style); the result bar, grid menu and tree entry points; an `INSERT … RETURNING` result exported without running again; the Save-dialog guard; a 1,000,000-row table cancelled from the Background Tasks popup (no file, no partial file) and then exported in 1000 batches; and the Korean dialog without English or missing translations.

## Check RegExp

Plugin `regex-check` (`renderer/features/regex-check`, `main/features/regex-check.js`, `renderer/features/regex-check/regex_check.py`) is PyCharm's Check RegExp intention for Python.

- Where: on the pattern string literal of `re.compile`, `re.search`, `re.match`, `re.fullmatch`, `re.findall`, `re.finditer`, `re.sub`, `re.subn` or `re.split` (first argument or `pattern=`; raw, bytes and triple-quoted literals; not f-strings), Alt+Enter (lsp's Show Context Actions) lists Check RegExp. Find Action has it too (`RegExp.Check`, no shortcut); elsewhere it says to put the caret on a pattern.
- The popup (`#rxPopup`, in `#editorArea`, below the literal): the function, the flags I M S X A as toggle buttons (pressed as the call passes them, positionally or `flags=`, `re.I` or `re.IGNORECASE`, combined with `|`; toggling never edits the code), the pattern as Python decodes it, a sample text box (prefilled with the call's subject string when that is a plain literal; what you type is kept per pattern for the session), matches highlighted behind the text as you type (150 ms debounce, only the newest answer is shown), the status (Matches: N matches / Matches for `re.match` and `re.fullmatch` / No match / Syntax error at position N: Python's message / timed out), and the groups of the selected match (‹ › between matches): whole match, then each group numbered, named ones as `2 ‹month›`, with their spans, or (no match) for a group that did not take part. The character a syntax error points at is marked in the pattern line and in the editor. Esc (handled before the global keymap) closes it and returns to the editor; so do a click outside, an edit, or another tab.
- Python semantics: patterns are evaluated by `regex_check.py` in a local interpreter (the project's, or the first local one when the project's is remote), started with `-I -X utf8` so nothing in the project shadows `re`; JSON lines over stdio with non-ASCII escaped. The literal is decoded with `ast.literal_eval`, so escapes in non-raw strings and bytes patterns are exactly Python's; spans come back in UTF-16 units (Korean, emoji). Python-only syntax such as `(?P<name>…)`, `(?P=name)`, possessive quantifiers and atomic groups works. A check running longer than 3 s (catastrophic backtracking; `PYIDE_REGEX_TIMEOUT_MS` overrides) stops the helper and says so; the next check starts a new one. The helper also stops when the project closes or another opens and when the interpreter becomes another one (`regex-check:stop`), so a venv's python.exe does not stay locked; `regex-check:helperPid` (tests) gives its process id. At most 1000 matches are listed.
- Syntax colors: in Python editors, those pattern literals get decorations for groups, character classes, escapes, quantifiers, anchors, alternation and (with `re.X` or `(?x)`) comments (`rx-*` classes, light and dark colors). Non-raw literals are mapped through their escapes, so `"\\d"` colors as `\d`. Files without an `re.<function>(` call are not scanned.
- `ide.regexCheck`: `open(offset?)`, `close()`, `isOpen()`, `state()` (the last answer, current match, mode, flags), plus `scanLiterals`, `decodeLiteral`, `regexAt` helpers.
- Test: `verify/regex-check.mjs` (ports 9397-9398; in CI; fixture `verify/fixtures/regex-check`): Alt+Enter and the context action, typing with highlights and debounce, numbered and named groups and match navigation, Esc, kept samples, positional and `flags=` flags and toggling, syntax error positions in raw and non-raw literals (popup and editor), escapes and `re.match` anchoring, Korean and emoji spans, a possessive quantifier, a triple-quoted `re.X` pattern, a catastrophic pattern timing out and the helper recovering, a non-pattern string, the syntax colors, and the Korean popup without English or missing translations.

## Code vision (usages and authors)

`renderer/features/code-vision` (plugin `code-vision`, depends on `lsp`) shows PyCharm's code vision above every top-level `class`/`def` and every member of a top-level (or nested) class, as muted Monaco code lenses (items apart, without Monaco's ` | `): "N usages" ("no usages", "1 usage") and the Git author of the declaration's lines, the author of the newest commit with `+N` for the other authors ("John Smith +2") and ` *` when some lines are not committed. Decorated declarations get the lens above the first decorator. Clicking "N usages" opens the only usage, or a popup of the usages (file and line order, typing filters) with Open in Find Tool Window (the lsp plugin's Find Usages); clicking the author lists Annotate with Git Blame and the commits that last changed those lines, newest first (each opens in the Git log). Settings › Editor › Code Vision: `editor.codeVision.usages` and `editor.codeVision.authors`, both on by default. Nothing is shown or asked while Power Save Mode is on. Implementations and overrides are not lenses (Pyright 1.1.406 has no `textDocument/implementation`); the type-hierarchy plugin shows them as gutter markers instead (see Go to Implementation, Super Method and Test).

Cost (see Runtime performance): the declarations come from a line scanner (strings, comments, brackets, continuations; ~0.2 ms for 2,000 lines, cached per model version); providing lenses never asks the language server. Counts are `textDocument/references` through `ide.lsp.request` only, asked only for declarations in an editor's view (plus a line), only after edits pause (1.2 s since the last Python edit), one request at a time, and every edit cancels the run (Pyright 1.1.406 cannot stop a references request it has started, so that one finishes). Pyright's cost grows with the number of project files whose text holds the name (parsed and bound for every count: a name in 5,500 files took 15-22 s and grew Pyright to 2.4 GB), so a name found in more than 300 files is not counted automatically: its lens reads "usages" (사용 위치) with a tooltip giving the number of files, and a click counts it (status-bar task "Finding usages of X" after 0.5 s) and shows the count until an edit touches the name. The number of files comes from `main/features/code-vision.js` (plugin main module): a worker keeps a word -> files index of the project's .py/.pyi files (Find in Files' skip list, virtualenvs and folders marked Excluded skipped, at most 20,000 files, files over 2 MB left out; the words of each file's class statement bases are kept too, for the type-hierarchy gutter markers), checked again by mtime and size only when a renderer sends a file epoch the worker has not seen (the epoch moves when a Python file is saved, on `filesChanged` and on a project-watch event; code vision and type hierarchy each spell their own) or the Excluded folders changed; IPC `code-vision:fileCounts(root, names, epoch)` -> `{files, counts}`. A count still running after 2 s shows the uncounted lens until its answer arrives (then the count); such a name is then counted only on click for the session, unless it is in 30 files or fewer (then Pyright was busy, not the name expensive). A count stays valid while the occurrences of the name in every open Python document are unchanged, no Python file outside them changed on disk (project watcher, `filesChanged`) and the server, interpreter and project are the same, so typing inside a function body asks nothing and typing a call asks only for that name. Stale counts stay on screen until the new value arrives, and a declaration not counted yet keeps an empty lens, so lines do not move when counts come in. A language server that just started answers references before it has listed the project's files (too few usages), so counting starts after its first diagnostics (`lsp:diagnostics`; or after 15 s). Files with more than 300 declarations get lenses only within 200 lines of the view (Monaco moves every lens on each keystroke), and the window follows scrolling. Authors come from one `git blame --porcelain --contents` (the vcs plugin's `git:blame` channel) per file, HEAD and saved text, for files in view: it reruns after a save or when HEAD moves (vcs emits `vcsHeadChanged({root, head})`), never while typing, never in an untrusted project (Safe Mode), and a file Git does not track simply has no authors. `ide.codeVision`: `symbols(model)`, `lenses(model)` (`[{line, kind: 'usages'|'authors'|'pending', title, key}]`), `stats()` (`references`, `cancelled`, `blames`, `runs`, `uncounted`, `overBudget`, `running`, `scheduled`), `limits` (`manyFiles`, `fewFiles`, `budgetMs`), `refresh()`, `showUsages(arg)`, `showAuthors(arg)`. `verify/i18n-lib.mjs` skips `.codelens-decoration` in `englishLeaks` (author names are user data).

- Test: `verify/code-vision.mjs` (ports 9020-9022; in CI; fixture `verify/fixtures/code-vision`, made into a temp repository with commits by Jane Doe, 김철수 and John Smith). A: the declarations (decorators, nested classes, a multi-line `async def`, a `def` inside a string or an `if` excluded), declarations below the view not counted, every count equal to Pyright's references plus hand-checked ones (a Korean function name too), the authors, the usages popup, navigation and the Find tool window, one usage opening directly, the commits popup and Annotate, a call typed in another open file updating the count, no blame while editing and one after a save (`Jane Doe *`), a commit moving HEAD (`Test User +1`), 200 characters typed with no references request while typing and at most one per declaration in view after, nothing (requests, blames, lenses) in Power Save Mode and the recount after it, the settings page and both switches, an untracked file. B: Korean (사용 위치 4개, 사용 위치 없음, the settings page, no missing translations) in a folder that is not a repository (no blame). C: an untrusted repository in Safe Mode runs no git blame.
- `verify/code-vision-perf.mjs` (ports 9023-9024; not in CI, it times things): perf-runtime.mjs's 2,000-line git project and two-line typing run with code vision off and on in fresh instances (`verify/out/code-vision-perf/results.json`). Measured: references requests 3 when the file opens (the declarations in view), 0 while typing, 1 after; Pyright CPU until quiet 15.2 s off / 15.7 s on in one run and 18.3 s / 14.2 s in another (machine noise is larger than the difference), quiet after the last key 3.7-4.2 s either way, keydown-to-paint median 11-14 ms either way, completion while typing 30-55 ms median either way. Monaco's own per-keystroke lens work: +0.6 ms per key with the ~90 lenses of the window (+1.3 ms with all 428 before the window).
- `verify/pyright-budget.mjs` (ports 9373-9374; in CI): a temp project whose `area` is in 321 files: not counted (no references request, tooltip with 321 files), counted on click and uncounted again after an edit; a references request held 3.5 s shows the uncounted lens after ~2 s and the count when it comes, is not asked again for a name in 41 files and is for a name in one file; 300 files written on disk move a name over the limit; semantic highlighting sends no request while 35 characters are typed and colors the new class name after the pause; three token requests for one file answer tokens, superseded, tokens; two `ide.lsp.documentSymbols` calls share one tree. `type-hierarchy:subclassFiles` names a file for the words of its class statements' bases (`shapes.Shape`, `Generic[int]`) and not for `metaclass=`, a subscript or the class's own name; marking a folder Excluded drops its files from the word counts and unmarking brings them back. B: the uncounted lens, its tooltip and the settings page in Korean. Measured on H1's 5,686-file project (open one module, 3 launches each): Pyright CPU until quiet 45.6 s -> 18.0 s with usages on (usages off: 19.4 s in the same batch), working set 2,362 MB -> 534 MB, slowest completion in the 25 s after the open 21.1 s -> 5.5 s (the first completion after opening, same with usages off), median completion 1.8-3.0 s -> 14-17 ms.

## Semantic highlighting and Type Hierarchy

Pyright 1.1.406 answers neither `textDocument/semanticTokens` nor `textDocument/prepareTypeHierarchy`. Before building either feature, basedpyright 1.40.2 (MIT, the Pyright fork with semantic tokens and inlay hints) was measured against it in a temp folder with lsp.js's initialization, on the verify fixtures and perf-runtime.mjs's 2,000-line module (Electron's Node, 2026-10-22):

- Diagnostics: with basedpyright's own default rule set the fixtures got many new reports (lsp +5, breadcrumbs 0 → 26, refactor 0 → 59). With `typeCheckingMode: standard` sent in the `basedpyright.analysis` section the 16 fixture folders matched Pyright, except that unused-name hints carry a rule code and two Flask route functions are no longer "not accessed".
- Requests on verify/fixtures/lsp: hover and signature help text identical (inlay hints read them), definition, references, rename, document symbols and highlights, call hierarchy and workspace symbols identical. Code actions differ: basedpyright adds "import os" and "Add `# pyright: ignore[rule]`" quick fixes, which duplicate the lsp plugin's own. No `prepareTypeHierarchy` either ("Unhandled method").
- Cost: `initialize` ~270 ms both; first diagnostics of the 2,000-line file 1.3-1.4 s Pyright, 1.9-2.6 s basedpyright. Typing two lines with completion, hover and document symbols: Pyright 4.0-4.5 s CPU, completion median 29-39 ms (max 45-82 ms); basedpyright 7.2-8.8 s, median 53-85 ms, max 1.1-1.7 s (its completion alone costs 6.1 s against 2.7 s). Asking its semantic tokens in the pauses, as Monaco does: completion median 258 ms, max 2.1 s, 17.9 s CPU. Working set after typing 210-325 MB Pyright, 395-506 MB basedpyright. Its full semantic tokens of an analyzed file take ~40 ms, but after an edit they wait for the checker and every request behind them waits too.
- Size: 21.8 MB unpacked without source maps (Pyright 14.6 MB), 2.9 MB LZMA-compressed (Pyright 2.3 MB): +0.6 MB on the 103 MB installer to replace Pyright, +2.9 MB to ship both.
- The alternative, a sidecar that reads the syntax tree, colored the same as basedpyright 96.8% of the 8,823 names basedpyright colored in 7 files (the fixture, refactor_server.py, embed_server.py and the standard library's dataclasses, argparse, json.encoder and http.server), a different color for 1.3%, and colored 886 attribute uses basedpyright leaves plain (values of unknown type), at 50-170 ms per 1,400-2,800-line file in its own process.

Decision: keep Pyright, compute both features in PyIDE. No new dependency.

- Semantic highlighting: `renderer/features/semantic-highlight` (plugin `semantic-highlight`), `main/features/semantic-highlight.js` and `semantic_server.py`. A Monaco document semantic tokens provider for Python sends the text to the sidecar once the text has been unchanged for 400 ms (1,000 ms above 5,000 lines; Monaco throttles its requests instead of debouncing them, so it asked every few hundred ms while typing, each a full parse: 28-31 requests and 12.5-14.9 s of sidecar CPU for two typed lines in a 20,000-line file, now 1 request and 0.19 s); an edit meanwhile answers busy, which keeps the last colors moved with the edits. In main only the newest waiting request per window and file is kept while one runs; the one it replaces answers `{error: {code: 'superseded'}}`. The sidecar builds the module's scopes with `ast` and classifies every name: parameters, `self`/`cls`, classes, functions and methods (declarations and uses), properties and attributes, modules, constants (UPPER_CASE at module or class level), builtins, decorators (`@` and the name), type parameters (TypeVar assignments and PEP 695), keyword arguments, forward references in string annotations. Imported names resolve by reading, never importing, the other module: the project, Pyright's bundled typeshed stdlib stubs, then the interpreter's sys.path (cached by mtime; a missing module is looked for again after 10 s). Instances of classes it can see are followed through constructor calls, annotations and return annotations; an attribute of a value of unknown type is a method when called and a property otherwise. It runs in the project interpreter, else the newest other local interpreter (any Python 3.8+; remote interpreters are skipped; one that cannot start is skipped until the interpreter list changes), isolated (`-I -X utf8 -B`: nothing on PYTHONPATH, in the user site-packages or next to the script can shadow what it imports; PYTHONPATH and the user site-packages are still searched when it reads modules), and stops when the project closes or another opens and on an interpreter change. A file that does not parse (typing in progress) keeps its last colors, moved with the edits. Colors are theme rules for the token types `py-<type>[.<modifier>]` added to `pycharm-dark` and `pycharm-light` (`window.PYCHARM_LIGHT`, exposed by the settings plugin like core's `PYCHARM_DARK`), so Dark and Light each have PyCharm-like defaults. Settings › Editor › Color Scheme: the switch `editor.semanticHighlighting` (default on) and Dark/Light colors per kind in `editor.colorScheme.python` (`{dark: {kind: '#rrggbb'}, light: {...}}`, Restore Defaults). `ide.semanticHighlight`: `enabled()`, `tokens(model)` (decoded), `colors(theme)`, `stats()`.
- Type Hierarchy: `renderer/features/type-hierarchy` (plugin `type-hierarchy`, depends on `lsp` and `navigation-bar`), action `TypeHierarchy` (Ctrl+H in a Python editor, the editor context menu, Navigate › Type Hierarchy, which also lists Call Hierarchy now). The class is the one the name at the caret refers to (definition + document symbols, so a usage in another file works), else the innermost class around the caret. Supertypes read the class statement's bases from the editor's text (unsaved edits included) and ask for each one's definition; `Container[Dog]` is Container, keyword arguments are not bases, and a class without bases derives from `object`. Subtypes are the class's references that are a base in another class statement, across the project. Bases keep their declaration order; nodes load their children when expanded; double-click or Enter opens a class at its name; rows name the module (`animals.base`). It shows in navigation-bar's Hierarchy tool window (bottom, order 19) with Supertypes Hierarchy / Subtypes Hierarchy (the default) toggles; navigation-bar now hosts hierarchy kinds: `ide.navigationBar.showHierarchy(kind, item, mode?)` with `kind = {id, modes(), mode, head(mode), children(node, mode) -> [{item, sites?, siteUri?, leaf?}], sort?}`, and `kind()` names the one shown. `ide.typeHierarchy`: `show(mode?)`, `classAt`, `supertypes`, `subtypes`, `basesOf`, `gutterMarks(editor)` (see Go to Implementation, Super Method and Test).
- Tests: `verify/semantic-highlight.mjs` (ports 9030-9031; in CI): 51 names' kinds on `verify/fixtures/semantic-highlight/shapes.py`, the colors Monaco draws for each kind in Dark and Light, a custom color and Restore Defaults, the switch off (no requests while typing) and on, a function typed just now, a syntax error keeping the colors, the interpreter fallback and no-python answer, the settings page, Korean. `verify/type-hierarchy.mjs` (ports 9032-9033; in CI) on `verify/fixtures/type-hierarchy`: subtypes across files and their expansion, supertypes with multiple inheritance in order up to object, generics both ways, Ctrl+H from a usage, from inside a method and outside any class, unsaved edits, double-click and Enter, the context menu, Call Hierarchy and Type Hierarchy sharing the window, Korean.

## Visual Scripting (`*.pyflow`)

`renderer/features/visual-script` (plugin `visual-script`, depends on `run`; main module
`main/features/visual-script.js`) opens `*.pyflow` files as a Blueprint-style node graph that turns into readable
Python and runs like a script. `model.js` owns the file format and pure edit helpers, `nodes.js` the node types,
`codegen.js` the generator and `editor.js` the canvas; all four add to `window.PyideVisualScript` (`VS`), and
`index.js` loads them in that order, registers the `visual-script` text viewer and connects the graph to the IDE.

- File format (`model.js`): `{format: 1, nodes: [{id, type, x, y, props?, inputs?}], links: [{from: {node, pin}, to:
  {node, pin}}]}`, serialized deterministically (2-space indent, fixed key order, nodes by id number, links by
  from/to, defaults left out, unknown keys kept, final newline) so diffs stay readable; `VS.parse` throws readable
  errors with line and column, and an empty file is a new graph with one Start. A node id is letters A-Z and a-z,
  digits and _ only (the generated code names its locals after it); any other id is a parse error. The edit helpers (`addNode`,
  `removeNodes`, `moveNodes`, `setProp`, `setInputLiteral`, `connect`, `disconnect`, `disconnectAll`, `duplicate`,
  `pasteNodes`) return a new graph and never mutate; `connect` refuses incompatible types and cycles through
  `VS.lastError`.
- Node types (`nodes.js`): the registry (`VS.registerNodeType`, `nodeTypes`, `nodeType`, `pinsOf(node, graph)`) and the
  built-in library: Flow (Start, If, While, For Range, For Each, Break, Continue), Variables, Values, Math, Text (Join,
  conversions, Format Text), Input/Output (Print, Ask User), Lists, Functions (Define Function, Return, Call Function),
  Python (Call Python, Python Expression, Python Code) and Comment. Data types are any, number, int, string, bool,
  list, dict, none; `any` connects to everything and `int` to `number`. Pins a type makes from the node (Format Text
  fields, parameters) are user names and are never translated.
- Code (`codegen.js`, `VS.compile(graph, {fileName}) -> {code, map, errors}`): one statement per exec node under
  `def main():` and one `def` per Define Function, pure nodes inlined with only the parentheses Python needs,
  exec-node outputs in locals such as `answer_n7`, and `map[line]` -> node id for every generated line. Set Variable
  names are script variables (Blueprint's graph variables): a `def` that stores one (Set Variable, or a Python Code
  input or output of that name) declares it `global` after its `def` line, any function reads it, and a function
  parameter of the same name stays local. A value wired from a step must come from one that always runs before its
  consumer in the same function, earlier in an enclosing block (a loop's index and item only inside its body,
  `outputScope: 'body'`; a parameter anywhere in its function); otherwise the consumer's input pin gets an error that
  names the step and suggests Set Variable. Errors (no or two Start nodes, a required input left empty, a cycle,
  incompatible wires, an unset variable, an unknown function, invalid names, a value from a step that does not always
  run first, a function and a variable with the same name, Break/Continue outside a loop, Return outside a function)
  still produce code where the node in error raises `RuntimeError`; unreachable steps are a warning. Python Code keeps
  lines that start inside a string exactly (no re-indent, no tab expansion; `c.rawLine`). An Any field keeps a number
  with a leading zero or more than 15 integer digits as the text typed (`01012345678`); an integer a double cannot hold
  in a Number or Integer field is stored as its digits and written as typed. Text from the file reaches the code only
  validated: node ids and stored parameter names must be identifiers, a stored default is pasted only when it is a
  plain literal (`VS.isPythonLiteral`; otherwise that argument becomes required), and a Format Text spec cannot hold
  `{`. Python Code and Python Expression stay the visible escape hatch.
- The tab (`editor.js`): a `text: true` viewer over the file's document, and the document is the only state: every user
  operation (a drag, a wire, a literal, a paste) is one edit between undo stack marks, so dirty state, Ctrl+S, close
  prompts, Local History, crash recovery and Open as Text come from the core, and Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y / Ctrl+Shift+Z on the
  canvas are the document's undo and redo. Another change of the document is parsed and drawn again; invalid JSON dims
  the canvas and shows the error with Open as Text. HTML nodes over an SVG layer of cubic wires on a 16 px grid; exec
  pins are triangles, data pins circles coloured by type (`--vs-type-*`), headers tinted by category (`--vs-cat-*`);
  inline literal fields and props in the node body; comments are notes behind nodes. Pan with the middle or right
  button or Space+drag, wheel zoom 25% to 200%, Ctrl+0, Fit. The first open fits the graph, never above 100%, and a
  graph that fits at 100% stays at 100%. The view is kept per file in `visualScript.view`.
- Editing: double-click, Tab or right-click › Add Node… opens the palette (search by English or Korean title and
  keywords; a whole title first, then a whole keyword or Python name, then a title's start, then any match, ties in
  category order: 입력 and input put Ask User first); a wire dropped on empty space offers only the types that fit and wires the pick. Drag
  between pins to connect (an incompatible pin turns the wire red and the status line says why), Alt+click a wire or
  pin to delete its wires, click / Shift / Ctrl / box selection, drag to move (16 px snap, Alt for none), Delete,
  Ctrl+C / Ctrl+X / Ctrl+V (JSON as `application/x-pyide-pyflow` and text), Ctrl+D, Ctrl+A. These keys are the
  canvas's own and fire only while it has focus. Problems are compiled 300 ms after each change: red outline, badge,
