  and "No problems" / "N problems" in the status line (click: select the first). A field commits when it is left;
  an IDE shortcut pressed while typing (Ctrl+S, Ctrl+F4, Shift+F10) commits it first and keeps the keyboard in it with
  the same selection, so Ctrl+F4 asks to save the typed text. Keys a text field handles itself (Ctrl+Z/Y/A/X/C/V, word
  moves) stay the field's.
- Run (`index.js`): configuration type `visual-script` (`forFile` claims `*.pyflow`, so Shift+F10 on the tab,
  Ctrl+Shift+F10, the run widget, Run › Run, Rerun, Stop and stdin work as for a script; the run plugin's
  `currentPyFile` accepts a viewer tab whose file another type's `forFile` claims). A script with problems is not run:
  the Run console lists them with their nodes and the canvas selects the first. Otherwise main writes the code to
  `<userData>/visual-script/<sha1 of the lower-cased path>/<name>_pyflow.py` (never named like a module, which Python
  would import instead of the real one; other .py files in that folder are removed) and runs it in the configuration's interpreter with the .pyflow's folder as working directory
  and on PYTHONPATH. Traceback links into the generated file open the .pyflow and select the node instead, and a
  failed run marks the innermost frame's node with the exception line (`markRuntimeError`).
- The tab's toolbar: Run (this script as its context configuration), Show Code (a read-only Monaco pane beside the
  canvas that recompiles as the graph changes; selected nodes highlight their lines, a click on a line selects its
  node; remembered in `visualScript.showCode`) and Export as Python… (`<name>.py` next to the script, or, when that name would hide a
  module the code imports, a standard module or an installed package (`VS.symbols.packages()`), a name the user picks
  in a dialog that suggests `<name>_script.py` and refuses a name that still hides a module; asks before
  overwriting with a Local History checkpoint, then opens it; also Find Action `VisualScript.ExportPython`).
  Project tree › New › Visual Script (after Python Package in file-templates' submenu) makes Start wired to Print
  "Hello" and opens it. The file icon is `nodeGraph` (`tools/file-icons/nodeGraph*.svg`).
- `tab.visualScript` (editor API): `graph()`, `selection()`, `select(ids)`, `centerOn(id)`, `fit()`, `view()`,
  `setView(v)`, `errors()`, `markErrors(list)`, `markRuntimeError(nodeId, message)`, `onChange(fn)`, `onSelect(fn)`,
  `toolbar`, `side`, `codePane()`.
- 200 nodes pan, zoom and drag at 60 fps with about 6 ms of main-thread work per frame (drag uses transforms, the grid
  is its own compositor layer). The node layer is its own compositor layer while the view rests and from opening; a
  zoom drops that until the scale has rested 400 ms, so text is drawn sharp at the new scale. A pan, or Space held,
  puts a sheet with the grab cursor over the canvas (`.vs-pan-sheet`, styled in editor.js) instead of restyling the
  graph, so a pan of 300 nodes starts within 24 ms (median; before 41 ms, with a 50-90 ms long task at the start and
  end of a pan under load). While Space is held the nodes get no hover and a right-click shows the canvas menu, since
  the sheet covers them. The zoom label is written only when the scale changes. Test: `verify/perf-visual-script.mjs`
  (port 9067, not in CI: it times things): middle, right and Space + drag pans and pans after a zoom, first frame and
  the frame after release (median < 34 ms, worst < 50 ms) and no long task.
- Tests: `verify/visual-script-codegen.mjs` (pure Node: a dozen graphs run with the system Python, traceback mapping,
  every error, parse errors, determinism, immutability, Korean coverage, 150 random graphs, 250 random expression
  trees against their parenthesized form, py_compile of every generated file); `verify/visual-script-editor.mjs`
  (ports 9090-9094: every canvas interaction with real CDP input, save/reload, Open as Text, close prompt, broken
  files, 200-node frame times, Light theme); `verify/visual-script.mjs` (ports 9095-9096: Run by Shift+F10, Run menu,
  Rerun, the tab button and Ctrl+Shift+F10 with stdin, compile-error refusal, runtime error mark and link, Show Code
  both ways, Export and overwrite, New › Visual Script, icon, readable labels at 100% in both themes, and a Korean
  session without English in the tab, palette, menus and dialogs). Fixtures: `verify/fixtures/visual-script`.

- Project and package functions (`symbols.js`, `python/vs_symbols.py`, `main/features/visual-script.js`): the palette's
  Project category lists the project's modules as branches (`pkg.sub.mod` from the root, or from `src/` in a src
  layout): top-level functions, `New Class` (the constructor) and `Class.method` entries, with the signature and the
  docstring's first line as tooltip; typing searches them too. Typing a module name and a dot (`math.`, `random.`,
  `pandas.`) or Browse Package… loads an installed module. A pick makes a Python Function (`py.function`), Create
  Object (`py.new`) or Python Method (`py.method`) node that stores the signature in its props (`{module, name,
  params: [{name, kind?, default?, annotation?}], returns?, async?}`, plus `class` and `kind` for methods), so a graph
  compiles without the index: one input per parameter typed from its annotation (optional when it has a default,
  `*args` a list, `**kwargs` a dict), `result` typed from the return annotation. Codegen writes `from geo.figures import
  area` and `area(2)`, or `import geo.figures` and `geo.figures.area(2)` when two modules give the same name or the
  name is taken; an empty optional argument is left out and later ones go by keyword; async functions run through
  `asyncio.run`. When the index has a different signature the node says "The function's parameters changed" with
  Update (keeps the wires of parameters that still exist); a function that is gone is a problem ("geo.figures.area no
  longer exists.") and Run refuses.
- The index (`visual-script:symbols`): .py files read with `ast` in the project interpreter (`-I`, never imported),
  skipping virtual environments, `node_modules`, hidden, cache and build folders, files over 1 MB, at most 3000 files
  and 20 s; cached in main per project by path, mtime and size, read when a .pyflow first opens and refreshed after a
  .py save (debounced), a file change and an interpreter change. The reply lists the modules of skipped files and
  whether the scan was truncated; only a complete index says a project module's function is gone, and a function in a
  skipped module is never gone (its status is unknown). A package module (`visual-script:module`) is imported
  in the project interpreter with `inspect`, only when it resolves outside the project folder (stdlib, site-packages:
  main refuses names of the project's own top-level files and folders, and the helper checks every step's location
  before importing), with a 10 s limit, cached per interpreter. Safe Mode: nothing is read or loaded, and every
  channel refuses an untrusted project.
- `VS.compile` also returns `nodeLines[nodeId]` (the node's own generated lines, imports left out) and
  `pinExpr[nodeId][pin]` (the Python expression of a data output once its node ran) for the debugger.
- Tests: `verify/visual-script-codegen.mjs` adds the three node types run against the fixture project with the
  system Python (optional, keyword, `*args`/`**kwargs`, classes, methods, dataclass, async, name clashes and aliases,
  packages), pins and annotation types, stale and missing symbols, Update, `pinExpr` and `nodeLines`.
  `verify/visual-script-project.mjs` (ports 9086-9087, fixture `verify/fixtures/visual-script-project`): the index's
  modules and what it leaves out, palette branches, tooltips and picks with real keys, a graph of project calls run with
  real output, math/random/json through a dotted search with the same output as Python, Browse Package…, main refusing
  project modules, a signature edited and saved (warning, Update, run), a removed function refusing the run, Korean,
  and Safe Mode (no entries, main refuses).
- Debugging (`debug.js`): Shift+F9, the Debug button and Run › Debug on a script's tab debug it like a Python file
  (the type's `debugTarget` compiles the graph and the debugger runs the generated file with debugpy; Rerun compiles
  again). Steps take breakpoints (F9 or Ctrl+F8 on the selected node, the dot on its top-left corner, node menu ›
  Add Breakpoint; Edit Condition… adds a Python condition); they are kept per script in the
  `visualScript.breakpoints` and `visualScript.breakpointConditions` settings, never in the file, and become line
  breakpoints on each node's first generated line (`nodeLines`) at start and whenever toggled. While paused the
  script's node is outlined and brought into view, Frames name the script and node (`main, demo.pyflow: Print (n3)`), Variables name a step's output after its node (`For Range (n3) = 0` for `index_n3`; the generated name is in the tooltip and is what Watches, Evaluate and conditions use; Set Variable names stay as they are),
  Step Into a project function opens its .py and coming back outlines the node again, and resting the pointer on a
  data pin or wire shows its value in a bubble beside the node (a wire's by the pointer), never over the paused node's
  title (`pinExpr` evaluated in the selected frame; only locals and side-effect-free pure nodes, Python Expression is
  never run again). An uncaught exception marks its node. F9 is Resume while paused.
  The debugger's hook: `ide.debugger.registerGeneratedSource({owns, breakpoints, label, variableLabel})` (a generated file's
  breakpoints and frame labels come from its owner and a stop there does not open it), `ide.debugger.stopped()`,
  `ide.debugger.sendBreakpoints(path)`; Shift+F9 debugs a viewer tab whose file a run type with `debugTarget`
  claims; `debugFile(file, {rerun})`.
- Run and Debug put the project root, `src/` in a src layout, and the script's folder on PYTHONPATH.
- `tab.visualScript` also has `revealNode(id)`, `setDebugState(state)`, `onBreakpointClick(fn)`, `addNodeMenu(fn)`,
  `setValueProvider(fn)`.
- Test: `verify/visual-script-debug.mjs` (ports 9082-9084, fixture `verify/fixtures/visual-script-debug`).

- Python files as visual scripts (`python/py2flow.py`, `visual-script:fromPython(req: {root, interp, source})` ->
  `{ok, graph, lines: {nodeId: [first, last line]}, statements, fallback}` or `{ok: false, error: {message, line?},
  reason?}`; the text is parsed with `ast` in the project interpreter, `-I`, never run, and refused in Safe Mode): Show as
  Visual Script (`VisualScript.ShowAsFlow`, the editor context menu of a .py file, the project tree) opens a read-only
  canvas of the file in a tab to the right (`mountEditor(host, {..., readOnly: true})`: no Add Node, read-only fields,
  nodes select and the view pans and zooms, but a drag, a wire, Delete, a paste, Ctrl+Z or a double-click change
  nothing). It follows the document 500 ms after typing stops; a syntax error dims the last graph and the note names
  the line. Selecting a node puts the caret on its first line in the .py editors, and a click in the editor selects the
  node of its line. Closing the file closes its preview. Convert to Visual Script… (`VisualScript.ConvertFromPython`,
  the preview's button, the tree) writes `<name>.pyflow` next to the file (asks before overwriting, with a Local
  History checkpoint), opens it and says how many statements stayed Python Code.
- What becomes nodes: a statement becomes nodes only when the generated code does what it did, otherwise it is a
  Python Code node with its own text (comments in it kept), so a converted file prints and exits as before. Set Variable
  names are script variables, so a function's own variables become nodes only when no other scope uses those names and
  the function is not recursive; functions with defaults, decorators, `*args`, `yield`, `global` or a name the
  generated code uses (`main`) stay Python Code. A loop variable used only in its body is wired from the loop; one used
  after it starts the body with Set Variable. Module code runs inside `main()`, so names a Python Code node binds there
  (import, def, class) that a function made of nodes reads, or a `global` statement names, are declared `global` in a
  Python Code node right after Start. `input()` and calls of functions made of nodes become steps of their own (Ask
  User, Call Function) only when nothing with a side effect would then run in another order (`x = int(input())`, not
  `total += f(x)`). Other calls are Call Python without a module (`bump()`, `p.move(1)`, `print(a, b)`) or a Python
  Expression. Float literals that Number fields would print differently (`1.0`, `1e20`) and integers beyond 2^53 stay
  Python Expressions. Standalone comment lines and docstrings become Comment nodes. Layout: steps in a column per block
  depth, the values wired into a step to its left.
- If has a third run-order output, Done, as loops have: the steps after the if statement.
- Java visual scripts (`*.jflow`, `VS.languageOf(path)`): the same graph format and canvas, compiled by `VS.compile(graph, {language: 'java'})` to one Java class named after the file (`VS.javaClassName`: `demo.jflow` -> `Demo`). Each node type has `java` hooks beside its `compile` ones (nodes.js); codegen keeps everything else (wires, names, problems, the run-before checks, the line map) and only changes literals (`5L`, `2.5`, `null`), braces, errors (`if (true) throw new RuntimeException(…)`, so nothing after it is an unreachable statement) and the file around it: script variables are `static Object` fields, functions `static Object name(Object …)` with the body in `if (true) { … } return null;`, Start is `main`. Values are Object and every operation goes through the class `Rt` appended to the file (`VS.javaRuntime`), which follows Python: int as Long and float as Double (`/` always a float, `//` and `%` floor like Python, `**`), `+` on text and lists, `and`/`or` giving an operand, comparisons across int and float, truthiness, `print` with Python's spelling of floats (`0.30000000000000004`, `1e-05`, `12345678500.0`), `True`/`False`/`None` and list repr, `input` (EOFError at the end of input), `int()`/`float()` from text, negative indexes, ranges, and Format Text fields with conversions and the common format specs (fill, align, width, `.Nf`, `d`, `%`, `e`); its errors read like Python's (`ZeroDivisionError: division by zero`, `IndexError`, `TypeError: unsupported operand type(s) for +: 'str' and 'int'`). Java Expression (`java.expr`) and Java Code (`java.code`: inputs as Object locals in its own block, outputs declared before it) are Java's escape hatches; a node only the other language has (Python Code, Call Python, project functions in a .jflow; Java nodes in a .pyflow) is a problem at that node. Java keywords and the names the class uses (`main`, `args`, `Rt`, the class name) are refused as names. The palette of a .jflow lists the node types with `java` hooks and no Project or Packages category; Show Code shows the Java class.
- Run (`java-visual-script` configuration, `forFile` claims `*.jflow`; fields: file, program arguments, environment): main writes `<userData>/visual-script/<sha1 of the path>/<name>_jflow.java` (`visual-script:writeJava`) and runs it in source-file mode (`visual-script:runJava`: `java -Dfile.encoding=UTF-8 -Dstdout.encoding=UTF-8 -Dstderr.encoding=UTF-8 <file> args…` in the script's folder) with a JDK 11 or newer found by `visual-script:java` (JAVA_HOME, a JDK under the install root `~/.pyide`, `java` on PATH, then Program Files' Java, Eclipse Adoptium, Microsoft, Zulu, Amazon Corretto and BellSoft folders). A compile error (`<file>:12: error: …`) or the first stack frame of an uncaught exception that is not in `Rt` marks its node with the message, and the console line gets a link that selects it. Debugging a .jflow is not available yet. Export as Java… writes `<ClassName>.java` next to the script (the file name javac wants for the public class), asking before it overwrites. Project tree › New › Visual Script (Java) makes a `.jflow` that prints Hello. The icon is `nodeGraph`, as for `.pyflow`.
- Tests: `verify/visual-script-java.mjs` (pure Node with the system Python and a JDK: programs converted by py2flow.py, compiled to Python and to Java and run with the same input; the Java version must print, exit and fail like the Python one; language-only nodes, keywords, Java Expression and Java Code, the stack trace's line map); `verify/visual-script-java-ui.mjs` (ports 9058-9059: open, palette, Show Code, Run with stdin, a runtime error's mark and link, Export as Java, New › Visual Script (Java), Korean).
- Tests: `verify/visual-script-from-python.mjs` (pure Node with the system Python: programs converted, compiled and run
  with the same output and exit code as the original, which statements stay Python Code, line ranges, comments, layout,
  a syntax error, and every fixture .py of the suites converting without problems); `verify/visual-script-preview.mjs`
  (ports 9068-9069, fixture `verify/fixtures/visual-script-preview`: the menus, the read-only canvas with real input,
  both ways of selecting, typing and a syntax error, Convert and the overwrite question, closing, Korean).

`verify/visual-script-editor.mjs`, `verify/visual-script.mjs`, `verify/visual-script-project.mjs` and `verify/visual-script-debug.mjs` take `--viewport=1008x681` to emulate CI's 1024x768 screen (inner 1008x681, canvas 647x263); run both sizes before pushing changes to the editor.

## Monaco 0.57 and IME input

PyIDE uses monaco-editor 0.57 (pinned exactly in package.json) through its AMD build in `min/vs`, loaded by `renderer/index.html` (`min/vs/loader.js`) and `renderer/core/ide.js`. Since 0.53 that build is made by Vite: `vs/editor/editor.main` appends its own `editor.main.css` and sets `self.MonacoEnvironment.getWorker`, which starts the editor, JSON, CSS, HTML and TypeScript workers from the hashed scripts in `min/vs/assets` (a blob worker that calls `importScripts` on their `file:` URL). PyIDE keeps that `getWorker` and does not define `MonacoEnvironment` itself. Monaco's message bundle for the UI language (`min/vs/nls/lang/ko.js`) is a plain script that sets globals, so `ide.js` adds it as a `<script>` before it requires `editor.main`. (Monaco's `vs/nls` loader plugin would `require()` it as an AMD module, which never resolves.) Internal AMD modules (`vs/...` other than `vs/editor/editor.main`) are no longer reachable. Inline completion providers must implement `disposeInlineCompletions` (it was `freeInlineCompletions`).

Editors take keyboard and IME input through the browser's EditContext API (`editContext`, on by default in Chromium). The focused element is `div.native-edit-context`, not a textarea (`textarea.ime-text-area` is only Monaco's IME helper). While a Korean syllable is being composed, Monaco draws it in the line itself, so the line keeps its syntax colors. Monaco 0.52 drew a textarea over the line in the plain foreground color until the syllable was committed. Dialogs treat `.native-edit-context` like a textarea: Enter there is a new line, and a dialog whose first field is an editor starts there.

The installer leaves out Monaco's `dev`, `esm` and its nested `node_modules`, the AMD worker copies in `min/vs/language/*/*.worker.js` (the `assets` copies are used), the message bundles other than Korean, and `dompurify` (bundled into the min build).

Test: `verify/ime-korean.mjs` (port 9170; in CI) on `verify/fixtures/ime-korean`. It drives IME compositions with CDP (`Input.imeSetComposition` per jamo, `Input.insertText` to commit) in the middle of a colored Python line. While a syllable is being composed, each token keeps its color, nothing is drawn over it and the syllable shows in the line. It also checks the committed text and caret, several syllables, Backspace inside a composition (a 229 "Process" key and a shorter composition), a cancelled composition, undo, a composition replacing a selection, and Korean in the find widget, a notebook cell and the Python Console input. Clips of the line before and while a syllable is being composed are saved to `verify/out/ime-korean/before.png` and `composing.png`.

## Stress tests

Five suites (in CI) attack the features of the last releases with hostile and extreme input, offline against stand-ins:
- `verify/stress-minecraft-content.mjs` (ports 8610-8619): malformed, truncated, zip-slip and zip-bomb archives, hostile modpack indexes, a failing or hanging stand-in Modrinth, dependency cycles, concurrent Market installs.
- `verify/stress-minecraft-runtime.mjs` (ports 8620-8629): reserved and invalid names, ports out of range or taken, Start, Stop and Rerun clicked 20 times, damaged server descriptions, HotSwap with compile errors and 30 saves a second, 50 servers.
- `verify/stress-editor-languages.mjs` (ports 8630-8639): 5 MB files, 100k-character lines, 10k-deep JSON, XML and HTML, exploding Emmet abbreviations, 50 carets, crashing, hanging or garbage-printing Prettier and ESLint, unreachable SQL sources. Freezes are measured.
- `verify/stress-terminal-ports-run.mjs` (port 8640): venv paths with `& ^ % ! ( ) ; $ ’` and 200+ characters (no command injection), broken activate scripts, 20 terminals at once, bad port numbers and misbehaving tunnel tools, 50 saves for Rerun on Save, hostile requirements files, exe names that climb out of dist.
- `verify/markdown-large.mjs` (port 8650): a Markdown file over 1 MB (`MD_LIVE_LIMIT` in the viewers plugin) is not re-rendered while typing; Refresh Preview renders it, and a small file previews live.

Downloads (`main/installer/servers.js`) end with an error after 60 s without data instead of undici's 300 s.
